# A. The case on paper


## The documents-only case: Art. 1898 proved element by element on paper (their Answer, their Exh. 5, Exh. B/E/F/H), the one gap (pleaded 1980s payment) and three paper moves to close it

### The documents-only case

Civil Case No. 26-360 · 19 October 2026 · internal; for Jonathan and counsel; nothing filed or sent. No witness is relied on below. Every fact is on a paper already in the record, in the defendants' own verified Answer, in the defendants' own exhibits, or in a public record to be obtained by certified copy. Rules quoted from lawphil (2019 Revised Rules on Evidence, A.M. No. 19-08-15-SC). Tags: [VERIFY] · [OBTAIN] · [COUNSEL].

> 

**The answer.** On paper alone, every element of Article 1898 is proved, and two of the three by the defendants' own papers. Article 1898: if the agent contracts "exceeding the scope of his authority, and the principal does not ratify the contract, it shall be void if the party with whom the agent contracted is aware of the limits of the powers granted by the principal."

1. **Outside the scope.** Paragraph 7 allows sale only to holders of Llamanzares contracts to sell or those who paid Llamanzares. The defendants' own receipt exhibit has 111 slips and not one from Llamanzares or Violeta. The first is dated 2003. The only slip marked "Down payment" is dated 6 January 2005 and signed for Cesar.

2. **Not ratified.** The principals disowned the power in 2005, eleven years before the deed. No paper signed by any principal approves the sale. The "confirmation" is signed by Cesar alone.

3. **Aware of the limits.** The power, paragraph 7 included, has been on the title since 21 June 1996. Their verified Answer says "Gloria checked the SPA."

**The one gap.** Their Answer pleads a ₱3,000 down payment to Llamanzares "in the 1980s." No paper supports it, and their own receipt exhibit contradicts it. It is their defence, and "burden of proof never shifts" (Rule 131, §1). Part 3 lists three moves that turn the gap into a nail, all by paper.

F); and that the limits were annotated on the title in 1996 and the defendant avers she checked the instrument (Exh. B; Answer, ¶44[h]), the Deed of Absolute Sale of 29 September 2016 is void (Art. 1898).

#### 3. The gap, and three paper moves to close it

**The gap.** Answer ¶40: "in the 1980s they were told they could buy the area they occupied … they paid a downpayment of Php3,000.00 in installments." No contract to sell, no Llamanzares receipt, no Violeta receipt. Their own best paper, Exhibit 5, puts the down payment in 2005, paid to Cesar's side. It is their affirmative defence; the burden to prove it is theirs and "never shifts" (Rule 131, §1).

1. **Ask for the Llamanzares paper.** Salvador swears that in about 1992 he and Cesar went to Llamanzares's house "para kunin ang mga papeles ng lupain" (S15). If any contract to sell or Llamanzares receipt for the Balanes exists, it came into the agent's side's hands then. A subpoena for Salvador to bring the Llamanzares papers when he testifies puts it to the test. If they are not produced: "evidence willfully suppressed would be adverse if produced" (Rule 131, §3[e]). [COUNSEL: timing and form; this is a witness subpoena, not a discovery motion]

2. **The Registry's own pattern.** Sales made during Llamanzares's administration appear as deeds annotated on the mother title: De Arao and King 1980, Yasir 1982, De Leon 1983, Cruz and Iligan 1988, Vergara 1990 [VERIFY dates on the certified memoranda; only De Leon's (deed 1982, presented 1 Feb 1983) is checked]. A certified copy of those memorandum pages shows what a Llamanzares purchase looked like on paper. No Balane entry appears before 2021. [OBTAIN: certified copies of the T-4497, T-32916 and T-32917 memorandum pages]

- **Summary judgment, on paper.** Annex "A" of the draft Motion to Resolve now carries three more facts from their own papers: the 6 January 2005 "Down payment" slip, the 2006 surveyor's slip calling the land the heirs' lot, and Answer ¶44(h). The one controverted matter left on Article 1898 is the pleaded 1980s payment, which has no paper.

- **Trial.** If the motion is not granted, the same papers are the memorandum's Section III, and the witnesses only confirm what the papers already show.

#### *Zschoche v. Balane*, Civil Case No. 26-360, MTC Mercedes — the provisions each side will put in front of Judge Cu, and which words she will key in on

**Prepared for counsel · 13 October 2026 · internal, not a pleading.** Every provision below was read in the official text this week (lawphil.net copies of RA 386, PD 1529, RA 11576, RA 7279 and the Rules). Quoted words are exact. Where the text is paraphrased it says so.

**Why this document exists.** In seven signed writings this judge has cited no case. She cites the statute or rule by section and date, writes "Considering that," and disposes. The case will therefore be decided on which section's words fit the exhibit in front of her. This is the map of those collisions: for each battleground, the defendants' provision, ours, the exact words she will read, the exhibit that settles the fit, and the expected result on her method.

---


**Document:** War of the Statutes  
**Source:** `WAR_OF_THE_STATUTES_2026-10.md`  
**Section:** 0. The reading order she will follow

---

#### 0. The reading order she will follow

A rule-first judge writing a decision in an accion reivindicatoria will go down this list in this order. Our memorandum should be built in the same order, one section per line, each with the article pinpointed and the exhibit page beside it.

| # | Question she must answer | The controlling text | The exhibit she will hold against it |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Does this court have jurisdiction and is the attack on the title direct? | BP 129 §33(3) as amended by RA 11576; PD 1529 §48 | ARP 00256 (assessed value); her own 6 Feb 2026 order reciting the caption "Cancellation of TCT No. 079-2021002126" |
| 2 | What authority did the agent have, in writing? | Civil Code Arts. 1874, 1878 ¶5, 1877 | Exh. E ¶7; Exh. B, entry of 21 Jun 1996 |
| 3 | Was the sale within that authority? | Arts. 1898, 1900, 1902 | Answer ¶¶40, 43 (tenancy); Exh. 5 (first payment 2003; no Llamanzares paper); Salvador S31 |
| 4 | Is the result void or merely unenforceable? | Art. 1874 ("shall be void") vs Art. 1403(1); Art. 1409(7); Art. 1410 | The same |
| 5 | Does revocation or its non-registration change anything? | Arts. 1921, 1922; PD 1529 §§52, 64 | Exh. F; Exh. G; Exh. B (no revocation annotated; SPA annotated 1996) |
| 6 | Was the buyer in good faith, and until when must she have been? | Arts. 526, 527, 528; PD 1529 §§51, 53 | Their Annex "1" (2020 SPA); King's 9 Jul 2021 pleading; Elevado's Aug 2021 SPA pull; the 15 Sep 2021 "my property" SPA |
| 7 | Could a specific lot be titled to her at all? | Arts. 496, 1878 ¶¶12, 15 | Exh. B, Entry 2021003236; Def. Exh. 1 p. 2 (survey 8 Sep 2020, approved 6 Apr 2021) |
| 8 | What happens to the improvements? | Arts. 448, 546, 548 (theirs) vs 449, 450, 451, 452, 546 ¶1, 1678 (ours) | Answer ¶¶40, 43 (lessees); Exh. 5 p. 1 (2006 survey receipt); permits return |
| 9 | Fruits and damages | Art. 544 (theirs) vs 549, 2208, 2229, 2232 (ours) | Answer ¶27 (rooms rented); Exh. J (demand received 22 Aug 2025); the five dated documents |
| 10 | The counterclaim | Arts. 19, 20, 21, 2217, 2219 (theirs) vs Art. 2208 and Rule 6 §7 | The judgment on the title |

**Section:** 1. Jurisdiction and the "collateral attack" gate

---

#### 1. Jurisdiction and the "collateral attack" gate

| | Text | Who cites it |
|---|---|---|
| **BP 129 §33(3), as amended by RA 11576 (30 Jul 2021)** | First-level courts have "exclusive original jurisdiction in all civil actions which involve title to, or possession of, real property or any interest therein where the assessed value of the property or any interest therein does not exceed Four hundred thousand pesos (P400,000.00) exclusive on [sic] interest, damages of whatever kind, attorney's fees, litigation expenses and costs." | Both; she will check it first |
| **PD 1529 §48** | "A certificate of title shall not be subject to collateral attack. It cannot be altered, modified, or canceled except in a direct proceeding in accordance with law." | Defense (10th defense, ¶46) |

**What she keys in on.** The words "direct proceeding." The Complaint's caption, which she herself recited in her 6 February 2026 order, prays for "Cancellation of Transfer Certificate of Title No. 079-2021002126." That is a direct proceeding against that certificate. §48 bars only attacks made in passing in some other case. The defense's §48 point has a real target only if we attack **Special Proceeding No. 2156** (the replacement duplicate) or **TCT 079-2021002127** (the owners' own title): we attack neither. The partition's nullity is pleaded as the ground for the cancellation prayed, not as a side-swipe at another title.

**Our reply in one sentence.** "This is the direct proceeding §48 requires; the only certificate attacked is the one named in the caption."

**Expected result.** Jurisdiction found: ARP No. GR-2023-II-07-001-00256 (Exh. D) states the current assessed value as **₱257,670.00** (market value ₱4,294,420 at a 6% assessment level, tax effectivity 2024; the "Previous Assessed Value" line shows ₱200,750), under the ₱400,000 threshold. §48 not engaged. On the plaintiff suing alone: Art. 487 ("Any one of the co-owners may bring an action in ejectment") "covers all kinds of action for the recovery of possession," including accion reivindicatoria, and "a co-owner may bring such an action, without the necessity of joining all the other co-owners as co-plaintiffs" (*Catedrilla v. Lauron*, G.R. No. 179011, 15 Apr 2013, quoting Tolentino), so Geraldine and Marcia are not necessary parties and relief may run in favor of all three.

**Document:** War of the Statutes  
**Source:** `WAR_OF_THE_STATUTES_2026-10.md`  
**Section:** 2. The authority: what the agent could do, in writing

---

#### 2. The authority: what the agent could do, in writing

| | Text | Who cites it |
|---|---|---|
| **Art. 1874** | "When a sale of a piece of land or any interest therein is through an agent, the authority of the latter shall be in writing; otherwise, the sale shall be void." | Both. Defense: the SPA is the writing. Ours: the writing excludes her. |
| **Art. 1878 ¶5** | A special power of attorney is necessary "To enter into any contract by which the ownership of an immovable is transmitted or acquired either gratuitously or for a valuable consideration." | Both |
| **Art. 1877** | "An agency couched in general terms comprises only acts of administration, even if the principal should state that he withholds no power or that the agent may execute such acts as he may consider appropriate, or even though the agency should authorize a general and unlimited management." | Ours (against ¶8 of the SPA and against the 1991 and 2012 instruments) |

**What she keys in on.** Art. 1874 says *the authority* must be in writing; she will read the writing. Paragraph 7 of Exh. E (Annex B, p. 3) is the writing: "in favor of third persons in possession of CONTRACTS TO SELL executed by the previous administrator, MR. BEN LLAMANZARES and/or to those who have paid their downpayments or have made partial payments therefor to the latter … after prior consultation with us." Two things in the writing limit the power: the class and the consultation. Nothing in the writing mentions partition or subdivision; paragraph 8's "any and every act … requisite, necessary or proper" is the general language Art. 1877 confines to administration.

**The defense's reading.** "Read as a whole, it authorizes negotiation, terms, documents and proceeds; that is a power to sell" (Answer ¶39). True, for the class.

**Our reading.** Concede the power to complete a sale; hold the class and the condition. The strict-construction rule (*Bautista-Spille*, G.R. 214057) is the only case worth one sentence here, and only if she wants one.

**Expected result.** She finds the authority limited to the class, after consultation, because the words are on the page.

---


**Document:** War of the Statutes  
**Source:** `WAR_OF_THE_STATUTES_2026-10.md`  
**Section:** 3. Was the sale within the authority? Art. 1898 against Arts. 1900 and 1902

---

#### 3. Was the sale within the authority? Art. 1898 against Arts. 1900 and 1902

| | Text | Who cites it |
|---|---|---|
| **Art. 1898** | "If the agent contracts in the name of the principal, exceeding the scope of his authority, and the principal does not ratify the contract, it shall be void if the party with whom the agent contracted is aware of the limits of the powers granted by the principal." | Ours |
| **Art. 1900** | "So far as third persons are concerned, an act is deemed to have been performed within the scope of the agent's authority, if such act is within the terms of the power of attorney as written, even if the agent has in fact exceeded the limits of his authority according to an understanding between the principal and the agent." *(second clause paraphrased from the text)* | Defense (apparent authority, ¶45) |
| **Art. 1902** | "A third person with whom the agent wishes to contract on behalf of the principal may require the presentation of the power of attorney, or the instructions as regards the agency. Private or secret orders and instructions of the principal do not prejudice third persons who have relied upon the power of attorney or instructions shown them." *(first sentence paraphrased; second quoted)* | Defense |
| **Art. 1911** | "Even when the agent has exceeded his authority, the principal is solidarily liable with the agent if the former allowed the latter to act as though he had full powers." | Defense (the owners "allowed Cesar to continue wielding" authority, ¶45) |

**What she keys in on.** Art. 1900 protects acts "within the terms of the power of attorney **as written**." Art. 1902 protects reliance against "**private or secret** orders." Art. 1898 voids the contract where the buyer "is aware of the limits of the powers granted." The class and the consultation are not private orders; they are the written terms, and they were annotated on the title from **21 June 1996** (Exh. B, p. 3), which under PD 1529 §52 is "constructive notice to all persons." So Arts. 1900 and 1902 cut for us, and Art. 1898's condition ("aware of the limits") is met by the registry itself.

**Art. 1911's trap.** It makes the principal liable where the principal "allowed" the agent to act as though with full powers. The defense will say the owners let Cesar sell for years (the 1999 Hansol and 2005 Era sales). Answer: those sales were before the 2005 revocation and, for Hansol, under the 1991 SPA; "allowed" requires the owners' conduct, and from 15 August 2005 their conduct was a written revocation (Exh. F) and, in 2020, a published one (Exh. G). Art. 1911 also creates *liability*, not a valid transfer of land; it does not override Art. 1874's "shall be void."

**Expected result.** Sale outside the written terms; buyer charged with the limits; void under Arts. 1874 and 1898.

**Document:** War of the Statutes  
**Source:** `WAR_OF_THE_STATUTES_2026-10.md`  
**Section:** 4. Void or merely unenforceable? Art. 1898 (with Art. 1874) against Art. 1403(1)

---

#### 4. Void or merely unenforceable? Art. 1898 (with Art. 1874) against Art. 1403(1)

> **Caution (18 Oct 2026, second-chair review).** Art. 1874 voids a sale made through an agent whose authority is *not* in writing. Here the authority is in writing (Exh. E), and the sale is outside its terms. The operative provision is therefore **Art. 1898**: a contract the agent makes "exceeding the scope of his authority" which the principal does not ratify "shall be void if the party with whom the agent contracted is aware of the limits of the powers granted by the principal." Awareness is supplied by the 1996 annotation (PD 1529 §52) and by the buyer's own pull of the full instrument in August 2021 (Def. Exh. 7). Art. 1874 is cited for the rule that the authority must be in writing, and for the reasoning in *Cosmic Lumber* and *Unchuan* that a sale outside the written power is a sale without written authority for that sale. Lead with Art. 1898.

| | Text | Who cites it |
|---|---|---|
| **Art. 1403(1)** | Unenforceable, unless ratified: "Those entered into in the name of another person by one who has been given no authority or legal representation, or who has acted beyond his powers." | Defense (6th defense, ¶42: "not necessarily void ab initio … Ratification may be express or implied") |
| **Art. 1910** | "The principal must comply with all the obligations which the agent may have contracted within the scope of his authority. As for any obligation wherein the agent has exceeded his power, the principal is not bound except when he ratifies it expressly or tacitly." *(second sentence paraphrased)* | Defense (tacit ratification by accepting payments) |
| **Art. 1874** | "… otherwise, the sale shall be void." | Ours |
| **Art. 1409** | "The following contracts are inexistent and void from the beginning: … (7) Those expressly prohibited or declared void by law. These contracts cannot be ratified." | Ours |
| **Art. 1410** | "The action or defense for the declaration of the inexistence of a contract does not prescribe." | Ours |

**What she keys in on.** The word "void" in Art. 1874 and the words "declared void by law … cannot be ratified" in Art. 1409(7). Art. 1403(1) is the general rule for unauthorized contracts; Art. 1874 is the special rule for land sold through an agent, and it uses "void," not "unenforceable." The Supreme Court says the same (*Cosmic Lumber*: "ipso jure … void"; *Unchuan*: "cannot be ratified … laches will not set in"), but she will reach it from the two articles alone.

**The ratification facts.** Payments were made to a revoked agent and his son (Exh. 5; Annex Z) and never reached any owner; the Deed of Confirmation (Annex G, p. 1) is the agent confirming himself. There is no act of the principals to ratify with, and Art. 1409 says a void sale cannot be ratified anyway.

**Expected result.** Void; imprescriptible; the laches and estoppel defenses (¶43) fall with it.

**Document:** War of the Statutes  
**Source:** `WAR_OF_THE_STATUTES_2026-10.md`  
**Section:** 5. Revocation and notice: Arts. 1921 and 1922 against PD 1529 §64

---

#### 5. Revocation and notice: Arts. 1921 and 1922 against PD 1529 §64

| | Text | Who cites it |
|---|---|---|
| **Art. 1921** | "If the agency has been entrusted for the purpose of contracting with specified persons, its revocation shall not prejudice the latter if they were not given notice thereof." | Both. Defense: she is a "specified person" (Llamanzares-era buyer) never notified. Ours: she was never one. |
| **Art. 1922** | "If the agent had general powers, revocation of the agency does not prejudice third persons who acted in good faith and without knowledge of the revocation. Notice of the revocation in a newspaper of general circulation is a sufficient warning to third persons." | Defense: publication only from 2020 forward (¶41). Ours: **do not** argue the publication as "sufficient warning"; Cesar's powers were special, not general. |
| **PD 1529 §64** | "Any person may, by power of attorney, convey or otherwise deal with registered land and the same shall be registered with the Register of Deeds … Any instrument revoking such power of attorney shall be registered in like manner." | Defense (5th defense): the revocation was never registered. |
| **PD 1529 §52** | "Every conveyance, mortgage, lease, lien, attachment, order, judgment, instrument or entry affecting registered land shall, if registered, filed or entered in the office of the Register of Deeds … be constructive notice to all persons from the time of such registering, filing or entering." | Ours: the SPA with its limits was entered in 1996. |

**What she keys in on.** Art. 1921's words "specified persons." The SPA names them: Llamanzares contract holders and payors to Llamanzares. If the buyer is not one, Art. 1921 gives her nothing and the revocation is beside the point because the sale was never inside the agency. §64's "shall be registered" is a direction to register, not a condition of validity (*Republic v. Ciruelas*, G.R. 239505: registration under §64 is not "a prerequisite to its validity"); and §64 only matters for a sale within the power. §52 answers §64: what was registered, in 1996, was the SPA itself, limits included.

**Two cases to cite sparingly, because they cut both ways.** *Central Surety v. Hodges* applied Art. 1922 against the principal where the agent dealt "with the public in general"; Ramos will say Cesar, who sold to many buyers, had general powers. The answer is the SPA's own words: a named class, after consultation. *Republic v. Ciruelas* holds that §64 registration is not a prerequisite to an SPA's validity; Ramos will turn that into "nor is the non-registration of the revocation fatal to our reliance." The answer is §52: what charged her was the registered instrument with its limits, whatever was registered later.

**The sequence to keep.** First: outside the class, so revocation irrelevant. Second, in the alternative: the 2020 publication is a fact bearing on good faith before registration (section 6), not an Art. 1922 effect. Never both ways.

**Expected result.** Art. 1921 not engaged; §64 not a defense to a sale outside the power.

**Document:** War of the Statutes  
**Source:** `WAR_OF_THE_STATUTES_2026-10.md`  
**Section:** 6. Good faith, and until when: Arts. 526 to 528 against PD 1529 §§51 and 53

---

#### 6. Good faith, and until when: Arts. 526 to 528 against PD 1529 §§51 and 53

| | Text | Who cites it |
|---|---|---|
| **Art. 527** | "Good faith is always presumed, and upon him who alleges bad faith on the part of a possessor rests the burden of proof." | Defense |
| **Art. 526** | "He is deemed a possessor in good faith who is not aware that there exists in his title or mode of acquisition any flaw which invalidates it. He is deemed a possessor in bad faith who possesses in any case contrary to the foregoing." | Ours |
| **Art. 528** | "Possession acquired in good faith does not lose this character except in the case and from the moment facts exist which show that the possessor is not unaware that he possesses the thing improperly or wrongfully." | Ours |
| **PD 1529 §51** | "The act of registration shall be the operative act to convey or affect the land insofar as third persons are concerned." | Ours |

**What she keys in on.** Three words: "**aware**" (Art. 526), "**from the moment facts exist**" (Art. 528), and "**operative act**" (§51). The presumption in Art. 527 is for possessors; the "innocent holder" in §53 is read through Art. 526: a buyer "not aware that there exists in his title or mode of acquisition any flaw which invalidates it." Her mode of acquisition was a purchase from an agent of three absent owners she had paid rent to; the flaw was on the title since 1996 and in her hands by August 2021 (Elevado's certified copy of the SPA, Annex B, p. 1). Under §51 the conveyance affected the land only on **23 November 2021**; *Duenas* reads §51 to require good faith to last until then. Between the deed and that date the facts "showing that the possessor is not unaware" are: her own 28 May 2020 SPA to sue over the land (their Annex "1"); the pleading received by her side's lawyer on 9 July 2021 with the published revocation attached (Annex N); and her sworn "my property" of 15 September 2021 (Annex K, p. 9).

**Her habit that helps.** She does not characterize. Art. 528 does not ask her to; it asks whether "facts exist." We put the dated facts on the table and let the article do the work.

**Expected result.** Not an innocent holder for value; §53's protection not available; bad faith at the latest from the dated notices and, on the inquiry rule, from the deed.

**Section:** 7. The partition: Art. 496 against nothing

---

#### 7. The partition: Art. 496 against nothing

| | Text | Who cites it |
|---|---|---|
| **Art. 496** | "Partition may be made by agreement between the parties or by judicial proceedings. Partition shall be governed by the Rules of Court insofar as they are consistent with this Code." | Ours |
| **Art. 1878 ¶¶12, 15** | A special power is necessary "(12) To create or convey real rights over immovable property" and "(15) Any other act of strict dominion." | Ours |
| **Art. 493** | "Each co-owner shall have the full ownership of his part and of the fruits and benefits pertaining thereto, and he may therefore alienate, assign or mortgage it … But the effect of the alienation or the mortgage, with respect to the co-owners, shall be limited to the portion which may be allotted to him in the division upon the termination of the co-ownership." *(second sentence paraphrased)* | The defense may reach for it; it does not help them: no co-owner sold anything, and even a co-owner's sale is "limited to the portion … allotted … upon the termination of the co-ownership," which is the partition they do not have. |

**What she keys in on.** "By agreement between the **parties**." The parties to a partition of co-owned land are the co-owners. No co-owner signed the Partition-Subdivision Agreement of 15 March 2017; no SPA ever gave Cesar a power to partition; and Entry 2021003236 (Exh. B, p. 6) says the land was subdivided "in accordance with subdivision plan number (LRA) PSD-E2020005406," a plan the defendants' own Exh. 1, p. 2, dates "DATE OF SUBD/CONS SURVEY: SEPTEMBER 8, 2020 · DATE APPROVED: APRIL 6, 2021 · GEODETIC ENGINEER: CHRISTOPHER D. MANGIGA." The defense has no article for this; their only answer is §48 (section 1) and "regularity," and the instrument itself has never been produced.

**Expected result.** No valid partition; a title to a specific lot could not issue; TCT 079-2021002126 cancelled on this ground alone, whatever she finds on the deed.

**Section:** 8. The improvements: Arts. 448 and 546 against Arts. 449 to 452, 546 ¶1 and 1678

---

#### 8. The improvements: Arts. 448 and 546 against Arts. 449 to 452, 546 ¶1 and 1678

| | Text | Who cites it |
|---|---|---|
| **Art. 448** | "The owner of the land on which anything has been built, sown or planted **in good faith**, shall have the right to appropriate as his own the works … after payment of the indemnity provided for in Articles 546 and 548, or to oblige the one who built … to pay the price of the land …" *(after the quoted opening, paraphrased)* | Defense (12th defense, ¶49) |
| **Art. 546** | "Necessary expenses shall be refunded to every possessor; but only the possessor **in good faith** may retain the thing until he has been reimbursed therefor. Useful expenses shall be refunded only to the possessor **in good faith** with the same right of retention …" | Both |
| **Art. 548** | "Expenses for pure luxury or mere pleasure shall not be refunded to the possessor in good faith …" | Defense |
| **Art. 449** | "He who builds, plants or sows in bad faith on the land of another, loses what is built, planted or sown without right to indemnity." | Ours |
| **Art. 450** | "The owner of the land on which anything has been built, planted or sown in bad faith may demand the demolition of the work … at the expense of the person who built, planted or sowed …" | Ours |
| **Art. 451** | "In the cases of the two preceding articles, the landowner is entitled to damages from the builder, planter or sower." | Ours |
| **Art. 452** | "The builder, planter or sower in bad faith is entitled to reimbursement for the necessary expenses of preservation of the land." | Ours (it fixes the only thing a bad-faith builder gets) |
| **Art. 1678** | A lessee who makes "in good faith, useful improvements which are suitable to the use for which the lease is intended" is entitled, on termination, to have the lessor "pay the lessee one-half of the value of the improvements at that time"; if the lessor refuses, "the lessee may remove the improvements." *(paraphrased with quoted phrases)* | Ours, for everything built while renting (*Parilla v. Pilar*, G.R. 167680: Art. 448 "does not apply where one's only interest is that of a lessee under a rental contract") |

**What she keys in on.** The words "in good faith" in Arts. 448 and 546, which send her back to Art. 526 (aware of a flaw) and Art. 528 (from the moment facts exist), and the word "lessee," which is in the defendants' own Answer (¶¶40, 43: "renting"). Three periods, three texts: lessee (Art. 1678) until 29 September 2016; buyer not in good faith (Arts. 449 to 452) after it; possessor on notice (Art. 528, 549) after the demand of 22 August 2025.

**Their cases, her method.** *Macasaet* and *Casibang* applied Art. 448 where the owner invited or permitted the building. She will not go to the cases; she will ask whether the owners consented. The exhibits say the owners' administrator rented to them, and that in 2006 they themselves wrote that the lot was "owned by the Heirs of Worrick Keesey" (Exh. 5, p. 1).

**Expected result.** Forfeiture of post-deed improvements (Art. 449); necessary expenses only, on receipts, no retention (Arts. 452, 546 ¶1); Art. 1678 option reserved to the owners for anything proved to predate the deed.

**Section:** 9. Fruits and damages: Art. 544 against Arts. 549, 2208, 2229, 2232

---

#### 9. Fruits and damages: Art. 544 against Arts. 549, 2208, 2229, 2232

| | Text | Who cites it |
|---|---|---|
| **Art. 544** | "A possessor in good faith is entitled to the fruits received before the possession is legally interrupted." | Defense (keeps the rentals) |
| **Art. 549** | "The possessor in bad faith shall reimburse the fruits received and those which the legitimate possessor could have received …" | Ours |
| **Art. 2232** | "In contracts and quasi-contracts, the court may award exemplary damages if the defendant acted in a wanton, fraudulent, reckless, oppressive, or malevolent manner." | Ours |
| **Art. 2229** | Exemplary damages "are imposed, by way of example or correction for the public good, in addition to the moral, temperate, liquidated or compensatory damages." | Ours |
| **Art. 2208 ¶¶1, 2, 5** | Attorney's fees may be recovered "(1) When exemplary damages are awarded; (2) When the defendant's act or omission has compelled the plaintiff to litigate … (5) Where the defendant acted in gross and evident bad faith in refusing to satisfy the plaintiff's plainly valid, just and demandable claim." | Ours |

**What she keys in on.** Art. 544's "good faith" (again Art. 526) and "legally interrupted"; Art. 2232's "fraudulent." She will not write "fraudulent" on an impression. She will write it if five dated documents leave no other word: the deed's "in hand paid" of 29 Sep 2016 against receipts to Feb 2017; the petition of 20 Oct 2016 by a revoked agent calling himself "Administrator," listing a title cancelled in 1999 and hiding the sale; the partition dated 15 Mar 2017 under a plan surveyed 8 Sep 2020; the engineer's certificate of 8 Sep 2020 that the survey was made "with the full knowledge and consent of the owner(s)" when the only agent was dead; the sworn "my property" of 15 Sep 2021 over land titled to three others. The Answer's own ¶27 ("certain rooms on the property were occasionally rented out after 2016") supplies the fruits; Exh. J supplies the interruption date (22 Aug 2025).

**The predicate she will check first.** Art. 2234: "While the amount of the exemplary damages need not be proved, the plaintiff must show that he is entitled to moral, temperate or compensatory damages before the court may consider the question of whether or not exemplary damages should be awarded." So the judgment must first award compensatory damages (the fruits, or reasonable compensation for use and occupation from 22 Aug 2025) or temperate damages ("when the court finds that some pecuniary loss has been suffered but its amount can not, from the nature of the case, be proved with certainty," Art. 2224; "reasonable under the circumstances," Art. 2225), and only then exemplary damages. A prayer that asks for exemplary damages with no compensatory or temperate award beside it fails on the text.

**Expected result.** Accounting of fruits from the demand (and, on a bad-faith-from-the-deed finding, from the deed), with a temperate or compensatory award as the Art. 2234 predicate; exemplary damages and fees if the five documents are in and tied to Art. 2232's words; the *Duenas* figures (₱200,000 / ₱100,000) as the template.

#### 10. The counterclaim: Arts. 19 to 21 and 2219 against Art. 2208 and the result on title

| | Text | Who cites it |
|---|---|---|
| **Arts. 19, 20, 21** (abuse of rights; acts contrary to law; acts contrary to morals) and **Art. 2219** (moral damages) | The counterclaim's only possible legal frame for "harassment," "malicious," "evident bad faith" (¶¶52 to 57); the Answer pleads the conclusions, not the articles. | Defense |
| **Art. 2208** | Attorney's fees are the exception, not the rule; they require one of the enumerated grounds. | Both |
| **Rule 6 §7** | A compulsory counterclaim arises out of the same transaction; it stands or falls with the main action. | Ours |

**What she keys in on.** Whether the plaintiff "acted in bad faith" in suing. A suit that succeeds on title cannot have been malicious. The ARTA referrals the defense will call harassment are official acts of national agencies on their own findings (Annexes T, U, V), offered, if at all, for that limited purpose.

**Expected result.** Counterclaim dismissed with the main judgment.

#### 11. The procedural provisions she enforces (where cases are lost before the merits)

| Provision | Her words that show she enforces it | What we do |
|---|---|---|
| **Judicial Affidavit Rule §§2, 10** (file and serve five days before the hearing; late filing waived; one late submission allowed for a valid reason, with a fine) | "Liberally applying the rules of procedure … No further liberality shall be accorded" (11 Mar 2026) | One motion for leave, one paragraph, fine tendered; the supplemental affidavit or nothing |
| **Rule 18 §7** (pre-trial order controls "unless modified before trial to prevent manifest injustice") | She announced the governing rules unprompted (6 Feb 2026) | Read the 13 May order now; move to modify before 14 Oct if the partition and improvement issues are not in it |
| **Rule 30 §5(f)** ("rebutting evidence only") | "final and intransferable unless the ground for resetting is allowed under the rules" (4 Sep 2026) | Everything that is not rebuttal goes in by 9 Dec; rest subject to rebuttal |
| **Rule 21 §§1 to 4** (subpoena duces tecum must describe the documents with reasonable particularity and show relevancy) | "Noting both counsels timely compliance" | The ten-custodian request (memo Annex 3), returnable 2 Dec |
| **Rule 132 §13** (impeachment by prior inconsistent statement: show the writing, fix time and place, let the witness explain) | Her 6 Jan 2021 order reverses her own warrants on a rule, not on an impression | Foundation for Salvador's 25 Nov 2025 letter laid on cross, or it is never used |
| **Rule 132 §40** (tender of excluded evidence) | The exclusion of Gloria's affidavit at pre-trial | Prepared both ways; her admissions help us more than her denials hurt |
| **Rule 129 §4** (judicial admissions need no proof) | — | Answer ¶¶18, 27, 28, 40, 43, 44(b): publication, rentals, demand, tenancy |
| **Rules on Electronic Filing** | "strictly compliant with the Rules of Civil Procedure, specifically the amendments thereto, as well as the Rules on Electronic Filing" (12 Mar 2026) | Every filing perfect in form; her caption copied exactly |
| **Constitution Art. VIII §15(1)** (decision within ninety days of submission) | — | Memoranda end with the decision table; expect a decision about August 2027 |
| **Rule 132 §35** (the offer of a witness's testimony is made when the witness is called; documentary evidence offered after the testimonial evidence) and **Rule 30 §5(g)** (case deemed submitted once evidence is admitted unless the court directs memoranda) | — | Counsel states the purpose of each witness's testimony when calling the witness; when resting, ask on the record for leave to file memoranda |

*Texts stated in substance but not fetched this week (verify before quoting): PD 1096 §301; Judicial Affidavit Rule §§2, 5, 10; Rule 112 §1; the 2019 amendments' numbering of Rules 18, 30 and 132 (checked against the lawphil 1997 text with later amendments).*

**MOTION TO RESOLVE THE MOTION TO RENDER SUMMARY JUDGMENT 
WITH ALTERNATIVE PRAYER FOR AN ORDER UNDER RULE 35, SECTION 4**

PLAINTIFF, through counsel, respectfully states:

1. On ____ April 2026, plaintiff filed her Motion to Render Summary Judgment. The defendants filed their Comment/Opposition, received on 27 April 2026. No reply was required. The motion was thereby submitted for resolution.

2. Section 15, Article VIII of the Constitution provides, in its first paragraph, that all cases or matters "must be decided or resolved … three months for all other lower courts," and, in its second paragraph, that "[a] case or matter shall be deemed submitted for decision or resolution upon the filing of the last pleading, brief, or memorandum required by the Rules of Court or by the court itself."

3. Plaintiff respectfully submits that the motion remains unresolved. [Counsel: delete this paragraph and the motion if the pre-trial order of 13 May 2026 disposed of it.]

4. Rule 35, Section 3, as amended, provides that "[t]he motion shall cite the supporting affidavits, depositions or admissions, and the specific law relied upon. The adverse party may file a comment and serve opposing affidavits, depositions, or admissions within a non-extendible period of five (5) calendar days from receipt of the motion. Unless the court orders the conduct of a hearing, judgment sought shall be rendered forthwith if the pleadings, supporting affidavits, depositions and admissions on file, show that, except as to the amount of damages, there is no genuine issue as to any material fact and that the moving party is entitled to judgment as a matter of law."

5. Rule 35, Section 4, as amended, provides that "[i]f on motion under this Rule, judgment is not rendered upon the whole case or for all the reliefs sought and a trial is necessary, the court may, by examining the pleadings and the evidence before it and by interrogating counsel, ascertain what material facts exist without substantial controversy, including the extent to which the amount of damages or other relief is not in controversy, and directing such further proceedings in the action as are just. The facts so ascertained shall be deemed established, and the trial shall be conducted on the controverted facts accordingly."

6. Whatever the Court's view of the motion as a whole, the facts listed in Annex "A" appear on the pleadings, on the exhibits attached by both parties, and on instruments of public record whose contents neither party disputes. The defendants' Comment/Opposition contests the legal effect of those documents, not what they say. Ascertaining them now confines the trial, already set on fixed dates, to the matters actually and in good faith controverted, and serves the orderly and prompt disposition of the case.

7. This motion asks for no resetting of any trial date.

**WHEREFORE**, plaintiff respectfully prays that the Court:

1. RESOLVE the Motion to Render Summary Judgment; and

2. in the alternative, should judgment not be rendered on the whole case, ASCERTAIN, under Rule 35, Section 4, the facts set out in Annex "A" as facts existing without substantial controversy, which shall be deemed established, and directing that the trial proceed on the controverted matters identified in paragraph 2 of Annex "A".

Other just and equitable reliefs are likewise prayed for.

Daet for Mercedes, Camarines Norte, ____ October 2026.

**2. Matters actually and in good faith controverted**, on which trial should proceed:

1. Whether, before 16 March 1992, defendant Gloria H. Balane or her spouse held a contract to sell executed by Ben Llamanzares, or paid him a down payment or partial payment for the portion (Answer ¶40; Reply).

2. Whether the defendants had notice, before 23 November 2021, of the revocation of 15 August 2005 or of the limits of the authority under paragraph 7, and the diligence exercised (Answer ¶¶41, 44).

3. The improvements claimed, their dates and values, and the damages and fruits claimed by either side (Answer ¶¶27, 49; Complaint).

**Section:** 1. EXECUTIVE SUMMARY AND THE WINNING THEORY

---

#### 1. EXECUTIVE SUMMARY AND THE WINNING THEORY

##### The goal
One judgment, in this court, that:
1. declares the Deed of Absolute Sale of **29 September 2016** (Doc. No. 304, Page 61, Book CXVI, Notary Donald E. Asis) **null and void**;
2. **cancels TCT No. 079-2021002126** and **ARP No. GR-2023-II-07-001-00256** and restores Lot 2-X-6-I-4-C-1 (2,587 sqm) to the three registered owners;
3. orders the defendants and everyone admitted by them to **vacate**;
4. declares the post-2016 improvements **forfeited without indemnity** (Art. 449) and denies any right of retention;
5. orders an **accounting of fruits** from **22 August 2025** (receipt of the demand);
6. awards **exemplary damages (₱200,000.00) and attorney's fees (₱100,000.00)**, the figures the En Banc awarded in *Duenas*; and
7. **dismisses the counterclaim**.

##### The question the court must answer
Was Gloria H. Balane a purchaser in good faith and for value when she bought, from an agent, a 2,587-square-meter portion of land registered to three sisters who were never consulted and never paid?

##### The silver bullet: the class limit in the agent's own authority
The defendants' entire case rests on the **Special Power of Attorney of 16 March 1992** (plaintiff's Exh. E; defense Exh. 7; Annex B). They call it "sufficient written authority to sell." Read its paragraph 7. Cesar M. de la Fuente was appointed:

> "to negotiate for the sale of the above-described real estate property **in favor of third persons in possession of CONTRACTS TO SELL executed by the previous administrator, MR. BEN LLAMANZARES and/or to those who have paid their downpayments or have made partial payments therefor to the latter** … Said sale shall be upon such terms and conditions … **after prior consultation with us**, and to sign and execute the necessary documents … as well as to receive the proceeds."

Two limits, both on the face of a public instrument that the Registry annotated on **TCT T-52540 on 21 June 1996** (Exh. B; Annex C, p. 2):

- **A class of buyers:** holders of a Llamanzares contract to sell, or persons who had paid Llamanzares a down payment or partial payment. The Balanes, by their own verified Answer, were **tenants** paying monthly rent (Answer ¶¶40, 43, 44[b]; Annex A, pp. 3–4). They produced no Llamanzares contract and no Llamanzares receipt. Their earliest receipt is **October 2003**, to Cesar's side (Annex D, p. 2).
- **A condition:** prior consultation with the owners. None was ever made. Their own collector swears the owners "never came home" and that Cesar's contact was the owners' Manila counsel (Salvador JA S31; Annex Q, p. 4).

A sale outside the written authority is **void** (Civil Code Art. 1898, read with Arts. 1874 and 1878 ¶5); "the sale ipso jure is consequently void" (*Cosmic Lumber Corp. v. CA*, G.R. No. 114311, 29 Nov 1996). Because the limits were annotated on the title from 1996, the buyer is charged with them (PD 1529 §52). **The revocation of 2005 is not needed to win; it is the backup.** Lead with the class, and the defendants' best fact (the SPA on the title since 1996, no revocation annotated) becomes ours: the title sent every buyer to the instrument, and the instrument excludes her.

##### Why we win on this court's method
The orders in this case decide by **statute, section and exhibit**, in short English paragraphs, with exact particulars in the dispositive. The seven writings in hand cite no case law. Put **Art. 1898 beside Exh. E ¶7 and Exh. B page 3**, **Art. 496 beside Entry No. 2021003236**, and **Art. 449 beside the deed's date and the demand's receipt**, and the judgment writes itself in the court's own form. Everything in this playbook is built to give the court exactly that, and nothing that requires it to characterize anyone.

##### The one sentence that carries the case
*Cesar sold to a buyer outside the class his authority allowed, without the consultation it required, and the buyer knew the owners, paid them rent for decades, never asked them, and finished the paperwork years after the agent was dead and his authority had been published as revoked.*

**Document:** Master trial playbook (6 parts)  
**Source:** `MASTER_TRIAL_PLAYBOOK_2026-10.md`  
**Section:** 3. THE THREE PILLARS OF OUR CASE

---

#### 3. THE THREE PILLARS OF OUR CASE

##### Pillar A — The Void Agency

**The rule.** Art. 1874: a sale of land through an agent without written authority "shall be void." Art. 1878 ¶5: a special power is required "to enter into any contract by which the ownership of an immovable is transmitted." Art. 1898: a contract by an agent "exceeding the scope of his authority," not ratified, "shall be void if the party with whom the agent contracted is aware of the limits of the powers granted." A power of attorney "must be strictly construed, and courts will not infer or presume broad powers from deeds which do not sufficiently include property or subject under which the agent is to deal" (*Bautista-Spille v. NICORP*, G.R. No. 214057, 19 Oct 2015). "For the principal to confer the right upon an agent to sell real estate, a power of attorney must so express the powers of the agent in clear and unmistakable language" (*Cosmic Lumber*). An unauthorized agent's land conveyance is void (*Alcantara v. Nido*, G.R. No. 165133, 19 Apr 2010), though Ramos will distinguish it easily, since the agent there had no written authority at all; use it for the Art. 1874 rule only, and rest the class argument on the SPA's text and *Bautista-Spille*.

**The fact.** Paragraph 7 of Exh. E (Annex B, p. 3) names the class and the condition. "In possession" governs **contracts to sell**, not the land, so an occupant is not in the class by occupying. The Balanes were tenants (Answer ¶¶40, 43; rent receipts to 2014, Annex D), with no Llamanzares contract and no Llamanzares payment; their first payment is October 2003, on a receipt signed "Von dela Fuente" (Def. Exh. 5, p. 2; Annex D, p. 2); the receipt itself does not state the signer's relationship to Cesar, though Salvador's own affidavit identifies him as Cesar's son. Nobody consulted the owners: Salvador S31 (Annex Q, p. 4). The SPA describes only the undivided T-4497 parcel (Annex B, p. 2): use this for strict construction, not as a stand-alone "never covered this lot," because the Registry carried the SPA onto T-52540 in 1996 and the 2005 Era sale was registered under it.

**The class clause, word by word.** "In favor of third persons in possession of CONTRACTS TO SELL executed by the previous administrator, MR. BEN LLAMANZARES and/or to those who have paid their downpayments or have made partial payments therefor to the latter."

| Words | What they do | The defense's reading | Why the defense's reading fails |
|---|---|---|---|
| "third persons in possession of CONTRACTS TO SELL" | Sub-class 1: holders of a written contract to sell | "in possession" means in possession of the land, so any occupant qualifies | The object of "in possession of" is "CONTRACTS TO SELL," and contracts are what the next words say Llamanzares "executed." Reading "possession" as possession of land requires deleting the words that follow it. |
| "executed by the previous administrator, MR. BEN LLAMANZARES" | Ties sub-class 1 to one named man | — | The clause names a person, not a description. No contract to sell from Llamanzares to any Balane has ever been produced or pleaded. |
| "and/or to those who have paid their downpayments or have made partial payments therefor" | Sub-class 2: payors | A tenant who paid rent "paid" | "Therefor" means for the sale. Rent is paid for occupancy, not for a purchase. Their own receipts bill "Payment for the month of." |
| "to the latter" | Payments must have been made to Llamanzares, the last person named | "the latter" is loose; payments to Cesar count | The only person "the latter" can refer to is Llamanzares. Every receipt the Balanes hold is signed by Cesar's son, the earliest in **October 2003**. |
| "have paid … have made" (present perfect, 16 Mar 1992) | The class was closed on the date of the SPA | Later payments brought them in | Payments after 1992, and all payments to Cesar, cannot create membership in a class defined by payments already made to Llamanzares. |
| ¶6, "interested third parties" | The purpose clause | "Interested third parties" is broad, so ¶7 is illustrative | ¶7 begins "Accordingly" and is the operative grant; the specific controls the general. |
| ¶7, "after prior consultation with us" | A condition on every sale | A private arrangement between owners and agent | See the next box. |

**A "downpayment" needs an object and a price; a bare payment to Llamanzares is neither.** Sub-class two is not a separate, looser door. A down payment is a payment on a sale, and "there is no contract unless … consent … object certain … [and] cause" concur (Art. 1318). A sale requires "a determinate thing" and "a price certain" (Art. 1458), a thing "particularly designated or physically segregated from all others of the same class" (Art. 1460), and is perfected only on "a meeting of minds upon the thing … and upon the price" (Art. 1475). Earnest money is "part of the price and … proof of the perfection of the contract" (Art. 1482): it presupposes a perfected sale to be part of. So a member of sub-class two is someone who had agreed with Llamanzares on an identified portion and a price and had started paying for it: the same people as sub-class one, caught at an earlier documentary stage. On the defendants' own evidence no object or price existed in Llamanzares's time: the portion was first "segregated" by a survey they paid for in **December 2006** (Annex D, p. 1); "Lot Area: 2,587" first appears on a receipt dated **7 August 2016** (Annex D, p. 29); and the price of ₱250,000 was fixed by Cesar (Gloria S33 [COND]), not by Llamanzares. A ₱3,000 payment in the 1980s with no lot, no price and no receipt is, at most, money handed to the administrator, which on their own Answer is what rent was. It is not a down payment "therefor."

**Who must prove membership.** The defense pleads it as its fourth affirmative defense (Answer ¶40) and as the first of its ten good-faith circumstances (¶44[a] to [c]). An affirmative allegation is proved by the party who makes it (Rule 131 §1), and the buyer who claims good faith carries that burden anyway (*Bucton*; *Duenas*). They must produce a Llamanzares contract or a payment to Llamanzares. They have produced neither.

**What they have instead, and the holes in it.** Three accounts of the "down payment" that do not match: Gloria, ₱3,000 in the 1980s, no receipt, recorded in Manuel Violeta's "brown columnar book" which "went to Cesar and then the Agcaoili Law Office" (S20 to S24 [COND]); Salvador, already paid by "around 1992" (S14 to S15); the only receipt marked "Down payment," ₱20,000 on **6 January 2005** to Salvador (Annex D, p. 3). Nothing shows the pleaded ₱3,000 "credited" in the 2016 price (¶40); the receipts total more than the price. **Two things to run down now:** whether the Agcaoili office ever received any Violeta ledger (if it did and it names a Balane, the defense's class claim gains a document; if it did not, Gloria's sworn account of where the book went is wrong), and the Llamanzares-era sales that are on the T-4497 title itself (the 1977 "Deeds of Final Sale" by "Mary Worrick-Keesey, thru her Atty.-in-fact Benjamin Llamanzares," Exh. I pp. 7 to 9), which show what a real member of the class looked like: a registered instalment buyer with a registered deed. The Balanes have no such paper.

**The principal's own reading, sworn in 2023.** Patricia's affidavit of 22 February 2023 (corpus doc 187, ¶4): the owners "granted on March 16, 1992, Mr. Cesar de la Fuente limited authority to perfect the sale of portions of said property in favor of those who already possessed Contract to Sell, or have had made down/partial payment to Benjamin Llamanzares." Their collector's 2025 letter says the same from the other side (Annex Y).

**A crack in our own Reply to manage before the memorandum.** Our Reply of April 2026 (¶14) answered the Art. 1921 defense by saying the 1992 agency "was not created for the purpose of dealing with the defendants specifically **or with any other specified persons**." The second clause overshoots: the SPA does name specified persons, the Llamanzares class, and our theory depends on it. The memorandum should restate the point correctly: Art. 1921 protects only the persons the agency was entrusted to deal with; the SPA names that class; the defendants are outside it; and on either reading Art. 1921 gives them nothing. Do not let Ramos read ¶14 aloud before we have said this ourselves.

**What "prior consultation with us" means, and why it is not a "secret order."** The clause does not define consultation; its plain meaning is that before the agent fixed the terms of any sale he had to refer it to the three owners. The defense will reach for Art. 1902 ("Private or secret orders and instructions of the principal do not prejudice third persons who have relied upon the power of attorney") and Art. 1900 (acts "within the terms of the power of attorney as written" bind the principal "even if the agent has in fact exceeded the limits of his authority according to an understanding between the principal and the agent"). Both articles protect a buyer against limits she could not see. This limit is in the instrument itself, annotated on the title since 1996 (PD 1529 §52), and therefore one of the "terms … as written," not an "understanding" outside them. A buyer "put upon inquiry" must "discover upon his peril the authority of the agent" (*San Pedro v. Ong*) and ascertain "the nature and extent of the agent's authority" (*Yoshizaki*); reading paragraph 7 and asking Cesar for proof that the owners had been consulted, or writing to the owners at the California address on their own tax declaration, was the inquiry the law demanded, and none was made (Salvador S31; Gloria S43 [COND]). **Weight it correctly:** the class is the stronger limit because it is objective and the buyer could test it against her own papers; consultation is the corroborating limit, proved through their own collector (no owner ever spoke to Cesar; his only contact was the Agcaoili office) and through the agent's duty to "act in accordance with the instructions of the principal" (Art. 1887). The Answer never alleges that any consultation occurred. Lead with the class; use consultation to show both that the sale was outside the written terms and that the buyer never asked.

**Their own witness reads it our way.** Salvador's letter (Annex Y): the instruction reached "the people … who possesses CONTRACT OF SALE issued by Benjamin Llamanzares"; everyone else was "appease[d]" with paper.

**Concede the right thing.** Our affidavit says the SPA was "limited to negotiation" (Q26, Q53). Paragraph 7 also lets Cesar "sign and execute the necessary documents" and "receive the proceeds," so a court will find a power to complete a sale **for the class**. Concede that wording on cross and hold the class. The class needs no construction against the grant; it is the grant.

**The revocation, in its place.** On **15 Aug 2005** the owners disowned every SPA but the new one (Exh. F, Annex H); it was published **25 Sep–9 Oct 2020** (Exh. G); it was never annotated. Order of argument: (1) the sale was outside the agency, so revocation is irrelevant to her; (2) in the alternative, the publication is a fact bearing on her good faith before registration. **Never** argue that the 2020 publication was Art. 1922 "sufficient warning" while also arguing Art. 1922 does not apply; Cesar's powers were special and class-limited, not general (*Central Surety v. Hodges*, G.R. No. L-28633, 30 Mar 1971: Art. 1922 governs an agent authorized to deal "not with a particular person, but with the public in general"). Art. 1921 protects only the "specified persons"; she was never one. PD 1529 §64 (registration of a revoking instrument) is a notice device, not a condition of validity (*Republic v. Ciruelas*, G.R. No. 239505, 17 Feb 2021), and only matters for a sale **within** the power. Both of those cases cut both ways and should be cited sparingly: *Central Surety* applied Art. 1922 against the principal where the agent dealt with the public, so Ramos will say Cesar, who sold to many, had "general" powers; the answer is that the SPA's own words confine him to a named class. *Ciruelas* says registration is not a prerequisite to an SPA's validity, which Ramos can turn into "nor is non-registration of the revocation fatal to our reliance"; the answer is that §52 charged her with the registered instrument's limits whatever was or was not registered later.

**Salvador is not Cesar.** Cesar's 29 Feb 2012 paper to Salvador (Annex Z) passed administration only, from an agent revoked in 2005: nothing to delegate, and a substitute never holds more than the agent had. **Do not argue that substitution was forbidden**: Art. 1892 permits a substitute unless prohibited, and *Escueta v. Lim* (G.R. No. 137162, 24 Jan 2007) upheld a substitute's sale under an SPA silent on substitution. Argue "nothing left to substitute." Every receipt Salvador signed from 2012 to the "Full payment" of Feb 2017 came from a man who was neither owner nor agent.

**Consequences.** A void contract "produces no effect either against or in favor of anyone and cannot be ratified … laches will not set in against a void transaction, as in this case, where the agent did not have a special power of attorney to dispose of the lots" (*MCIAA v. Unchuan*, G.R. No. 182537, 1 Jun 2016); the action does not prescribe (Art. 1410); "registration does not vest title" (*Heirs of Gonzales v. Basas*, G.R. No. 206847, 15 Jun 2022). The estate receipts went to a revoked agent and no owner saw a peso; the agent's own Deed of Confirmation (Annex G, p. 1) is not the principals' ratification.

##### Pillar B — The Bad Faith Buyer

**Burden and standard.** "The burden of proving the status of a purchaser in good faith and for value lies upon one who asserts that status. This onus probandi cannot be discharged by mere invocation of the ordinary presumption of good faith" (*Heirs of Bucton v. Spouses Go*, G.R. No. 188395, 20 Nov 2013). The same rule in their own words: "it is the party who claims to be an innocent purchaser for value who has the burden of proving such assertion, and it is not enough to invoke the ordinary presumption of good faith" (*Duenas v. Metrobank*, G.R. No. 209463, En Banc, 29 Nov 2022); "the burden of proving good faith lies with the second buyer … which is not discharged by simply invoking the ordinary presumption of good faith" (*Vallido v. Pono*, G.R. No. 200173, 15 Apr 2013). Art. 527's presumption governs possessors; IPV status is an affirmative claim.

**A buyer from an agent must inquire.** "Every person dealing with an agent is put upon inquiry, and must discover upon his peril the authority of the agent … If a person makes no inquiry, he is chargeable with knowledge of the agent's authority" (*San Pedro v. Ong*, G.R. No. 177598, 17 Oct 2008). "In case the buyer does not deal with the registered owner … the law requires that a higher degree of prudence be exercised" (*Bucton*). "Despite the presence of an alleged SPA from the registered owners, respondent should have inquired with the registered owners as to the extent of the authority of the attorney-in-fact, especially when the property is co-owned" (*Spouses Yabut v. Nachbaur*, G.R. No. 243470, 12 Jan 2021). The presumption applies only if the seller is the registered owner, in possession, and the buyer unaware of any defect in his capacity; "absent one or two of the foregoing conditions, then the law itself puts the buyer on notice" (*Duenas*; *Chua v. Republic*, G.R. No. 253305, 2 Aug 2023).

**All three conditions fail.** The seller was not the registered owner; he was not in possession (she was, as a tenant; and he swore under oath he was: Annex F, p. 14); two days before the deed he swore the duplicate was lost (Annex C, p. 3), the *Vallido* warning sign. She knew the owners (rent for decades), held their California address on a tax declaration she herself cited in 2020 (Annex M, p. 4), and knew their Manila counsel was the contact (Salvador S31). She never asked. *Bucton* found it "inconceivable" that buyers who knew the owners made no inquiry. The defense's *Sarili* line (face inspection of a notarized SPA suffices; G.R. No. 193517, 15 Jan 2014) defeats her: the face of this SPA contains the class and the consultation clause. *Tolentino v. Latagan* (G.R. No. 179874, 22 Jun 2015) is **adverse** (buyer upheld as IPV) and distinguishable (buyer dealt with the title-holder, not an agent).

**Good faith had to last until 23 November 2021.** "Buyers of registered land must be continuing purchasers for value and in good faith until the registration of the conveyance. In the event a buyer … is made aware of any claim or interest of some other person in the property, or of any defect or restriction in the title of the seller or in his or her capacity to convey title, the buyer shall no longer be considered to be in good faith even if he or she subsequently registers the conveyance" (*Duenas*, En Banc). PD 1529 §51: registration is "the operative act."

**The notice ladder before registration** (every rung documented in Phase 3):

| Date | What the buyer's side learned | Source |
|---|---|---|
| 15 Aug 2019 | Owners' counsel: SPA revoked 2005, later acts void — copied to the lawyer who handled the papers | Annex J |
| 11 Feb 2020 | Gloria has a lawyer on this parcel | Annex O, p. 8 |
| 28 May 2020 | Gloria arms an agent to sue and be sued over the land, by the owners' tax declaration, withholding the power to sell | Annex M (their Annex "1") |
| Jun 2020 | The dead agent's deed is moved for taxes, with penalties, under a cancelled title | Annex E, p. 2; Annex K, p. 5 |
| Jul–Sep 2020 | The Prosecutor refuses to call anyone owner and sends the parties to court | Annex O, pp. 6, 8 |
| 25 Sep–9 Oct 2020 | Revocation published in the Daet weekly | Annex I |
| Nov 2020–Jan 2021 | Warrants and bail over the right to the land; a co-defendant pays ₱180,000 | Annex O, pp. 9–10 |
| 9 Jul 2021 | A pleading stating the revocation, with the newspaper attached, received by her sister's lawyer | Annex N, pp. 1, 6, 9 |
| 12–16 Aug 2021 | Her processor pulls the SPA's full text, ¶7 included | Annex B, p. 1 |
| 15 Sep 2021 | She swears T-52540 is "my property" | Annex K, p. 9 |

**The Answer never denies this window.** Every notice denial is "before the September 2016 sale" (¶¶15, 19, 41; Annex A, pp. 1, 3–4). ¶32 extends only "no annotation" to the title's issuance. The one legal point counsel must still supply: imputation of an agent's and counsel's knowledge to the principal under a litigation-and-administration SPA.

**Tenancy seals it.** Possession as lessee is not possession as owner (Arts. 525, 540); "mere possession with a juridical title, such as by a … lessee … cannot ripen into ownership" (*Esguerra v. Manantan*, G.R. No. 158328, 23 Feb 2007); "a person whose occupation of realty is by sheer tolerance of the owner is not a possessor in good faith" (*NHA v. Manila Seedling Bank*, G.R. No. 183543, 20 Jun 2016). Use tenancy to kill ownership-by-possession and to prove knowledge of the owners; do not use Art. 1436 estoppel as a stand-alone bar to the deed (she claims to derive from the owners, not to deny them).

##### Pillar C — The Impossible Partition

**The rule.** Art. 1877: "An agency couched in general terms comprises only acts of administration." Art. 1878 requires a special power "(12) To create or convey real rights over immovable property" and "(15) Any other act of strict dominion." Art. 496: "Partition may be made by agreement between the parties or by judicial proceedings." The parties to a partition of co-owned land are the co-owners.

**The fact.** Neither SPA grants any power to partition or subdivide; the 1992 SPA's general clause (¶8, "any and every act … requisite, necessary or proper") is exactly what Art. 1877 confines to administration, and it was revoked in 2005 anyway. No co-owner signed or knew of the Partition-Subdivision Agreement of **15 Mar 2017** (Doc. 402/Pg. 81/Bk. CXXII). Its chronology is impossible on its face: it says the land was subdivided "in accordance with subdivision plan (LRA) PSD-E2020005406," a plan **surveyed 8 Sep 2020 and approved 6 Apr 2021** — dates the **defendants' own Exh. 1, page 2** recites. In Asis's Book CXXII the Deed of Confirmation of **28 Mar 2017 is Doc. 115** while this agreement of **15 Mar 2017 is Doc. 402**: the later act sits 287 entries earlier. Cesar was alive on 15 March 2017 and died 21 June 2017; he is the only person who could have purported to sign for the owners, and if anyone else did so after his death the instrument is worse than unauthorized. The survey of 8 Sep 2020 was certified "with the full knowledge and consent of the owner(s)" when no owner knew and the agent was three years dead (Annex L, p. 2).

**Their best answer on the survey, and ours.** The defense will say the field survey was done in **2006** (Princess's Villafuerte receipt of 29 Dec 2006, Annex D, p. 1), while Cesar was alive and "under his management," and that the 2020 plan merely formalized it for LRA approval on the strength of that earlier consent. Four answers, in order of strength. (1) Consent from Cesar in 2006 was consent from nobody: his authority had been revoked on **15 Aug 2005**, and even the 1992 SPA never contained a power to subdivide or partition. (2) The 2020 plan certifies a survey "**SURVEYED: September 8, 2020**," not 2006; an LRA-approved plan certifies the survey it describes, and the DENR file will show whether it was a re-survey, who requested it, and whether any 2006 plan was ever submitted. (3) The partition's annotation says the land was subdivided "in accordance with subdivision plan number (LRA) PSD-E2020005406," a number that did not exist until the EPEB entry of **17 Sep 2020**; if the instrument itself names that plan, it could not have been written on 15 Mar 2017 whatever happened in the field in 2006. That is why the instrument must be read, not inferred. (4) On 8 Sep 2020 the registered owners were the three sisters; the engineer certified their "full knowledge and consent" when none of them knew, the only agent was dead, and the Balanes were not owners. On the defense's best version, the certification is still false as to the owners. *Until the DENR file and the instrument are in hand, the antedating is an inference, marked as such; the chronology, the revocation and the absence of any partition power are facts.*

**An independent ground to cancel the title.** The Balane title rests on two instruments: the deed and the partition. Even if the court upheld the deed, a buyer of a portion of co-owned registered land holds at most an interest in the co-ownership; a title to a specific bounded lot can issue only on a partition made by the co-owners or ordered by the court. None exists. **TCT No. 079-2021002126 falls on any view of the deed.** Plead it in the alternative so the court has a second, narrower path to the same order.

**What not to argue.** Never say Cesar lacked authority for the 1993–2000 subdivisions that produced T-52540 itself. Those titles issued to the owners, the owners hold and sue on T-52540, and that is ratification (Art. 1910); the argument would attack our own root of title and reopen every lot in the 2-X-6 tree. The 1993 Subdivision Agreement (Annex AA) has one use: it names Cesar's actual transferees, and no Balane is among them. Aim "no power to partition" at the 2017 instrument only.

**Special Proceeding 2156** is used for what Cesar said and hid, not to annul the order. *Strait Times v. CA* (G.R. No. 126673, 28 Aug 1998) voids a replacement duplicate only if the original was not in fact lost, and that attack belongs in the Court of Appeals, not here. We cannot yet prove the duplicate was not lost; we can prove the petition concealed the sale, listed a dead title, and reached the owners by posting only.

**Document:** Master trial playbook (6 parts)  
**Source:** `MASTER_TRIAL_PLAYBOOK_2026-10.md`  
**Section:** 3A. THE WAR OF THE STATUTES, IN PLAIN WORDS

---

#### 3A. THE WAR OF THE STATUTES, IN PLAIN WORDS

The orders in this case turn on the text of the section, not on case law. These are the ten places where their article meets ours, and the one word in each that decides it. The full text of every provision, with the exhibit that fits it, is in *WAR_OF_THE_STATUTES_2026-10.pdf* (memo Annex 4).

| # | The collision | Their article | Our article | The deciding word, and why |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Can this court cancel the title? | PD 1529 §48: no "collateral attack" | §48 itself: cancellation allowed "in a direct proceeding" | **direct.** Our caption prays cancellation of TCT 079-2021002126; the judge recited it in her first order. This is the direct proceeding. |
| 2 | What could Cesar do? | Art. 1874: the SPA is the written authority | Art. 1874: the writing limits the sale to Llamanzares contract holders, after consultation | **writing.** She reads the instrument; the class and the condition are on its face. |
| 3 | Was this sale inside the writing? | Arts. 1900, 1902 (rely on the POA "as written"; "secret orders" do not bind), 1911 (owners "allowed" him) | Art. 1898 (void where the buyer "is aware of the limits"); PD 1529 §52 (annotation = notice) | **written.** The class and consultation are written terms annotated in 1996, not secret orders. Art. 1911 creates liability, not a valid land transfer; from 2005 the owners' conduct was a revocation. |
| 4 | Void, or merely fixable? | Art. 1403(1): "unenforceable, unless ratified"; Art. 1910: tacit ratification | Art. 1874: "shall be void"; Art. 1409(7): void by law, "cannot be ratified"; Art. 1410: imprescriptible | **void.** The special rule for land sold through an agent says void. Nothing void is ratified, and the money never reached an owner. |
| 5 | Does the revocation, or its non-registration, matter? | Art. 1921 (specified persons not notified); Art. 1922 (publication only prospective); PD 1529 §64 (revocation "shall be registered") | Art. 1921's own words; PD 1529 §52; *Ciruelas* (§64 is not a validity condition) | **specified.** She was never one of the specified persons, so revocation is beside the point. Never argue Art. 1922 both ways. |
| 6 | Good faith, until when? | Art. 527 (presumed); PD 1529 §53 ("innocent holder for value") | Arts. 526 ("aware"), 528 ("from the moment facts exist"); PD 1529 §51 (registration = operative act) | **aware.** Dated facts fill the window to 23 Nov 2021: her 2020 SPA, the July 2021 pleading, the August 2021 SPA pull, the September 2021 "my property." The judge finds facts, not character. |
| 7 | Could a specific lot be titled to her? | nothing | Art. 496 (partition "by agreement between the parties"); Art. 1878 ¶¶12, 15 | **parties.** No co-owner signed; no SPA allowed partition; the plan cited did not exist until 2020. The title falls here even if the deed stands. |
| 8 | What about the houses? | Arts. 448, 546, 548 (builder "in good faith": indemnity and retention) | Art. 526 (defines good faith); Art. 1678 (lessee's improvements); Arts. 449–452 (bad-faith builder) | **renting.** Their own Answer. Renters go to Art. 1678; bad-faith buyers to Art. 449; only preservation expenses refunded, no retention. |
| 9 | Rents and damages | Art. 544 (good-faith possessor keeps fruits) | Arts. 549, 2232, 2229, 2208 | **fraudulent,** proved by five dates, not adjectives: "in hand paid" vs. receipts to Feb 2017; the 2016 petition hiding the sale; the 2017 partition under a 2020 plan; the 2020 "consent of the owners" with the agent dead; the 2021 "my property." |
| 10 | Their counterclaim | Arts. 19–21, 2219 (malicious suit) | Rule 6 §7; Art. 2208 | **title.** A suit that wins on title was not malicious; the referrals are the agencies' acts. |

Three words carry the case in this court's form: **void** (Art. 1898), **aware** (Art. 526), **parties** (Art. 496). Each is on the page of an exhibit already in the record.
