**Section:** 4. DESTROYING THE DEFENSE'S CLAIMS

---

#### 4. DESTROYING THE DEFENSE'S CLAIMS

| The defense's best argument | Our tactical rebuttal and the evidence |
|---|---|
| **The 1992 SPA is sufficient written authority to sell**: it refers to the sale, its terms, the documents and the proceeds (Answer ¶39); annotated on the title since 1996 (Exh. B). | Agreed that Cesar could complete a sale **for the class**. The same paragraph names the class (Llamanzares contract holders / payors to Llamanzares) and the condition (prior consultation). Strict construction (*Bautista-Spille*) forbids reading them out. She was a tenant with no Llamanzares paper (Annex A, pp. 3–4; Annex D, pp. 2, 9–26); no consultation (Annex Q, p. 4). The 1996 annotation charged her with the limits (PD 1529 §52). Their witness's letter restates the class (Annex Y). |
| **The 1996 annotation summarized the power as "to sign, negotiate for the sale"; a buyer reading the title saw a power to sell, not a class limit.** (Ramos's best single sentence.) | PD 1529 §52 makes the registered **instrument** constructive notice, not the Registry's one-line memorandum of it; the annotation told every buyer where the instrument was. A buyer who stops at the summary has not inquired: "mere inspection of the face of such public document already constitutes sufficient inquiry" (*Sarili*) presupposes inspecting the instrument, and *Yabut* requires inquiry with the owners even when an SPA is produced. Her own processor pulled the full instrument in August 2021 and registered it (Def. Exh. 7, p. 1 footer): the buyer's side held paragraph 7 before registration. |
| **Gloria was in the class**: rent to Llamanzares, a 1980s down payment "recognized and later credited" (¶40; Salvador S14–16; Gloria S20–22, S35 [COND]). | No contract, no receipt, no book entry for any 1980s payment; the only "Down payment" is **6 Jan 2005, ₱20,000, to Salvador** (Annex D, p. 3). Rent is not a down payment; the "Occupant" receipts run to 2014 (Annex D). Three defense accounts of the down payment conflict (₱3,000 in the 1980s / "by 1992" / ₱20,000 in 2005). The burden is theirs (*Bucton*). *Double edge: the 2005 receipt predates the revocation; answer with the class, not the revocation.* |
| **No notice before the September 2016 sale**; publication binds only from 2020 forward; the revocation was never annotated (¶¶15, 19, 41; Salvador S32; Gloria S57, S63–64 [COND]). | The decisive window is 2016 to **23 Nov 2021** (*Duenas*). Their denials stop at 2016. The ten-rung notice ladder (Pillar B) fills the window, from their own Annex "1" to the "my property" SPA. Non-annotation of the revocation is beside the point when the SPA itself, annotated in 1996, excludes her. |
| **At most unenforceable and ratified** (Art. 1403[1], Art. 1910): the estate accepted payments; the owners stayed silent (¶42). | A sale outside a special power is **void**, not unenforceable (*Cosmic Lumber*; *Unchuan*); void contracts cannot be ratified (Art. 1409). Payments went to a revoked agent and were never remitted; no owner received anything (Jonathan). The Deed of Confirmation is the agent's act, not the principals'. |
| **Estoppel and laches**: unquestioned "from 1975 … to the time that it was offered for them to purchase" (¶43). | Silence toward a paying tenant is not acquiescence in a sale. Laches does not run against a void contract (*Unchuan*); the action is imprescriptible (Art. 1410). Registration was 2021; suit 2026. |
| **Good faith, ten circumstances** (¶44): she checked the SPA, the mother title, the Registry, the tax records and found nothing; her sister's registered purchase (Def. Exh. 3, 4) showed Cesar's sales were accepted. | The Registry after 10 Oct 2016 showed the agent's loss affidavit and the court-made duplicate (Annex C, p. 3). Def. Exh. 4 is **Rosalina Hansol's 1999 deed under the 1991 SPA**, not a Balane deed (no "second version" exists; object to the D2 label). All three *Duenas* conditions fail. She never asked the owners (Salvador S31; Gloria S43 [COND]). |
| **The grievance is against Cesar's estate; the owners clothed him with apparent authority** (¶¶38, 45): the 2005 Era sale, the 1999 Hansol sale. | Both predate the revocation; Hansol rests on the 1991 SPA. Art. 1902 protects reliance on the power "as written," and as written it excludes her. The estate is not a necessary party to declare a void deed between owner and buyer (counsel to confirm). |
| **Collateral attack** on Spec. Proc. 2156 and the partition; they do not hold the duplicate of T-2021002127 (¶46). | We do not attack the order; we read the petition and the testimony for what they admit and hide. The partition is attacked as an act no agent could do and, on its dates, could not have done (Pillar C). Entry 2021003236 is already in Exh. B. |
| **BUILDERS IN GOOD FAITH**: reimbursement of the price, taxes, registration, survey, "necessary and useful improvements, with the corresponding right of retention" (¶49; Arts. 448, 546, 548); improvements "at least ₱100,000" (Gloria S68–71 [COND]). **This is their exit if they lose on title.** | **Three periods, three answers.** *(1) 1975–Sep 2016, tenants:* Art. 448 with 546 "applies only to a possessor in good faith, i.e., one who builds on land with the belief that he is the owner thereof" and "does not apply where one's only interest is that of a lessee under a rental contract" (*Parilla v. Pilar*, G.R. No. 167680, 30 Nov 2006). A lessee's improvements fall under **Art. 1678**: the owners choose between paying one-half of the value and requiring removal; **no retention**. *(2) Sep 2016–Nov 2021, buyers:* a buyer who fails the inquiry the law demands is in bad faith from the deed; a bad-faith builder "loses what is built … without right to indemnity" (**Art. 449**); demolition at his expense (Art. 450); damages (Art. 451); only necessary expenses refundable (Art. 546 ¶1), without retention. *(3) After 22 Aug 2025:* good faith lost "from the moment facts exist" (Art. 528); all fruits owed, including the room rentals the Answer admits (¶27) (Art. 549). **Their cases answered:** *Macasaet v. Macasaet* (G.R. Nos. 154391-92, 30 Sep 2004) and *DepEd v. Casibang* (G.R. No. 192268, 27 Jan 2016) applied Art. 448 where the owner himself invited or permitted the building; no owner here consented to anything, the only relationship was a lease (*Parilla*), and ¶49 pleads ownership by purchase, not tolerance. **The list, item by item:** price → against Cesar's estate, which their own ¶38 calls the party whose "acts, receipts, collections" are "the very foundation" of the case; CGT, DST, transfer tax, surcharge, interest, registration → expenses of a void registration, not necessary expenses of the land; survey → an expense of the same void act; real property taxes actually paid → the one necessary expense, refundable on official receipts only, no retention; improvements → Art. 1678 before the deed, Art. 449 after; the other families' houses → not theirs. **Evidence:** Building Official permits or a no-record certification (PD 1096 §301 requires a permit and proof of right over the land; the Building Official is **defendant Erwin**, who refused the owners' request); Assessor's declarations on the buildings (owner, date, value); BFP, CANORECO and barangay clearances; dated satellite imagery; Shirley De Leon's 2020 account (Exh. N); the 2006 survey receipt "owned by the Heirs" (Annex D, p. 1). |
| **Counterclaim: harassment**, demand letter sent to the Sangguniang Bayan, ARTA complaints (¶¶52–57; Princess S21–25). | Every referral is a national agency's own act on its own findings (Annexes T, U, V). Keep them out of the case-in-chief; use on cross of Princess and Erwin for interest and bias only if they claim neutrality; one exhibit, one sentence of purpose, on rebuttal if the counterclaim is pressed. The counterclaim stands or falls with the title. |
| **Patricia's 2023 affidavit of loss, entered on T-2021002127, ratified the partition and the sale** (the ratification line, Art. 1910). | A void sale cannot be ratified (Art. 1409). An affidavit that a duplicate was lost is not an act adopting a sale or a partition; it was entered by the Registry on the titles it was then carrying. Only Patricia can say what she meant: her own testimony (by affidavit, and by videoconference if the court allows) is the clean answer, and it is now a listed gap. |
| **Impeaching Jonathan**: "Jayjay Moreno" (Princess S14); affidavit Q25/Q53 "negotiate only"; Complaint ¶25 ("September 24") and ¶50 ("annotated only in 2021"); Patricia's 2023 affidavit on T-2021002127. | Errata manifestation **before 14 October**; the class limit conceded in the affidavit's place; the 2023 affidavit explained as protection of the owners' interest, not consent; the name explained once, by Jonathan, before the defense raises it, if counsel agrees. Exhibits E and H were filed incomplete (p. 1 of 2; pp. 2–3 of 4): file complete certified copies before Ramos objects; the missing pages (the eCAR page; the Elevado SPA) help us. |
| **Attacking King** (11 Nov) **and de los Reyes** (2 Dec): King was complainant in Crim. 20-6030 before this judge, later prosecuted for perjury, and "bought the rights" to a house on this land in 2015; the CC 8563 caption lists "Francia Delos Santos" as King's co-defendant against Juntilla and Torralba, and the verification reads "Francia A. Delos Reyes" [VERIFY identity with our Exh. M affiant], so both are adverse parties to the defendants' family in a related suit. | Prepare her: acquitted by RTC Br. 41 (25 Sep 2023); CA dismissed Torralba/Juntilla's certiorari (17 Jul 2025) and reconsideration (4 Dec 2025). Answer in one sentence and move on. Never raise 20-6030 ourselves beyond one question to Jomil. |
| **The ten families on the lot**: "the owners let anyone settle" (tolerance, laches). | They never paid anyone; some predate 2016 (contradicting exclusive possession of a "specific portion" since 1975 and the 2020 consent certification); some hold Salvador's "deals," which are void (Art. 1898) and impeach him. A judgment binds non-parties who are "a guest or occupant of the premises with the permission of the defendant" or "a trespasser, squatter or agent of the defendant" (*Sunflower Neighborhood Assn. v. CA*, G.R. No. 136274, 3 Sep 2003); independent entrants need a separate action, with RA 7279 §28 governing eviction. Document them (names, lot, entry date, who admitted them, every paper they hold) by 4 Nov. |

**Section:** 5. THE COURTROOM PLAYBOOK

#### 5. THE COURTROOM PLAYBOOK

##### 5.1 The court's own form

**What the orders say** (four orders here: 6 Feb, 11 Mar, 12 Mar, 4 Sep 2026; two in Crim. 20-6030: 27 Nov 2020, 6 Jan 2021; Annex AB; Annex O, pp. 9–10). This section describes the documents, not the judge.

- **No case law in the seven writings in hand.** Each cites statutes and rules by section and date ("Section 72 of Republic Act 10951 which was promulgated on August 29, 2017"; "Paragraph 2, Section 1, Rule 112 of the 2019 Amended Rules of Court") and proceeds **rule → "Considering that" → WHEREFORE**, in one-sentence paragraphs, in English.
- **Exact particulars in the dispositive.** Receipt numbers, amounts in words and figures, bank branch, payee.
- **On procedure, the orders state:** "Liberally applying the rules of procedure … **No further liberality shall be accorded**"; incorrect pleadings "shall warrant appropriate sanction"; compliance with the Amended Rules and the Rules on Electronic Filing is required; the trial dates are "**final and intransferable** unless the ground for resetting is allowed under the rules"; "both counsels' timely compliance" is recorded.
- **Authority to act is checked on the record.** "Atty. Karlo M. Dialogo … has no authority to prosecute this case"; representatives must be "fully authorized in writing."
- **A prior act was recalled on a rule, without comment on anyone.** Warrants issued 27 Nov 2020, recalled 6 Jan 2021 as "prematurely filed."
- **The words "Summary Procedure" do not appear.** The 6 Feb order names the Amended Rules of Civil Procedure, the Revised Rules on Evidence and the Rules on Electronic Filing. Do not use the label until counsel confirms it from the pre-trial order.

**How we adapt:**

| What the orders do | Our filing / testimony / judgment |
|---|---|
| Statute by section | Memorandum built on Arts. 1898, 1874, 1878, 496, 449, 546, 549, 2232, 2208 and PD 1529 §§51–52, each pinpointed; the few decisive cases with G.R. number, date and one holding sentence |
| Rule → application → disposition | Each section: rule, exhibit-tagged facts, "considering that," relief; the memorandum ends with a **decision table** (rule / exhibit page / fact / disposition) in the same form |
| Exact particulars | Prayer drafted as a WHEREFORE with the deed's notarial numbers, title number, ARP number, lot, area, days, amounts in words and figures |
| Form and timeliness | The caption copied exactly ("079-2021002126"; "ARP NO. GR-2023-II-07-001-00256"); e-filing, transmittal and service complete; compliance filed on time with proof; any reset request names the rule ground in its first sentence |
| Late filing allowed once (JA Rule §10[a]) | Leave asked once, in one paragraph: valid reason, no prejudice, fine tendered. Keep it for Patricia's rebuttal affidavit if a rebuttal affidavit needs it |
| Authority on the face | Jonathan's SPA identified by exhibit on every appearance and in the offer |
| No characterization | We prove the facts by **dates on paper**, never by adjectives (see 5.3) |
| Our own errors | Corrected by us before they are raised: Complaint ¶¶25, 50; affidavit purpose clause ("September 26, 2019" → 29 Sep 2016; "28,981" → 28,891 sqm) |

**What the orders suggest about the form of the decision** (an inference from seven short writings, nothing more): deed void on Art. 1898 with Arts. 1874/1878 against Exh. E ¶7; title cancelled on Art. 496 as the second path; possession ordered with a fixed period; improvements and damages only to the extent dates and amounts are proved; counterclaim dismissed; Gloria's affidavit the one open question (admitted as the single late filing the rule allows, or the late filing held a waiver). Where a ruling against us would come from: **form**, before the merits: our own errors left for Ramos, the incomplete Exhibits E and H, a "Summary Procedure" label, an appearance without authority on its face, or a brief that argues both that Art. 1922 does not apply and that the 2020 publication was Art. 1922 "sufficient warning."


##### 5.2 The Cross-Examination Traps

**Salvador Osum dela Fuente** (defense Exh. 5 sponsor; the "class" witness)

| Trap | Document in hand |
|---|---|
| Read the "Occupant's Name" and "Payment for the month of" lines on the 2007 and 2013–14 receipts. *Is a monthly bill to an "Occupant" rent?* | Annex D, pp. 9–11, 22–26 |
| Who registered TIN [TIN withheld] ("Heirs of Mary W. Keesey Estate") and ordered the 1998 printing of the receipt books? Did any owner? | Annex D, p. 9 (form imprint) |
| Why is Receipt No. 0219 dated 18 Nov 2016 and No. 0218 dated 27 Nov 2016 when Nos. 0221–0223 are dated Sep–Oct 2016? Why is No. 0226 (30 Jan 2017) after No. 0227 (7 Jan)? *Were these written at the time of payment?* | Annex D, pp. 30–32 |
| Was the ₱250,000 "in hand paid" on 29 Sep 2016? Then what is Receipt No. 0228, "Full payment," of Feb 2017? | Annex E, p. 1; Annex D, p. 32 |
| S31: the owners never came home and your father dealt with the Agcaoili law office in Manila. *Did anyone consult the owners before selling to Mrs. Balane? Could they have been reached?* | Annex Q, p. 4 |
| S29: "others" processed the transfer. *Who paid the BIR on 15 June 2020, three years after your father died? Who presented the papers in November 2021?* | Annex E, p. 2; Annex K |
| January 2017, Spec. Proc. 2156: your father swore he was in possession. *Who was living on the 2,587 sqm?* | Annex F, p. 14 |
| The 29 Feb 2012 paper: your father's signature? Did any owner sign or know? Where is the power to sell? Who notarized it? *(Argue "nothing left to substitute," never "substitution forbidden.")* | Annex Z |
| **Lay the foundation for his 25 Nov 2025 letter** (Rule 132 §13): show it, fix date and place, ask if he wrote and signed it, let him explain. Then: *which Llamanzares contract or receipt did the Balanes hold? What documents did you issue to the other families on this lot, and what did they pay you for?* | Annex Y |
| Demand the **original receipts** for Annex D, pp. 2, 21, 31, 32 (faint digits). | — |

**Princess Balane Torralba** (Municipal Councilor)

| Trap | Document |
|---|---|
| S10: no part in the sale "except the survey." About 45 receipts in her name 2007–2012, including ₱15,000 "partial payment for lot." | Annex D, pp. 9–12, 21 |
| The 29 Dec 2006 survey receipt she paid: "lot **owned by the Heirs of Worrick Keesey**." | Annex D, p. 1 |
| S11: "no objection from anyone, ever." Her mother's 28 May 2020 SPA "to institute or file action … and defend me." King's sworn account that Princess said in 2020 "**May usapin pa yan**" (there is still an issue on that). | Annex M; Annex N, p. 10 |
| Which structures went up after Sep 2016, who paid, under what permit? | Building Official return |
| Bias, **only if she claims neutrality**: the Ombudsman docket IC-OC-JUL-26-1214. One question, then stop. | Annex U |

**Engr. Erwin H. Balane** (Municipal Engineer and Building Official)

| Trap | Document |
|---|---|
| S14/S23: he saw Cesar show the SPA "as proof he was authorized to sell." *Did you read paragraph 7? Whose contracts to sell? Whose down payments? Whose consultation?* | Annex B, p. 3 |
| S16: he requested the 2026 certified copies and keeps the family's papers. *Where is the owner's duplicate of T-2021002127? Who held the T-52540 duplicate issued in 2017 when it was surrendered in 2021?* | Annex C, p. 3; Annex K |
| He appears nowhere on the 2020 plan or the Registry files. *Who told Engr. Mangiga the owners consented on 8 Sep 2020?* | Annex L, p. 2 |
| *Does your office hold a building permit or certificate of occupancy for any structure your family built on this lot? Who signed the application as owner? Will you produce them?* | Building Official return; PD 1096 §301 |
| The 5 Feb 2026 letter ("deportation … prison time") and the two CSC referrals, **only if he claims good faith toward the owners' representative.** | Annexes S, T, V |

**Gloria H. Balane** (only if her affidavit is admitted and she appears from Canada)

| Trap | Document |
|---|---|
| S43: no contact with the owners, ever. | Annex R |
| S50: deed after full payment — against Receipt No. 0228, Feb 2017. | Annex D, p. 32 |
| S57: no notice of dispute before her title — against her own 28 May 2020 SPA. | Annex M |
| S59: twenty years in public office (kagawad 1997–2004; councilor 2004–2017). | COMELEC/SB certification to obtain |
| S63–64: first learned in Aug 2025 — against the "my property" SPA of 15 Sep 2021 and the pleading received by her side on 9 Jul 2021. *Were you in Daet on 15 September 2021?* | Annex K, p. 9; Annex N |

**Efren Balane** (if called): Sangguniang Bayan Resolution No. 103-86 (16 Jul 1986) lists "Hon. Efren Balane – Acting Kagawad" present, unanimously approving a resolution reciting "Mary Worrick Keesey, represented by her Attorney-In-Fact, Mr. Benjamin Llamanzares" (Annex W; identity to verify). The 2003 and 2005 receipts are in his name.

**Jomil Torralba**: one question — in 2020 he posted ₱180,000 in cash bonds in a case before this court over who had the right to this land (Annex O, p. 10). Stop.

**Objections to have ready:** Def. Exh. 4 mislabeled (Hansol 1999 deed; no second Balane deed exists); Gloria's affidavit marks exhibits with numbers conflicting with the Answer's list; originals of Exh. 5 for the faint digits.

##### 5.3 The Rebuttal Sequence That Ends the Case

Rebuttal is "rebutting evidence only" (Rule 30 §5[f]); this court will not let it become a second case in chief. The records custodians (R2, R4, R5, R7) prove our own claim, so they testify **in chief on 2 December**; at rebuttal they are recalled only to meet a specific thing a defense witness swore. Every item below is keyed to that sworn statement; everything else goes in during our five dates. Rest on 9 Dec **subject to rebuttal** and ask for rebuttal dates after 10 Mar 2027.

| They will have sworn | The rebuttal that ends it |
|---|---|
| Llamanzares-era buyers; installments, not rent; no notice (Salvador) | **R1** Salvador's 25 Nov 2025 letter (foundation laid on cross) + Cesar's 2012 delegation; Jonathan on no consultation, no money |
| "I checked the Registry and found nothing" (¶44[h], ¶32; Gloria S56–57) | **R2** Registry custodian: Primary Entry Book (presenter in 2021), who surrendered the 2017 duplicate, Elevado's Aug 2021 pull of the SPA |
| "No dispute, no notice until 2025" (Gloria S57, S63; Princess S11) | **R3** Their own Annex "1" read back; King's 9 Jul 2021 pleading with the Aquino stamp; this court's 6 Jan 2021 order, one page, no comment |
| "Survey under Cesar's management" (Gloria S36); Erwin "nothing to do with it" | **R4** DENR Region V file + Engr. Mangiga: who applied, paid, and signed as the owners' representative on 8 Sep 2020 |
| "The partition is regular and final" (¶46) | **R5** Clerk of Court notarial custodian: Asis Books CXVI and CXXII; who appeared for Doc. 402 and Doc. 115; the duplicate original of the agreement |
| "Fully paid before the deed" (Salvador; Gloria S33, S50; ¶44[f]) | **R6** Their own Exh. 5, receipt by receipt (Annex D, pp. 29–32) |
| "Builders in good faith, ₱100,000, retention" (¶49) | **R7** Building Official and Assessor returns, dated imagery, put in during our dates; used in rebuttal only against whatever dates or values they swear to |
| Harassment counterclaim (Princess S21–25) | **R8** Only if pressed: Annexes T, U, V, one sentence of purpose |
| Attacks on Jonathan | **R9** Answered in chief, never left for rebuttal |

**The five dated documents that lock Art. 2232 exemplary damages without an adjective:** (1) the deed of 29 Sep 2016 recites "in hand paid" against receipts to Feb 2017; (2) the petition of 20 Oct 2016 by a revoked agent calling himself "Administrator," listing a title cancelled in 1999, hiding the sale; (3) the partition dated 15 Mar 2017 following a plan surveyed 8 Sep 2020, numbered before an instrument thirteen days older; (4) the certificate of 8 Sep 2020 that the survey was made "with the full knowledge and consent of the owner(s)" when the only agent was three years dead; (5) the sworn "my property" of 15 Sep 2021 over land titled to three others. Beside them, the boarded-up door of 2020 (Exh. N). The word "fraud" need not appear.

**Order of the rebuttal day:** custodians first (R2, R5, R4, then R7), because records cannot be argued with; then R1 and R3; Jonathan last, short, on the three facts only he can give. Then rest.


##### 5.4 The Proposed Judgment (the WHEREFORE she can adopt)

> Considering that the Special Power of Attorney dated March 16, 1992 (Exhibit "E"), annotated on Transfer Certificate of Title No. T-52540 on June 21, 1996 (Exhibit "B"), authorized Cesar M. de la Fuente to sell only to third persons holding contracts to sell executed by the previous administrator or who had paid him down payments or partial payments, and only after prior consultation with the registered owners; considering that defendant Gloria H. Balane, by her own Answer, occupied the property as a lessee and offered no contract to sell executed by the previous administrator and no receipt of any payment to him, the only receipt marked "Down payment" being dated January 6, 2005 and issued by another (defendants' Exhibit "5", page 3); considering that no consultation with the registered owners was made, as the registered owner Patricia Keesey Zschoche testified and as the defendants' own witness Salvador Osum dela Fuente admitted (S31); and considering that under Section 52 of Presidential Decree No. 1529 the annotation was constructive notice of its limits to all persons from June 21, 1996, the Deed of Absolute Sale dated September 29, 2016 (Exhibit "H") was executed beyond the scope of the written authority and is void. Considering further that a buyer of registered land must remain a purchaser in good faith until the registration of the conveyance and that the conveyance was registered only on November 23, 2021; considering that by her own Answer defendant Gloria H. Balane and her family had paid rent for the premises to the registered owners' administrator (paragraphs 40, 43 and 44[b]); that on May 28, 2020 she executed before the Philippine Consulate General in Toronto a Special Power of Attorney (Annex "1" to the Answer) to institute and defend actions over the property, describing it by the registered owners' own tax declaration and withholding the power to sell; that on August 16, 2021 her representative Karen Gay A. Elevado obtained from the Registry of Deeds a certified copy of the Special Power of Attorney of March 16, 1992, including its paragraph 7 (defendants' Exhibit "7"); and that on September 15, 2021 she executed a Special Power of Attorney describing land then registered in the names of Geraldine K. Hoppe, Patricia K. Zschoche and Marcia Ellen Keesey as "my property," the defendants were not purchasers in good faith from the execution of the deed on September 29, 2016, they being charged from June 21, 1996 with the limits of the authority under which they claimed, and in any event from the facts existing before registration which show that they were not unaware of those limits. Considering that the deed recites the price as "in hand paid" while the defendants' own receipts show payments continuing to February 2017 (defendants' Exhibit "5", pages 31 to 33); that the agent's petition of October 20, 2016 for a new owner's duplicate did not disclose the sale made three weeks earlier; that the Partition-Subdivision Agreement dated March 15, 2017 recites a subdivision plan surveyed on September 8, 2020 and approved on April 6, 2021 (defendants' Exhibit "1", page 2); and that on September 15, 2021 defendant Gloria H. Balane described land registered in the names of others as "my property," the defendants acted in a reckless and oppressive manner within the meaning of Article 2232 of the Civil Code; and considering that their refusal to vacate after the demand received on August 22, 2025 (Exhibit "J-1") compelled the plaintiff to litigate to protect her interest (Article 2208, paragraph 2).
>
> **WHEREFORE**, judgment is rendered: (1) declaring the Deed of Absolute Sale dated September 29, 2016, Doc. No. 304, Page No. 61, Book No. CXVI, series of 2016 of Notary Public Donald E. Asis, NULL AND VOID; (2) Directing the Registry of Deeds for the Province of Camarines Norte to CANCEL Transfer Certificate of Title No. 079-2021002126 in the name of Gloria H. Balane and to ISSUE, in lieu thereof, a new transfer certificate of title over Lot 2-X-6-I-4-C-1, plan (LRA) PSD-E2020005406, with an area of Two Thousand Five Hundred Eighty-Seven (2,587) square meters, in the names of Geraldine K. Hoppe, Patricia K. Zschoche and Marcia Ellen Keesey, upon payment of the lawful fees; (3) declaring NULL AND VOID Tax Declaration ARP No. GR-2023-II-07-001-00256 and every declaration in the name of Gloria H. Balane over the said lot, and directing the Municipal Assessor of Mercedes and the Provincial Assessor for the Province of Camarines Norte to CANCEL the same; (4) ordering the defendants and all persons claiming under them, including every occupant admitted with their permission, to VACATE within THIRTY (30) CALENDAR DAYS from finality; (5) declaring FORFEITED without indemnity all improvements introduced after September 29, 2016 (Art. 449), without prejudice to necessary expenses under Art. 546 ¶1 if proved, with no right of retention, and reserving to the owners the Art. 1678 option for any structure proved to predate the deed; (6) Ordering the defendants, jointly and severally, to render an ACCOUNTING of all rentals and fruits received from the said lot from August 22, 2025, the date of receipt of the demand, until possession is surrendered, and to pay the same to the plaintiff, and, in any event, to pay the plaintiff temperate damages of ______ Pesos (₱______) under Article 2224 of the Civil Code for the use and occupation of the lot from August 22, 2025, or reasonable compensation at the monthly rate proved; (7) Ordering the defendants, jointly and severally, to pay the plaintiff exemplary damages of Two Hundred Thousand Pesos (₱200,000.00) under Articles 2229 and 2232 of the Civil Code, the plaintiff having been found entitled to the compensatory and temperate damages in paragraph 6 (Article 2234), and attorney's fees of One Hundred Thousand Pesos (₱100,000.00) under Article 2208, paragraphs (1), (2) and (5), with costs; and (8) DISMISSING the counterclaim. SO ORDERED.

*Notes for counsel. (a) Paragraph 2: naming the non-party co-owners is within the action, since Art. 487 "covers all kinds of action for the recovery of possession" and a co-owner "may bring such an action, without the necessity of joining all the other co-owners" (*Catedrilla v. Lauron*, G.R. No. 179011, 15 Apr 2013); cancelling the Balane title without ordering a new one would leave Lot C-1 untitled; the alternative, reinstating T-52540 by cancelling both derivative titles, touches the owners' own TCT 079-2021002127 and the non-party co-owners, so the new-title form is proposed. (b) Paragraph 6: Article 2234 bars exemplary damages unless the plaintiff is first shown entitled to moral, temperate or compensatory damages; the temperate or compensatory award is the predicate, and its figure must be proved or reasonably estimated (Article 2225). (c) The good-faith clause rests only on the defendants' own pleading and instruments and on the Registry's certification, not on the neighbour's pleading of July 9, 2021, so it stands even if that notice is never imputed to Mrs. Balane. (d) Jurisdiction: Exhibit "D" states the current assessed value of the lot as Two Hundred Fifty-Seven Thousand Six Hundred Seventy Pesos (₱257,670.00) (market value ₱4,294,420 at six percent; tax effectivity 2024), within the ₱400,000 limit of BP 129 Section 33(3) as amended by RA 11576. The exemplary-damages figure is the amount the En Banc awarded in Duenas; counsel to confirm the damages jurisdiction of this court and adjust.*

**Document:** Master trial playbook (6 parts)  
**Source:** `MASTER_TRIAL_PLAYBOOK_2026-10.md`  
**Section:** 6. IMMEDIATE ACTION ITEMS AND MISSING EVIDENCE

---

#### 6. IMMEDIATE ACTION ITEMS AND MISSING EVIDENCE

##### 6.1 The clock

| By when | Action | What is lost if late |
|---|---|---|
| **NOW** | **Read the 13 May 2026 pre-trial order.** Trial opened on 14 October, so the Rule 18 §7 window to modify the order "before trial" has closed. The partition and the improvements are nonetheless in issue by the defendants' own pleading: the tenth defense (¶46) puts the partition's validity in issue and the twelfth (¶49) puts the improvements in issue, and the Complaint prays cancellation of the title that the partition created. If the order's statement of issues is narrower, counsel relies on those pleadings and, failing that, on Rule 10 §5 (issues tried with the express or implied consent of the parties are treated as if raised in the pleadings). Records custodians not named in the order come in under subpoena, with leave if the defense objects; the opening words of Rule 30 §5 ("unless the court for special reasons otherwise directs") are the other route. | Arguing an issue the order does not contain invites an objection this judge will sustain; the pleadings are the answer. |
| **Before 14 Oct** | File the **errata manifestation** (Complaint ¶25 "September 24" → 29 Sep 2016; ¶50 "annotated only in 2021" → 21 Jun 1996; affidavit purposes [g], [h], Q11–12). Confirm the governing procedure from the order. File the **Registry and Clerk of Court records requests** (drafted; keep stamped copies). Decide which theory leads (class limit) and how Q26/Q53 are handled. | Ramos opens cross with our own errors. |
| **14 Oct** | Jonathan testifies; counsel states the purpose of his testimony when calling him (Rule 132 §35). Ask to sub-mark Entry 2021003236 as **B-5**. Announce on the record the subpoenas below, returnable **2 Dec**, and the complete certified copies of Exhibits E and H. | Late subpoena requests refused as delay. |
| **By 21 Oct** | File the subpoena requests. Jonathan has now testified, so a supplemental affidavit means **recalling him**, which needs leave "for good reasons" (Rule 132 §9) and uses the one late filing the rule allows; weigh it against putting the same facts in through Patricia. Locate Jonathan's own 2025 letter to Salvador. | The one allowed late filing spent elsewhere, or refused as untimely. |
| **By 28 Oct** (five days before 4 Nov) or **27 Nov** (for 2 Dec) | **Patricia's judicial affidavit.** The Complaint (¶63) reserved her as Exh. O; if the pre-trial order kept the reservation, her affidavit is filed five days before the date she testifies, otherwise with a motion for leave. She is the only first-hand witness that no consultation was ever made, that no owner received any payment, and what her 22 Feb 2023 affidavit of loss meant. She is in California: ask the court for testimony by videoconference under the Supreme Court's videoconferencing guidelines (A.M. No. 20-12-01-SC) [counsel to confirm the current rule and the consular or approved venue], or a deposition under Rule 23 before a consular officer. | Non-consultation rests on Salvador's cross alone, and the 2023 affidavit is left to the defense to characterize. |
| **By 30 Oct** | Shirley De Leon confirmed for 4 Nov; any supplement to Exh. N filed. | Waiver. |

PSA death certificate of Cesar. SB Res. 103-86 certified with attendance; Gloria's elective terms (COMELEC / SB Secretary). | The occupant question stays unmanaged into the defense stage. |

| **13 Jan–10 Mar 2027** | Cross per 5.2; **Rule 132 §13 foundation** for Salvador's letter; demand originals; object to Def. Exh. 4's label. | The letter cannot impeach him in rebuttal. |
| **Rebuttal** | Affidavits five days before each date; custodians by subpoena (JA Rule §5); each exhibit tied to the sworn statement it rebuts. | Excluded as new matter or late. |
| **After** | Memoranda ending with the decision table; decision within 90 days of submission; expect an appeal to the RTC within 15 days (no immediate execution in a recovery-of-ownership judgment), so every finding must carry an exhibit page. | A judgment won on characterization is reversed; one won on dates and pages is affirmed. |

Realistic calendar: our evidence closes 9 Dec 2026; theirs 10 Mar 2027; rebuttal Mar–Apr 2027; memoranda by about May; decision by about **August 2027**.

### Civil Case No. 26-360, Zschoche v. Balane: counsel focus sheet

MTC Mercedes, Judge Cu · plaintiff's dates 14 Oct, 4 Nov, 11 Nov, 2 Dec, 9 Dec 2026 · 14 October 2026, updated 15 October · internal, not a pleading. Everything here is backed by the Master Trial Playbook, the War of the Statutes and the bound dossier (exhibit pages); this sheet is the short version.

> 

The case in one line

Cesar sold to a buyer outside the class his written authority allowed, without the consultation it required; the buyer paid the owners rent for decades, never asked them, and finished the paperwork years after the agent was dead and his authority had been published as revoked. **Lead with the class. Revocation is the backup. Never argue Article 1922 both ways.**

The order of argument, every time

Class (Exh. E ¶7; Exh. B p. 3) → void (Art. 1898, with Arts. 1874 and 1409) → not an innocent purchaser (Arts. 526, 528; PD 1529 §51) → no partition (Art. 496) → consequences (Arts. 449, 452, 546 ¶1, 1678, 549, 2208, 2232, 2234) → counterclaim falls.

The three words that decide it on this court's method

**void** (Art. 1898) · **aware** (Art. 526) · **parties** (Art. 496). A fourth sits in the SPA itself: **therefor** (a down payment is paid for a sale, and a sale needs an object and a price: Arts. 1318, 1458, 1460, 1475, 1482).

Proc. 2156; Civil Case 8563 through Mrs. King). The drafted subpoena request is filed after the defense rests. Assessor, SB Secretary and Building Official: subpoena at rebuttal only.

She is the only first-hand witness to no consultation, no payment and what her 2023 affidavit of loss meant, so her affidavit answers specific defense evidence (Rule 30 §5[f]): the deed's Article 1623 recital, the estate-named receipts, Erwin's "first with Ben Llamanzares," Salvador's Agcaoili answer. M affiant she is an adverse party in a related suit; keep her to Q1–Q9 or drop her. ### THE BAD FAITH OF THE DEFENDANTS

**Patricia Keesey Zschoche v. Gloria H. Balane, et al.**

Civil Case No. 26-360, Municipal Trial Court, Mercedes, Camarines Norte

Accion reivindicatoria, declaration of nullity of the Deed of Absolute Sale of 29 September 2016, cancellation of TCT No. 079-2021002126 and ARP No. GR-2023-II-07-001-00256, accounting and damages

Itemized summary with annexes, prepared for counsel · 13 October 2026 · internal work product, not a pleading · nothing in it has been filed, served or sent

> 

**What this document is.** It states, in the order the court will need them, the facts that show the defendants bought, papered and registered this land in bad faith; the Philippine statute and the Supreme Court rulings that decide each point; the defendants' case at its strongest and the answer to it; the plan for the plaintiff's five trial dates, the defendants' five dates, and rebuttal; and the judgment we ask the court to write. Every fact carries an annex page. Every rule carries its article or section. Every case carries its G.R. number and date, and only cases whose text was read this week are cited.


**Marks.** [IN EVIDENCE] the page is already marked or attached in the record. [TO OFFER] we hold the document but must still offer it or obtain a certified copy. [CONDITIONAL] usable only if the court admits Gloria Balane's excluded judicial affidavit. [INFERRED] a conclusion drawn from documents, not stated in one. [VERIFY] a digit or name that must be checked on the original before anyone says it in court.

| Part 1 | The decision this case turns on, and the judgment we are building toward |
|---|---|
| Part 2 | What happened, in order: the itemized facts, each tied to an annex page |

**Document:** Bad-faith dossier: itemized summary (Parts 1 to 7; annexes in the PDF)  
**Source:** `BALANE_BAD_FAITH_SUMMARY_2026-10.html`  
**Section:** PART 1. THE DECISION THIS CASE TURNS ON

---

#### PART 1. THE DECISION THIS CASE TURNS ON

The court has to answer one question. Was Gloria H. Balane a purchaser in good faith and for value when she bought from an agent, Cesar M. de la Fuente, a 2,587-square-meter portion of land registered in the names of three sisters who were never consulted and never paid?

If she was not, the rest follows by statute. A sale of land by an agent outside the written authority is void (Civil Code, Articles 1874, 1878 paragraph 5 and 1898). A void contract cannot be ratified and the action to declare it void does not prescribe (Articles 1409 and 1410). Registration adds nothing to a void deed (Presidential Decree No. 1529, Sections 51 and 53). A builder in bad faith loses what she built without indemnity (Article 449). A possessor in bad faith returns the fruits (Article 549). Exemplary damages and attorney's fees follow a finding of bad faith (Articles 2208, 2229 and 2232).

The Supreme Court has settled who carries the burden. "The burden of proving the status of a purchaser in good faith and for value lies upon one who asserts that status. This onus probandi cannot be discharged by mere invocation of the ordinary presumption of good faith." (_Heirs of Bucton v. Spouses Go_, G.R. No. 188395, 20 November 2013.) The En Banc has settled when that good faith must exist. "Buyers of registered land must be continuing purchasers for value and in good faith until the registration of the conveyance." (_Duenas v. Metropolitan Bank and Trust Co._, G.R. No. 209463, 29 November 2022.)

Gloria Balane bought on 29 September 2016 and registered on 23 November 2021. Her good faith therefore has to survive five years, one month and twenty-five days. The record shows it did not survive the first day, and it shows what she and her household learned in every year after.

**The forward target.** A judgment that declares the deed void, cancels TCT No. 079-2021002126 and the tax declaration issued on it, restores possession to the registered owners, forfeits the improvements built after 29 September 2016, orders an accounting of fruits from the date of the demand, and awards exemplary damages and attorney's fees. The En Banc disposition in _Duenas_ is the template: forfeiture of improvements without indemnity, exemplary damages, and attorney's fees because the owners "were compelled to litigate." Part 6 writes it out in the form this court uses.

**The sentence that carries the case.** Cesar sold to a buyer outside the class his authority allowed, without the consultation it required, and the buyer knew the owners, paid them rent for decades, never asked them, and finished the paperwork years after the agent was dead and his authority had been published as revoked.


**Document:** Bad-faith dossier: itemized summary (Parts 1 to 7; annexes in the PDF)  
**Source:** `BALANE_BAD_FAITH_SUMMARY_2026-10.html`  
**Section:** PART 2. WHAT HAPPENED, IN ORDER

---

#### PART 2. WHAT HAPPENED, IN ORDER

Each item is one fact, with its source page. Items are numbered F1 to F41 and are referred to by number in Parts 3 to 6, so nothing is said twice.

##### Stage one: the defendants were the owners' tenants (1975 to 2016)

F1. The defendants' own verified Answer admits that the family occupied the 2,587-square-meter portion from 1975 as renters of the owners' administrator. "They paid monthly rent to the previous administrator Ben Llamanzares through Manuel Violeta" (paragraph 40); "from 1975 while they were renting the property, to the time that it was offered for them to purchase" (paragraph 43); "they regularly paid rent to the previous administrator" (paragraph 44[b]). These are judicial admissions under Rule 129, Section 4. They need no proof. [Annex A, pp. 3, 4] [IN EVIDENCE]

F2. All three defense witnesses swear the same. Salvador dela Fuente: the Balanes agreed to buy "ang lupain na kanilang nirerentahan" (the land they were renting) and he collected "mga bayad sa upa" (rent payments). Princess Balane Torralba: "noong una ay nirerentahan lang ito ng aking magulang" (at first my parents only rented it). Engr. Erwin Balane: "noong una, umuupa lamang ang aming pamilya sa property na iyon" (at first our family only rented that property). [Annex Q, pp. 2, 4 (Salvador); pp. 5, 7 (Princess); pp. 8, 9 (Erwin)] [IN EVIDENCE when affirmed]

F3. The owners' written authority to Cesar is the Special Power of Attorney of 16 March 1992, executed in Los Angeles by Geraldine K. Hoppe, Patricia K. Zschoche and Marcia Ellen Keesey. Paragraph 6 states its purpose: to settle unpaid real estate taxes by negotiating a sale "to interested third parties." Paragraph 7 then confines the sale to a class and a condition. Cesar is appointed "to negotiate for the sale of the above-described real estate property in favor of third persons in possession of CONTRACTS TO SELL executed by the previous administrator, MR. BEN LLAMANZARES and/or to those who have paid their downpayments or have made partial payments therefor to the latter. Said sale shall be upon such terms and conditions and under such covenants which said Attorney-in-Fact may deem fit, proper and convenient, after prior consultation with us, and to sign and execute the necessary documents, agreements and other legal papers pertaining thereto as well as to receive the proceeds of the aforementioned sale in our collective behalf." The words "in possession" govern "CONTRACTS TO SELL," not the land. The class is holders of a Llamanzares contract to sell, or persons who had paid Llamanzares a down payment or partial payment. Every sale required prior consultation with the owners. The property the SPA describes is the mother parcel: "covered by Transfer Certificate of Title No. T-4497 ... Lot 2 of the subdivision plan (LRC) Psd-12802 ... ONE HUNDRED THIRTY NINE THOUSAND ONE HUNDRED THIRTY TWO SQUARE METERS (139,132)," held by the three sisters as "undivided interests" after their 1988 extrajudicial settlement (paragraphs 1 to 3). It names no derivative title and no lot of T-52540. [Annex B, pp. 2, 3 (paragraphs 1 to 9)] [IN EVIDENCE: plaintiff's Exh. E; defense Exh. 7]

F4. That SPA was annotated on TCT No. T-52540 on 21 June 1996. The entry reads "appointing CESAR M. DELA FUENTE, as atty-in-fact of GERALDINE K. HOPPE ET AL., to sign, negotiate for the sale of the parcel of land in this title." This is the defendants' best fact, and it is also ours: from 1996 the registry itself sent every buyer to the instrument, and the instrument contains the class limit and the consultation clause (PD 1529, Section 52). Complaint paragraph 50 says the SPA was annotated only in 2021. That is wrong and must be corrected before the defense uses it. [Annex C, p. 2] [IN EVIDENCE: Exh. B]

F5. No defendant was ever in the class. The defendants plead a 1980s down payment of Three Thousand Pesos (P3,000.00) to Llamanzares through Violeta (Answer paragraph 40). There is no receipt for it. The earliest receipt in their Exhibit 5 is dated October 2[5], 2003 [VERIFY day], from Efren Balane, One Thousand Pesos (P1,000.00), "as payment for lot in Mercedes." The only receipt that says "Down payment" is dated 6 January 2005, Twenty Thousand Pesos (P20,000.00), signed "Von dela Fuente," that is, Salvador. Both are payments to Cesar's side, not to Llamanzares. Neither is a contract to sell. [Annex D, pp. 2, 3; Annex A, p. 3] [IN EVIDENCE: defense Exh. 5]

F6. On 15 August 2005 the three owners revoked Cesar's authority. The instrument, acknowledged the same day before Vice Consul Noemi T. Diaz in Los Angeles, appoints a different attorney-in-fact and ends: "Any Special Power of Attorney in favor of any person other than [the new attorney-in-fact] is hereby disowned and should be deemed without force and effect." The revocation was never annotated on T-52540. [Annex H, pp. 1, 2] [IN EVIDENCE: Exh. F]

F7. On 29 December 2006, after the revocation, Princess Balane paid Three Thousand Pesos (P3,000.00) to Villafuerte Engineering and Surveying Office for a "subdivision survey of lot owned by the H[ei]rs of Worrick Keesey segregating a portion thereof in favor of the above-named." The receipt is in the defendants' own Exhibit 5. In 2006 the family paid to survey land it was told, in writing, belonged to the Keesey heirs. [Annex D, p. 1] [IN EVIDENCE]

F8. From March 2007 to September 2014 the family paid on printed receipts of the "HEIRS OF MARY W. KEESEY ESTATE," each with an "Occupant's Name" line and a "Payment for the month of" line. Fifteen receipts bill a named month: March, April, May, June and July 2007 (payor Princess Balane, One Thousand to Two Thousand Pesos each); September 2013 to September 2014 (payor "Golly Balane" or "Mrs. Golly Balane," Two Thousand Pesos each). A monthly bill in the name of an "Occupant" is rent. The defendants offered this series "to prove rents paid" (Answer paragraph 61[e]). [Annex D, pp. 9, 10, 11, 22 to 26; Annex A, p. 8] [IN EVIDENCE]

F9. Princess Balane Torralba, now a Municipal Councilor, was the named payor on about forty-five receipts from 2007 to 2012 [VERIFY count], including a Fifteen Thousand Peso (P15,000.00) "partial payment for lot" [VERIFY year]. Her judicial affidavit swears she had no part in the sale "maliban na lang sa tumulong ako sa gastusin sa pagpa-survey" (except that I helped with the survey expense). [Annex D, pp. 9 to 12, 21; Annex Q, p. 6 (Princess)]

F10. Salvador dela Fuente swears the owners never personally dealt with Cesar after 1992 and that nobody consulted them: "Wala po. Hindi naman sila umuuwi sa Pilipinas. Ang madalas lang po na kausap ng aking ama ay ang Agcaoili law office sa Maynila" (No. They never came home to the Philippines. The one my father usually spoke to was the Agcaoili law office in Manila). The owners' Manila counsel was known and reachable. The consultation the SPA required was never sought. [Annex Q, p. 4 (Salvador)]

F31. On 12 to 16 August 2021 Karen Gay Elevado obtained from the Registry a certified copy of the 1992 SPA (Ref. No. 2021004150). F32. The notary's name on the copy in hand is faint. [VERIFY: fresh certified copy of page 4 of 4 of RD Ref. 2025001098] [Annex K, p. 9]

F33. On 21 September 2021 the owners' tax declaration was revised to show Lot 2-X-6-I-4-C-1 under the new plan, on a "letter request" not in hand. On 18 November 2021 a second eCAR, No. 201900675224, issued under T-52540, reusing the 15 June 2020 payments; no new tax was paid. On 23 November 2021 the Registry entered the 1992 SPA (again), the Deed of Confirmation, the sale and the partition, cancelled T-52540 "totally," and issued TCT No. 079-2021002126 to Gloria and TCT No. 079-2021002127 to the owners. The sale was registered one thousand eight hundred eighty-one days after the deed and one thousand six hundred sixteen days after the agent's death. [Annex C, pp. 3, 4; Annex E, p. 2; Annex K, p. 6; Annex L, p. 4] [IN EVIDENCE: Exh. B-2 to B-4; defense Exh. 1 and 6]

ERWIN H. BALANE, in his capacity as Municipal Engineer." [Annex V, pp. 1, 2]

F39. About ten families now live in houses on the lot. None of them ever paid rent to the Balanes or to the owners. Some were there long before the 2016 deed. Others are there under "deals" made by Salvador dela Fuente, the defense's own witness and collector. Nothing in the record describes them; the Answer admits only that "certain rooms on the property were occasionally rented out after 2016" (paragraph 27). Which houses stand on the 2,587-square-meter lot, when each family entered, and what paper each holds from Salvador or from Llamanzares are not yet established. [JONATHAN'S ACCOUNT, 13 October 2026; not in the record; to be documented, Part 7 item 16]

F40. On 29 February 2012 Cesar delegated to his son Salvador O. dela Fuente the powers of the 1991 SPA, quoted in the instrument as general control and supervision, collection of sums due with power to sign receipts, and representation before the BIR, the Registry and the DAR. The delegation mentions no power to sell and does not mention the 1992 SPA. It was notarized by Atty. Elaine Gay R. Belen (Doc. No. 55, Page 17, Book IX, series of 2012). The owners never authorized Salvador and never knew of this instrument. Salvador is not Cesar: by 2012 Cesar had held no authority for seven years, so there was nothing to pass on; what the instrument purports to pass is administration only; and a substitute can never hold more than the agent had, so no power to sell existed anywhere in this chain. Every receipt Salvador signed for the Balanes from 2012 to the "Full payment" of February 2017 was therefore issued by a person with no authority from the owners of any kind. [Annex Z, pp. 1, 2] [TO OFFER: copy in hand; obtain the notarial register entry]

F41. On 25 November 2025 Salvador dela Fuente wrote to the plaintiff's representative, over his signature, a two-page account of his father's administration.
