The letter is addressed to the plaintiff's representative under the name "Jonathan Ildefonso Moreno," the name Princess Balane Torralba's affidavit also uses; Jonathan's own letter that prompted it must be located before this one is used. [Annex Y, pp. 1, 2] [TO OFFER: through Jonathan as recipient, and by confronting Salvador with his signature on cross]


**Document:** Bad-faith dossier: itemized summary (Parts 1 to 7; annexes in the PDF)  
**Source:** `BALANE_BAD_FAITH_SUMMARY_2026-10.html`  
**Section:** PART 3. WHAT THE FACTS PROVE

---

#### PART 3. WHAT THE FACTS PROVE

Each element below is a rule the court must apply, stated by article or section, followed by the facts (by F-number) that satisfy it and the one ruling that decides it.

##### 3.1 The sale was outside the written authority, so it is void

**Rule.** Article 1874: "When a sale of a piece of land or any interest therein is through an agent, the authority of the latter shall be in writing; otherwise, the sale shall be void." Article 1878, paragraph 5, requires a special power "to enter into any contract by which the ownership of an immovable is transmitted." Article 1898: a contract made by an agent "exceeding the scope of his authority" which the principal does not ratify "shall be void if the party with whom the agent contracted is aware of the limits of the powers granted by the principal." A power of attorney "must be strictly construed, and courts will not infer or presume broad powers from deeds which do not sufficiently include property or subject under which the agent is to deal" (_Bautista-Spille v. NICORP Management_, G.R. No. 214057, 19 October 2015). Where the agent acts outside the power, "the sale ipso jure is consequently void" (_Cosmic Lumber Corp. v. Court of Appeals_, G.R. No. 114311, 29 November 1996).

**Application.** The written authority is F3. It permits a sale only to holders of Llamanzares contracts to sell or persons who paid Llamanzares, and only after prior consultation with the owners. Gloria Balane was a tenant (F1, F2, F8) whose earliest payment was to Cesar's side in 2003 (F5). No consultation was ever made (F10). The limits were on the title from 1996 (F4) and in the buyer's hands by August 2021 at the latest (F31). Article 1898 makes the contract void because the buyer is charged with the limits (PD 1529, Section 52).

**The class clause, word by word.** "In favor of third persons in possession of CONTRACTS TO SELL executed by the previous administrator, MR. BEN LLAMANZARES and/or to those who have paid their downpayments or have made partial payments therefor to the latter." Sub-class one is holders of a written contract to sell; the object of "in possession of" is the contracts, which are what the next words say Llamanzares "executed," so the defense's reading that "possession" means possession of the land requires deleting the words that follow it. Sub-class two is persons who "have paid … or have made partial payments therefor to the latter": "therefor" means for the sale, so rent is not a payment under the clause, and "the latter" can only be Llamanzares, so payments to Cesar's side do not count. The tense fixes the class as of 16 March 1992. Paragraph 6's "interested third parties" is the purpose; paragraph 7 begins "Accordingly" and is the operative grant. A "downpayment" presupposes a sale with an object and a price: "there is no contract unless … consent … object certain … [and] cause" concur (Article 1318); a sale requires "a determinate thing" and "a price certain" (Article 1458), a thing "particularly designated or physically segregated from all others of the same class" (Article 1460), and is perfected only on "a meeting of minds upon the thing … and upon the price" (Article 1475); earnest money is "part of the price and … proof of the perfection of the contract" (Article 1482). Sub-class two therefore consists of the same people as sub-class one at an earlier documentary stage: persons who had agreed with Llamanzares on an identified portion and a price and had begun paying for it. On the defendants' own evidence no object or price existed in Llamanzares's time: the portion was first segregated by the survey they paid for in December 2006 (F7); "Lot Area: 2,587" first appears on a receipt of 7 August 2016 (Annex D, p. 29); the price of ₱250,000 was fixed by Cesar (Gloria S33, conditional). A ₱3,000 payment in the 1980s with no lot, no price and no receipt is money handed to the administrator, which on their own Answer is what rent was; it is not a down payment "therefor." Membership is the defense's affirmative allegation (Answer ¶40, ¶44[a] to [c]) and the buyer's burden (_Bucton_): they must produce a Llamanzares contract or a payment to Llamanzares, and have produced neither. What they have are three accounts of a down payment that do not match: ₱3,000 in the 1980s without a receipt, recorded in Manuel Violeta's "brown columnar book" said to have gone "to Cesar and then the Agcaoili Law Office" (Gloria S20 to S24, conditional); "already paid" by about 1992 (Salvador S14 to S15); and the only receipt marked "Down payment," ₱20,000 on 6 January 2005 to Salvador (Annex D, p. 3). Nothing shows the pleaded ₱3,000 "credited" in the 2016 price. Two things to run down: whether the Agcaoili office ever received any Violeta ledger, and the Llamanzares-era instalment sales recorded on the T-4497 title itself (the 1977 "Deeds of Final Sale" by "Mary Worrick-Keesey, thru her Atty.-in-fact Benjamin Llamanzares"), which show what a member of the class looked like. The principal's own sworn reading, 22 February 2023 (doc 187, ¶4): "limited authority to perfect the sale of portions of said property in favor of those who already possessed Contract to Sell, or have had made down/partial payment to Benjamin Llamanzares."

**A crack in our own Reply.** Our Reply of April 2026 (¶14) answered the Art. 1921 defense by saying the agency "was not created for the purpose of dealing with the defendants specifically or with any other specified persons." The second clause overshoots: the SPA does name specified persons, the Llamanzares class, and this dossier's theory depends on it. The memorandum must restate the point: Art. 1921 protects only the persons the agency was entrusted to deal with; the SPA names that class; the defendants are outside it; on either reading Art. 1921 gives them nothing. Say it before the defense reads ¶14 aloud.

**Their own witness reads the clause the same way.** Salvador's 2025 letter describes the owners' instruction as reaching only "the people in Mercedes who possesses CONTRACT OF SALE issued by Benjamin Llamanzares," and treats everyone else as a problem to be "appease[d]" with documents (F41). That is the class limit, stated by the man who collected the Balanes' money.

**The property description: use it for specificity, not as a stand-alone "the SPA never covered this lot."** The SPA describes only the undivided 139,132-square-meter T-4497 parcel (F3). Under _Bautista-Spille_ a court "will not infer or presume broad powers from deeds which do not sufficiently include property or subject under which the agent is to deal," and a 2,587-square-meter cut from Lot 2-X-6-I-4-C is nowhere in the instrument. That supports strict reading of the class and the consultation clause. It should not be run as a separate nullity ground, because T-52540 descends from T-4497 on the face of Exhibit B, the Registry carried the SPA onto T-52540 in 1996 (F4), and the 2005 sale to Mariquita Era of another portion of the same lot was registered under it. The defense would answer all three in one sentence. The better use is in 4.1: the SPA they rely on and the revocation notice they say they could not connect to their land describe the property in exactly the same words.

**Why this leads, and not "no power to sell."** Paragraph 7 also lets Cesar "sign and execute the necessary documents" and "receive the proceeds." A court reading the whole clause will find a power to complete a sale for the class. Our affidavit's "limited to negotiation" wording (Q26, Q53) should be conceded on cross and the class limit held. The class limit needs no construction against the grant; it is the grant.

##### 3.2 The buyer dealt with an agent and had to inquire at her peril

**Rule.** "Every person dealing with an agent is put upon inquiry, and must discover upon his peril the authority of the agent ... If a person makes no inquiry, he is chargeable with knowledge of the agent's authority, and his ignorance of that authority will not be any excuse" (_San Pedro v. Ong_, G.R. No. 177598, 17 October 2008). "In case the buyer does not deal with the registered owner of the real property, the law requires that a higher degree of prudence be exercised by the purchaser" (_Bucton_). "Despite the presence of an alleged SPA from the registered owners, respondent should have inquired with the registered owners as to the extent of the authority of the attorney-in-fact, especially when the property is co-owned" (_Spouses Yabut v. Nachbaur_, G.R. No. 243470, 12 January 2021). The presumption of good faith applies only when three conditions concur: the seller is the registered owner, the seller is in possession, and the buyer is unaware of any defect in the seller's capacity to convey; "absent one or two of the foregoing conditions, then the law itself puts the buyer on notice and obliges the latter to exercise a higher degree of diligence" (_Duenas_, restated in _Chua v. Republic_, G.R. No. 253305, 2 August 2023).

**Application.** The seller was not the registered owner. The seller was not in possession; the buyer was, as a tenant (F1, F16). Two days before the deed the seller swore the owner's duplicate was lost (F11), the warning sign the Court named in _Vallido v. Pono_, G.R. No. 200173, 15 April 2013 ("during the sale ... [the seller] did not have the owner's duplicate copy of the title"). The buyer knew who the owners were, paid them rent for decades (F8), held a tax declaration giving their California address (F24), and knew their Manila counsel was the family's contact (F10). She never asked them. In _Bucton_ the Court found it "inconceivable" that buyers who personally knew the owners made no inquiry. That is this case.

##### 3.3 Good faith had to last until 23 November 2021, and it did not

**Rule.** "In the event a buyer of registered land who has yet to register the conveyance is made aware of any claim or interest of some other person in the property, or of any defect or restriction in the title of the seller or in his or her capacity to convey title, the buyer shall no longer be considered to be in good faith even if he or she subsequently registers the conveyance" (_Duenas_, En Banc). PD 1529, Section 51: registration "shall be the operative act to convey or affect the land insofar as third persons are concerned."

**Application.** Between the deed and the registration the buyer's side learned, in order: that the owners' counsel had declared the agent's acts void (F21, through the lawyer who handled the papers); that a lawyer was needed to hold the ground (F22); that suits had to be filed and defended over the land (F23); that the agent was dead and the papers had to be moved by others, with penalties (F20, F25); that the Provincial Prosecutor would not call her the owner (F26); that the revocation was published in the local weekly (F27); that a court had issued warrants over the right to the land (F26); that a pleading served on her sister's own lawyer stated the revocation and attached the notice (F30); and that the SPA's own text, pulled from the Registry by her processor, confined the sale to a class she was not in (F31). She then swore the land was "my property" (F32) and registered (F33). The Answer's denials of notice are all limited to "before the September 2016 sale" (paragraphs 15, 19, 41); the Answer never denies notice between 2016 and 2021. [Annex A, pp. 1, 3, 4]

##### 3.4 Revocation: the order of argument

**Rule.** Article 1921: "If the agency has been entrusted for the purpose of contracting with specified persons, its revocation shall not prejudice the latter if they were not given notice thereof." Article 1922: "If the agent had general powers, revocation of the agency does not prejudice third persons who acted in good faith and without knowledge of the revocation. Notice of the revocation in a newspaper of general circulation is a sufficient warning to third persons." The Court applied Article 1922 to an agent authorized to deal "not with a particular person, but with the public in general" (_Central Surety & Insurance Co. v. Hodges_, G.R. No. L-28633, 30 March 1971). PD 1529, Section 64, requires a revoking instrument to be registered, but the Court treats registration as a notice device, not a condition of validity (_Republic v. Ciruelas_, G.R. No. 239505, 17 February 2021).

**Application.** Lead with the class (3.1): if the buyer was never one of the "specified persons," the revocation is irrelevant to her, because the sale was never within the agency. Argue the 2020 publication (F27) only as a fact bearing on good faith before registration (3.3), never as an Article 1922 "sufficient warning," because Cesar's powers were special and class-limited, not general, and we cannot say both. Do not plead the non-annotation of the revocation as a defect in our own case; answer it with Section 52 and the 1996 annotation of the SPA itself (F4).

##### 3.5 The tenant's possession was never possession as owner

**Rule.** Article 525: possession is "either in the concept of owner, or in that of the holder of the thing ... the ownership pertaining to another person." Article 540: "Only the possession acquired and enjoyed in the concept of owner can serve as a title for acquiring dominion." Article 1436: "A lessee ... is estopped from asserting title to the thing leased ... as against the lessor." Possession "with a juridical title, such as by a ... lessee ... cannot ripen into ownership" (_Esguerra v. Manantan_, G.R. No. 158328, 23 February 2007); "a person whose occupation of realty is by sheer tolerance of the owner is not a possessor in good faith" (_National Housing Authority v. Manila Seedling Bank Foundation_, G.R. No. 183543, 20 June 2016).

**Application.** "Occupancy since 1975" (Answer paragraph 44[a]) is tenancy by their own admission (F1, F2, F8). It adds nothing to the deed, it defeats the Article 448 "builder in good faith" claim (paragraph 49) for everything built while renting, and it is their own proof of actual knowledge of the owners. Use it for those three things. Do not use estoppel as a stand-alone bar to the deed; the buyer does not deny the owners' title, she claims to derive from it.

##### 3.6 Void means imprescriptible, not ratifiable, and not cured by registration

**Rule.** Article 1409: void contracts "cannot be ratified." Article 1410: "The action or defense for the declaration of the inexistence of a contract does not prescribe." "A void contract produces no effect either against or in favor of anyone and cannot be ratified. Similarly, laches will not set in against a void transaction, as in this case, where the agent did not have a special power of attorney to dispose of the lots" (_Mactan-Cebu International Airport Authority v. Unchuan_, G.R. No. 182537, 1 June 2016). "One can sell only what he owns or is authorized to sell, and the buyer can acquire no more right than what the seller can transfer legally ... Registration does not vest title; it is merely the evidence of such title" (_Heirs of Gonzales v. Spouses Basas_, G.R. No. 206847, 15 June 2022).

**Application.** This answers the sixth defense (Article 1403[1], ratification), the seventh (estoppel and laches) and the premise of the eighth (the title). The estate receipts (F8) are payments to an agent whose authority was revoked; no owner received or saw a peso, which the plaintiff's representative will state under oath. The agent's own Deed of Confirmation (F19) is not the principals' ratification.

##### 3.7 The consequences the court can order

**Rule.** Article 449: "He who builds, plants or sows in bad faith on the land of another, loses what is built, planted or sown without right to indemnity." Article 450: the owner may demand demolition at the builder's expense. Article 451: the owner "is entitled to damages from the builder." Article 549: "The possessor in bad faith shall reimburse the fruits received and those which the legitimate possessor could have received." Article 2232: exemplary damages where the defendant "acted in a wanton, fraudulent, reckless, oppressive, or malevolent manner." Article 2208, paragraphs 1, 2 and 5: attorney's fees where exemplary damages are awarded, where the defendant's act "compelled the plaintiff to litigate," or where the defendant "acted in gross and evident bad faith in refusing to satisfy the plaintiff's plainly valid, just and demandable claim." In _Duenas_ the En Banc ordered that the bad-faith builder "forfeits what it has built ... without any right to be paid indemnity," awarded exemplary damages because the bad faith "resulted in the deprivation of petitioners' right to lawfully use and possess their property for a considerable number of years," and sustained attorney's fees because the owners "were compelled to litigate." In _Heirs of Sarili v. Lagrosa_, G.R. No. 193517, 15 January 2014, the Court remanded "for the proper application of Article 449 in relation to Articles 450, 451, 452 and the first paragraph of Article 546" to the house the bad-faith buyers had built.

**Application.** The improvements built after 29 September 2016 are forfeit. Fruits are owed at least from receipt of the demand (F34) and, on a finding of bad faith from the outset, from the deed. Exemplary damages rest on F11 to F19 and F28 to F33 taken together: a false receipt recital, a lost-title petition that hid the sale, a dead agent's name used for a 2020 survey certified as made with the owners' consent, a partition dated before its plan existed, and a sworn "my property" over land titled to others. Attorney's fees rest on the ignored demand (F34). Counsel to confirm the damages ceiling that applies in this court.

##### 3.8 The replacement duplicate: evidence of bad faith, not a nullity claim here

**Rule.** PD 1529, Section 109, allows a new duplicate "after notice and due hearing." "A trial court does not acquire jurisdiction over a petition for the issuance of a new owner's duplicate certificate of title, if the original is in fact not lost" (_Strait Times, Inc. v. Court of Appeals_, G.R. No. 126673, 28 August 1998). But "the nullity of the reconstituted certificate does not by itself settle the issue of ownership" (same), and the Supreme Court cases reached that result through annulment petitions in the Court of Appeals, not collaterally in a first-level court.

**Application.** We cannot yet prove the duplicate was not lost. Use Special Proceeding No. 2156 for what Cesar said and hid (F13 to F17), not to void the order. The tenth defense (collateral attack) is thereby answered: we do not attack the order; we read it.

##### 3.9 The families living on the lot: who the judgment reaches, and how possession is recovered


**Application.** The route depends on one fact: who let the ten families in (F39). If the Balanes admitted them, they are "occupant[s] of the premises with the permission of the defendant" and the writ in this case reaches them; it also shows the defendants disposing of the owners' land as their own, which goes to the relief in Part 6. If they entered on their own, they are not bound by this judgment; the owners recover from them by a separate action (forcible entry within one year of entry, otherwise accion publiciana), with Section 28 governing the eviction. Either way, the families never paid anyone, so there are no fruits to account for from them and no tenancy for the defense to exploit; the structures are theirs, not the defendants' "improvements" under paragraph 49 of the Answer. The defense's laches theme will reach for them. The answer is that the owners' silence toward strangers is not acquiescence in a sale by their agent, and that the action to declare a void deed does not prescribe (Article 1410).

**The families who hold Salvador's paper.** Salvador never held the owners' authority (F40). The argument is not that substitution was forbidden: Article 1892 lets an agent appoint a substitute unless the principal prohibits it, and the Supreme Court has upheld a sale made by a substitute under an SPA that was silent on substitution because the agent "is not prohibited from appointing a substitute" (_Escueta v. Lim_, G.R. No. 137162, 24 January 2007). The defense would answer that point with that case. The argument is that there was nothing to substitute. Cesar's authority ended in 2005, the 2012 instrument passes administration only, and a substitute cannot exceed the agent's own power. Any allocation, sale or "deal" Salvador made over portions of the lot was without written authority to sell and is void (Article 1874); the families who paid him hold nothing against the owners and are not "transferees" of the Balanes. For the Balanes the same fact cuts deeper: from 2012 they paid a man who was neither the owner nor the owner's agent, and never asked the owners (3.2). For this case their value is as proof: their receipts, if they are on the same "Heirs of Mary W. Keesey Estate" forms as Exhibit 5, show Salvador treating the same lot as available to several payors while the Balanes claim exclusive possession of a "specific" portion since 1975, and they impeach him as a witness with his own interest in the outcome. The families who were there before 2016 contradict the Balanes' exclusive possession and the 2020 survey certified as made "with the full knowledge and consent of the owner(s)" (F28). Any of them who hold a Llamanzares contract of sale or receipt would be the very class the 1992 SPA named (F3); the Balanes are not.

**On the stand.** Jonathan says only what he knows: that the families are there, that they never paid the owners, and whether the family ever admitted them. He does not estimate dates or numbers he has not counted, and he does not say who let them in unless he knows it. He does not mention Salvador's letter unless counsel has decided to use it, because it opens the "Moreno" name.

##### 3.10 No power to partition: the 2017 Partition-Subdivision Agreement that created the Balane title

**Rule.** Article 1877: "An agency couched in general terms comprises only acts of administration," even if the principal states that he withholds no power or that the agent may do whatever he considers appropriate. Article 1878 requires a special power of attorney "(5) To enter into any contract by which the ownership of an immovable is transmitted or acquired," "(12) To create or convey real rights over immovable property," and "(15) Any other act of strict dominion." Article 496: "Partition may be made by agreement between the parties or by judicial proceedings." The parties to a partition of co-owned land are the co-owners.

**Application.** Neither the 1991 SPA (administration only, F14) nor the 1992 SPA (sale to a class, after consultation, F3) grants any power to partition or subdivide; paragraph 8 of the 1992 SPA, "to do and perform any and every act ... requisite, necessary or proper," is a general clause that Article 1877 confines to administration. The 1992 SPA was revoked in 2005 in any event. The Partition-Subdivision Agreement dated 15 March 2017 (Doc. No. 402, Page 81, Book CXXII, Asis) split T-52540 into Lot 2-X-6-I-4-C-1 for Gloria and Lot 2-X-6-I-4-C-2 for the owners (F18, F33). Cesar was alive on that date and is the only person who could have purported to sign for the owners; the instrument itself is not yet in hand (F29). If he signed it, he partitioned co-owned registered land without any power to do so, which is an act of strict dominion under Article 1878 and void as to the owners. If someone else signed for the owners after his death, the instrument is worse than unauthorized. On its stated date it also precedes by three and a half years the survey it says it was made under (F28). The Balane title is the direct product of that partition. The prayer therefore asks the court to cancel TCT No. 079-2021002126 and restore Lot 2-X-6-I-4-C-1 to the three owners' names (Part 6, paragraph 2), which undoes the only effect of the partition that ever left the family.

**Their best answer on the survey, and ours.** The defense will say the field survey was done in 2006 (Princess's Villafuerte receipt of 29 December 2006, F7) while Cesar was alive and "under his management" (Gloria S36), and that the 2020 plan merely formalized it for LRA approval on the strength of that earlier consent. Four answers. First, consent from Cesar in 2006 was consent from nobody: his authority had been revoked on 15 August 2005, and even the 1992 SPA never contained a power to subdivide or partition. Second, the 2020 plan certifies a survey "SURVEYED: September 8, 2020," not 2006; the DENR file will show whether it was a re-survey, who requested it, and whether any 2006 plan was ever submitted. Third, the partition's annotation says the land was subdivided "in accordance with subdivision plan number (LRA) PSD-E2020005406," a number that did not exist until the EPEB entry of 17 September 2020; if the instrument itself names that plan, it could not have been written on 15 March 2017, whatever happened in the field in 2006. That is why the instrument must be read, not inferred. Fourth, on 8 September 2020 the registered owners were the three sisters; the engineer certified their "full knowledge and consent" when none of them knew, the only agent was dead, and the Balanes were not owners. On the defense's best version the certification is still false as to the owners. Until the DENR file and the instrument are in hand, the antedating remains an inference and is marked as one; the chronology, the revocation and the absence of any partition power are facts.

**An independent ground for cancelling the title.** The Balane title rests on two instruments, the deed and the partition. Even if the court were to uphold the deed, a buyer of a portion of co-owned registered land holds at most an interest in the co-ownership; a separate title to a specific, bounded lot can issue only on a partition "by agreement between the parties" (Article 496), that is, by the co-owners, or by judicial partition. No owner made or consented to any partition. TCT No. 079-2021002126 therefore cannot stand on any view of the deed, and its cancellation does not depend on the court first finding the sale void. This gives the court a second, narrower path to the same order, and it should be pleaded in the memorandum in the alternative.

**What not to argue.** Do not say Cesar had no authority for the subdivisions of 1993 to 2000 that produced T-52540 itself. Every title in that chain issued to the owners, the owners hold and sue on T-52540, and Article 1910 treats that as ratification; attacking those subdivisions would attack our own root of title and reopen every other lot in the 2-X-6 tree. The 1993 Subdivision Agreement on T-4497 has one use here: it names the transferees Cesar's mandate actually ran to, and the Balanes are not among them. [Annex AA, p. 1] Aim "no power to partition" at the 2017 instrument only, the one act of reallotment the owners never knew of and never adopted.

##### 3.11 "Builders in good faith": the attack on the twelfth defense and the reimbursement counterclaim

The twelfth defense (Answer paragraph 49) and the counterclaim ask for reimbursement of the price, the real property taxes and arrears, the registration and transfer expenses, the survey expenses and "the value of necessary and useful improvements, with the corresponding right of retention until full reimbursement," under Articles 448, 546 and 548. Gloria's excluded affidavit values the improvements at "at least" One Hundred Thousand Pesos (P100,000.00) (S68 to S71) [CONDITIONAL]. This defense is the defendants' exit if they lose on title: it would let them stay until paid. It must be taken apart period by period, because the law is different in each.

**Rule.** Article 526: a possessor in good faith is one "who is not aware that there exists in his title or mode of acquisition any flaw which invalidates it"; everyone else is a possessor in bad faith. Article 528: good faith is lost "from the moment facts exist which show that the possessor is not unaware that he possesses the thing improperly or wrongfully." Article 546: necessary expenses "shall be refunded to every possessor; but only the possessor in good faith may retain the thing until he has been reimbursed," and useful expenses "shall be refunded only to the possessor in good faith with the same right of retention." Article 449: the builder in bad faith "loses what is built ... without right to indemnity." Article 549: the possessor in bad faith "shall reimburse the fruits received and those which the legitimate possessor could have received." Article 1678: a lessee who makes useful improvements in good faith is entitled, at the end of the lease, to one-half of their value at the lessor's option, failing which the lessee may remove them; there is no right of retention. The Supreme Court holds that Article 448, read with Article 546, "applies only to a possessor in good faith, i.e., one who builds on land with the belief that he is the owner thereof," and "does not apply where one's only interest is that of a lessee under a rental contract" (_Parilla v. Pilar_, G.R. No. 167680, 30 November 2006).

**The three periods.**

| Period | What the Balanes were | What the law gives them |
|---|---|---|
| 1975 to 29 September 2016 | Tenants of the owners' administrator, by their own Answer (F1, F2, F8). Not owners, not claimants of title. | Article 1678 only (_Parilla_). Whatever they built while renting is a lessee's improvement: the owners choose between paying one-half of its value at the end of the lease and letting them remove it. No retention. No Article 448 indemnity. The burden of proving which structures date from this period, and their value, is theirs. |
| 29 September 2016 to 23 November 2021 | Buyers from an agent outside his written authority, who never asked the owners (3.1, 3.2) and who learned of the revocation and the dispute before registering (3.3). | A buyer who fails the inquiry the law demands is not in good faith from the day of the deed (_Bucton_; _Duenas_). A builder who is not in good faith "loses what is built ... without right to indemnity" (Article 449), and the owners may demand demolition at the builder's expense (Article 450) and damages (Article 451). Only necessary expenses are refundable (Article 546, first paragraph), without retention. |
| After 22 August 2025 (receipt of the demand) and after service of summons | Possessors who have been told, in writing, that the owners reject the deed (F34). | Bad faith beyond argument from that date (Article 528). All fruits from that date, including the room rentals the Answer admits (paragraph 27), are owed to the owners (Article 549). Anything built or rebuilt after that date is forfeit. |

**Their best answer, and the reply.** The defense will cite the cases that applied Article 448 to people who built with the landowner's consent: _Macasaet v. Macasaet_, G.R. Nos. 154391-92, 30 September 2004 (parents who invited their children to build on their lots) and _Department of Education v. Casibang_, G.R. No. 192268, 27 January 2016 (an owner who "permitted the construction of building and improvements to conduct classes on his property"). In both, the owner himself knew of and approved the building. Here no owner ever consented to any structure: the only relationship was a lease through the administrator, with rent receipts to prove it (F8), and a lease is exactly the case _Parilla_ takes out of Article 448 and puts under Article 1678. Nor do the defendants plead tolerance; they plead ownership by purchase (paragraph 49 rests on the deed). Judged as the buyers they say they are, they are buyers in bad faith, and a buyer in bad faith is a builder in bad faith for everything built after the deed. The 2006 survey receipt (F7) closes the last door: in the year they paid to carve out their lot, they wrote that it was "owned by the Heirs of Worrick Keesey."

**The reimbursement list, item by item.** The price: no owner received a peso (F8, 3.6); a claim for its return lies against the estate of the agent who took it, which the defendants themselves say is the party whose "acts, receipts, collections" are "the very foundation" of the case (paragraph 38). Capital gains tax, documentary stamp tax, transfer tax, surcharges, interest and registration fees (F25, F33): expenses of registering a void sale, not expenses "necessary" for the preservation of the land, and not refundable by the owners. The survey (F7, F28): an expense of the same void transaction, incurred to cut the owners' land without their consent. Real property taxes actually paid on the land (paragraph 44[i]): the one item that is a necessary expense; refundable under Article 546, first paragraph, to the extent proved by official receipts, with no right of retention. Improvements: Article 1678 before the deed, Article 449 after it. The "right of retention until full reimbursement" exists only for a possessor in good faith (Article 546), which they are not.

**The evidence to put in, and where it comes from.**

- **No building permits.** Presidential Decree No. 1096, the National Building Code, Section 301, requires a building permit from the Building Official before any person may erect, construct, alter, repair or convert a structure, and the application must show the applicant's right over the land. Engr. Erwin H. Balane, a defendant, is the Municipal Engineer and Building Official, and his office refused the owners' request for the permit records (F35). A subpoena duces tecum to the Office of the Building Official for every permit, application, plan and certificate of occupancy for structures on the lot in the names of Efren, Gloria, Erwin, Princess, Jomil or Ferdinand Balane, or a certification of no record, settles the point either way: an application shows what right over the land they claimed and when; no permit means the structures went up outside the law, which no builder in good faith does.

- **Tax declarations on the improvements.** The Municipal Assessor's declarations of buildings on the lot: in whose name, declared when, at what value. These fix the dates and the valuation the defendants themselves gave the government, against the "at least P100,000" they now claim. A subpoena to the Municipal Assessor is needed; the Provincial Assessor does not hold them.

- **Dates of construction from sources the defendants do not control.** Fire safety clearances (Bureau of Fire Protection), electric-service connection records (CANORECO), barangay construction clearances, and dated satellite imagery of the lot (Google Earth historical imagery and NAMRIA orthophotos), which show year by year when each roof appeared. The imagery goes in through Jonathan with the capture dates, as demonstrative evidence; counsel to confirm the route.

- **Their own conduct in 2020.** Shirley D. De Leon (Exh. N) swears that in 2020 Gloria came to her house on the land, said she had bought it, tried to evict her, and that the defendants later boarded up her door, and that other neighbours were harassed because Gloria claimed to have bought "other portions" too. A possessor who evicts neighbours by self-help on a claim of purchase, two years before registering and while the revocation was in the newspaper, is not building in the belief of an untroubled title.

- **Their own exhibits.** The 2006 survey receipt (F7); the rent receipts to 2014 (F8); the post-deed payments (F12); the Answer's admission of room rentals (paragraph 27) for the fruits.

- **What is not theirs.** The houses of the families on the lot (F39) are not the defendants' improvements and cannot be claimed or valued by them.

**On cross.** Erwin Balane: does your office hold a building permit or a certificate of occupancy for any structure your family built on this lot; who signed the application as owner; will you produce them. Princess Balane Torralba: which structures were built after September 2016, who paid, and under what permit. Gloria, if she appears: the P100,000 figure, the dates, and whether any owner was ever asked before anything was built.

**Relief.** Part 6, paragraph 5, already declares the post-deed improvements forfeited under Article 449 and reserves necessary expenses under Article 546, first paragraph. For structures proved to predate the deed, the judgment should reserve to the owners the Article 1678 option, to pay one-half of the proved value or to require removal, exercisable after the defendants prove the date and value of each structure. No retention in either case.


**Document:** Bad-faith dossier: itemized summary (Parts 1 to 7; annexes in the PDF)  
**Source:** `BALANE_BAD_FAITH_SUMMARY_2026-10.html`  
**Section:** PART 4. THE DEFENDANTS' CASE AT ITS STRONGEST, AND THE ANSWER

---

#### PART 4. THE DEFENDANTS' CASE AT ITS STRONGEST, AND THE ANSWER

Their side is stated as they pleaded and swore it. Nothing here is softened. The answer to each is the rule and the F-numbers that meet it.

| Their point (source) | Their best evidence | The answer |
|---|---|---|
| The 1992 SPA is a written authority to sell: it refers to the sale, its terms, the documents and the proceeds (Answer paragraph 39). | Paragraph 7 of the SPA; the 1996 annotation "to sign, negotiate for the sale" (F4). | Agreed that Cesar could complete a sale for the class. The same paragraph names the class and requires consultation (F3). Strict construction (_Bautista-Spille_) forbids reading the class out. Gloria was outside it (F5, F8); no consultation (F10). Concede the "negotiation only" wording in our affidavit and hold the class. |
| Gloria was in the class: a tenant from 1975 who paid rent to Llamanzares and a 1980s down payment, "recognized and later credited" (paragraph 40). | The Answer's own narrative; Salvador S14 to S16; Gloria S20 to S22, S35 [CONDITIONAL]. | The class is holders of a Llamanzares contract to sell or payors to Llamanzares. No contract, no receipt, no entry in any book is produced for the 1980s payment. The first receipt is 2003, to Cesar's side (F5). Rent is not a down payment; the "Occupant" receipts run to 2014 (F8). Burden is theirs (_Bucton_). |
| No notice of the revocation before the September 2016 sale; publication binds only from 2020 forward (paragraphs 15, 19, 41). | Nothing annotated on T-52540 (F4, F6); Salvador S32; Gloria S57, S63, S64 [CONDITIONAL]; Gloria lived in Canada. | The decisive window is 2016 to 23 November 2021 (_Duenas_). Their denials stop at 2016. The record of 2019 to 2021 is F21 to F32. The non-annotation of the revocation is beside the point when the SPA itself, annotated in 1996, excludes her (F3, F4). |
| At most the contract is unenforceable and was ratified by acceptance of payments and long inaction (paragraph 42, Article 1403[1]). | The estate receipts (F8); Cesar's confirmation (F19); the owners' silence. | A sale outside a special power is void, not unenforceable (_Cosmic Lumber_, _Unchuan_); void contracts cannot be ratified (Article 1409). The receipts went to an agent revoked in 2005 and were never remitted; the confirmation is the agent's, not the principals'. |
| Estoppel and laches: the family was never questioned "from 1975 ... to the time that it was offered for them to purchase" (paragraph 43). | The Answer itself; our own Complaint's dates. | Silence toward a paying tenant is not acquiescence in a sale (F1). Laches does not run against a void contract (_Unchuan_); the action is imprescriptible (Article 1410). Registration was in 2021 and suit in 2026. |
| Good faith and for value, ten circumstances (paragraph 44): she checked the SPA, the title and the Registry and found nothing. | Gloria S56, S57 [CONDITIONAL]; the sister's registered purchase (defense Exh. 3, 4); the receipts. | Checking the Registry would have shown, from 10 October 2016, the agent's loss affidavit and the court-ordered duplicate (F11, F17). The sister's deed (defense Exh. 4) is a 1999 sale under the 1991 SPA and says nothing about 2016. The three _Duenas_ conditions all fail (3.2). She never asked the owners (F10; Gloria S43 [CONDITIONAL]). |
| The grievance is against Cesar's estate; the owners clothed him with apparent authority for years (paragraphs 38, 45). | The 2005 sale to Mariquita Era under the same SPA, registered 2005 (Exh. B, p. 3); the 1999 Hansol sale. | Both predate the revocation and, for Hansol, rest on the 1991 SPA. Article 1902 protects reliance on the power "as written," and as written it excludes her. The estate of Cesar is not a necessary party to declare a void deed as between owner and buyer; counsel to confirm. |
| Collateral attack on the reissuance and partition; they do not hold the duplicate of T-2021002127 (paragraph 46). | Special Proceeding No. 2156 is final (F17); the Registry file is facially complete (Annex K). | We do not attack the order; we use the petition and testimony for what they admit and hide (3.8; F13 to F16). The partition is attacked as an act no agent could do and, on its dates, could not have done (F18, F29). |
| Builders in good faith: reimbursement and retention (paragraph 49, Articles 448, 546). | Improvements of at least One Hundred Thousand Pesos (Gloria S68 to S71 [CONDITIONAL]). | Everything built while renting is a lessee's improvement under Article 1678, not Article 448 (_Parilla v. Pilar_, G.R. No. 167680); everything built after 2016 was built by a buyer in bad faith and is forfeit (Article 449). Necessary expenses alone are refundable (Article 546, first paragraph), as _Duenas_ remanded, and with no right of retention. The taxes and fees of a void registration are not necessary expenses of the land. Full attack in 3.11. |
| The counterclaim: harassment, "unjustified, malicious," moral and exemplary damages (paragraphs 52 to 57); Princess on the demand letter sent to the Sangguniang Bayan (S21 to S24). | The ARTA complaints and referrals (F36 to F38) will be painted as a campaign against the family. | Every referral is an official act of a national agency on its own findings, not the plaintiff's characterization. Keep them out of the case-in-chief. Use them on cross of Princess and Erwin for interest and bias only if they claim neutrality or harmony, and on rebuttal if the counterclaim is pressed. See Part 5. |

##### 4.1 What hurts us, and the prepared answer

- **Our own Complaint says the SPA was annotated only in 2021 (paragraph 50) and dates the deed 24 September 2016 (paragraph 25).** Both are wrong on our own Exhibit B. File the errata before the defense uses them.

- **Our Reply ¶14 said the 1992 agency was not for "any other specified persons."** That contradicts the class theory. Restate it in the memorandum before the defense uses it (3.1).

- **Exhibit H is page 1 of a 2-page certified copy; Exhibit E is pages 2 and 3 of a 4-page copy.** The missing pages help us (the eCAR page; the Elevado SPA). File complete certified copies by supplemental offer. [Annex X]

- **Patricia's own 2023 affidavit of loss was entered on cancelled T-38838 and on T-2021002127,** the title created by the partition we attack. The answer: the entry protected the owners' interest in whatever title the Registry was carrying; it is not consent to the partition. [Annex G, p. 4]

- **The Bicol Post notice names only T-4497,** not T-52540 or Lot 2-X-6-I-4-C. The answer: the SPA the defendants rely on names only T-4497 as well (F3), so a buyer who read her own authority knew the land she bought was the T-4497 estate; the notice names the same title, names Cesar, and voids "any and all transactions" after 2005; and T-52540 descends from T-4497 on the face of Exhibit B. Jonathan can say this from the exhibits: "The SPA describes T-4497. The notice describes T-4497. My exhibit B shows T-52540 came from T-4497."

- **The Provincial Prosecutor's 2020 resolutions call Gloria "the landowner."** Use those resolutions only for the agency and the date of the dispute; the prosecutor expressly declined to decide ownership (F26).

- **The 6 January 2005 "Down payment" receipt predates the revocation.** The defense will say the sale was agreed while the SPA was in force. The answer is the class: a 2005 payment to Cesar is not a Llamanzares contract or a payment to Llamanzares (F3, F5).

- **The 11 November witness was the complainant in Criminal Case No. 20-6030 before this court and was later prosecuted for perjury and acquitted.** Prepare her on that history; do not raise 20-6030 ourselves beyond one question to Jomil Torralba.

- **The ten families on the lot (F39)** will be used for tolerance and laches ("the owners let anyone settle"). Prepared answer: the owners dealt with the land through their administrator and their tenants; strangers' entry is a separate wrong the owners will pursue; it does not validate a void deed. If the Balanes admitted them, turn it: the defendants peopled the owners' land with occupants who pay nothing.

- **Gloria lived in Toronto** and could not have read a Daet weekly. The answer is that she acted through a sister and lawyers who were in Daet and were served (F23, F30), and that she herself swore "my property" in Daet in 2021 (F32). Whether an agent's or counsel's knowledge is the principal's is the one legal point still to be supplied by counsel.

#### PART 5. THE CAMPAIGN

##### 5.0 The court we are writing for: Judge Lourdes Clarissa Donnatilla K. Cu

Every statement here rests on her own signed words, in the four orders she has issued in this case (6 February, 11 March, 12 March and 4 September 2026) and the two she issued in Criminal Case No. 20-6030 over this parcel (27 November 2020 and 6 January 2021). What we do not hold is as important as what we do: the 13 May 2026 pre-trial order, any ruling on Gloria's affidavit or on our summary judgment motion, and the 28 August order. Those must be obtained before anything in this part is treated as settled. [Annex AB; Annex O, pp. 9, 10]

**How she rules.** She cites no Supreme Court decision in any of the seven writings. She cites the statute or rule by section and date ("Section 72 of Republic Act 10951 which was promulgated on August 29, 2017"; "Paragraph 2, Section 1, Rule 112 of the 2019 Amended Rules of Court") and writes rule, then "Considering that," then WHEREFORE. Her one reasoned order is about three hundred thirty words in eight paragraphs of one sentence each. Her WHEREFORE paragraphs carry exact particulars: receipt numbers, amounts in words and figures, the bank branch, the payee. She writes only in English.

**How she runs the case.** She announced the governing rules in her first order without being asked: "this case is governed by the Amended Rules of Civil Procedure, the Revised Rules on Evidence, and the Rules on Electronic Filing." She has never written "Summary Procedure." She gives one indulgence and says so: to defense counsel, "Liberally applying the rules of procedure ... No further liberality shall be accorded," with a warning that further incorrect pleadings "shall warrant appropriate sanction." She makes the calendar hard: the trial dates are "final and intransferable unless the ground for resetting is allowed under the rules," and she records "both counsels' timely compliance." She checks who may act: she noted a lawyer's presence and ruled that he "has no authority to prosecute this case," and the pre-trial notice in her name requires a representative to be "fully authorized in writing." She will undo her own act on a rule: she issued warrants on 27 November 2020 and recalled them forty days later as "prematurely filed," without one word about the complainant's motives. She does not characterize parties.

**What it changes in our papers.**

- The memorandum is built on the articles and sections, each pinpointed, in her order: rule, exhibit-tagged facts, "considering that," relief. Case law is kept to the few decisions that decide a point, each with its G.R. number, date and one holding sentence. That is how Parts 3 and 6 of this dossier are written, and it is why the prayer in Part 6 is drafted as a WHEREFORE she can adopt line by line, with the deed's notarial numbers, the title number, the ARP number, the lot, the area and the days.

- Every filing is perfect in form: her caption copied exactly, including "079-2021002126" and "ARP NO. GR-2023-II-07-001-00256"; e-filing, transmittal and proof of service in order; the SPA by which Jonathan acts identified by exhibit on the face of every appearance and the offer of evidence.

- Every date is treated as final. Any resetting request names the rule ground in its first sentence. Compliance is filed on time with proof, because she records it.

- We ask for leave once, in one paragraph, with the reason, the absence of prejudice and readiness to pay the fine. This applies to the supplemental affidavit, any new witness, and the complete certified copies of Exhibits E and H. We do not argue "substantial justice" at length.

- We correct our own errors before she finds them: Complaint paragraphs 25 and 50 and the affidavit's purpose clause, by manifestation, before 14 October.

- We do not call this a "Summary Procedure" case in any filing unless counsel confirms it from the pre-trial order. The defense has used that label; she has not.

- Nothing in Filipino goes into a filing except quoted exhibit text, with a translation.

**What it changes in the hearing room.** Jonathan's answers are short, in English, and tied to an exhibit; when a question asks for a characterization, the answer is "which exhibit says this" turned into testimony: "Exhibit B, page 3, shows the annotation of 21 June 1996." Counsel's objections are stated by rule and section, not by speech. Re-direct stays inside the scope of cross. Nothing is said about the judge's 2020 orders on this parcel; if the defense raises them to attack the 11 November witness, the answer is the acquittal and the Court of Appeals' dismissal, in one sentence, and no more.

**What it means for the forward target.** A judge who writes operative orders with exact particulars and who voids her own acts on a rule is a judge who will cancel a title if the rule and the exhibit are put in front of her plainly: Article 1874 and Article 1898 beside Exhibit E paragraph 7 and Exhibit B page 3; Article 496 beside Entry 2021003236; Article 449 beside the deed's date and the demand's receipt. The plan in 5.1 to 5.3 and the judgment in Part 6 are written to give her exactly that and nothing else.

**Risks to hold.** The exclusion of Gloria's affidavit at pre-trial is known only from the parties' accounts and the ground they give differs (the defense says a scanned copy; we said a late filing). Whichever way she rules on the motion to admit, Parts 4 and 5 are written to work with the affidavit in or out. The record intersection in Criminal Case No. 20-6030 (a current defendant was an accused; our 11 November witness was the complainant) is a fact about the parties, not about the judge, and we do not raise it.

##### 5.3 Rebuttal: the sequence that ends the case

**The objective** is complete victory in one judgment: nullity of the deed, cancellation of the title and the tax declaration, possession, forfeiture of the post-2016 improvements, an accounting of fruits, exemplary damages, attorney's fees, and dismissal of the counterclaim. On this court's method (5.0) that judgment is written only if, when she sits down to write, every operative paragraph has an article and an exhibit page beside it and nothing is left to characterization. Rebuttal is where the last pairs are completed and the defense's own witnesses are turned into our exhibits.

**What the rules allow, and the trap in them.** After the defendants rest, the plaintiff may "adduce rebutting evidence only," unless the court for good reasons permits evidence on the original case (Rule 30, Section 5[f]). This court gives one indulgence and ends it; she will not let rebuttal become a second case in chief. So every rebuttal item below is tied to a specific statement the defense will have made under oath, and everything that is not rebuttal goes in during our own five dates, by subpoena returns before 9 December. Rebuttal witnesses need judicial affidavits in time, or a subpoena where the witness is a government custodian or declines to execute one (Judicial Affidavit Rule, Section 5). On 9 December, when we rest, counsel reserves rebuttal on the record and asks for rebuttal dates to be fixed after 10 March 2027.

**The defense case we expect by 10 March 2027, and the rebuttal that answers each.**

**The five documents that lock exemplary damages.** This court does not characterize parties, so Article 2232 ("wanton, fraudulent, reckless, oppressive, or malevolent") has to be proved by dates on paper, and the memorandum should present them as five dates and nothing else. First, the deed of 29 September 2016 recites the price "in hand paid," and the defendants' own receipts show payments to February 2017 (F11, F12). Second, the petition filed 20 October 2016 for a replacement duplicate, by an agent revoked in 2005, calling himself "Administrator," listing a title cancelled in 1999, and not mentioning the sale made three weeks earlier (F13 to F16). Third, the partition dated 15 March 2017 that follows a plan surveyed on 8 September 2020, with its notarial number earlier than an instrument dated thirteen days later (F18, F29). Fourth, the geodetic engineer's certificate of 8 September 2020 that the survey was made "with the full knowledge and consent of the owner(s)," when no owner knew and the only agent had been dead three years (F28). Fifth, the sworn statement of 15 September 2021, two months before registration and a year after the publication, calling land titled to three other people "my property" (F32). Beside them, the 2020 self-help on the parcel sworn to by Shirley D. De Leon (3.11). The word "fraud" need not appear in our memorandum; the dates say it.

**The order of the rebuttal day.** Custodians first, because their records cannot be argued with: R2 (Registry), R5 (notarial), R4 (survey), then R7 if needed. Then R1 and R3, the defense's own words against their own documents. Jonathan last, short, on the three facts only he can give: no consultation, no money received, no consent to any survey or partition. Then rest, finally.

**The memorandum's last page: the decision table.** For a judge who writes rule, application, disposition, the memorandum should end with the table she can lift into her decision. Each row is one operative paragraph of Part 6.

##### 5.4 Timing: when each blow must be struck, and what is lost if it is late

This court records who complied on time and gives one indulgence. Three rules fix the clock. The Judicial Affidavit Rule requires affidavits and exhibits to be filed and served not later than five days before the hearing at which the witness testifies; a late filing is deemed waived, and the court may allow a late submission only once, for a valid reason, without prejudice to the other side, and on payment of a fine (Sections 2 and 10). The pre-trial order controls the issues, the witnesses and the exhibits, "unless modified before trial to prevent manifest injustice" (Rule 18, Section 7). Rebuttal is "rebutting evidence only" (Rule 30, Section 5[f]). Everything below follows from those three.

| By when | What must be done | What is lost if it is not |
|---|---|---|
| **Now, before 14 Oct** | Read the 13 May 2026 pre-trial order (Part 7, item 1). Check whether its statement of issues covers the nullity of the 2017 partition and the Article 496 ground (3.10), the Article 1678 and 449 treatment of improvements (3.11), and whether its witness and exhibit lists leave room for records custodians and the complete certified copies. If not, counsel moves to modify the pre-trial order before trial opens, in one paragraph, on the ground that the partition entry is already in Exhibit B and the Answer itself raised Article 448. | An issue not in the pre-trial order cannot be tried; this court will hold us to the order. The partition ground and the improvements attack would be argued only on appeal, if at all. |
| **Before 14 Oct** | File and serve the errata manifestation (Complaint ¶¶25, 50; affidavit corrections). Confirm the governing procedure from the order. File the Registry and Clerk of Court requests (the two letters drafted) and keep the stamped copies; the fifteen-day follow-up clock runs from the stamp. | The defense opens cross with our own errors; the records arrive after our dates close. |
| **14 Oct** | Jonathan testifies. Ask the court to sub-mark Entry 2021003236 as B-5. Counsel announces, on the record, that the plaintiff will offer complete certified copies of Exhibits E and H and will request subpoenas duces tecum to the Registry, the Clerk of Court, DENR Region V, the Building Official and the Municipal Assessor, returnable on 2 December. | Subpoenas asked for late are refused as delay, and the returns are not in evidence when we rest. |
| **By 21 Oct** | Counsel files the subpoena requests (5.5). Jonathan's supplemental affidavit, if leave is sought (Q60 to Q66), is filed with a one-paragraph motion for leave: valid reason, no prejudice, fine tendered. Jonathan's own 2025 letter to Salvador is located (Part 7, item 17). | The single indulgence is spent on something else, or refused as untimely. |
| **By 30 Oct** (five days before 4 Nov) | Shirley D. De Leon confirmed; her affidavit is already filed (Exh. N). Any supplemental affidavit for her, if counsel wants the 2020 self-help detail expanded, filed and served by this date. | Waiver of the supplement. |
| **By 6 Nov** (five days before 11 Nov) | Donata King's supplemental affidavit identifying her 9 July 2021 Verified Answer and its Bicol Post annexes, with the Branch 41 certified copy, filed and served with the motion for leave if the first indulgence was not yet used. Prepare her on Crim. Case 20-6030 and the perjury acquittal. | The 9 July 2021 notice (F30) comes in only through a custodian, or not at all in chief. |
| **By 27 Nov** (five days before 2 Dec) | Subpoena returns in hand: Registry (Primary Entry Book, both entry files, the partition instrument or a no-record certification), Clerk of Court (Asis registers and duplicate originals), DENR Region V (plan file), Building Official (permits or no-record certification), Municipal Assessor (ARPs 00246, 00247, 00274, 00276 and the declarations on the buildings), BIR RDO 64, Provincial Treasurer. Custodians' affidavits or their appearance under subpoena scheduled for 2 December. Francia de los Reyes limited to Q1 to Q9 or dropped. | Whatever is not in by 2 December cannot be offered in chief on 9 December, and most of it is not "rebuttal." |
| **2 Dec** | Custodians testify or their certified returns are identified. The complete certified copies of Exhibits E and H, the Deed of Confirmation (RD Ref. 2022006350), the Special Proceeding No. 2156 record, the tax declarations and the plan file are marked. | Gaps in the chain that the defense will exploit on their dates. |
| **9 Dec** | Formal offer of evidence, each exhibit with a one-line purpose. Rest **subject to rebuttal**, on the record, and ask that rebuttal dates be fixed after 10 March 2027. | Without the reservation, the court may treat the case as submitted after the defense rests. |
| **13 Jan to 10 Mar 2027** | Cross of each defense witness per 5.2. For Salvador, lay the foundation for his 25 November 2025 letter under Rule 132, Section 13: show him the letter, fix the date and place, ask whether he wrote and signed it, let him explain. Demand the original receipts. Object to the mislabeled defense Exhibit 4 and to affidavit markings that conflict with the Answer. | Without the foundation on cross, the letter cannot impeach him in rebuttal. |
| **Five days before each rebuttal date** | Rebuttal affidavits filed and served; government custodians under subpoena where they decline to execute one (Judicial Affidavit Rule, Section 5). Each rebuttal exhibit tied in the offer to the sworn defense statement it rebuts (5.3). | Rebuttal evidence excluded as new matter or as late. |
| **After rebuttal** | Memoranda within the period the court fixes, ending with the decision table (5.3). Decision due within ninety days of submission (Constitution, Article VIII, Section 15[1]). | |
| **After judgment** | Expect an appeal to the Regional Trial Court within fifteen days (Rule 40). Execution of a judgment in an action for recovery of ownership is not immediate on appeal, unlike ejectment; the record must therefore be built so that every finding of fact carries an exhibit page, which is what survives review. | A judgment won on characterization is reversed; one won on dates and pages is affirmed. |

**The realistic calendar.** Our evidence closes 9 December 2026. Theirs closes 10 March 2027. Rebuttal falls in March or April 2027, memoranda by about May, and a decision within ninety days of submission, that is, by about August 2027 if the court keeps its own calendar as strictly as it keeps ours. Complete victory in this court is a judgment in 2027 with findings that hold on appeal. Every date above exists to make that judgment possible and that appeal futile.

**Source:** `BALANE_BAD_FAITH_SUMMARY_2026-10.html`  
**Section:** PART 6. THE JUDGMENT WE ASK FOR

---

#### PART 6. THE JUDGMENT WE ASK FOR

Written the way this court writes: the rule by section, "considering that," then an operative WHEREFORE with exact particulars. Counsel adapts the figures and the exhibit letters to the record as offered.

> 

Before this court is a Complaint for Accion Reivindicatoria, Recovery of Possession, Declaration of Nullity of Deed of Absolute Sale, Cancellation of Transfer Certificate of Title No. 079-2021002126 and Declaration of Real Property ARP No. GR-2023-II-07-001-00256, Accounting, and Damages.

Article 1874 of the Civil Code provides that when a sale of a piece of land is through an agent, the authority of the latter shall be in writing, otherwise the sale shall be void. Article 1898 provides that a contract made by an agent exceeding the scope of his authority, and not ratified by the principal, is void if the other party is aware of the limits of the powers granted.

Considering that the Special Power of Attorney dated March 16, 1992 (Exhibit "E"), annotated on Transfer Certificate of Title No. T-52540 on June 21, 1996 (Exhibit "B"), authorized Cesar M. de la Fuente to sell only to third persons holding contracts to sell executed by the previous administrator or who had paid him down payments or partial payments, and only after prior consultation with the registered owners; considering that defendant Gloria H. Balane, by her own Answer, occupied the property as a lessee of the registered owners and offered no contract to sell executed by the previous administrator and no receipt of any payment to him, the only receipt marked "Down payment" being dated January 6, 2005 and issued by another (defendants' Exhibit "5", page 3); considering that no consultation with the registered owners was made, as the registered owner Patricia Keesey Zschoche testified and as the defendants' own witness Salvador Osum dela Fuente admitted (S31); and considering that under Section 52 of Presidential Decree No. 1529 the annotation of the said Special Power of Attorney was constructive notice of its limits to all persons from June 21, 1996, the Deed of Absolute Sale dated September 29, 2016 (Exhibit "H") was executed beyond the scope of the written authority and is void.

Considering further that a buyer of registered land must remain a purchaser in good faith until the registration of the conveyance and that the conveyance was registered only on November 23, 2021; considering that by her own Answer defendant Gloria H. Balane and her family had paid rent for the premises to the registered owners' administrator (paragraphs 40, 43 and 44[b]); that on May 28, 2020 she executed before the Philippine Consulate General in Toronto a Special Power of Attorney (Annex "1" to the Answer) to institute and defend actions over the property, describing it by the registered owners' own tax declaration and withholding the power to sell; that on August 16, 2021 her representative Karen Gay A. Elevado obtained from the Registry of Deeds a certified copy of the Special Power of Attorney of March 16, 1992, including its paragraph 7 (defendants' Exhibit "7"); and that on September 15, 2021 she executed a Special Power of Attorney describing land then registered in the names of Geraldine K. Hoppe, Patricia K. Zschoche and Marcia Ellen Keesey as "my property," the defendants were not purchasers in good faith from the execution of the deed on September 29, 2016, they being charged from June 21, 1996 with the limits of the authority under which they claimed, and in any event from the facts existing before registration which show that they were not unaware of those limits. Considering that the deed recites the price as "in hand paid" while the defendants' own receipts show payments continuing to February 2017 (defendants' Exhibit "5", pages 31 to 33); that the agent's petition of October 20, 2016 for a new owner's duplicate did not disclose the sale made three weeks earlier; that the Partition-Subdivision Agreement dated March 15, 2017 recites a subdivision plan surveyed on September 8, 2020 and approved on April 6, 2021 (defendants' Exhibit "1", page 2); and that on September 15, 2021 defendant Gloria H. Balane described land registered in the names of others as "my property," the defendants acted in a reckless and oppressive manner within the meaning of Article 2232 of the Civil Code; and considering that their refusal to vacate after the demand received on August 22, 2025 (Exhibit "J-1") compelled the plaintiff to litigate to protect her interest (Article 2208, paragraph 2).

WHEREFORE, judgment is rendered:

1. Declaring the Deed of Absolute Sale dated September 29, 2016, executed by Cesar M. de la Fuente in favor of Gloria H. Balane, Doc. No. 304, Page No. 61, Book No. CXVI, series of 2016 of Notary Public Donald E. Asis, NULL AND VOID;

2. Directing the Registry of Deeds for the Province of Camarines Norte to CANCEL Transfer Certificate of Title No. 079-2021002126 in the name of Gloria H. Balane and to ISSUE, in lieu thereof, a new transfer certificate of title over Lot 2-X-6-I-4-C-1, plan (LRA) PSD-E2020005406, with an area of Two Thousand Five Hundred Eighty-Seven (2,587) square meters, in the names of Geraldine K. Hoppe, Patricia K. Zschoche and Marcia Ellen Keesey, upon payment of the lawful fees;

3. Declaring NULL AND VOID Tax Declaration ARP No. GR-2023-II-07-001-00256 and every tax declaration issued in the name of Gloria H. Balane over the said lot, and directing the Municipal Assessor of Mercedes and the Provincial Assessor for the Province of Camarines Norte to CANCEL the same;

4. Ordering the defendants and all persons claiming under them, including every occupant admitted to the premises with their permission, to VACATE the said portion and to surrender possession to the plaintiff within THIRTY (30) CALENDAR DAYS from finality;

5. Declaring that the defendants have FORFEITED, without right to indemnity, all improvements introduced on the said portion after September 29, 2016, pursuant to Article 449 of the Civil Code, without prejudice to the refund of necessary expenses under the first paragraph of Article 546, if proved, and without any right of retention; and declaring that, as to any structure the defendants prove to have been built before September 29, 2016 while they held the premises as lessees, the plaintiff and her co-owners retain the option under Article 1678 of the Civil Code to pay one-half of its value at the termination of the lease or to require its removal;

6. Ordering the defendants, jointly and severally, to render an ACCOUNTING of all rentals and fruits received from the said lot from August 22, 2025, the date of receipt of the demand, until possession is surrendered, and to pay the same to the plaintiff, and, in any event, to pay the plaintiff temperate damages of ______ Pesos (P______) under Article 2224 of the Civil Code for the use and occupation of the lot from August 22, 2025, or reasonable compensation at the monthly rate proved;

7. Ordering the defendants, jointly and severally, to pay the plaintiff exemplary damages of Two Hundred Thousand Pesos (P200,000.00) under Articles 2229 and 2232 of the Civil Code, the plaintiff having been found entitled to the compensatory and temperate damages in paragraph 6 (Article 2234), and attorney's fees of One Hundred Thousand Pesos (P100,000.00) under Article 2208, paragraphs (1), (2) and (5), with costs; and

8. DISMISSING the counterclaim.

SO ORDERED.

Notes for counsel. (a) Paragraph 2: naming the non-party co-owners is within the action, since Art. 487 "covers all kinds of action for the recovery of possession" and a co-owner "may bring such an action, without the necessity of joining all the other co-owners" (_Catedrilla v. Lauron_, G.R. No. 179011, 15 Apr 2013); cancelling the Balane title without ordering a new one would leave Lot C-1 untitled; the alternative, reinstating T-52540 by cancelling both derivative titles, touches the owners' own TCT 079-2021002127 and the non-party co-owners, so the new-title form is proposed. (b) Paragraph 6: Article 2234 bars exemplary damages unless the plaintiff is first shown entitled to moral, temperate or compensatory damages; the temperate or compensatory award is the predicate, and its figure must be proved or reasonably estimated (Article 2225). (c) The good-faith clause rests only on the defendants' own pleading and instruments and on the Registry's certification, not on the neighbour's pleading of July 9, 2021, so it stands even if that notice is never imputed to Mrs. Balane. (d) Jurisdiction: Exhibit "D" states the current assessed value of the lot as Two Hundred Fifty-Seven Thousand Six Hundred Seventy Pesos (P257,670.00) (market value P4,294,420 at six percent; tax effectivity 2024), within the P400,000 limit of BP 129 Section 33(3) as amended by RA 11576. The exemplary-damages figure is the amount the En Banc awarded in _Duenas_; counsel to confirm the damages jurisdiction of this court and adjust.

# C. Strategy: rollout, traps, their path to victory


## How this unfolds: the record, the lock-in, the trap

**Document:** How this unfolds: the record, the lock-in, the trap  
**Source:** `MEMO_PACK_2026-10-15/05_TRAP_MAP.html`  
**Section:** Preamble and summary

---

### How this unfolds: the record, the lock-in, the trap

Civil Case No. 26-360 · 15 October 2026 · for counsel · replaces the plan to call records custodians in our case-in-chief on 2 December.

> 

**The essence.** The defense story is already frozen. Salvador, Princess and Erwin swore their judicial affidavits on 1 April 2026, and changing them now needs the court's leave. Nearly every key claim in those affidavits is contradicted by a certified record, and most of those records the defense does not know we hold.

If we show the records now, we give them five months to explain, drop a witness, or ask leave to amend. If we hold them, their witnesses affirm their affidavits in January, commit to them on cross, and the records land on rebuttal, when no new affidavit can repair the story.

**So:** prove our case with what is already marked, gather the rest quietly now, lock them in on cross, and spring the records on rebuttal.


**Document:** How this unfolds: the record, the lock-in, the trap  
**Source:** `MEMO_PACK_2026-10-15/05_TRAP_MAP.html`  
**Section:** 1. Why the rules allow it

---

#### 1. Why the rules allow it

- **The pre-marking bar covers only evidence-in-chief.** "No evidence shall be allowed to be presented and offered during the trial in support of a party's evidence-in-chief other than those that had been earlier identified and pre-marked during the pre-trial, except if allowed by the court for good cause shown" (Guidelines on pre-trial, A.M. No. 03-1-09-SC). [Quoted from secondary reproductions; the official text was not reached. Counsel confirms.] Rebuttal and impeachment evidence fall outside it.

- **The bar also means the custodians do not belong in our case-in-chief.** The Registry file, the notarial registers and the DENR file were never pre-marked. Calling them on 2 December would need "good cause" leave. That needs leave the court has said it will not readily give, and it shows our hand.

- **Rebuttal is "rebutting evidence only"** (Rule 30 §5[f]). That is exactly what these records are, once a defense witness has sworn the claim they contradict.

- **Gloria's own papers are admissions of a party.** They are evidence in their own right and need no foundation. A non-party witness's own prior statement, such as Salvador's 2025 letter, needs the Rule 132 §13 foundation on cross: show it, fix the date and place, and let him explain.


**Document:** How this unfolds: the record, the lock-in, the trap

#### 2. Our case-in-chief already has what it needs

The class limit stands on pre-marked exhibits and the defendants' Answer:

- the SPA's paragraph 7 (Exh. E);

- its 1996 annotation (Exh. B);

- the deed, which names no title and recites "in hand paid" (Exh. H);

- the revocation (Exh. F) and the 2020 notice (Exh. G);

- the 2020 survey date on the Balane title (Exh. C) and the 2017 partition entry (Exh. B);

- the demand (Exh. J);

- the tenancy admitted in Answer ¶¶40 and 43.

That carries the demurrer. On 9 December we complete Exhibits E and H from certified copies, read the offer, and rest subject to rebuttal. We call no custodians and file no subpoenas before then.

| **Salvador:** fully paid before the deed; his father had authority | His own receipts to February 2017 against "in hand paid"; "which power gave the authority to sell?" | The 1991 power, administration only (Registry file pp. 2–4) |

| **Ratification:** the receipts and Patricia's 2023 affidavit | "No owner signed any of these receipts?" | Patricia; her same-day adverse claim calling the sale void (Exh. I) |

**The single strongest trap is the partition.** It is dated 15 March 2017, cites a plan not surveyed until 8 September 2020, and Cesar died on 21 June 2017. If the defense calls it regular, the notarial register and the DENR file show it could not have been made when it says. Then Article 2232 needs no adjective.

#### 6. What could spoil it, and the answer

|---|---|
| The court holds a record "should have been presented in chief" | Hold back only what answers a specific sworn defense claim. Every element of our own claim is already in the pre-marked exhibits. |
| Ramos demurs and presents no evidence, so the trap never springs | The case-in-chief wins on its own. If a granted demurrer is reversed on appeal, the defense loses its right to present evidence. |
| Ramos drops a witness | Fewer doors for rebuttal, but the case-in-chief stands. Their pleading claims still need proof they no longer have. |
| A request alerts them | Order the quiet list from lowest risk upward; leave the high-risk offices to rebuttal subpoenas. |
| Rebuttal affidavits are late | File each five days before the rebuttal date, keyed to the transcript; keep the once-only leave for Patricia. |

## The overwhelm / proof plan: demurrer, elements proved twice, rebuttal doors

**Document:** The overwhelm / proof plan: demurrer, elements proved twice, rebuttal doors  
**Source:** `OVERWHELM_PLAN_2026-10-14.html`  
**Section:** Preamble and summary

---

### Civil Case No. 26-360: the overwhelm plan

Prepared 14 October 2026, after Jonathan's direct testimony · internal, for counsel · not a pleading · nothing here is filed or served without counsel.

> 

**Why Ramos let Jonathan testify without a fight.** His strongest move does not depend on cross. After we rest on 9 December he can ask for dismissal "on the ground that upon the facts and the law the plaintiff has shown no right to relief" (Rule 33, §1). If our proof of any element rests only on an attorney-in-fact who learned of the sale in late 2022, he will say that element is hearsay and unproved. If the court denies the demurrer, he loses nothing: "he shall have the right to present evidence." Under the 2019 amendments, a denial cannot be challenged before judgment [Rule 33 §2 as amended; counsel to confirm the official text].

**So "overwhelm" means one thing.** By 9 December, every element of our claim must be proved at least twice in our own formal offer, and never only through Jonathan. Ramos can then attack Jonathan all he likes and the case still stands. The tools are the defendants' own words, certified public records, and witnesses who saw things themselves.

**Consultation must be proved, and not by hearsay.** Non-consultation is part of our own claim, so it is ours to prove (Rule 131 §1: a party must present evidence "on the facts in issue necessary to establish" its claim). On cross, Ramos established that Jonathan was not present for the events before 2025 (as recorded in the consultation gap plan written earlier today). Anything Jonathan says about 1992 to 2016 is therefore not proof of those events. The buyer's burden on her own good faith (_Bucton_) is a backstop, not a substitute. Part 2A sets out what each piece of proof is good for.

> 

**Decision, 14 October: Patricia testifies on rebuttal, not in our case-in-chief.** Three consequences follow.

1. **The case-in-chief must survive a demurrer without her.** It rests on the class limit, proved by the certified SPA, the defendants' Answer and our pre-marked exhibits. Consultation stays pleaded, but it is not proved first-hand until she testifies.

2. **She can only answer what they put in.** Rebuttal is "rebutting evidence only, unless the court, for good reasons and in the furtherance of justice, permits them to adduce evidence upon their original case" (Rule 30 §5[f]). If no defense witness or exhibit claims the owners were consulted, approved, knew, or ratified, her denial has nothing to rebut. It then depends on that exception, from a judge who has written "No further liberality shall be accorded." So we must make sure the defense puts those claims into evidence. Part 2C lists the openings.

3. **Her filing date is a question for counsel.** The Judicial Affidavit Rule requires affidavits "not later than five days before pre-trial" (§2[a]), and hers was not filed then. Counsel decides whether a rebuttal affidavit still needs the once-only leave for late filing (§10[a]). If it does, that leave stays reserved for her.
