#### 2A. No hearsay: what each piece of proof can and cannot do

| Proof | Admissible for | Not admissible for |
|---|---|---|
| Jonathan's testimony | What he did and saw himself: the 2025 Registry requests, the demand, the papers he obtained, what he observed on the lot | Anything before he was present: the SPA's making, consultation, payments, the 2016 sale |
| Patricia's testimony | What she signed (Exh. E, Exh. F), that no one consulted her, that she received nothing, what she meant by the 2023 affidavit of loss | Whether her sisters were consulted, unless they told her directly; she says "ask them" |
| Exh. F, the 2005 revocation | The act of revocation itself, an operative act offered for the fact that it was made, not for the truth of a statement in it | Proof that Cesar or Gloria knew of it |
| Exh. G, the Bicol Post notice (25 Sep, 2 and 9 Oct 2020) | An act, not a statement offered for its truth: the owners publicly declared every transaction by Cesar after 15 Aug 2005 "unauthorized and, therefore, VOID," before the sale was registered. That proves the owners did not ratify (Arts. 1898, 1910) and gave the world notice. It also routes inquiries to Agcaoili, the one channel Salvador names (S31). Owners who had approved the sale would not publish this, and the court may draw that inference | Direct proof that no one was consulted in 2016. It does not say so, it dates from 2020, and as the owners' own later statement in their favor Ramos will call it self-serving. It corroborates Patricia; it does not replace her |
| Agcaoili letter of 15 Aug 2019 | That the family's counsel took a written position in 2019 and sent it to Daet, once authenticated by its author or by Patricia as the client | Proof that no consultation happened in 2016; that is hearsay unless its author testifies to it from her own knowledge |
| The Answer and its annexes; the defense witnesses' answers on cross | Anything they admit, without further proof (Rule 129 §4) | Nothing they deny; a denial only shifts the fight to the documents |
| Salvador's affidavit S31 ("the owners never came home") | Cross-examination of Salvador, once he testifies in January | Our case-in-chief. It is not our evidence before 9 December, so it cannot defeat a demurrer |
| Gloria's excluded affidavit ("no direct communication" with the sisters) | Nothing, unless the court admits it on the defendants' motion | We never move to admit it ourselves |
| Certified Registry and notarial records | What they record: dates, entries, who appeared, what was signed and by whom | The state of mind of anyone |

**Section:** 2B. Your point on T-52540, in the form that survives

---

#### 2B. Your point on T-52540, in the form that survives

The SPA names one property: the undivided 139,132 square meters under T-4497 (Exh. E ¶3). It never names T-52540, Lot 2-X-6-I-4-C, or any later lot. The 2016 deed does not name T-52540 either. The only papers that connect this sale to T-52540 are Cesar's own Deed of Confirmation of 28 March 2017, which no owner signed, and the Registry's registration stamp of 23 November 2021, made after his death. An agent cannot widen his own authority by confirming his own act; only the principal ratifies (Art. 1910). Read strictly, as _Bautista-Spille_ requires ("courts will not infer or presume broad powers from deeds which do not sufficiently include property or subject under which the agent is to deal"), the power does not reach a sale described only by a later subdivision lot that the SPA never names.

**How Ramos will answer it, and the reply.** He will say T-52540 is part of the T-4497 parcel, that the Registry carried the SPA onto T-52540 in 1996 "to sign, negotiate for the sale of the parcel of land in this title" (Exh. B p. 3), and that the 2005 Era sale went through under it. Our reply: the Registry's carry-over is a clerical act; under PD 1529 §52 the instrument, not the Registry's summary, defines the power, and that is the same rule we rely on for the class limit. The Era sale came before the revocation. **Use it alongside the class and the consultation, not instead of them.** On its own, the point invites the one-sentence answer that a power over the whole covers a part. Two cautions: do not argue that the subdivisions creating T-52540 were themselves unauthorized, because T-52540 is the owners' own title and our Exhibit B; and if Ramos offers the 1991 Agcaoili SPA annotated on 10 October 2016 (Exh. B p. 5) as another source of authority, it fails: the 1991 SPA (Doc. No. 260, Page No. 53, Book No. I, Series of 1991, Makati, acknowledged before the Agcaoili notary) is in our own certified Registry file Ref. 2025001098, pp. 2 to 4, and in the Spec. Proc. 2156 record. It grants general control and supervision of the properties, payment of taxes, collection of money with power to sign receipts, and representation before the BIR, the Registry and DAR. It grants no power to sell. A power in general terms "comprises only acts of administration" (Art. 1877), and a sale needs a special power (Art. 1878[5]).

**Section:** 2C. Opening the doors Patricia will walk through

---

#### 2C. Opening the doors Patricia will walk through

The defense is already opening these doors: the first five items are in their marked exhibits and filed affidavits. A door opens in law only when they offer that evidence at trial, through a witness who affirms the affidavit or an exhibit in their formal offer. If Ramos drops a witness or an exhibit, that door closes, so on cross counsel puts each point on the record anyway.

| What the defense puts in | What Patricia answers on rebuttal |
|---|---|
| The deed (Def. Exh. 6) recites "the provision of Article 1623 … had been complied with," which means the co-owners were given written notice | Whether she ever received any notice of a sale to Gloria Balane |
| Receipts headed "Heirs of Mary W. Keesey Estate" (Def. Exh. 5) | Whether she ever authorized those receipt books or received any of the money |
| Erwin's affidavit: the purchase began "first with Ben Llamanzares" | Whether, to her knowledge as an owner, Llamanzares ever contracted with the Balanes (only if she knows first-hand) |
| Salvador S30–S31: no one questioned his father's authority; his father dealt with the Agcaoili office | Whether the Agcaoili office ever relayed a proposed sale to the Balanes; the 2019 letter and the 2020 notice |
| Erwin S14/S23: Cesar showed the SPA "as proof he was authorized to sell" | What the SPA authorized, and to whom, as its signatory |
| The 2023 affidavit of loss offered as ratification (if they offer it) | What she meant, and the adverse claim she swore the same day |
| Gloria's affidavit, if the court admits it: Cesar's authority came from the sisters | That she gave none after 15 August 2005 |

**On cross, counsel asks the questions that put these claims on the record.** For example, ask Salvador: "Did the owners approve your father's sales?" Ask Erwin: "Who told your mother the owners agreed?" Any answer suggesting consent, knowledge or approval becomes something she can rebut. Order the transcript of every defense date, so her affidavit quotes the exact testimony it answers.

**Section:** 3. The less irritating route: records and cross, not discovery

---

#### 3. The less irritating route: records and cross, not discovery

1. **Quiet gathering now; the subpoena request held for rebuttal.** The custodian records were not pre-marked, so in our case-in-chief they would need "good cause" leave (A.M. No. 03-1-09-SC), which needs leave the court has said it will not readily give, and shows our hand. Jonathan obtains certified copies in his own name now; the subpoena request is filed after the last defense witness, returnable on the rebuttal date. See the trap map (memorandum pack, Annex A).

2. **Cross-examination of their own witnesses**, in place of a request for admission and a motion for production. Discovery after trial has begun is uncommon here and may be refused as late. Each fact the request would have asked them to admit is put to the defense witness who knows it, with the document in hand, including the rents they charge (the peso figure). See the cross-examination checklist (memorandum pack, Annex E). The drafted request and motion are held in reserve.

3. **Patricia, prepared now for rebuttal.** Gather her answers now; finalize her affidavit after the defense rests, keyed to the transcripts. On 9 December, rest _subject to rebuttal_ and ask for rebuttal dates after 10 March 2027.

4. **The once-only leave for a late affidavit** (JA Rule §10[a]) is kept for Patricia's rebuttal affidavit unless counsel concludes it is not needed. King stays on Exhibit L, and her 9 July 2021 Verified Answer comes in as a certified Branch 41 record, obtained by Mrs. King and offered on rebuttal.

#### 4. The calendar that stacks it

| Date | What lands | What it takes from Ramos |
|---|---|---|
| Now to 9 Dec | Quiet gathering of certified records; nothing filed in court. | Nothing; they see nothing. |
| By 31 Oct | Patricia's answers gathered by counsel for the draft affidavit (not filed). | Nothing yet; this protects the rebuttal. |
| 4 Nov | Shirley De Leon (Exh. N). | Possession and self-help in 2020. |
| 11 Nov | Donata King (Exh. L), kept to her affidavit as filed. | "No notice before 2025." |
| 2 Dec | Francia de los Reyes (Exh. M), if counsel keeps her. No custodians. | Nothing new. |
| 9 Dec | Formal offer, one-line purpose per exhibit, read against the table in Part 2. Rest subject to rebuttal; ask for rebuttal dates after 10 March 2027 and for memoranda. | The demurrer. Every element is in the offer at least twice. |
| 13 Jan to 10 Mar 2027 | Defense witnesses. Cross opens the doors in Part 2C. Order every transcript. | Their claims of consent, approval and ratification, on the record. |
| Rebuttal (after 10 Mar 2027) | Patricia by videoconference, her affidavit filed five days before, each answer tied to a defense statement; the certified records gathered quietly, and subpoenas filed after the defense rests, each answering a sworn claim. | Consultation, payment, ratification, the 2023 affidavit. |

**The one open gap that money depends on.** Exemplary damages need a temperate or compensatory award first (Art. 2234), and its figure must be proved or reasonably estimated. Nobody in our case yet gives a monthly rental value for the lot or the rooms. The route: on cross, ask Princess and Erwin how many rooms are rented, since when, and for how much a month (Answer ¶27); their answer is the figure. Fallback: a local witness, which would need leave.


**Document:** The overwhelm / proof plan: demurrer, elements proved twice, rebuttal doors  
**Source:** `OVERWHELM_PLAN_2026-10-14.html`  
**Section:** 5. What Jonathan does from here

---

#### 5. What Jonathan does from here

- **Nothing new on the stand.** If cross comes on 4 November, the cross notes govern: short answers, exhibit and page, concede small truths, never testify to anyone's mind.

- **Off the stand, he is the engine.** He gets Patricia's answers to counsel by 31 October for the rebuttal draft, collects the completed certified Exhibits E and H, pulls the certified Civil Case 8563 record, and orders the transcript of every defense date.

- **He brings the bundle** to every date and produces nothing unless asked.

## The Balanes' path to victory, and how we close it

**Document:** The Balanes' path to victory, and how we close it  
**Source:** `BALANE_TRAJECTORY_2026-10-16.html`  
**Section:** Preamble and summary

---

### The Balanes' path to victory, and how we close it

Civil Case No. 26-360 · 16 October 2026 · standalone study document, for Jonathan and counsel · written as Atty. Ramos would plan it · read with the trap map in the memorandum pack (Annex A) · Part 6 is reserved for the transcript of Jonathan's testimony of 14 October.

> 

**Their best path in one paragraph.** They win if the court believes Gloria paid Ben Llamanzares a down payment in the 1980s, which would put her inside the paragraph 7 class, and if our proof that no owner was consulted never gets in. Gloria's affidavit could carry the first, if the court admits it. The second comes if Patricia's rebuttal is excluded or her standing is attacked. Then the sale looks like one the SPA allowed, made under an annotated power with no revocation on the title. Everything else they have either buys time, saves money, or waits on our mistakes.

**The two cards that matter:** the 1980s down payment, and Patricia. We close both before trial without revealing anything we hold.

**Expect late witnesses and papers for what the record lacks.** Their own papers already point to where they would come from. Gloria's excluded affidavit (S20 to S24) says the 1980s payment was written in Manuel Violeta's "brown columnar book," which went "to Cesar and then the Agcaoili Law Office." That is the plaintiff's own lawyers. The Answer's side also claims rights "from a certain Kenneth Asistin Jr." Part 3 maps the cast they could produce, the gates they must pass, and the fixed facts that test a late story.


**Document:** The Balanes' path to victory, and how we close it  
**Source:** `BALANE_TRAJECTORY_2026-10-16.html`

#### 1. Their trajectory, stage by stage

|---|---|---|---|---|
| Now to Dec | Push the pending motion (19 May 2026) to admit Gloria's excluded affidavit, so she can testify to a 1980s down payment first-hand | A first-hand witness inside the class | Our Comment-Opposition stands. If she is admitted, her own S43 (no contact with the owners) helps us. Her account has no receipt, and her family paid as "Occupant" every month to 2014. Her amounts conflict: ₱3,000 in the 1980s, "by 1992," and ₱20,000 in 2005 to Salvador. | An order on the motion; a notice that she will testify by videoconference |
| Now to Dec | Pull the guardianship petition from RTC Branch 41. It says the co-owners, "by reason of their advanced age," are "unable to effectively manage" their property | An attack on Exh. A, on Jonathan's authority, and on Patricia's competence as a witness | The petition cannot be changed now, so we answer it. A petition is an allegation, not a finding, and competence is presumed. Read whole, its ground is "advanced age, prolonged residence abroad, and the practical inability to personally attend" to acts in the Philippines: inability to be present, not inability of mind. Her own recent sworn acts show her competence: the Makati affidavits of February 2023 and the apostilled SPA of November 2025 (Exh. A). Her live testimony by videoconference shows it best. We never raise the petition ourselves. | Ramos cites or subpoenas the guardianship record |

D), within BP 129 §33(3) as amended; the court took cognizance on 6 February. Be ready to comply the same week: identify Cesar's heirs now. Salvador, their witness, is his son. | A motion, or the court's own order to join |

We did: paragraph 7 on its face (Exh. E); their admitted tenancy; a deed eleven years after the revocation (Exhs. F, H); their burden to prove their own good faith (_Bucton_). If it is denied, they must present evidence. If it is granted and reversed, they lose the right to present it (Rule 33 §1). | A motion filed within the period after we rest |

If needed, ask for the "good reasons" exception (Rule 30 §5[f]). The class leg does not depend on rebuttal. | Their objection to our rebuttal affidavits |
| After judgment | Appeal to the RTC, then the CA; keep possession and the room rents meanwhile; press retention | Time and money even in defeat | Fruits from 22 August 2025 (Art. 549) and a temperate-damages figure on the record (the rents from cross); retention denied under Arts. 449 and 1678; a record exact enough to be affirmed | Notice of appeal |

#### 2. Their case on the merits, in the order Ramos will argue it

| Their argument | Strength | Our answer |
|---|---|---|
| **1. Gloria was in the class.** She paid Llamanzares in the 1980s; Cesar "recognized and later credited" it (Answer ¶40; Salvador S14–S16; Erwin "first with Ben Llamanzares") | Their best card | Object at once to Salvador and Erwin on any 1980s payment: neither says he was there, so it is hearsay unless they show personal knowledge. No Llamanzares receipt exists. Monthly "Occupant" receipts ran to 2014, after the supposed purchase. The only "Down payment" is from 2005, to Salvador. Cesar's own 1993 list of his buyers names no Balane (held for rebuttal). |
| **2. Consultation was a private instruction** (Art. 1902); a buyer may rely on the power "as written" (Art. 1900) | Medium | Paragraph 7 is in the registered instrument, so it is part of the power "as written." The buyer was charged with it from 1996 (§52), and her side pulled the full SPA in August 2021 (Def. Exh. 7). Patricia answers on rebuttal. |
| **3. The owners let Cesar act as though he had full powers for decades** (Art. 1911): the 1993 subdivision, the 1999 Hansol sale, the 2005 Era sale, the estate-named receipts | Medium | All of it predates the 2005 revocation, or is the agent's own doing. Gloria never dealt with the owners (her S43), so she relied on Cesar's word, not on anything the owners did. The owners published the revocation in 2020, before registration (Exh. G). |
| **4. No notice of the revocation;** it was never annotated (Arts. 1921, 1922; §52) | Medium | The class limit makes the revocation unnecessary; it is our backup, not our lead. The notices before registration all come from their own side or from records: the 2020 Toronto SPA, the August 2021 pull of the SPA, the "my property" SPA, and King's 2021 pleading. |

At most unenforceable, and ratified** by acceptance of payments and Patricia's 2023 affidavit (Arts. 1403, 1910) | Low to medium | Art. 1898 makes it void where the buyer is aware of the limits (she is charged with them from the 1996 annotation), and void cannot be ratified (Art. 1409). No owner received anything. The same day as the 2023 affidavit, she swore the sale was void (Exh. I). |
| **6. Builders in good faith,** reimbursement and retention (Arts. 448, 546, 548) | Medium, and their money exit | Lessees until 2016 (Art. 1678); bad-faith buyers after (Art. 449); no retention. Permit and declaration records on rebuttal. |
| **7. Laches and estoppel:** twenty years of silence; Jonathan learned only in 2022 | Low | A void contract is imprescriptible (Art. 1410). Registration was in 2021 and suit in 2026. The owners' counsel wrote in 2019 and published in 2020. |

**Section:** 3. Late witnesses and papers: how they get in, and how they are tested

---

#### 3. Late witnesses and papers: how they get in, and how they are tested

##### 3.1 The gates they must pass

Their witnesses and exhibits were fixed at pre-trial. Anything new must get through a gate, and each gate is a ruling this judge makes strictly.

- **A witness not in their pre-trial brief.** Parties are bound by their pre-trial briefs. Evidence-in-chief not identified at pre-trial is barred "except if allowed by the court for good cause shown" (A.M. No. 03-1-09-SC; counsel to confirm the official text). Objection: the facts were known to them since 1975, so there is no good cause; and the court wrote "No further liberality shall be accorded."

- **A late or supplemental judicial affidavit.** Allowed only once, for a valid reason, without undue prejudice, and with a fine (JA Rule §10[a]). Oppose every request: the story was available in April 2026.

- **Gloria's excluded affidavit.** Its admission motion has been pending since 19 May 2026, and it carries the Violeta-ledger story. Our Comment-Opposition stands.

- **A "newly discovered" paper** (a Llamanzares contract, the ledger, a letter "from the owners"). It was not pre-marked, so it needs leave. Object, and demand the original and its custody history: who held it, where, since when, and why it is surfacing now.

- **Sur-rebuttal after Patricia.** They may ask to answer her with a new "consultation" witness. Sur-rebuttal is the court's grace, not a right. Object to anything beyond what Patricia actually said.

- **Stretching an existing witness on re-direct.** Re-direct is limited to matters raised on cross. Object to new matter.

##### 3.2 The cast they could produce, most likely first

| Late witness or paper | What they would claim | The fixed facts that break it |
|---|---|---|
| A Violeta relative, or Violeta himself, with the "brown columnar book" | Entries of Gloria's 1980s payments toward the purchase | Gloria herself put the book in Cesar's hands and then the Agcaoili office. Ask why it is with them now. It must be a Llamanzares-era book in Violeta's hand showing a payment to Llamanzares before 1992; Salvador had no knowledge of the property then (his S15 dates his first contact to about 1992), so he cannot vouch for it, only for what was collected from Llamanzares in 1992 and where it went. Its paper, ink and format are compared with genuine Llamanzares-era instruments. Monthly "Occupant" payments continued to 2014. Violeta's dates of service and death are fixed first. |
| Kenneth Asistin Jr. | He held rights from Llamanzares and sold them to the Balanes | Identity, age and residence fixed first. No instrument is in the Registry or in Cesar's 1993 list of buyers. A sale of "rights" by an occupant is not the class in paragraph 7, which requires a Llamanzares contract or a payment to Llamanzares. |
| A Llamanzares relative or former employee | "Ben agreed to sell to Gloria" | Ben Llamanzares's date of death. The genuine 1977 Deeds of Final Sale on T-4497 show what his real sales looked like: registered deeds. No Balane appears among them or in the 1993 list. |
| An old neighbor | Saw Gloria pay Llamanzares or Violeta in the 1980s | Ask for the year, the amount, the place, who else was present, and what paper changed hands. Why is the witness in no pleading or affidavit before 2027? Then check every answer against the anchors. |
| A "consultation" witness | Heard Cesar call the owners or the Agcaoili office in 2016 and get approval | Cesar's whereabouts and condition in 2016 to 2017 (Spec. Proc. 2156; death on 21 June 2017). Patricia's own denial. The Agcaoili letter of 2019 calls every post-2005 act unauthorized. Exh. G. |
| A surveyor or engineer | The 2006 survey was the real basis, and the owners consented then | The 2006 receipt Princess paid says the lot is "owned by the Heirs." The plan says "SURVEYED: September 8, 2020." The DENR file shows who signed the consent. |
| A notary's clerk or instrumental witness | The partition was signed by Cesar in March 2017 | Atty. Asis's register for Doc. 402 and its neighbors, fixed quietly now; the plan number did not exist until 2020. |
| A backdated receipt on the estate's printed forms | A payment before the 2005 revocation, or before 1992 | The receipt books were printed in 1998 (printer's imprint, 4C's Press, Daet), so no genuine receipt on them can predate that. Their serial numbers already run out of order. |
| "Harassment" witnesses for the counterclaim | Jonathan threatened occupants or officials | All of Jonathan's dealings are in writing; the ARTA resolutions are agency findings; no blotter exists. From now on, Jonathan has no contact with occupants or defense witnesses. |
| A recanting witness of ours | King, De Leon or de los Reyes "takes back" her affidavit | Their affidavits are sworn and filed. The defense side once prosecuted Mrs. King for perjury, and she was acquitted (RTC Branch 41, 25 Sep 2023). Any approach to our witnesses is written down and reported to counsel at once. |

##### 3.3 The anchors to fix now, quietly

A late story fails on dates it cannot know in advance. Each anchor below is fixed now, before any new witness appears, and none needs a court filing.

- **Benjamin Llamanzares:** date of death (PSA), and the dates of his administration.

- **Manuel Violeta:** whether he is alive, his date of death if not, and his role. Ask the family and its Makati counsel whether the "brown columnar book" exists, and if it does, what it actually shows. It may list the Balanes as renters.

- **Kenneth Asistin Jr.:** who he is, his age, where he lived, and any declaration or instrument in his name.

- **The genuine Llamanzares paper:** the 1977 Deeds of Final Sale annotated on T-4497, as the benchmark of form, notary and wording.

- **The receipt books:** the printer's imprint and any authority to print, which fixes the earliest possible date of a genuine receipt.

- **Cesar in 2016 and 2017:** where he was and his condition, from the Spec. Proc. 2156 record and his death certificate.

- **Patricia's whereabouts:** her passport entries for any date a witness says she approved something in person.

- **Gloria's whereabouts:** in Mercedes through her council terms to 2017; in Toronto by May 2020 (the consular SPA).

- **The closed list:** the defense witnesses named in the pre-trial order and in Answer ¶59. Anyone else needs leave.

##### 3.4 Testing a late witness who gets in

1. **Gate first.** Object on the record before any testimony: not in the pre-trial brief, no good cause, prejudice.

2. **Lock checkable details.** Ask for the date, the amount, the place, who was present, what paper changed hands, who kept it, and when the witness last saw it. Short questions, one fact each.

3. **Prior silence.** Ask why the witness is absent from the verified Answer, the pre-trial brief, and the three defense affidavits of April 2026.

4. **Interest.** Ask about kinship, tenancy under the Balanes, and employment by a municipality where Princess is a councilor and Erwin the Municipal Engineer.

5. **Do not confront on cross with the anchors.** Let the details stand; the anchors land on rebuttal.

6. **No label in court.** The dates and the exhibits do the work. Any remedy for false testimony is counsel's decision after judgment, and is never used as a threat.

##### 3.5 The pattern already on their own papers

The court can see this without our saying a word:

- receipts whose serial numbers run out of order;

- a deed reciting "in hand paid" against receipts that run to February 2017;

- a partition dated before its own plan existed;

- a 2020 tax clearance (eCAR) naming a title cancelled in 1999;

- the agent's loss affidavit signed two days before the deed.

A witness who appears late fits that pattern. We present the pattern as dates, never as accusation.


**Document:** The Balanes' path to victory, and how we close it  
**Source:** `BALANE_TRAJECTORY_2026-10-16.html`  
**Section:** 4. Where they can genuinely win, ranked

---

#### 4. Where they can genuinely win, ranked

1. **The 1980s down payment is believed and our consultation proof is shut out.** This is the only path that defeats the class leg. It needs Gloria admitted, credible, and unrebutted. Close it with hearsay objections to Salvador and Erwin, a document-by-document cross of Gloria, and Patricia keyed to whatever Gloria says.

2. **Patricia is cast as incapable through the guardianship papers.** That would weaken Exh. A and her rebuttal at once. The petition cannot be changed now, so the answer must be ready: the petition alleges, it does not find; its ground is absence and inability to attend in person; her sworn acts of 2023 and 2025, and her own voice on rebuttal, show a competent owner.

3. **Jurisdiction.** It is not likely to succeed, but it is fatal if it does, and it can be raised on appeal. Keep the authority verified.

4. **Money and time.** Retention and a long appeal keep them in possession and collecting rent. Answer it with the fruits and a damages figure on the record.

5. **Joinder.** This only becomes a risk if the court orders it and we are not ready.


**Document:** The Balanes' path to victory, and how we close it  
**Source:** `BALANE_TRAJECTORY_2026-10-16.html`  
**Section:** 5. Quiet moves now that close or blunt their paths

---

#### 5. Quiet moves now that close or blunt their paths

1. **Guardianship: know the exact words, prepare the answer.** Obtain the petition as filed and any order issued in it, and read their exact wording. Prepare the competence answer above. Keep Patricia's affidavit consistent with the petition: she lives abroad and acts through her son, which is what the petition says. Nothing is said about it in this case unless Ramos raises it.

2. **Hearsay objections, scripted.** For Salvador and Erwin on anything before they were personally involved: "Objection, no personal knowledge; the witness was not present." This reveals nothing.

3. **Cesar's heirs identified,** with an address for each, in case the court orders joinder.

4. **Jurisdiction authority verified** and kept in counsel's file.

5. **Gloria's cross finished** before the court rules on her affidavit, so her admission is never a surprise.

6. **The anchors in Part 3.3 fixed,** starting with Llamanzares, Violeta and the "brown columnar book."

7. **No contact** between Jonathan and any occupant or defense witness from now on; any approach to our witnesses written down and reported to counsel.

What to look for: anything that sets up a later witness (a name, a ledger, a phone call, a meeting); anything conceding Gloria's 1980s payment or the family's dealings with Llamanzares; anything on how Jonathan knows what he knows (the personal-knowledge line for a demurrer); anything on his mother's age, health or ability (the guardianship line); anything on the ARTA and Ombudsman complaints (the counterclaim line); and any concession about the SPA's terms or the 1996 annotation.

Statutes and rules quoted here were read in their official text this week. The "incapable of pecuniary estimation" and assessed-value authorities are stated in substance and are to be fetched before use. The definition of an incompetent in the guardianship rules (Rule 92) is likewise to be checked against the petition's wording.

### Cross-examination checklist: their own witnesses prove our facts

Civil Case No. 26-360 · defense dates 13 January to 10 March 2027 · for counsel · replaces the request for admission and the motion for production, which are held in reserve and not filed.

> 

**Why this route.** Discovery after the trial has begun is uncommon and may be refused as late. Cross-examination of the defense witnesses, on what they swore and the exhibits they sponsor, is what the court expects. Every answer becomes their own evidence. Ask short, closed questions, each tied to a document, and stop when the answer is on the record. **Show only their own exhibits and our marked exhibits.** Never show a record held for rebuttal: ask the question that commits the witness, and let the record answer it later. A question that a witness cannot answer truthfully in their favor also opens a door for Patricia's rebuttal.

**When a witness mentions a paper we have not seen** (a Llamanzares contract, a receipt book, a rent ledger, a permit): ask whether it exists, who holds it and where it is, and ask the court in open court that it be produced at the next setting. Ask once, politely; the record of a refusal is enough.

**Source:** `MEMO_PACK_2026-10-15/04_CROSS_CHECKLIST.html`

**Section:** 5. What this leaves to documents alone

#### 5. What this leaves to documents alone

- Already in evidence: the deed naming no title (Exh. H), the survey date (Exh. C p. 2), the registration entries (Exh. B). Held for rebuttal: Cesar's confirmation instrument, the "my property" SPA, the notarial registers, the DENR file, the Primary Entry Book (see the trap map, Annex A).

- **Counsel's note on one trade-off.** Rule 26 §5 says a party who never served a request for admission on facts "within the personal knowledge" of the other side may be barred from proving them, "unless otherwise allowed by the court for good cause." Our proof rests on public records and on the defendants' own testimony, so the risk is small, but counsel should weigh it. The drafted request for admission is held in reserve if counsel prefers to serve it.

# D. Witnesses


## The witnesses in the court's own form: each witness mapped to her ten questions, the article words, and the Considering-that line it earns

**Section:** Preamble and summary

---

### The witnesses, in the court's own form

Civil Case No. 26-360, Zschoche v. Balane, MTC Mercedes · 19 October 2026 · internal; for Jonathan and counsel; nothing filed or sent. 

The court's writings in hand cite statutes by article and section and run rule → "Considering that" → WHEREFORE, with exact particulars (playbook §5.1). The ten questions and their articles are those already set out in the War of the Statutes (§0 and §12). This document asks one thing of each witness: **which question does the testimony answer, under which article's words, and what line does it earn in an order written in that form?** The italic "Considering that" lines are our drafting in that form, for counsel's memorandum and decision table. They are not a prediction of any ruling. Tags: [VERIFY] not yet on a certified copy · [COUNSEL] counsel's call.

> 

**Read this first.**

1. **Five of the ten questions are answered by documents alone.** Jurisdiction (Q1), the written authority (Q2), void or unenforceable (Q4), revocation (Q5) and the partition (Q7) turn on Exhibits B, E, F, G and H, the Registry entries, and the articles. No witness on either side can move them. Keep every witness off them.

2. **The class question (Q3) is answered by their own witnesses.** Their Answer (¶40) says the family "paid monthly rent." Salvador says he was sent to collect "mga bayad sa upa" (S19). Princess says "noong una ay nirerentahan lang ito ng aking magulang" (S7). Their only proof of a Llamanzares purchase is what Salvador says Llamanzares told him and Cesar around 1992 (S14 to S15), with no contract to sell and no Llamanzares receipt.

3. **Awareness under Article 1898 is in their verified Answer.** "Gloria checked the SPA" before and during the transfer (¶44[h]). Paragraph 7 is in that SPA. Whoever read the SPA read the limits. This holds even if Gloria's own affidavit stays out.

4. **Good faith until 23 November 2021 (Q6) is answered by dated facts, not opinions.** They come from Princess's and Jomil's own record in the King cases and from Mrs. King's 2021 pleading. Article 528 asks only whether "facts exist"; the dates do the work.

5. **Improvements, fruits and damages (Q8, Q9) need dates and amounts.** Who built what and when; which rooms were rented; when the demand was made. Exemplary damages need a compensatory or temperate award first (Art. 2234).

| **Q3 The sale outside it** | **Art. 1898 "exceeding the scope … aware of the limits"; Art. 1900 "as written"** | Answer ¶¶40, 43, 44(h); Def. Exh. 5 | **Salvador, Princess, Gloria (theirs); Patricia on consultation; de los Reyes on use** |
| Q4 Void, not unenforceable | Art. 1898; Art. 1409(7) "cannot be ratified"; Art. 1910 | Exh. E; Exh. F; the Deed of Confirmation | Patricia (rebuttal): no ratification. Salvador: payments never reached an owner. |

The weight sits on Q3 and Q6. Q3 is carried by their witnesses in January. Q6 is carried by the King records, put to Princess and Jomil in January and offered on rebuttal. Our November witnesses carry Q6, Q8 and Q9 only, and lightly. That is why they are kept to one thing each.

#### 2. Their witnesses (January): where the case is decided

##### Salvador Osum dela Fuente: decides Q3

| What he swears | S19: his father sent him "maningil at mangolekta ng mga bayad sa upa at ng mga partial payment para sa bilihan ng lupa." S16: Cesar dealt "sa mga nagrerenta." S14 to S15: around 1992, at Ben Llamanzares's house, they learned the Balanes had agreed to buy "ang lupain na kanilang nirerentahan" and "nakapag-down payment na po sila." | In her form | Considering that the defendants' witness testified that he collected "bayad sa upa" and partial payments for his father, and that the receipts he identified (Exh. 5) were issued by the agent's side and none by Ben Llamanzares, the defendant Gloria H. Balane is not shown to be among the persons described in paragraph 7 of the Special Power of Attorney (Exh. E); the sale of 29 September 2016 exceeded the agent's written authority (Art. 1898). |

Then the receipts: ₱602,750 receipted against a ₱250,000 price, and ₱219,000 after a deed that says "in hand paid." |

##### Princess Balane Torralba: Q3, Q6, Q7

Answer p. 9: she will testify to "the absence of any notice of revocation or dispute." |
|---|---|
| Feeds | **Art. 1898** (renter first, buyer later, undated). **Art. 496**, "by agreement between the parties": the survey she paid for is dated 8 September 2020, more than three years after Cesar's death in June 2017 [VERIFY: PSA death certificate]. **Art. 528**, "from the moment facts exist": blotter entry 2763, 19 May 2020. |
| In her form | Considering that the defendant Princess B. Torralba stated on 19 May 2020, as entered in the blotter of Barangay San Roque (entry No. 2763), that there was a pending question on the land ("May usapin pa yan"), and that she paid for the subdivision survey conducted on 8 September 2020, the defendants' possession was not free of notice of a dispute from that date (Art. 528), and the segregation of the portion was not made by agreement of the co-owners (Art. 496). |
| The risk | The blotter entry is the Kings' report of her words ("diumano"). As an official entry it proves the report was made; for the truth of the words, ask her on cross first. If she admits it, the entry is not needed. [VERIFY: certified copy of page 43] |

| What he will say | Answer p. 9: his residence "whenever he is ashore from sea service," the family's occupancy, "the absence of any notice of dispute prior to the filing of the case," and "the limited and intermittent nature of any room rentals." |

Torralba" (6 Jan 2021). Civil Case 8563: his and Juntilla's lawyer received the Bicol Post notices on 9 Jul 2021. |
| Feeds | **Art. 528** and **PD 1529 §51** (good faith must last to the operative act of 23 Nov 2021). **Art. 549**, fruits: his "room rentals" and Answer ¶27. |

**Document:** Witness pack: the three neighbours (De Leon, King, de los Reyes): one thing each, lines to withdraw, cross, documents for counsel, the jurat commission question

---

### Witness pack: the three neighbours

Shirley De Leon (Exh. N) · Donata King (Exh. L) · Francia de los Reyes (Exh. M) 

Civil Case No. 26-360, Zschoche v. Balane, MTC Mercedes · prepared 19 October 2026 for Atty. Barandon · internal draft; nothing filed or sent; counsel decides. 

Tags: [VERIFY] = not yet checked against a document · [ASK HER] = only the witness can say; counsel asks, nobody else · [COUNSEL] = counsel's call.

> 

**If you read one page, read this.**

1. **The notarial stamp on all three affidavits (and on Jonathan's) reads "valid until 12/31/2025." The jurats are dated 23 January 2026.** Either the 2026 commission exists and the old stamp was used, or it does not. Please tell us the 2026 commission number so the file can be corrected. If there was no commission on 23 January, the cure is simple and must be done before the first neighbour testifies: each witness re-swears the same affidavit before a currently commissioned notary (Atty. Andrade's commission runs to 31 December 2026), and it is filed and served at least five days before her hearing. Details in Part 1.

2. **Mrs. King's cases are our proof of notice before registration, not baggage.** Two facts come out of the 2020 to 2025 cases between Mrs. King and the Balane side. First, no one in any of them ever decided ownership: the prosecutor wrote that his office "cannot pass upon the issue of ownership" and told the parties "to file appropriate civil action," and the Daet trial court and the Court of Appeals both recite that ruling. Second, the Balane side was served with the Bicol Post notice of revocation inside those cases before registration: Mrs. King's Verified Answer in Civil Case 8563, with the three Bicol Post issues annexed, was stamped received by the law office of Jomil Torralba and Nelly Juntilla on 9 July 2021, 137 days before Gloria's title issued on 23 November 2021. Nelly Juntilla is Gloria's attorney-in-fact under the 28 May 2020 power their own Answer attaches; Jomil is a defendant here. Part 3 sets out how this comes in.

3. **Who testifies on 4 November is not yet settled: Mrs. King or Shirley De Leon.** Jonathan's choice on 15 October was King first, pending your word. Either works. Our leaning is De Leon on 4 November and King on 11 November, for one practical reason: the extra week lets counsel hold the certified copies of the King case records before Atty. Ramos crosses her, so that if he opens those cases, re-direct is ready. Please confirm the order, and the two women will be told the exact date.

4. **Each neighbour proves one thing well.** De Leon: what Gloria Balane did in 2020. King: who has occupied the house since 1990, and, through her own cases, that the Balane side knew in 2020 and 2021 that their ownership was contested and that Cesar's authority had been revoked. De los Reyes: the fish-drying use and continuous residence since 1979. None of them can speak to the 1992 power, the 2005 revocation itself, the 2016 deed or consultation. Those stay with the documents and with Patricia on rebuttal.

5. **Four lines in the affidavits as filed will draw a motion to strike.** Withdraw them on direct rather than defend them: King Q9 "kasama na ang mga defendants"; De Leon Q16 "ginugulo"; de los Reyes Q2 to Q3 "maimpluwensiya" and Q9 "malamang na sinamantala … koneksyon at impluwensiya." Part 2 to Part 4 give the exact words.

6. **No supplemental affidavit for any neighbour.** The rule allows a late submission once. That one allowance is reserved for Patricia. What the neighbours can add is small and can be drawn out on direct within the questions already sworn, or left out.

7. **Documents to carry for the three of them fit in one folder**: the filed affidavits; each witness's original PhilID; Exhibit B and Exhibit C (the two titles, for location questions); and, for your own hand, the papers listed in each witness's section. The most important are the certified copies of the King case records (Part 3.5). They are needed whether they come in on re-direct, if Atty. Ramos opens the cases, or on rebuttal, when Princess and Jomil swear there was no dispute before the 2025 demand.

**Section:** Part 1 · Before any neighbour testifies

---

#### Part 1 · Before any neighbour testifies

##### 1.1 The notarial commission on the jurats

**What the papers show.** The affiant's jurat on Exhibits K, L, M and N was taken on 23 January 2026 at Daet by Atty. Bonifacio T. Barandon, Jr. as notary public. The stamp under each jurat reads: "Admin Matter No. 2023-424 valid until 12/31/2025 · Roll of Attorneys No. 40309 · IBP O.R. No. 511950, 01/06/2025 (CY 2025) · PTR No. 1337940, 01/02/2025." The lawyer's attestation on each affidavit was sworn the same day before Atty. Darren J. Andrade, whose stamp reads "Commission Serial No. 2025-3, until December 31, 2026." The verification of the Complaint was notarized in Quezon City by a different notary (NP-160, 2025-2026). So the only question is the affiants' jurats.

**Why it matters.** The Judicial Affidavit Rule requires a jurat by a notary public (Section 3). If no commission covered 23 January 2026, Atty. Ramos can move to strike Exhibits L, M and N as unsworn before each neighbour takes the stand, and the witness would then have no direct testimony on file. For Exhibit K, cross has already been taken, so the objection is most likely waived. [COUNSEL]

**What we ask.** One sentence from counsel: the number and dates of the notarial commission in force on 23 January 2026. If it exists, the file is corrected and nothing else is needed; the court looks to the commission, not to the stamp. [VERIFY: the 2004 Notarial Rules text was not fetched; the two-year commission period and the effect of a lapsed commission are stated from general knowledge only]

**If there was no commission that day.**

- Each neighbour re-swears the **same affidavit, same text, same exhibit letter**, before Atty. Andrade or another notary with a current commission. Nothing is added. It is filed and served as the re-sworn copy of the affidavit already on file.

- Timing: at least five days before the witness's hearing. For 4 November that is **Friday 30 October**; for 11 November, **Friday 6 November**; for 2 December, **Friday 27 November**. Better: all three on the same day in the last week of October, so the question never arises again.

- Expect Atty. Ramos to call the re-sworn copy a late affidavit. The answer is that it is the same affidavit, served with the Complaint in February, with its oath regularized; and in any event the witness swears to it again on the stand. [COUNSEL]

- Fallback if nothing is done: when the witness is called, she affirms the affidavit under oath in open court before cross begins. Many courts accept this; this court's own view is not known. [COUNSEL]

**A second point on the jurats, low risk.** The examining lawyer also took the oath of his own witnesses. That is common practice and we do not expect it to be raised; it is noted here only so that it is not a surprise. [VERIFY rule text; not fetched]

##### 1.2 The order of witnesses

| Date | One plan | The other plan | Decision |
|---|---|---|---|
| Wed 4 Nov, 1:30 PM | Shirley De Leon (Exh. N) | Donata King (Exh. L) | Jonathan chose King first on 15 October, pending counsel. Our leaning is De Leon first: her day needs no outside papers, and it gives counsel the extra week to hold the certified King case records (Part 3.5) before King is crossed. [COUNSEL] |
| Wed 11 Nov, 1:30 PM | Donata King | Shirley De Leon | |
| Wed 2 Dec, 1:30 PM | Francia de los Reyes (Exh. M), or no third neighbour (see Part 4) | Decide by 20 November. | |
| Wed 9 Dec | Formal offer of plaintiff's exhibits | Separate note. | |

The 4 September 2026 order calls these settings "final and intransferable." Under the Judicial Affidavit Rule the court does not consider the affidavit of a witness who does not appear (Section 10). [VERIFY exact wording of §10(b)] Attendance is therefore the first task: each woman is told the date and the time, 1:30 PM at MTC Mercedes, and is brought to court. Nobody discusses her answers with her except counsel.

##### 1.3 What is common to all three

- **Language.** The affidavits are in Filipino, which the Rule allows (Section 3: a judicial affidavit is prepared in the language known to the witness, with a translation only if that language is neither English nor Filipino). No translation is required. If the court wants an English copy for the record, counsel can supply one later; it is not a reason to delay.

- **The purposes paragraph.** Each affidavit's "Purposes" is generic ("to prove the material allegations"). On direct, state the one purpose that matters for that witness in a sentence (Part 2 to Part 4 give it). Under the Rules, the purpose for which a witness's testimony is offered is stated when she is called. [COUNSEL]

- **Personal knowledge only.** Each neighbour says what she saw, heard said to her, or did, with a year. Nothing about what the defendants knew or intended, nothing learned from Jonathan, nothing about the owners' dealings with Cesar.

- **No adjectives.** No "land grabber," "fake," "illegal," "influential." The defendants are described by what they did and when.

- **Nothing outside the record through a neighbour.** The exhibits were pre-marked at pre-trial. A paper not marked is not offered through a neighbour on direct. If cross opens a door, counsel may use re-direct with the court's leave; the paper is in counsel's folder, not the witness's hands.

- **One folder for the three days:** Exhibits L, M, N as filed; the originals of each PhilID (King's is marked Exh. Q, de los Reyes's Exh. P, De Leon's is not marked: her number is in her jurat); Exhibit B (the heirs' title) and Exhibit C (the Balane title, with its technical description on page 2) for any question about where a house stands.


**Document:** Witness pack: the three neighbours (De Leon, King, de los Reyes): one thing each, lines to withdraw, cross, documents for counsel, the jurat commission question  
**Source:** `WITNESS_PACK_NEIGHBOURS_2026-10-19.html`  
**Section:** Part 2 · Shirley De Guzman De Leon (Exhibit N)

---

#### Part 2 · Shirley De Guzman De Leon (Exhibit N)

> 

**Who she is.** 58, single, Purok 4, Brgy. San Roque, Mercedes. Over fifty years on a lot next to the Balane lot. Her father bought the lot they live on. PhilID [ID no. withheld] (original to be brought; not marked as an exhibit).

**Her one thing.** **What Gloria Balane did in 2020**: came to the De Leon house, said she had bought the land "mula sa mga may-ari," told them to leave, offered to pay them to leave, and later that year her people nailed wood and roofing sheets across the De Leon door, which the family had removed. That is first-hand, dated, and it is theirs to answer.

**Why it matters.** It is the only sworn account from our side of the defendants acting on the land as owners in 2020, the year before registration, and the year they themselves were giving Jomil Torralba a power "to defend the property" from Toronto (their Answer, Annex 1, 28 May 2020). It goes to possession, to the demand for rents and damages, and to the good-faith question up to 23 November 2021.

##### 2.1 What she proves first-hand (keep to these)

| Q | Her words (Exh. N) | What it proves |
|---|---|---|
| 5 | The Balanes live on land "katabi lang ng lupa kung saan ako naninirahan, at dating bahagi din ang lupa na ito ng orihinal na titulo ni Mary Worrick Keesey." | Adjacency; she is a next-door witness, not a visitor. |
| 7 | Princess Torralba's family lives there now; Efren and Gloria also lived there but, as far as she knows, are abroad. | Who occupies the house today. |
| 10 to 11 | "Noong taong 2020 po ay nagtungo sa bahay namin si Gloria Balane … Pinapaalis niya kami sa lupa … dahil nabili na umano niya ito mula sa mga may-ari." | Gloria's own words in 2020: she claimed to have bought from "the owners." She knew the land had owners other than Cesar. |
| 12 | "Hindi po ako pumayag dahil ang tatay ko na ang naunang nakabili ng lupa na tinitirhan namin mahigit limampung (50) taon na ang nakalipas." | Her family's own basis for being there. See 2.3 before this line is used. |
| 13 | Gloria offered to pay them to leave; they refused. | An offer to buy out a neighbour on land she claims to own. |
| 14 to 15 | "pinasok ng mga tauhan ni Gloria Balane ang lupa namin at binarikadahan nila ang pinto ng bahay namin sa pamamagitan ng pagpapako ng kahoy at yero" … "Agad po namin itong pinabaklas." | Self-help on the land in 2020 by people acting for Gloria. Matches Mrs. King's own sworn account of 27 May 2020 (wood and roofing-sheet fences, men saying they acted for Gloria's sister). Corroboration, not the same incident. [VERIFY: same month?] |

##### 2.2 Lines to withdraw or fix on direct

- **Q16**: "may ibang mga kapitbahay din namin ang **ginugulo** ng mga Balane dahil nabili na din umano ni Gloria ang iba pang mga bahagi." Hearsay about other households and a characterization. Withdraw it. If counsel wants the point, it comes through the other neighbours themselves.

- **Q9**: "alam na alam po ng mga taga-doon na si Mary Worrick Keesey ang matagal nang nagmamay-ari." Reputation in the community about ownership. Keep it short; do not build on it. The ownership is proved by Exhibit B, not by her.

- **Purposes (b)**: "primitive owner since time immemorial." Overstated; the title chain begins with a 1934 original certificate. Say "the registered owner" instead.

- **Q8**: who owns the land. She may say what she knows; the title does the rest. Do not ask her to explain the title.

##### 2.3 The line Atty. Ramos will use: "ang tatay ko na ang naunang nakabili"

Her father "bought" the lot they live on more than fifty years ago. Atty. Ramos will turn this into: sales to occupants were routine on this land, so Gloria's purchase was routine too. The answer is in **who sold it to him and when**, and that is the one fact that must be known before she takes the stand. [ASK HER, through counsel, by 28 October]

**What the records show.** The Registry's memorandum on TCT T-32916 (the title for Lot 2-X-4) carries two entries for a buyer named **Antonio de Leon**: a Deed of Sale of a Portion of a Titled Property presented on 1 February 1983 (93 square meters for ₱1,470, Doc. No. 264, Page 85, Book I, Series of 1982, Notary Archimedes Yanto) and an Affidavit of Confirmation by Cesar de la Fuente presented on 6 February 1998 identifying the lot sold as **Lot 2-X-4-D**, 98 square meters, of plan Psd-05-017527 (Doc. No. 504, Page 2, Book II, Series of 1996, Notary Ernesto B. de Jesus). The family's title tree lists Antonio de Leon under **TCT T-46595**. The Assessor's sketch places Lot 2-X-4-D in the block along the Daet-Mercedes road near Pescador Street, which is in the Lot 2-X-4 block, not in the Lot 2-X-6 block where the Balane lot (Lot 2-X-6-1-4-C, off Delos Reyes Boulevard) sits. [VERIFY: whether Antonio de Leon is her father, and whether Lot 2-X-4-D is the house she lives in; the two blocks may or may not touch at her house]

**Why either answer helps.**

- **If her father is Antonio de Leon and bought in 1982**: this is the class of buyer the 1992 power describes in paragraph 7, a Llamanzares-era purchaser, with a registered deed, later confirmed by Cesar in 1998, and a title. It shows what a proper sale on this land looked like: a deed on the title within months, confirmation, a title in the buyer's name. Gloria has none of that for 2016. The 1998 confirmation by Cesar is before the 2005 revocation and within the class, so it does not help the defence.

- **If her father bought from Llamanzares or Violeta earlier and never registered**: she is one of the very people paragraph 7 protected, and the family may hold a contract or receipts from that era. Those papers would be worth more than her testimony. Ask whether anything is in the house.

- **If he bought from someone else entirely**: she stays a neighbour witness to 2020 and nothing more, and the line is left exactly as sworn ("my father bought it"), with no follow-up.

**A second De Leon on the record.** The 18 May 2015 sale of the Ferrer house to the Kings places that house "sa pagitan nina Gng. Nieves de Leon at Gng. Jocelyn Estayo, sa harapan nito ay Mercedes to Daet Road" (Civil Case 8563 record, King's Exh. A). The Kings' house stood on the parcel Gloria bought. So a Nieves de Leon lived next to the parcel's road frontage in 2015. If Nieves is Shirley's mother or relative, Shirley's "katabi" lot is that one, and Antonio de Leon's titled Lot 2-X-4-D may be a different family lot. [ASK HER: who is Nieves de Leon; which lot is her house on]

On the stand the honest answer is the short one: "Binili ng tatay ko kay ____ noong ____." Nothing is added about the owners, the agent or paragraph 7; counsel argues that in the memorandum.

##### 2.4 Cross she will face, and the honest answer

| Atty. Ramos | Her honest answer |
|---|---|
| "Gloria Balane was in Canada in 2020. She could not have come to your house." | Say the month, if she can. Gloria signed a power before the Philippine Consulate in Toronto on 28 May 2020 (their Annex 1), so a visit in the first months of 2020, or after a return later that year, is consistent with the record. [ASK HER: which month; who was with Gloria; what words she used] |
| "You did not report the barricade to the barangay or the police." | Whatever is true. If there was a blotter entry, say so. [ASK HER] If none: "Pinabaklas namin agad." That is enough. |
| "Your lot is not the lot in this case. You have no interest in this case." | "Tama po. Katabi po ang lupa ko. Nagsasalaysay ako sa nakita ko." Her lack of interest is her strength; concede it with a smile. |
| "Your father bought land here. So people bought from the owners' people all the time." | "Binili ng tatay ko kay ____ noong ____." Stop. (See 2.3.) |
| "You know the owners are the Keeseys only because people say so." | "Opo, iyon ang alam ng mga taga-roon. At ang titulo ang magsasabi." Then stop. |
| "Jonathan asked you to testify. He showed you documents." | Whatever is true. "Hiniling niya po na sabihin ko ang nakita ko. Ang sinasabi ko ay ang nakita ko noong 2020." |
| "The people who nailed the door: you did not see Gloria send them." | Say only what she saw and heard: who they were, what they said, whose name they used. If they said they came for Gloria, say that. If not, say she does not know who sent them. |
| "Your affidavit was prepared by plaintiff's lawyer in his office." | "Opo, sa Filipino, na naiintindihan ko. Binasa ko bago ko pinirmahan." |

##### 2.5 Documents for counsel to prepare or carry (De Leon day)

| # | Document | Use | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Exhibit N as filed (and, if the commission question requires it, the re-sworn copy filed five days before) | Her direct testimony | On file. Re-swearing: see 1.1 |
| 2 | Her PhilID, original | Identity; matches the number in the jurat | She brings it |
| 3 | Exhibit C, page 2 (technical description of the Balane lot) and Exhibit B | If asked where her house stands relative to the Balane house | In the record |
| 4 | Her father's paper: deed, tax declaration, or title (T-46595 if it is his); or any Llamanzares-era contract or receipts in the house | Counsel's hand only, to know the true answer to 2.3; offered only on re-direct with leave, if cross makes the father's purchase an issue | [ASK HER through counsel by 28 Oct]. A certified copy of the T-32916 memorandum page carrying the 1983 and 1998 entries can be obtained quietly from the Registry if needed |
| 5 | Their Answer, Annex 1: Gloria's Special Power of Attorney signed in Toronto, 28 May 2020 | Counsel's hand only: fixes where Gloria was at the end of May 2020, if cross says she was never in Mercedes in 2020 | In the record (defendants' annex) |
| 6 | Barangay San Roque blotter entry for the 2020 barricade, if one exists; any dated photograph of the boarded door | Counsel's hand only; re-direct if the incident is denied | [ASK HER]; certified copy from the barangay if it exists |
| 7 | Mrs. King's sworn account of 27 May 2020 (wood and roofing-sheet fences, men acting for Gloria's sister), in the Civil Case 8563 record | Not for De Leon's day. Noted so that counsel sees the two accounts fit; it comes in later as a certified record if at all | In our file (dossier Annex N) |

**Don't-says for De Leon:** nothing about other neighbours' troubles (Q16); nothing about what the Balanes knew; no "land grabber" or "harassment"; nothing learned from Jonathan; no guess at who sent the men if they did not say.

**Section:** Part 3 · Donata Mabeza King (Exhibit L)

---

#### Part 3 · Donata Mabeza King (Exhibit L)

> 

**Who she is.** 61, widow of Pepe R. King (former barangay captain and kagawad), Road 2, Purok 4, Brgy. San Roque. Built her house there in 1990 and has lived there since. Kumare of Efren and Gloria (wedding sponsors). PhilID marked Exhibit Q.

**On direct, her one thing.** **Who has occupied the Balane house since 1990, year by year**: Efren and Gloria, now Princess and Jomil. She is the long-standing neighbour who has watched the house for thirty-six years.

**Her real weight: the cases between her and the Balane side.** They prove two things the defence denies. **(1) Ownership was never decided.** Every forum that touched the 2020 dispute said so and sent the parties to a civil court; no judge or prosecutor ever found Gloria the owner. **(2) The Balane side had the revocation in hand before registration.** Her Verified Answer in Civil Case 8563, with the Bicol Post notices annexed, was stamped received by their lawyer's office on 9 July 2021. Gloria's title issued on 23 November 2021. Their Answer here says Princess and Jomil will testify to "the absence of any notice of revocation or dispute prior to the later demand letter and complaint." These records answer that sworn claim.

**What she is not for.** Anything before 1990; the Llamanzares contracts; consultation; the 2016 deed.

##### 3.0 What her cases prove, and how it comes in

| When | What the record says | What it proves for us |
|---|---|---|
| 11 Feb 2020 | A letter "from their counsel" tells the Kings, through Pepe King, not to build "as the owner now needs the lot for her own use." (Prosecutor's resolution of 16 Sep 2020, pp. 64 to 65; the letter is in Juntilla's counter-affidavit in the prosecutor's file, not in ours.) | The Balane side asserted ownership against other occupants through a lawyer, 21 months before registration. |
| 11 May 2020 | "on May 11, 2020 they had a confrontation before the Punong barangay who advised them to desist from making construction." (Same resolution, p. 65.) | A dispute over this land was before the barangay. |
| 19 May 2020 | Barangay blotter entry 2763 (page 43, certified 20 May 2020): Princess came with a policeman and four tanod to stop the Kings' work "at sinabi diumano ni konsehal Toralba na 'May usapin pa yan'." | Princess herself, in a barangay record, said there was an issue on the land. Her affidavit here says she never met any opposition (S11). |
| 28 May 2020 | Gloria, in Toronto, appoints Nelly Juntilla "To institute or file action regarding property in Purok 4, Barangay San Roque … and defend me from any action." (Their Answer, Annex 1.) | Juntilla acts for Gloria on this land from that date. Their own Answer is verified by Juntilla as Gloria's attorney-in-fact under this same power. |
| 28 Jul 2020 | Provincial Prosecutor, joint resolution on Juntilla's robbery complaint and Mrs. King's grave coercion complaint: "this Office cannot pass upon the issue of ownership because it is only the court in a proper proceeding which can declare which party has a better right over the property … Parties hereto are advised to file appropriate civil action to establish who between them has the right over the property." | **Ownership not decided.** The parties, Juntilla for Gloria included, are told in writing that ownership is contested and belongs in a civil court. Sixteen months before registration. In the same resolution Jomil's own answer is recorded: he was "merely acting on instructions of the owner of the property in the person of Gloria Balane thru Nelly Juntilla." |
| 16 Sep 2020 | Same office, on reconsideration: the parties are "advised to clear for themselves, who has a better right of possession, if not ownership, of the property in court proceedings." [VERIFY wording on the certified copy] | Same, repeated. Copy-furnished to "Complainant and Respondents" by registry receipts 1666 to 1670 and 1694, all stamped 23 September 2020: the ruling was mailed to Jomil and Juntilla. |
| 25 Sep, 2 Oct, 9 Oct 2020 | The Bicol Post publishes the notice of revocation (our Exh. G, G-1; their Answer ¶18 admits the publication). | Constructive notice from October 2020. |
| 27 Nov 2020 to 6 Jan 2021 | Crim. Case 20-6030: warrants issue against Juntilla, Jomil Torralba and three guards; on 6 January 2021 the case is remanded to the prosecutor as prematurely filed and the warrants are recalled. Nothing is said about ownership. | Jomil, a defendant here, was himself in court over this land in 2020. "No dispute before 2025" cannot stand. |
| 9 Jul 2021 | Civil Case 8563 (RTC Br. 41; Juntilla and Jomil v. King, damages): King's Verified Answer ¶2: "The Deed of Absolute Sale did not ipso facto vested ownership to Gloria Balane, especially in the absence of authority of Cesar Dela Fuente who was stripped of the same, way back in 2005. Copies of newspaper containing revocation of SPA of Dela Fuente is attached." Annexes 1 to 3-c are the three Bicol Post issues. Stamped "RECEIVED … AQUINO Law Office … 09 JUL 2021." Her judicial affidavit of 8 July 2021 in the same case marks the Bicol Post issues as exhibits. | **Actual notice to the Balane side's lawyer of the revocation and of the publication, 137 days before Gloria's title issued.** Atty. Raymundo Aquino appeared for Juntilla and Jomil in the criminal cases and in the perjury case. |
| 17 May 2023 to 4 Dec 2025 | Perjury case brought by Jomil and Juntilla against King: convicted by MTC Daet (17 May 2023), acquitted by RTC Br. 41 (25 Sep 2023), certiorari dismissed by the Court of Appeals (17 Jul 2025), reconsideration denied (4 Dec 2025). The MTC decision and the CA decision both recite the prosecutor's ruling that ownership "must first be resolve[d] in an action with the proper court." | Two courts repeat that ownership was left undecided. Her acquittal answers the perjury attack on her credibility. |
| 23 Nov 2021 | Gloria's TCT 079-2021002126 issues. | Good faith had to last until this date. It did not. |

**How it comes in.** None of these records were pre-marked at pre-trial, and Mrs. King's affidavit does not mention them. So they are not offered through her on direct. There are two doors, and the defence opens both.

- **On cross of King.** If Atty. Ramos puts the perjury case or the criminal cases to her to attack her credibility, re-direct may follow the subject he opened: what was decided about ownership ("Wala po. Sabi ng piskal, sa korte dapat pagpasyahan"), and what she attached to her 2021 Answer and to whom it was served. Counsel holds the certified copies to show her. [COUNSEL]

- **On rebuttal.** Their Answer lists Princess for "the absence of any notice of revocation or dispute prior to the later demand letter and complaint" and Jomil for "the absence of any notice of dispute prior to the filing of the case." Once they swear to that, the certified records answer it directly: the prosecutor's resolutions, the 20-6030 orders, and the Civil Case 8563 Answer with its 9 July 2021 receipt stamp. Court records come in by certified copy. Jomil can also be confronted with them on cross. [COUNSEL]

**The hinge is one point of law.** Whether notice to Juntilla, Gloria's attorney-in-fact for actions over this property, or to her lawyer, counts as notice to Gloria. That needs counsel's authority. [NEEDS COUNSEL VERIFICATION] Without it, the records still prove that the Balane household knew the ownership was contested and that the revocation had been published and served, which defeats "no notice of dispute" from Princess and Jomil.

**Answer to their best use of the same records.** The prosecutor's resolutions also describe Juntilla's acts as "a form of an exercise of a lawful right" by "the landowner." Atty. Ramos may read that line. The answer is in the same resolution: the office said it "cannot pass upon the issue of ownership," and its "landowner" is the claim each side made, not a finding. No forum has ever found Gloria the owner.

**One caution.** The 9 October 2020 Bicol Post page annexed to the 2021 Answer (Annex 3-c) also carries an unrelated notice that we do not want in this case. For the text of our notice, use our own Exhibit G-1; use the 2021 Answer for the receipt stamp and the list of annexes. Counsel decides before any certified copy of that page is offered.

##### 3.1 What she proves first-hand (keep to these)

| Q | Her words (Exh. L) | What it proves |
|---|---|---|
| 2 | Knows Efren and Gloria as "kumpare at kumare ko po sila sa kasal"; their house is near hers; Gloria a former Municipal Councilor; Princess a Municipal Kagawad; Erwin the Municipal Engineer. | Closeness to the defendants, which cuts against bias. Public offices are facts of record. |
| 4 to 5 | The land is near her house in San Roque, "kung saan nakatayo ang bahay nina Efren at Gloria ngunit sa ngayon sina Jomil at Princess na ang nakatira." | Occupation she has seen. |
| 7 | The improvement: the Balane house, now lived in by Princess and Jomil. | What stands on the lot. |
| 8 | She and her husband built their house at Road 2, Purok 4 "noon pang taong 1990 at doon na kami tumira hanggang sa ngayon." | Her vantage point begins in 1990. |

##### 3.2 Lines to withdraw or fix on direct

- **Q9**: "Alam na alam ko po at halos lahat na tao sa nasabing lugar **kasama na ang mga defendants** na ang totoong may-ari … ay ang mga plaintiffs." What the defendants know is their state of mind; she cannot testify to it. Withdraw the words "kasama na ang mga defendants." Leave the rest short.

- **Q9**: "Mary **Kessey Worrick**." The name is misordered. Correct it orally: Mary Worrick Keesey.

- **Q2**: "Sa pagkakaalam ko sina Efren at Gloria ay naninirahan na sa Canada." Belief, not knowledge. Harmless; do not build on it.

##### 3.3 What on direct can be drawn out within the sworn questions [COUNSEL]

Without a new affidavit, the court may allow short clarifying questions on matters already in the affidavit. Two are worth it, if she can answer from her own eyes: (a) since 1990, who lived in the house year by year; (b) whether she ever saw the house empty or rebuilt, and in what year. Nothing about rent, Cesar's collectors, or what the Balanes told her, unless she heard it herself and counsel has heard her answer first. [ASK HER]

##### 3.4 Cross she will face, and the honest answer

| Atty. Ramos | Her honest answer |
|---|---|
| "You were charged with perjury over this same land." | "I was charged. The case ended in my favour." (RTC Branch 41 acquitted her on 25 September 2023; the Court of Appeals dismissed the other side's petition on 17 July 2025 and denied reconsideration on 4 December 2025.) [VERIFY: certified copies not yet in hand] Then stop. This opens re-direct (3.0). |
| "You filed a criminal case before this very court against Jomil Torralba and Nelly Juntilla, and it was thrown out." | "I filed a complaint. It was sent back to the prosecutor." Nothing about this judge's orders or the bonds. Stop. This also opens re-direct. |
| "The prosecutor himself called Gloria the landowner and said they had a lawful right to stop your construction." | "Sinabi rin po ng piskal na hindi niya mapagpapasyahan kung sino ang may-ari, at sa korte dapat dalhin." Then stop. Counsel has the resolution to show the line. |
| "You bought land rights there yourself from Ruby Ferrer in 2015, not from the owners." | Depends on a fact counsel must know first: is the Ferrer house on the Keesey land? If not: "Ibang lote po iyon." If yes: "Bumili ako ng bahay at karapatan sa pag-okupa. Hindi ko inaangkin ang titulo." [ASK HER; VERIFY against the 18 May 2015 kasunduan and the plan] |
| "You moved there in 1990. You know nothing of the 1970s or 1980s." | "Tama po. Mula 1990 ang nakita ko." |
| "You cannot know what the defendants knew." | "Hindi ko po alam ang nasa isip nila. Ang alam ko ay ang nakita at narinig ko." Concede and stop. |
| "You are kumare of the Balanes and now you testify against them." | "Kumare ko po sila. Sinasabi ko lang ang nakita ko." |
| "In July 2021 you yourself pleaded that Cesar's authority was revoked. You got that from Jonathan." | "Nakita ko po sa Bicol Post. Inilakip ko sa sagot ko, at natanggap iyon ng abogado nila noong Hulyo 2021." Then stop. This is the door to the receipt stamp; counsel shows her the certified copy on re-direct. |

##### 3.4a Re-direct, only if cross opened the cases

1. "Sa mga kasong iyon, may nagpasya ba kung sino ang may-ari ng lupa?" → "Wala po. Sabi ng piskal, hindi niya iyon mapagpapasyahan at sa korte dapat dalhin." (Show the 28 Jul 2020 resolution.)

2. "Ano ang inilakip mo sa iyong Sagot sa Civil Case 8563?" → "Ang tatlong labas ng Bicol Post tungkol sa pagbawi ng kapangyarihan ni Cesar." (Show the certified Answer, Annexes 1 to 3-c.)

3. "Kanino ipinadala ang Sagot na iyon, at kailan?" → "Sa abogado nila Jomil at Nelly, si Atty. Aquino, noong Hulyo 2021." (Show the receipt stamp of 9 July 2021.)

Each answer must be hers, from her own knowledge; counsel confirms each with her before the hearing. [ASK HER]

##### 3.5 Documents for counsel to prepare or carry (King day)

| # | Document | Use | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Exhibit L as filed (and the re-sworn copy if 1.1 requires it) | Her direct testimony | On file |
| 2 | Exhibit Q: her PhilID, original | Identity and residence | She brings it |
| 3 | Exhibit B and Exhibit C, page 2 | Location questions | In the record |
| 4 | **Civil Case 8563, certified copies:** the complaint of Juntilla and Jomil (to show their counsel and whether they pleaded Gloria's deed), and King's Verified Answer with Annexes 1 to 3-c and the "RECEIVED … AQUINO Law Office … 09 JUL 2021" stamp | **The notice before registration.** Re-direct if cross opens the cases; otherwise rebuttal against Princess and Jomil | Our copy of the Answer is in hand; the complaint is not. Request both from the RTC Br. 41 clerk **by 21 Oct** |
| 5 | **Provincial Prosecutor, certified copies:** joint resolution of 28 Jul 2020 and resolution on reconsideration of 16 Sep 2020 (NPS-V-09-INV-2020F-00383 and 2020G-00417), with Juntilla's counter-affidavit and the 11 Feb 2020 lawyer's letter attached to it | **Ownership never decided**; the Balane side told in writing to go to a civil court; who Gloria's lawyer was in February 2020 | Copies in our file come from the CA petition annexes. Request certified copies from the Provincial Prosecution Office **by 28 Oct**, with Jomil's and Juntilla's counter-affidavits and the registry return cards |
| 5c | Barangay San Roque blotter: page 43, entry 2763 (19 to 20 May 2020, Princess, "May usapin pa yan"), entry 2789 (27 May 2020), and the record of the 11 May 2020 confrontation | Cross of Princess against her S11; rebuttal | Certifications in our file (8563 annexes); certified copies from the barangay **by 28 Oct** |
| 5a | Crim. Case 20-6030, MTC Mercedes: warrant order of 27 Nov 2020 and order of 6 Jan 2021 | Jomil in court over this land in 2020; rebuttal against "no dispute" | Copies in our file; certified copies from this court's clerk **by 28 Oct** |
| 5b | The perjury case: RTC Branch 41 judgment acquitting her (25 Sep 2023), the MTC Daet decision (17 May 2023, which recites the prosecutor's ownership ruling), and the two Court of Appeals resolutions (17 Jul 2025, 4 Dec 2025) | Re-direct if the perjury charge is put to her; the recital that ownership was left to a civil court | [VERIFY] Obtain from the branch clerks and the CA **by 28 Oct** |
| 6 | The 18 May 2015 kasunduan with Ruby Ferrer, and the location of that house against the plan | Counsel's hand only: to know the true answer to the Ferrer question before it is asked | [ASK HER; VERIFY] |
| 7 | Barangay San Roque blotter certifications of 20 May and 27 May 2020 (annexed to her own 2020 complaint-affidavit) | Counsel's hand only; re-direct if she is asked whether she reported the May 2020 incidents | Copies in our file; certified copies from the barangay |

**Don't-says for King:** nothing about anyone's intentions or character; no "fake," "illegal," "land grabber"; no count of families on the land; nothing about what the owners or Cesar knew; no description of this court's orders in Criminal Case 20-6030 beyond "it was sent back to the prosecutor"; nothing about the ₱180,000 bonds; no claim that anyone on the Balane side read the Bicol Post, only that her Answer with the pages attached was served on their lawyer.

**Section:** Part 4 · Francia Abad de los Reyes (Exhibit M)

---

#### Part 4 · Francia Abad de los Reyes (Exhibit M)

> 

**Who she is.** 46, single, Purok 3, Brgy. San Roque; born there on 5 June 1979 and has lived there since. PhilID marked Exhibit P. She is the "concerned neighbour and friend" who telephoned Mrs. King on 27 May 2020, and was Mrs. King's co-defendant in Civil Case 8563.

**Her one thing.** **Use, not ownership**: "dati rati na nagtutuyo ng mga isda ang mag-asawang Efren at Gloria sa nasabing lupa" (Q4), and continuous residence since 1979 from which she can say when the Balane house went up, if she remembers. [ASK HER]

**The decision.** Her affidavit as filed carries the most damaging lines of the three. Either they are withdrawn on direct and she is kept to Q4 and Q5, or she is not called. A listed witness need not be called. [COUNSEL, by 20 November]

##### 4.1 What she proves first-hand (keep to these)

| Q | Her words (Exh. M) | What it proves |
|---|---|---|
| 4 | The land is near her; the Balane house stands on it; Jomil and Princess now occupy it; "dati rati na nagtutuyo ng mga isda ang mag-asawang Efren at Gloria sa nasabing lupa." | Fish-drying use by Efren and Gloria before the house: use of the land, not a claim of ownership. Fits their own Answer (renters since 1975). |
| 5 | Born in San Roque on 5 June 1979; there ever since. | Continuous vantage point over 47 years. |
| 7 (first sentence) | "Hindi ko po alam kung papaano nakapagtayo ng bahay doon ang mga defendants at kung ang mga defendants na ang siyang mga may-ari nito." | Honest limit of her knowledge. Good for her credibility. |

##### 4.2 Lines to withdraw on direct (all of these)

- **Q2 to Q3**: the defendants are "talagang mga kilala at **maimpluwensiyang** mga tao." Characterization. Withdraw.

- **Q4 and Q8**: "alam ko at ng lahat na naninirahan doon **kasama na ang mga defendants**"; "hayag sa lahat … mabubuklat sa Register of Deeds." Opinion on the defendants' knowledge and on public records. Withdraw the "kasama na ang mga defendants" clause; do not ask Q8.

- **Q7 (second sentence)**: "nalaman ko **kay Jonathan at sa mga dokumentong ipinakita niya sa akin** na diumano nabili ng mga defendants … kay Cesar De la Fuente na wala naman palang kapangyarihan." Hearsay from the plaintiff's son plus a legal conclusion. Withdraw. It also shows the witness was briefed with documents.

- **Q9**: "**Malamang na sinamantala** ng mga defendants … ang mga tunay na may-ari at malamang din pong sinamantala nila ang **koneksyon at impluwensiya nila sa gobyerno** … sensitibong posisyon." Speculation and characterization; it breaches the case's own rule. Withdraw in full. If this line is read into the record by the defence on cross, it hurts us more than anything she can add.

##### 4.3 Cross she will face, and the honest answer

| Atty. Ramos | Her honest answer |
|---|---|
| "Everything you know about the sale you learned from Jonathan." | "Opo, ang tungkol sa bentahan. Ang nakita ko mismo ay ang pagtutuyo ng isda at ang bahay." |
| "You were sued together with Mrs. King by the Balane side. You are her friend. You are not neutral." | "Kaibigan ko po si Mrs. King. Nasa kaso rin ako. Sinasabi ko ang nakita ko." |
| "You called 'probably' and 'influence.' You have no facts for that." | If the line was withdrawn on direct: "Binawi na po iyon; palagay ko lang iyon." If not withdrawn: there is no good answer. Withdraw it. |
| "When exactly did they dry fish there? Who? For how long?" | What she remembers, with years. If she cannot give a year, say so. [ASK HER first] |
| "When was the Balane house built?" | Only if she saw it. Otherwise "Hindi ko po matandaan ang taon." |

##### 4.4 Documents for counsel to prepare or carry (de los Reyes day)

| # | Document | Use | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Exhibit M as filed (and the re-sworn copy if 1.1 requires it), with the withdrawn lines marked in counsel's copy | Her direct testimony | On file |
| 2 | Exhibit P: her PhilID, original | Identity and residence | She brings it |
| 3 | Exhibit B and Exhibit C, page 2 | Location questions | In the record |
| 4 | Nothing else. No paper outside the record goes through her. | | |

**Don't-says for de los Reyes:** "malamang," "sinamantala," "impluwensiya," "koneksyon"; anything learned from Jonathan; anything about the Register of Deeds; anything about what the defendants knew.
