##### Standing rules that apply to these three days

- Jonathan adds nothing on the stand if recalled, and has no contact with the occupants or with the defence witnesses. Our own witnesses are prepared by counsel alone; Jonathan arranges attendance and transport, nothing more.

- No supplemental affidavit for a neighbour; the one allowance for a late affidavit is kept for Patricia on rebuttal.

- Nothing outside the pre-marked record is offered through a neighbour on direct. Held records (Registry file, Clerk of Court records, Building Official, barangay) stay for rebuttal.

- No adjectives about any defendant or about the court. The defendants are described by what they did and when.

- Nothing here is filed or sent without Jonathan's go through the Chief of Staff.

**Section:** Preamble and summary

---

### The King cases: what else they hold

Civil Case No. 26-360 · 19 October 2026 · internal research for Jonathan and counsel; nothing filed or sent. Sources read in full: the Court of Appeals record in CA-G.R. SP 181607 (petition with annexes, 110 pp; certiorari papers; the King/OSG filings; the CA decision and resolution; the Supreme Court petition for time), the Civil Case 8563 record held (King's Verified Answer with annexes, 44 pp; King's judicial affidavit with exhibits, 94 pp), and the "Balane Case Annexes" file (64 pp), checked against the defendants' own Answer and judicial affidavits in this case. Every item marked **✓** was checked by me on the page image. Tags: [VERIFY] not yet confirmed on a certified copy · [INFERRED] my conclusion · [COUNSEL] counsel's call.

> 

**What is new, in order of value.**

1. **Princess was recorded in the barangay blotter on 19 May 2020 saying "May usapin pa yan."** Entry 2763, page 43 of the San Roque blotter book, certified by the Punong Barangay on 20 May 2020. In this case she swears: "Sa buong panahon ng aking paninirahan doon, wala akong naranasang pagtutol mula sa sinuman" (her affidavit, S11). ✓

2. **Jomil swore in 2020 that he was acting "on instructions of the owner of the property in the person of Gloria Balane thru Nelly Juntilla."** His counter-affidavit before the prosecutor, as recorded in the joint resolution of 28 July 2020. In this case the Answer lists him to testify to "the absence of any notice of dispute prior to the filing of the case." ✓

3. **The prosecutor's ruling that ownership was undecided was mailed to the respondents.** The 16 September 2020 resolution is copy-furnished to "Complainant and Respondents" by registry receipts 1666 to 1670 and 1694, all stamped 23 September 2020. Jomil and Juntilla were respondents. ✓

4. **Gloria's side claimed the land as owner in writing from 11 February 2020.** Juntilla attached a letter "from their counsel, dated February 11, 2020," telling the Kings not to build "as the owner now needs the lot for her own use"; a confrontation before the Punong Barangay followed on 11 May 2020. ✓ The letter itself and its author are not in any file we hold.

5. **In 2020 the Balane side dated its possession from the 2016 sale.** The prosecutor recorded that Gloria bought by "a Deed of sale executed by Cesar M. DeLa Fuente as the attorney in fact of the owner Mary W. Keesey on September 2016 and since then they allegedly were in possession of the property as they were paying taxes thereon." Their Answer here puts the family on the land as renters "since 1975." ✓

6. **Another family's house stood on the parcel when Gloria bought.** The prosecutor describes the parcel as the land "in which the house purchased by Spouses Donata & Pepe King was built." Ruby Ferrer sold that house, "Kasama na rin … ang karapatan o Rights Ng Lupa," to the Kings on 18 May 2015, sixteen months before the deed. ✓ A buyer in good faith must look into the rights of others in possession. [COUNSEL]

7. **The Ferrer house stood "sa pagitan nina Gng. Nieves de Leon at Gng. Jocelyn Estayo," facing the Mercedes to Daet road.** A De Leon household is next to the compound's road frontage. Whether Nieves de Leon is Shirley's family is for Shirley to say. ✓ [ASK HER]

8. **None of this appears anywhere in the defendants' papers here.** A keyword search of their Answer and the judicial affidavits of Princess, Erwin, Salvador and Gloria finds no mention of King, Donata, Juntilla's 2020 complaints, Aquino, the prosecutor, the guards, the 2020 demolition, Crim. 20-6030, the perjury case, Civil Case 8563 or the Court of Appeals. Juntilla appears once, in the Answer's verification.

| 10 | Civil Case 8563: the plaintiffs' complaint was received by King on 10 Jun 2021; it relied on the deed (their ¶2) and claimed ₱2,500,000 moral and ₱500,000 exemplary damages. **Source:** `KING_CASES_DIG_2026-10-19.html`  
**Section:** 4. Left alone, and why

---

#### 4. Left alone, and why

- **Their filing quirks in the Court of Appeals**: Jomil's verification signed "in Cozumel, Mexico" with a blank Makati jurat; a certification of no related case while Civil Case 8563 was pending; a 2021 jurat recorded as "Series of 2020." These belong to other courts and invite a sideshow. Not used.

- **The type-weight difference on the prosecutor's 16 Sep 2020 resolution.** Seen on a photocopy of a certified machine copy only. No claim is made about it.

- **Gloria's citizenship in 2016 to 2021.** Her own affidavit attaches a Canadian passport and a Philippine identification certificate under R.A. 9225 dated 12 March 2025; her title says "FILIPINO CITIZEN." The Constitution allows a former natural-born citizen to take private land "subject to limitations provided by law" (Art. XII §8), and R.A. 8179 allows up to "five thousand (5,000) square meters in the case of urban land … for business or other purposes." Her 2,587 square meters falls within that, so this does not void the sale. Noted only as a fact about her papers. Not pursued. [COUNSEL]

- **King's own interest in the land** (the Ferrer house) stays in the witness pack as a cross risk to manage, not a point to press.

### JUDICIAL AFFIDAVIT OF PATRICIA KEESEY ZSCHOCHE

I, **PATRICIA ANN KEESEY ZSCHOCHE** [CONFIRM name as on her passport], seventy-nine (79) years of age, American citizen [CONFIRM; see note 16], divorced, retired [CONFIRM], residing at 8216 Pennington Drive, Huntington Beach, California, U.S.A., after having been sworn in accordance with law, state:

**PRELIMINARY STATEMENT**

I am answering the questions of ATTY. BONIFACIO T. BARANDON, JR., of Barandon Law Offices, Traders Road corner Shoppers' Street, Central Plaza Complex, Barangay Lag-On, Daet, Camarines Norte, who conducts this examination at by videoconference, I being at [the Philippine Consulate General in Los Angeles, or as the Court allows]. The examination is in English, a language I speak and understand. I answer fully conscious that I do so under oath, and that I may face criminal liability for false testimony or perjury.

**PURPOSE OF THIS TESTIMONY**

This testimony is offered in rebuttal, to answer [the specific testimony and exhibits of the defendants, by witness, date and transcript page], and to prove: (a) that I am one of the three registered co-owners of the land covered by Transfer Certificate of Title No. T-52540; (b) what authority my sisters and I gave Cesar M. de la Fuente in the Special Power of Attorney of March 16, 1992 (Exhibit "E"), and to whom; (c) that I was never consulted on, never approved, and never ratified any sale to Gloria H. Balane, and received no part of any price; (d) that I signed no instrument naming Transfer Certificate of Title No. T-52540, the deed of sale of September 29, 2016, the Deed of Confirmation of March 28, 2017, or the Partition-Subdivision Agreement of March 15, 2017; (e) that my sisters and I revoked Mr. de la Fuente's authority on August 15, 2005 (Exhibit "F") and published that revocation in 2020 (Exhibit "G"); (f) what my affidavits of February 2023 meant (Exhibit "I" and Exhibit "O-1"); and (g) to identify Exhibits "A", "E", "F", "G", "I", "O-1" and "O-2".

#### I. Who I am, and how I know these facts

Q1. Please state your relationship to Mary Worrick Keesey.

A1. I am one of her three children. My sisters are Geraldine Keesey Hoppe and Marcia Ellen Keesey. Our mother died in the United States on March 17, 1988. [her affidavits of February 2023, ¶1]

Q2. Who are the registered owners of the land covered by Transfer Certificate of Title No. T-52540?

A2. My sisters Geraldine K. Hoppe and Marcia Ellen Keesey, and I. [Exh. B, p. 1]

Q3. How did you and your sisters come to own the land?

A3. I inherited it. My sisters and I inherited the property from our mother. On December 7, 1988 we adjudicated her property among ourselves by an extrajudicial settlement, in equal undivided shares. It included the land under Transfer Certificate of Title No. T-4497, with an area of 139,132 square meters. [her affidavits, ¶¶2–3; Exh. E ¶4]

Q4. Where have you lived from 1988 to the present?

A4. In California, in the United States, throughout that time. In 1992 I lived in Huntington Beach. I now live at 8216 Pennington Drive, Huntington Beach, California. [CONFIRM] [Exh. E (1992 address, Huntington Beach); Exh. A (current address); she adds any other places and years]

Q5. Before 1992, who managed the land for you and your sisters?

A5. Our administrator, Mr. Benjamin Llamanzares. He negotiated the sale of portions of the land to third parties, and he kept the title until he died. [affidavit of loss, ¶3]

Q6. What did you know, at the time, about the persons Mr. Llamanzares had dealt with?

A6. I knew that he had negotiated sales of portions of the land to people who signed contracts to sell with him or paid him down payments or partial payments. I did not deal with those buyers myself. [CONFIRM]

#### II. The Special Power of Attorney of March 16, 1992

Q7. I show you a certified copy of a Special Power of Attorney dated March 16, 1992, marked Exhibit "E". What is it?

A7. It is the Special Power of Attorney my sisters and I signed on March 16, 1992, appointing Cesar M. de la Fuente as our attorney-in-fact.

Q8. Whose signature appears over the typed name "PATRICIA K. ZSCHOCHE"?

A8. Mine.

Q9. Where and before whom did you sign it?

A9. In Los Angeles, California, on March 16, 1992, before a notary public. The Philippine Consul in Los Angeles authenticated it the same day. [CONFIRM] [Exh. E: notary's acknowledgment, Los Angeles County; Consul Ramon C. Munda's authentication]

Q10. Please read paragraph 7 of Exhibit "E".

A10. (Witness reads paragraph 7 of Exhibit "E", page 3, aloud.)

Q11. Why did you and your sisters give Mr. de la Fuente this authority?

A11. We were in America. Mr. Llamanzares had already dealt with buyers of portions of the land, and the real estate taxes left by our mother were unpaid. We gave Mr. de la Fuente limited authority to complete the sale of portions of the land to those who already held contracts to sell from Mr. Llamanzares, or who had paid him down payments or partial payments, and to use part of the proceeds to pay the taxes. [CONFIRM] [her affidavits, ¶4 ("limited authority to perfect the sale of portions"); Exh. E ¶¶5–7]

Q12. To whom were sales to be made under that authority?

A12. Only to persons holding contracts to sell executed by Mr. Benjamin Llamanzares, or persons who had paid him down payments or partial payments.

Q13. What did the words "after prior consultation with us" mean to you when you signed?

A13. It meant that Mr. de la Fuente had to come to us first, before any sale, so that we could agree on the buyer and on the terms and conditions. He could not decide a sale by himself. [CONFIRM] [Exh. E ¶7: the sale is "upon such terms and conditions … after prior consultation with us"; she must put this in her own words]

Q14. Did Exhibit "E" authorize Mr. de la Fuente to subdivide the land or to sign a partition for you?

A14. No. It authorized only the sales I described. It says nothing about subdividing the land or signing a partition for us.

Q15. Did you sign any other power of attorney in favor of Mr. de la Fuente? [Ask only after counsel has read the 1991 instrument annotated on T-52540 on October 10, 2016 (Exh. "B", p. 5: Doc. No. 260, Page No. 53, Book No. I, Series of 1991, Makati). See note 4.]

A15. Yes. In August 1991, in Makati, my sisters and I signed a power of attorney for Mr. de la Fuente to manage and supervise our mother's properties, pay the taxes, collect what was due and sign receipts for it, and represent us before government offices. It did not authorize him to sell anything. [CONFIRM after counsel shows her the instrument] [Registry file Ref. 2025001098, pp. 2–4; Doc. No. 260, Page No. 53, Book No. I, Series of 1991]

#### COUNSEL'S WORKING NOTES. Not part of the affidavit; never filed or shown to the witness as answers.

| # | Point | Note |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Why she matters | She is the only first-hand witness to the purpose of the class (Q11–Q12), to no consultation (Q16–Q19), to no payment (Q20), and to non-ratification (Q24–Q28, Q32). Because she testifies on rebuttal, the case-in-chief must survive a demurrer on the class limit alone; her testimony then answers the defense's claims of consent and ratification. |
| 2 | "Mr. De La Fuente was well aware of this move" | Her 2023 affidavits (Exh. "I" ¶4; Affidavit of Loss ¶5) say this. On cross Ramos will ask how she knows. If she has no first-hand basis, she says so at Q31 and the point is left to the documents. Never let her guess. |
| 3 | "T-52540, now T-079-2021002127, with an area of 26,304 sqm" (Affidavit of Loss ¶4) | Ramos's ratification line: she herself treated the partition title as hers. Q40 lets her answer. The same day she swore the adverse claim calling the sale "null and void" (Exh. "I" ¶8), which is inconsistent with ratification. A void contract cannot be ratified (Art. 1409). |
| 4 | The 1991 power of attorney (Exh. "B" p. 5, annotated October 10, 2016, the same entry as Cesar's affidavit of loss) | In hand: certified Registry file Ref. 2025001098, pp. 2–4 (also in the Spec. Proc. 2156 record). It is a general power of administration: control and supervision, taxes, collections with receipts, representation before the BIR, the Registry and DAR. No power to sell (Arts. 1877, 1878[5]). Show it to her before Q15; if she cannot recall signing it, she says so. |
| 6 | She was in Makati in February 2023 | Ramos may ask why she never went to Mercedes or spoke to the Balanes. Answer in her words; do not volunteer travel history beyond the question. |
| 7 | Her sisters | She speaks only to what Geraldine or Marcia Ellen told her directly. Anything else: "Please ask them." The SPA required consultation "with us"; proof that she was not consulted is enough to show the condition failed. |
| 8 | The 2005 sale to Mariquita Era | Not in the affidavit. If asked on cross, it was registered under the SPA before the revocation; she answers only what she knew then. |
| 9 | Capacity and the guardianship petition | The petition before RTC Branch 41 seeks a guardian over the property interests of all three sisters on absence, not incapacity. Counsel confirms nothing in it can be read as saying she cannot testify, before her affidavit is filed. Keep it out of the affidavit. |
| 10 | Exhibit marks | Her affidavit is the reserved Exhibit "O". "O-1" (Affidavit of Loss, February 2023) and "O-2" (Agcaoili letter, August 15, 2019) are new marks; counsel decides whether to mark them now or seek leave. The Affidavit of Loss is also on the Registry's T-38838 (Entry 2023001323). |
| 11 | Filing | Now on rebuttal: see notes 14 and 15. Late filing is allowed only once, for a valid reason, without undue prejudice, with a fine (§10[a]). The draft motion for leave is in the consultation gap plan (CASE_FILE folder, §3); adapt it to rebuttal. |
| 12 | Testimony from California | Videoconference, on motion, filed with the affidavit (counsel to fetch the current Supreme Court guidelines), or a deposition before a Philippine consul (Rule 23). Videoconference keeps cross live and is preferred. |
| 14 | Rebuttal keying | Rebuttal is "rebutting evidence only" unless the court permits evidence on our original case (Rule 30 §5[f]). Keep a question only if it answers defense evidence: Q16–Q20 answer any claim of consultation, approval or contact (Salvador S30–S31; Erwin S14, S23; the deed's Article 1623 recital in Def. Exh. 6); Q21 answers the estate-named receipts (Def. Exh. 5); Q24–Q28 answer any claim that the owners signed or consented; Q38–Q40 answer the 2023 affidavit if offered as ratification; Q7–Q14 answer any defense reading of the SPA. Questions with no defense target are cut or moved to a request that the court allow them on our original case. |
| 15 | Filing date | The Judicial Affidavit Rule ties affidavits to the pre-trial (§2[a]). Counsel confirms whether a rebuttal affidavit needs the once-only leave (§10[a]); if it does, keep that leave for her. |
| 16 | Citizenship and the right to own | If Ramos raises a foreigner's capacity to own land, her answer is inheritance (A3). The 1987 Constitution, Article XII, Section 7: "Save in cases of hereditary succession, no private lands shall be transferred or conveyed except to individuals, corporations, or associations qualified to acquire or hold lands of the public domain" (text read on lawphil). The title came to the three sisters by the 1988 extrajudicial settlement of their mother's estate. A 2026 delayed report of her birth as a Philippine citizen was in process; she states her citizenship as it stands on the day she signs. |
| 13 | Her personal details | Age, civil status, occupation and current address come from her, not from the corpus. Her passport number appears in the 2023 affidavits; do not copy it into this affidavit. |

#### A. Direct: what happens before cross

1. **Affirm the judicial affidavit with the corrections stated on the record** (unless Atty. Barandon confirms that the errata Manifestation was actually filed; nothing in our files shows it was): purposes (g) and (h), "September 26, 2019" should read **29 September 2016**; Q11 and Q12, "28,981" should read **28,891 square meters** (2,587 + 26,304). If asked why: *"Clerical. The Registry's entry on Exhibit B, page 6, records the deed as Doc. 304, Page 61, Book CXVI, dated 29 September 2016. The areas are in Entry 2021003236 on the same page."* Do not say the deed itself is "stamped 29 September": the day digit on our copy of Exhibit H is not legible.
2. **Identify the exhibits** A to J as the affidavit does. Ask, through counsel, that the partition entry (Entry No. 2021003236) on Exhibit B be sub-marked **B-5**.
3. **Three wording points to soften on direct**, so cross cannot. **Q25** says Cesar "misappropriated" the proceeds: say he "never remitted or accounted for" them. **Q26** says the co-owners "directly informed Cesar" of the revocation: say you have no personal knowledge of what Cesar was told (Salvador denies it). **Q26** also calls Exhibit F "a new Special Power of Attorney": if Ramos reads it aloud, answer *"Exhibit F is dated 15 August 2005. It disowned every other power of attorney,"* and stop; ask Atty. Barandon beforehand whether to correct that wording out loud ("for 'a new Special Power of Attorney' read 'the instrument of 15 August 2005, Exhibit F'").
5. **"Negotiate only" (purpose [e], Q18, Q25, Q53): decide with Atty. Barandon whether to correct it on direct or handle it on cross.** If corrected on direct, the cross answer in B1 opens with *"I corrected that on direct."*
4. **The authority on the face of the appearance.** Counsel identifies the SPA by which Jonathan acts and its exhibit. This judge checks authority first.

**Section:** B. Cross-examination: the traps, in the order Ramos is likely to run them

#### B. Cross-examination: the traps, in the order Ramos is likely to run them

Format: **the question he will ask** → *the answer* → the exhibit. Where the study card had a different line, this one controls.

##### B1. The SPA and "negotiate only"

| He will ask | Answer | Exhibit |
|---|---|---|
| Your affidavit says (Q25, Q53) that the SPA was "limited to the negotiation of the sale which does not include the power to sell." The SPA also says "to sign and execute the necessary documents … and to receive the proceeds." You were wrong, weren't you? | (If corrected on direct: *"I corrected that on direct."*) *"The paragraph does say that. Whatever Cesar could do, paragraph 7 says he could do it only for third persons holding contracts to sell from Mr. Llamanzares, or who had paid Mr. Llamanzares, and only after consulting the owners. Mrs. Balane was neither, and no one consulted the owners."* | Exh. E ¶7 (CTC p. 3) |
| So Cesar could sell. | *"To the class in paragraph 7, after consultation. Yes."* | Exh. E |
| The SPA was annotated on T-52540 in 1996. Your Complaint says 2021. | *"The entry on Exhibit B, page 3, is dated 21 June 1996. The Complaint has the wrong date there; counsel will correct it. What was entered in 2021 was the sale and the partition."* Refer to a filed Manifestation only if Atty. Barandon confirms one was filed; nothing in our files shows it. | Exh. B p. 3 |
| The title told the world Cesar could sell. No revocation was ever annotated. | *"The title told the world to read the SPA. The SPA names the class and the consultation. The revocation was never annotated; that is correct."* | Exh. B; Exh. F |
| Isn't "in possession" in paragraph 7 about possession of the land? The Balanes were in possession. | *"The words are 'in possession of CONTRACTS TO SELL executed by the previous administrator.' Contracts, not land."* Stop there. | Exh. E ¶7 |
| They paid a down payment to Llamanzares in the 1980s. | *"I have no knowledge of any such payment. No receipt, no contract and no book has been produced. The earliest receipt in their Exhibit 5 is dated October 2003, signed 'Von dela Fuente.'"* Do not call the signer Cesar's son; nothing on the receipt says so. | Def. Exh. 5 p. 2 |
| What does "prior consultation with us" mean? Isn't that between the owners and Cesar? | *"It is in paragraph 7 of the instrument that was annotated on the title. It is not a private instruction. The owners were never consulted on this sale."* **Stop there.** Do not argue Art. 1902. | Exh. E ¶7 |
| The SPA describes T-4497, not T-52540. So it never covered this lot at all? | *"The SPA describes the mother title, T-4497. The notice in the Bicol Post names T-4497. How T-52540 came out of it is for counsel to show from the title chain."* Do not say "never covered," and do not say Exhibit B page 1 shows the link: page 1 recites a transfer from another title (reads T-49061), not T-4497. | Exh. E ¶2; Exh. G-1 |

##### B2. The revocation

| He will ask | Answer | Exhibit |
|---|---|---|
| When was the SPA revoked? | *"15 August 2005. Exhibit F."* | Exh. F |
| Your personal belief is it was earlier? | *"The instrument I can point to is dated 15 August 2005."* Do not volunteer 1995 or a draft. | Exh. F |
| Was the revocation ever communicated to Cesar? Annotated? Published before 2016? | *"I have no personal knowledge of what Cesar was told. It was not annotated. It was published in 2020, Exhibit G."* | Exh. G |
| Publication in 2020 cannot undo a 2016 sale. | *"The sale was registered on 23 November 2021. The publication was in September and October 2020."* Then stop; the law is for counsel. | Exh. B p. 6; Exh. G |
| Exhibit F, paragraph 4, gives the new attorney-in-fact power to recover properties "unlawfully or fraudulently sold." So your family was already alleging fraud in 2005? | *"That is the standard wording of the instrument appointing the new attorney-in-fact. It names no one."* Do not repeat the word. | Exh. F p. 2 |
| Cesar sold to Mariquita Era in April 2005 under the same SPA and it was registered. The family never objected. | *"April 2005 was before 15 August 2005. I cannot speak to a sale I was not part of."* | Exh. B p. 3 |

##### B3. Knowledge, delay, laches

| He will ask | Answer | Exhibit |
|---|---|---|
| When did you learn of the sale to Mrs. Balane? | *"Late 2022. The sale was registered on 23 November 2021; nothing was registered before that."* "Late 2022" is your own knowledge and is not in any exhibit or in the affidavit; say it only if asked, and do not add "after a tip" unless asked how. | Exh. B p. 6 |
| The Balanes have been there since 1975 and nobody objected for fifty years. | *"They were tenants of the owners' administrator; their own Answer says they paid rent. The owners had no reason to object to a tenant living on the land. The owners objected to a sale as soon as they learned of one: adverse claim, demand, this case."* | Answer ¶¶40, 43; Exh. J |
| Your mother and her sisters left everything to Cesar and never came home. | *"They appointed an attorney-in-fact with written limits. Living abroad is not consent to a sale outside those limits."* Do not say more about the sisters' conduct. | Exh. E |
| Your mother's own 2023 affidavit of loss was entered on T-38838 and on T-2021002127. She adopted the partition. | *"My mother recorded the loss of owner's duplicates she never held. The Registry entered it on the titles it was carrying. She did not sign or consent to any partition."* | Annex G p. 4 (if shown) |
| You yourself used the number T-2021002127 in correspondence. | *"That is the number the Registry gave the owners' remainder. Using the Registry's number is not consent to how it was created."* | — |

##### B4. Possession, structures, the families

| He will ask | Answer | Exhibit |
|---|---|---|
| Who built the structures on the lot? | *"I can't say who built what."* Never "I concede she built them." | — |
| The Balanes built in good faith for decades. | *"Their Answer says they were renting. What was built while renting was built by a tenant."* Stop. | Answer ¶¶40, 43 |
| There are families living on the lot. Your family let anyone settle there. | *"There are families there. They never paid the owners. The owners never admitted them. I do not know who let each of them in."* Do not estimate numbers or dates you have not counted. | — |
| Some of those families got their lots from Salvador. | *"I understand some hold papers from Salvador. I have not seen them all."* Nothing more; this is for the subpoena and the defense stage. | — |
| Did the owners ever ask for building permits? | *"I asked the Municipal Engineer's office for the records in January 2026 and was not given them."* Nothing about ARTA or the reply letter unless counsel has decided otherwise. | — |

##### B5. The deed and the signature

| He will ask | Answer | Exhibit |
|---|---|---|
| The deed says ₱250,000 "in hand paid." | *"That is what it says. Their own receipts show payments to February 2017, with one marked 'Full payment.'"* | Def. Exh. 5 pp. 31–33 |
| Did any owner receive any part of the price? | *"No."* | — |
| Did you ever see the owner's duplicate of T-52540? | *"Never. To my knowledge it was never in the family's hands. Exhibit B shows Cesar swore it lost on 27 September 2016 and a court-made duplicate was issued in 2017."* | Exh. B p. 5 |

##### B6. Jonathan personally

| He will ask | Answer | Exhibit |
|---|---|---|
| You introduced yourself in Mercedes as "Jayjay Moreno." Why a false name? | **No defense paper accuses you of using a false name.** Princess's affidavit (S14) says only that when you sheltered from the rain at Apuao Grande in 2022 she "knew [you] only by the name Jayjay Moreno"; S15 then says she was not the right person to talk to because her parents bought. The Answer, the Manifestation and the Comment-Opposition say nothing about it. Moreno is your family's name on your grandmother's side (the "Moreno-Worrick-Keesey family"; Doña Marciana Moreno Street), and you sign official letters that way: your 22 May 2025 letter to the Mayor is signed "Jonathan 'JJ Ildefonso Moreno' Zschoche." If asked: *"Moreno is my family name. I use it. I have never hidden that I am my mother's attorney-in-fact."* One sentence, then stop. The 22 May 2025 letter is not an exhibit; do not cite it from the stand. | Princess JA S14–S15 |
| You are a foreigner and cannot own land here. | *"I am my mother's attorney-in-fact. The owners are my mother and her sisters."* | Exh. A |
| You filed complaints against the defendants with ARTA, the Ombudsman, the CSC. You are harassing this family. | *"I asked government offices for records and for action on records requests. What those offices did with my requests is their act."* Stop. Do not list dockets. | — |
| You wrote to Salvador dela Fuente. | *"Yes."* Nothing about the reply unless counsel has decided to use it. | — |
| You have no personal knowledge of anything before you came to the Philippines. | *"I testify to the documents and to what I did. Where the affidavit states something from documents, the document is the source."* | — |

##### B7. Things never to say, whatever the question

#### C. The qualifier: re-direct, keyed to each cross point

Re-direct is limited to explaining or supplementing answers given on cross (Rule 132, Section 7); new matter needs leave. Each line below is **one question counsel can ask and the one-sentence answer**, usable only if the matching cross point was opened. Keep each to one exchange; this judge will not sit through a second direct.

| If cross opened… | Counsel asks | Jonathan answers |
|---|---|---|
| "Negotiate only" (B1) | "When you wrote 'limited to negotiation,' what limit were you describing?" | "The limit in paragraph 7: a sale only to people holding Llamanzares contracts or who paid Llamanzares, after consulting the owners. Mrs. Balane was outside it." |
| The 1996 annotation (B1) | "What did the 1996 annotation refer a buyer to?" | "To the SPA itself, Exhibit E, with paragraph 7 in it." |
| "In possession" (B1) | "Possession of what, according to the words of paragraph 7?" | "Of contracts to sell executed by Mr. Llamanzares." |
| The 1980s down payment (B1) | "Has any contract, receipt or ledger from Mr. Llamanzares to the Balanes ever been produced to you or filed in this case?" | "None." |
| Consultation (B1) | "Were your mother or her sisters ever consulted on a sale to Mrs. Balane?" | "No." (Jonathan can say this only if it is within his knowledge as attorney-in-fact; otherwise counsel uses Salvador's S31 on the defense dates.) |
| T-4497 vs T-52540 (B1) | "What title does the SPA describe, and what title does the published notice name?" | "Both name T-4497, the mother title." (The chain from T-4497 to T-52540 is counsel's to prove from the titles, not Jonathan's.) |
| "A new Special Power of Attorney" in Q26 (B2) | "What did the instrument of 15 August 2005, Exhibit F, do to every earlier power of attorney?" | "It disowned them and declared them without force and effect." |
| Revocation and publication (B2) | "Between the deed in 2016 and the registration in November 2021, what was published?" | "The revocation, in the Bicol Post, 25 September, 2 October and 9 October 2020, Exhibit G." |
| The Era sale (B2) | "What is the date of that sale?" | "28 April 2005, before the revocation." |
| When he learned (B3) | "What did the owners do after you learned of the sale?" | "An adverse claim, the demand of 18 August 2025, Exhibit J, and this case." |
| Fifty years of silence (B3) | "On what basis were the Balanes on the land before 2016, according to their own Answer?" | "As renters, paying monthly rent to the owners' administrator." |
| The 2023 affidavit of loss (B3) | "Did your mother sign or consent to the Partition-Subdivision Agreement of 15 March 2017?" | "No. None of the owners did." |
| Who built what (B4) | "What does their Answer say they were doing on the land from 1975?" | "Renting it." |
| The families (B4) | "Did the owners admit any of those families or receive anything from them?" | "No." |
| Building permits (B4) | "What happened to your request for the permit records?" | "The Municipal Engineer's office did not release them." (Stop there.) |
| The signature (B5) | "Whatever the signature, who had authority to sell this portion to Mrs. Balane?" | "No one. Not under paragraph 7, and not after 15 August 2005." |
| "In hand paid" (B5) | "According to their own Exhibit 5, when was the last payment on the price?" | "February 2017, receipt marked 'Full payment.'" |
| The duplicate (B5) | "Who, according to Exhibit B, swore the duplicate was lost, and when?" | "Cesar de la Fuente, 27 September 2016, two days before the deed." |
| "Moreno" (B6) | "What is Moreno?" | "My family's name on my grandmother's side. I use it. My authority to act for my mother is Exhibit A." |
| Foreigner (B6) | "Who owns the land in this case?" | "My mother and her two sisters, Exhibit B." |
| Harassment (B6) | "Did you write to any government office for any purpose other than records or action on records?" | "No." |
| The Salvador letter (B6) | Only if counsel has decided to use it: "Did you receive a reply?" | "Yes, a signed letter dated 25 November 2025." (Then it is marked for identification; its contents wait for Salvador's cross.) |

**Three things re-direct must not try to do:** introduce the ARTA or Ombudsman instruments, read Salvador's letter into the record before its foundation is laid on his cross, or get Jonathan to characterize anyone. If the defense did not open a door, it stays closed until rebuttal.

**Section:** D. The ten-second reset, for the stand

---

#### D. The ten-second reset, for the stand

Wide question, small answer. Which exhibit says this. Concede the small truth. Never a mind. Never a date you have not seen on paper. If you do not know, "I do not know." If you cannot say, "I can't say." Then stop talking.

# E. The payments: forensic audit and second-reviewer analyses


## Forensic accounting audit of the Balane payments (Def. Exh. 5)

**Document:** Forensic accounting audit of the Balane payments (Def. Exh. 5)  
**Source:** `FORENSIC_AUDIT_BALANE_PAYMENTS_2026-10-18.html`  
**Section:** Preamble and summary

---

### Forensic accounting audit plan: the Balane payments

Civil Case No. 26-360, Zschoche v. Balane · prepared 18 October 2026 for Jonathan and counsel · internal, not for filing · every figure below is read from a page image and cited to it; nothing is taken from the defense's summaries.

> 

**What the money says, in one paragraph.** The defendants' own receipts (Def. Exh. 5) show 110 payments totalling ₱602,750 to Cesar de la Fuente's side between October 2003 and February 2017. Their deed recites a price of ₱250,000 "in hand paid" on 29 September 2016. The receipts cannot be squared with that price on any reading: ₱219,000 was receipted after the deed, ending in a "Full payment" slip of February 2017; ₱382,650 was receipted from November 2014 onward, which already exceeds the price by ₱132,650; and if the 89 monthly slips of ₱2,000 or less (₱169,500) were price instalments, as the defense says, the overpayment grows to ₱352,750. Not one peso is shown reaching an owner. The money that can be traced went to taxes, penalties and fees in 2016 and 2020 (about ₱406,831), most of it penalties for a deed registered four years late. The population is complete enough to audit, and the anomalies in it are the kind a fraud examiner tests first: receipts out of serial order, a price recital contradicted by the payer's own receipts, and a ledger that the defense says exists but has never produced.


**Document:** Forensic accounting audit of the Balane payments (Def. Exh. 5)  
**Source:** `FORENSIC_AUDIT_BALANE_PAYMENTS_2026-10-18.html`  
**Section:** 1. First principles: how a fraud examination of payments is run

---

#### 1. First principles: how a fraud examination of payments is run

A forensic accountant does not start from the story. The method, in the order it is applied here:

1. **Predication and hypotheses.** State what the money is said to prove, then frame each claim as a hypothesis that evidence can confirm or refute. The defense's claims: a 1980s down payment to Llamanzares (membership in the SPA's class); a ₱250,000 price paid in instalments and completed before the deed; receipts issued in the estate's name (ratification by the owners); and taxes and arrears paid as an owner would. Our hypotheses are in Part 2.

2. **Define the population.** Identify every record that should exist for the claims (receipts, stubs, ledgers, bank records, tax receipts, the deed) and what portion is actually in hand. Audit the whole population, not a sample: here it is 111 slips on 33 pages.

3. **Provenance and custody.** For each record: who created it, who kept it, how it reached the file. A receipt kept by the payer proves payment to the collector, not receipt by the principal.

4. **Transcribe and classify.** One row per document, read from the image, with the exact words written, the form used, the serial number, the signatory. Classify by purpose (rent, price, tax, other) only on what the slip itself says.

5. **Test the documents** for existence, occurrence, authorization, completeness, cut-off, classification, valuation and sequence (Part 4). Words against figures; serial numbers against dates; forms against the dates they were printed; signatures against each other.

6. **Reconcile to independent sources.** The deed's price; the BIR's eCAR values; the Treasurer's tax ledger; the surveyor's receipt. Agreement between the payer's and the payee's records is evidence; disagreement is a finding.

7. **Trace source and application.** Where the money came from matters less here than where it went: owners, taxes, the agent, his son. Every peso is assigned to a destination or to "unknown."

8. **Report findings as facts tied to pages,** separate from inferences, with the gaps listed. No adjective substitutes for a reconciliation. Standards drawn on: the fraud-theory approach of the fraud examiners' profession, the auditor's response to fraud risk in the Philippine Standards on Auditing (PSA 240), and the Tax Code rules on official receipts (Appendix B). The first two are stated in substance; the Tax Code sections are quoted from lawphil.


**Document:** Forensic accounting audit of the Balane payments (Def. Exh. 5)  
**Source:** `FORENSIC_AUDIT_BALANE_PAYMENTS_2026-10-18.html`  
**Section:** 2. Hypotheses to test

---

#### 2. Hypotheses to test

| # | Hypothesis | Status after this review of the papers in hand |
|---|---|---|
| H1 | The ₱250,000 price was not paid before the deed of 29 September 2016; the recital "in hand paid" was false when signed. | **Supported by their own receipts.** ₱219,000 receipted after the deed; "Full payment" on 14 February 2017 (p. 33). |
| H2 | No payment reached any registered owner; everything went to Cesar, Salvador, taxes or fees. | **Consistent with every record.** No owner signature, bank record or remittance exists; Salvador swears the money went to his father, part of it to taxes (S23 to S25). Patricia's denial is first-hand; the rest is to be proved from Cesar's books and bank records (Part 7). |
| H3 | There was no down payment to Llamanzares in the 1980s; the earliest payment is October 2003, the only "Down payment" is January 2005. | **Supported.** No receipt, stub or ledger before 2003 (pp. 2 to 3). The defense itself says the 1980s payment has no receipt and sits in a ledger it does not hold (Part 6). |
| H4 | The ₱2,000 monthly payments were rent, not instalments on a price. | **Supported on the face of the documents.** Fifteen estate receipts name the month paid for; 89 slips are ₱2,000 or less at monthly intervals for eleven years; no slip shows a balance; the ₱250,000 was separately paid in 2016 to 2017. |
| H5 | The receipts were not all written when the payments were made. | **Indicated, not proved.** Serial numbers run against dates in 2014, 2016 and 2017; 80 serials (0137 to 0216) are absent between May and August 2016; one "Start 10k" note in another hand. Needs the stubs and a document examiner (Part 7). |
| H6 | The "Heirs of Mary W. Keesey Estate" was registered with the BIR as a taxpayer issuing official receipts by the agent, without the owners' knowledge. | **Indicated.** TIN [TIN withheld] and printing authority 451-1998 appear on every estate receipt; the 2020 tax certificate treats the sale under withholding tax rather than capital gains tax. Who registered the TIN is a BIR record (Part 7). |
| H7 | The true consideration exceeded ₱250,000, or the deed price was set below what was actually paid. | **Open.** ₱382,650 from November 2014 and ₱332,650 in 2016 to 2017 exceed the price. Salvador's explanation (part went to real property tax and "estate tax") is tested in Part 5; the estate tax had been paid in full in 1991 (Exh. E ¶5). |

**Section:** 4. Document tests and the exceptions found

---

#### 4. Document tests and the exceptions found

| # | Finding | Pages and particulars | Test |
|---|---|---|---|
| E1 | The deed's price recital is contradicted by the payer's own receipts | Deed of 29 Sep 2016: ₱250,000 "to me in hand paid" (Registry file p. 11; Exh. H). Receipts after that date: 0222 ₱60,000 (30 Sep), 0223 ₱10,000, 0219 ₱50,000, 0218 ₱20,000, 0224 ₱40,000, 0225 ₱30,000, 0227 ₱2,000, 0226 ₱3,000, 0228 "Full payment" (pp. 31 to 33): ₱219,000 | Cut-off |
| E2 | Receipts exceed the price | ₱382,650 from 17 Nov 2014; ₱602,750 in all. The slips show no running balance; the "Partial / Balance" lines are blank on every form that has them | Reconciliation |
| E3 | Serial numbers run against dates | 0122 (30 Jun 2014) before 0121 (31 Jul 2014); 0132 (9 Oct 2014) before 0124, 0128, 0130 (Oct to Dec 2014); 0129 dated 31 Mar 2016 between 0128 (Nov 2014) and 0130 (Dec 2014); 0219 (18 Nov 2016) and 0218 (27 Nov 2016) after 0220 to 0223 (Sep to Oct 2016); 0227 (7 Jan 2017) before 0226 (30 Jan 2017) | Sequence |
| E4 | Eighty serials unaccounted for in 2016 | 0136 (21 May 2016) is followed by 0217 (7 Aug 2016). A book of 50 leaves in duplicate (the imprint reads "25 Bks. (50x2) 0001-1250") does not skip 80 numbers for one payer; either other payers were receipted from the same book, or the numbers were used elsewhere | Sequence, completeness |
| E5 | Two serials far apart used the same day | 0019 and 0061, both 28 Mar 2007 (p. 9); 0019 for ₱5,000 "for lot," 0061 for ₱2,000 "March 2007" | Sequence |
| E6 | Words and figures differ on the "Full payment" receipt | 0228 (p. 33): words read "Four Thousand Pesos"; the figure, in a hand that writes 5 as a check mark (compare 0219, 0134), reads 4,000 or 5,000. Either way the slip is the last and smallest payment | Valuation |
| E7 | Rent format on the estate's own forms | "Payment for the month of" filled with a month on 15 slips, 2007 and 2013 to 2014 (pp. 9 to 11, 23 to 27); the printed form itself is a rent form (Occupant's Name; Payment for the month of) | Classification |
| E8 | The collector's forms changed five times | Four generic pad designs (2003 to 2012) and the printed estate book (2007, 2011, 2013 onward); the estate book was in use in March 2007 (0019, 0061) yet generic pads were used again from 2008 to 2012 | Existence, occurrence |
| E9 | The estate book predates nothing: printed 1998 | Imprint on every estate receipt: "RD64-016-99 25 Bks. (50x2) 0001-1250 · 4C's Press-Daet, CN Auth. No. 451-1998." No genuine receipt from this book can be older than 1998; none in the exhibit is older than 2007. Under Revenue Regulations No. 18-2012, receipt stock printed before 2013 stayed valid only to 30 June 2013 (extended to 30 August 2013 by Revenue Memorandum Circular No. 44-2013) [secondary sources; primary text to be obtained from the BIR]. Thirty-four estate-form receipts in the exhibit (0106 to 0228) are dated after 30 June 2013, on 1998 stock. That bears on the issuer's compliance and on whether a book kept past its validity was kept for one payer; it does not by itself date any entry | Occurrence |
| E10 | A marginal note in another hand | "Start 10k-" above the receipt of 27 Jun 2009 (p. 16), in different ink and hand from the collector's | Document integrity |
| E11 | Two signatories, one name | Four early slips carry the written name "Von dela Fuente"; all others a stylized signature. Salvador swears he issued the receipts (S19 to S22). Whether both are his hand is a question for him and, if needed, an examiner. The only paper appointing Salvador is Cesar's "delegation" of 29 February 2012 (Belen, Doc. 55/Pg. 17/Bk. IX/2012): it post-dates the 15 August 2005 revocation, refers to the 1991 power, carries no owner's signature, and cannot reach the 2003 to 2011 receipts. The point is framed as "no appointment before 2012 and nothing left to delegate after 2005," never as "no power to delegate" (Art. 1892 permits substitution unless prohibited) | Authorization |
| E12 | Payor names shift | Efren (2003 to 2005), "Golly" and "Mrs. Golly" (2005 to 2007, 2011, 2013 to 2016), Princess (2007 to 2012), Ferdinand once (₱8,000, 1 Jun 2008), "Gloria H. Balane" from November 2014. Identity of "Golly" is to be put to the witnesses, not assumed | Attribution |
| E13 | The 31 March 2016 coincidence | 0129, ₱40,000 from Gloria, dated 31 Mar 2016 (p. 29). The Treasurer's ledger shows the lot's real property tax for 2012 to 2016 paid in one transaction on 31 Mar 2016 under OR 0033800, ₱14,210 in all (Registry file p. 13). Salvador swears part of the Balane money went to "amelyar" (S24) | Source and application |
| E14 | Taxes paid in 2020 exceed the price | BIR eCARs (Registry file p. 12): selling price ₱250,000; fair market value ₱2,509,390; zonal ₱1,552,200; amounts paid 15 Jun 2020: ₱287,866.73 and ₱77,478.08 (the capital gains column reads "EXEMPT"). Provincial transfer tax 24 Jun 2020: ₱27,276, of which ₱14,429 is surcharge and interest (p. 21). Total ₱392,621, paid three years and nine months after the deed. The Tax Code requires the return on a sale of real property "within thirty (30) days following each sale or other disposition" (NIRC §51[C][2][b], lawphil). The two certificates carry numbers 201900074035 (19 Jun 2020, T-38838) and 201900675224 (18 Nov 2021, T-52540); both show the same payment date of 15 Jun 2020 | Reconciliation |
| E16 | Two TINs: the estate's and Cesar's | Every estate receipt prints TIN [TIN withheld] for "Heirs of Mary W. Keesey Estate." Cesar's own TIN on the Deed of Confirmation of 28 Mar 2017 is [TIN withheld] (Registry file p. 16, "TIN No. [TIN withheld], Atty-in-fact of Owner"). The receipts were issued under a taxpayer registration separate from the agent's own, in the estate's name; who registered it is a BIR record (P4) | Authorization |
| E15 | The first eCAR names a dead title | eCAR 201900074035 of 19 Jun 2020 is issued against T-38838, cancelled in 1999; eCAR 201900675224 of 18 Nov 2021 re-issues against T-52540, same figures, same payment date (p. 12) | Existence |

**Section:** 5. Reconciliations

---

#### 5. Reconciliations

##### R1. Price against receipts

| Reading of the defense's claim | Receipts that fit it | Result |
|---|---|---|
| Every receipt was an instalment on the ₱250,000 price (Answer ¶44[f]; Salvador S20) | ₱602,750 | Overpaid by ₱352,750. Impossible unless the price was far higher than the deed states |
| The monthly ₱2,000 slips were rent; the price was paid from November 2014 | ₱382,650 | Overpaid by ₱132,650. Salvador's tax explanation covers ₱14,210 of it (E13); the "estate tax" he mentions had been paid in full by 4 Dec 1991 (Exh. E ¶5) |
| The price was paid only in 2016 to 2017 | ₱332,650 | Overpaid by ₱82,650, and ₱219,000 of it came after the deed said the price was already paid |
| Gloria's own account: a ₱3,000 down payment in the 1980s, credited by Cesar (S21, S32) | None | No receipt, no stub, no ledger in hand; the only "Down payment" is ₱20,000 in 2005 to Salvador |

##### R2. Where the money went (source and application)

| Destination | Amount | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Registered owners (Hoppe, Zschoche, Keesey) | ₱0 shown | No owner signature, remittance or bank record in any paper; Patricia to testify she received nothing; Salvador: "ibinibigay ko po iyon sa aking ama" (S23) |
| Real property tax on the lot, 2012 to 2016 arrears | ₱14,210 | Paid 31 Mar 2016, Treasurer's ledger (Registry file p. 13); same day as receipt 0129 |
| Real property tax 2017 to 2021 | about ₱18,000 | Paid each January, ORs listed in the Treasurer's ledger; payor names to be obtained |
| BIR, 15 Jun 2020 | ₱365,345 | eCAR values (p. 12). On a ₱2,509,390 base the basic taxes would be far lower; the balance is surcharge and interest for late payment |
| Provincial transfer tax, 24 Jun 2020 | ₱27,276 | OR 0585696 (p. 21): tax ₱12,547, surcharge ₱3,137, interest ₱11,292, fees ₱300 |
| Surveyor, 29 Dec 2006 | ₱3,000 | Villafuerte professional receipt 0460 (p. 1), "lot owned by the Hrs of Worrick Keesey" |
| Cesar de la Fuente, Salvador, or unknown | the remainder of ₱602,750 | No accounting exists; the agent's books, if any, are with his estate or with the Agcaoili office (Part 6) |

The Balanes' total outlay on the papers in hand is therefore about ₱995,371 before registration fees, on a deed that says the lot cost ₱250,000. That is not the arithmetic of a ₱250,000 purchase. It is the arithmetic of eleven years of rent, a 2016 to 2017 price near ₱330,000, and ₱390,000 of taxes and penalties caused by registering a dead agent's deed four years late.


**Document:** Forensic accounting audit of the Balane payments (Def. Exh. 5)  
**Source:** `FORENSIC_AUDIT_BALANE_PAYMENTS_2026-10-18.html`  
**Section:** 6. The ledger book they say exists

---

#### 6. The ledger book they say exists

**What the defense says.** Gloria's excluded affidavit: the ₱3,000 down payment was paid to Manuel Violeta in three instalments of ₱1,000; "Wala pong ibinigay na resibo noon si Manuel Violeta, pero sinabi niya at nakita namin na itinala niya ang mga bayad sa kaniyang brown columnar book" (S21 to S22); and, "ayon kay Cesar M. de la Fuente," the book was turned over to Cesar and later to the Agcaoili Law Office (S24). The Answer pleads the same payment (¶40) without the book.

**What follows for the audit.**

- **It is the only record offered for the fact that puts Gloria inside the SPA's class.** Without it, the class claim rests on her word about events forty years ago, relayed through a dead man's statement about where the book went.

- **If the book exists, it is a rent ledger.** A columnar book kept by the administrator's collector records what tenants paid. The entry the defense needs would have to say "down payment," not "rent." Whoever holds it, its entries for the Balanes are either absent or are the strongest proof that they paid rent.

- **Its stated custody runs to the plaintiff's own former counsel.** That is dangerous in both directions. If it is at the Agcaoili office and shows rent, it ends the class claim. If it cannot be found, the defense will argue suppression. The audit therefore locates it first, before anyone asks for it in court.

- **Cesar's own books are the second ledger.** Salvador says he handed every payment to his father (S23). A collector who issued numbered official receipts in a registered taxpayer's name had a cash book or at least the duplicate leaves. The Tax Code requires receipts "prepared at least in duplicate" (Appendix B). The duplicates, the unused leaves and the other 25 booklets of the 1998 printing are the audit trail of every peso and of every other buyer.

##### 6A. If a ledger surfaces: what it must be

- **Llamanzares-era, in Violeta's hand.** To help the defense it must show a down payment to Llamanzares before Cesar's SPA of 16 March 1992, because that is what paragraph 7 requires ("down-payments or partial payments therefor to the latter"). Entries in any later hand, or any later ink or paper, are not that.

- **Salvador cannot vouch for it.** He had no knowledge of the property in the Llamanzares years; his own affidavit dates his first contact to "noong bandang 1992," when he and his father went to collect Llamanzares's papers (S14 to S15). He can speak only to what was handed over then, and to where it went.

- **Custody is the open question.** Gloria's account is that the book went to Cesar and then to the Agcaoili office (S24). P1 asks the Agcaoili office; on cross, Salvador is asked what papers were collected from Llamanzares in 1992, whether a columnar book was among them, and where it is now.

**Procedures:** P1, P2 and P8 below.


**Document:** Forensic accounting audit of the Balane payments (Def. Exh. 5)  
**Source:** `FORENSIC_AUDIT_BALANE_PAYMENTS_2026-10-18.html`  
**Section:** 7. Procedures, in the order they are run

---

#### 7. Procedures, in the order they are run

Each procedure names the record, where it is, who asks, the alert risk to the defense, and the hypothesis it serves. "Quiet" means obtained now in Jonathan's own name or through the family, with no court filing; "rebuttal" means by subpoena after the defense rests, as the trap route requires.

| # | Procedure | Source and who asks | Alert | Serves |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| P1 | Find the "brown columnar book." Ask Atty. Agcaoili-Orena's office, in writing, whether any ledger, cash book or papers of Manuel Violeta or Cesar de la Fuente were ever turned over to it, and what they show for the Balanes | Agcaoili & Associates; Patricia and Jonathan | None | H3, H4; closes the suppression argument before it is made |
| P2 | Fix Violeta and Llamanzares: dates of death (PSA), years of service, and any family member holding records | PSA; the family; Mercedes civil registrar | None | H3; anchors against any late "ledger" witness |
| P3 | Treasurer's official receipts for the lot's real property tax, 2012 to 2021: the payor named on each OR (0033800 of 31 Mar 2016; 0032388; 8672529; 0532865; 0046725; 0048923) | Municipal Treasurer, Mercedes; Jonathan as owner's attorney-in-fact (the Treasurer's office is an ARTA respondent's office) | Moderate; defer to rebuttal if the office is hostile | H2, H7; tests Salvador's "amelyar" explanation and Answer ¶44(i) |
| P4 | BIR registration of TIN [TIN withheld]: who registered "Heirs of Mary W. Keesey Estate," when, under what authority; the Authority to Print 451-1998 and the printer's logbook at 4C's Press, Daet | BIR RDO 64, Talisay; 4C's Press. Rebuttal subpoena; the printer's logbook may be asked for quietly | Low (printer); subpoena (BIR) | H6; shows the agent registered the estate as a business without the owners |
| P5 | BIR dockets of the two eCARs: who filed and paid on 15 Jun 2020, on what forms, the computation (basic tax, surcharge, interest), and why the first was issued against T-38838 | BIR RDO 64; rebuttal subpoena | Subpoena | H2, E14, E15; fixes who paid ₱365,345 and on what instructions |
| P6 | Provincial Treasurer OR 0585696 and its computation sheet; who presented the deed on 24 Jun 2020 | Provincial Treasurer, Daet; quiet request or rebuttal subpoena | Low | H2; the processor's identity (Elevado) |
| P7 | Asis notarial register for Doc. 304/Pg. 61/Bk. CXVI/2016 (the deed) and Doc. 115/Bk. CXXII/2017 (the confirmation): the price and parties as recorded, and who appeared | Clerk of Court, RTC Daet, notarial section; quiet certified copy | Low | H1, H7; whether the register records the same price |
| P8 | Demand on cross, and by rebuttal subpoena to Salvador, for the duplicate leaves of every receipt in Exh. 5, the unused leaves, the other booklets of the 1998 printing, and any cash book of his father | Salvador dela Fuente; Cesar's heirs | Rebuttal only | H2, H5; the audit trail of every peso and of the other buyers |
| P9 | Originals of Exh. 5 for examination: ink, pen pressure, form stock, staple holes, and whether the serial-order anomalies are consistent with later writing-up. Engage a questioned-document examiner only after the originals are produced. **Timing decision for counsel:** the second chair's ink-dating plan notes that solvent-loss ink-age methods read only within roughly six to twenty-four months of writing, so if any slip was written recently the window closes during 2027; an early motion for leave to sample conflicts with holding the records for rebuttal. Static comparison of inks and hands does not expire the same way. Counsel decides which; nothing is requested without that decision | Court, on production of the originals (JA Rule requires the originals be brought for comparison) | Defense stage, or earlier by counsel's decision | H5 |
| P10 | Owners' side: written confirmation from Patricia (and if possible her sisters) that no remittance, cheque, deposit or accounting was ever received from Cesar or Salvador from 1992 to 2017; any bank record of the heirs for the period | Patricia; the family's bank | None | H2 |
| P11 | The Agcaoili office's file: any accounting, remittance or correspondence from Cesar between 1992 and 2019 (its 2019 letter says every post-2005 act was unauthorized) | Agcaoili & Associates | None | H2, H3 |
| P12 | Spec. Proc. 2156 record (Cesar's October 2016 petition): what he swore about the land, its income and his custody, three weeks after the deed | RTC Branch 41; quiet certified copy | Low | H1, H2 |
| P13 | The surveyor's file: Villafuerte's receipt 0460 and job record for the 2006 "subdivision survey"; whether a plan was ever filed from it | Villafuerte Eng'g & Surveying Office, Daet; quiet | Low | Partition timeline; "owned by the Heirs" in 2006 |
| P14 | Room rentals since 22 Aug 2025: on cross of Princess and Erwin, the number of rooms, the monthly rate, who collects. Gloria's own affidavit concedes "limited and intermittent" room rentals (purpose 8) | Defense witnesses on cross | Defense stage | Damages figure (Art. 2234) |
| P15 | Reconstruct the collector's full customer list from serial gaps: every missing number between 0019 and 0228 was issued to someone. Cross Salvador on who, and compare with Cesar's 1993 list of buyers on T-4497 (Annex AA) | Ledger (Appendix A); Salvador on cross | Defense stage | H3, H5; the class as it actually operated |

**Source:** `FORENSIC_AUDIT_BALANE_PAYMENTS_2026-10-18.html`  
**Section:** 8. What this gives counsel

---

#### 8. What this gives counsel

- **On Salvador's cross,** the receipts prove themselves: price after the deed (E1); more than the price (E2); numbers out of order (E3, E4); his own hand on a ₱20,000 "Down payment" in 2005 and nothing earlier (H3); the tax explanation against the SPA's own recital that estate taxes were paid in 1991 (R1).

- **Against the class claim,** the only record of a 1980s payment is a ledger the defense does not hold, said by a dead man to be with the plaintiff's former counsel. P1 settles where it is and what it says before trial.

- **Against ratification,** no owner touched a peso; the "estate" name on the forms is the agent's own BIR registration (P4).

- **Against good faith,** a buyer who paid ₱392,621 in taxes and penalties in 2020, mostly for lateness, on a deed from a man dead three years, with the first tax certificate issued against a title cancelled in 1999 (E14, E15).

- **For the damages figure,** Gloria's affidavit admits room rentals; the number and rate come from cross (P14).


**Document:** Forensic accounting audit of the Balane payments (Def. Exh. 5)  
**Source:** `FORENSIC_AUDIT_BALANE_PAYMENTS_2026-10-18.html`  
**Section:** 9. Corrections and verification flags

---

#### 9. Corrections and verification flags

- **Receipt 0219 is ₱50,000** ("Fifty Thousand Pesos," p. 32). An earlier note in this file claimed it had been verified at ₱40,000; that was wrong. The Track A table was right.

- **Receipt 0228:** words "Four Thousand Pesos"; figure ambiguous (4 or 5). Recorded at ₱4,000; the difference changes no conclusion.

- Day digits read from the images and open to a second reading: 2003-10-25 (could be 20); 2008-01-25; 2008-10-17; 2012-01-01; 2014-12-04; 2016-03-07; 2016-12-14; 2017-02-14. None affects a finding.

- **Reconciliation with the second chair's examination record** (CASE_FILE/PARTIES/RECEIPTS_EXAMINATION_2026-10-18): same 111 slips. Its total is ₱602,950 against this ledger's ₱602,750; the whole difference is receipt 0134 (10 Apr 2016), read there as ₱700 and here as ₱500: the figure is "√00" in a hand whose 5 is written as a check mark (compare 0219, words "Fifty," figure "√0,000"). Day digits re-read at 300 dpi and confirmed as in this ledger: 30 May 2005, 4 Apr 2005, 2 Aug 2005, 3 Sep 2006, 17 Feb 2007, 4 Aug 2007, 30 Apr 2009, 30 Dec 2010, 7 Mar 2016. Still open to a second reading: 12 May 2005, 27 May 2006, 4 Dec 2014, 14 Feb 2017. The surveyor's receipt is No. 0460 (read 0160 there). None of these changes a finding.

- The BIR column headings on the eCAR (p. 12) place "EXEMPT" under capital gains tax and the ₱287,866.73 under the withholding column; the reading is from the certified copy and should be confirmed from the BIR docket (P5).

- The fraud examination method and PSA 240 are stated in substance; the Tax Code sections are quoted (Appendix B); the Rules on Evidence provisions on originals, business entries and private documents were not fetched this session and are to be quoted by counsel.

**Document:** Forensic accounting audit of the Balane payments (Def. Exh. 5)  
**Source:** `FORENSIC_AUDIT_BALANE_PAYMENTS_2026-10-18.html`  
**Section:** Appendix B. Law relied on

---

#### Appendix B. Law relied on

**National Internal Revenue Code (R.A. 8424), Section 237**, as published on lawphil: "All persons subject to an internal revenue tax shall, for each sale or transfer of merchandise or for services rendered valued at Twenty-five pesos (P25.00) or more, issue duly registered receipts or sales or commercial invoices, prepared at least in duplicate, showing the date of transaction, quantity, unit cost and description of merchandise or nature of service: Provided, however, That in the case of sales, receipts or transfers in the amount of One hundred pesos (P100.00) or more, or regardless of the amount, where the sale or transfer is made by a person liable to value-added tax to another person also liable to value-added tax; or where the receipt is issued to cover payment made as rentals, commissions, compensations or fees, receipts or invoices shall be issued which shall show the name, business style, if any, and address of the purchaser, customer or client …"

**Section 238:** "All persons who are engaged in business shall secure from the Bureau of Internal Revenue an authority to print receipts or sales or commercial invoices before a printer can print the same. No authority to print receipts or sales or commercial invoices shall be granted unless the receipts or invoices to be printed are serially numbered and shall show, among other things, the name, business style, Taxpayer Identification Number (TIN) and business address of the person or entity to use the same … All persons who print receipt or sales or commercial invoices shall maintain a logbook/register of taxpayers who availed of their printing services."

Bearing: the estate receipts are registered official receipts of a taxpayer "engaged in business," printed under an authority obtained in 1998 and serially numbered; their duplicates must exist; the printer must have a logbook naming who ordered them. All of that is independent evidence the defense has not produced.

**_Culaba v. Court of Appeals_, G.R. No. 125862, 15 April 2004** (read on lawphil): payments made to a man in the creditor's uniform who "issued SMC liquidation receipts which looked genuine" did not discharge the debt, because the payer "did not ascertain the identity and authority of the said supervisor"; "A person dealing with an agent is put upon inquiry and must discover upon his peril the authority of the agent"; "Persons dealing with an assumed agent are bound at their peril to ascertain not only the fact of agency but also the nature and extent" of the authority. Bearing: receipts from a collector whose authority the payer never verified are not payment to the principal, however genuine the forms look.

**Exhibit E (the 1992 SPA), paragraph 5:** the estate and transfer taxes due on the estate "has been paid in full to the Bureau of Internal Revenue … on December 4, 1991." Salvador's "estate tax" explanation for part of the Balane money (S24) is tested against this recital.

**Civil Code, Article 2234** and the damages predicate are as set out in the memorandum pack; not repeated here.

**Section:** 1. Bottom line

---

#### 1. Bottom line

- Def. Exh. 5 holds **111 receipts**: 110 on collection forms, plus 1 surveyor's receipt (p.1).
- The 110 collection receipts total **₱602,950** [VERIFY: sum of scan readings]. The deed price is ₱250,000 (DX6 p.1).
- **Nine receipts, totalling ₱219,000, are dated after 29 Sep 2016.** That is the date of the deed that recites ₱250,000 "in hand paid" (DX6 p.1). The last is No. 0228, Feb 2017, "Full payment for lot in San Roque, Mercedes C.N." (p.33).
- Most receipts carry no name of a collector. The estate forms (2007–2017) print the label **"Authorized Collector"** with no name (pp.9–11, 20, 23–33). No receipt names Cesar.
- Two signature forms appear. "Von dela Fuente" is written in cursive on 4 receipts, Oct 2003 to Apr 2005 (pp.2–3). A stylized "[?]dela Fuente" appears on all the others from Nov 2004 to Feb 2017.
- In visible form, **neither** signature resembles Cesar's 1991–2007 full-name signature or his 2016–2017 short mark (GROK_REVIEW/CESAR_SIGNATURE_COMPARISON pp.1–4). This is a scan observation only.
- Salvador's judicial affidavit says he collected the payments and issued these receipts (Def. JA Salvador, S18–S22, pp.4–5). It does not say who signed "Von dela Fuente" or the stylized form [VERIFY on cross].
- Serial numbers on the estate forms run out of date order at least 6 times (§4.3).

#### 4. Aggregates

##### 4.1 Totals and payors

| Payor as written | Receipts | Total ₱ | Date range | Pages |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Efren Balane | 6 | 30,100 | 25 Oct 2003 – 17 May 2005 | 2–3 |
| Golly / Mrs. Golly Balane | 44 | 135,850 | 20 May 2005 – 20 Sep 2016 | 3–9, 20, 23–31 |
| Princess / Princes Balane | 45 estate + 1 survey | 103,000 + 3,000 | 28 Mar 2007 – Jan 2012 [VERIFY]; survey 29 Dec 2006 | 1, 9–22 |
| Ferdinand Balane | 1 | 8,000 [VERIFY figure] | 1 Jun 2008 | 13 |
| Gloria / Gloria H. / Mrs. Gloria Balane | 14 | 326,000 | 17 Nov 2014 – Feb 2017 | 28–33 |
| **All collection receipts** | **110** | **602,950** | 2003–2017 | 2–33 |

- **The price is ₱250,000, recited as "in hand paid" in the deed dated 29 Sep 2016 (DX6 p.1).** The receipts total more than twice that.
- The receipts in Gloria's name alone (₱326,000) also exceed the price.
- "Golly" and "Gloria" appear to be the same person [INFERRED].
- **Receipts in Princess's name:** 45 on collection forms, 28 Mar 2007 to Jan 2012 [VERIFY], ₱103,000. Eight are on HMWK forms (Nos. 0019–0083, 2007). The other 37 are on generic forms E, G and H. Most are ₱2,000 a month.
- **By year (₱):** 2003: 1,000. 2004: 3,000. 2005: 44,100. 2006: 27,000. 2007: 21,000. 2008: 29,000. 2009: 24,000. 2010: 16,000. 2011: 12,000. 2012: 15,000. 2013: 12,000. 2014: 69,000. 2015: none. 2016: 323,850. 2017: 9,000.
- **Large payments (₱10,000 or more):**
  - 6 Jan 2005: ₱20,000 "Down payment" (p.3).
  - Jan 2012: ₱15,000 (p.22).
  - Eleven HMWK receipts from 17 Nov 2014 to Dec 2016, totalling ₱366,000 (pp.28–32).

##### 4.2 Payments after the 29 Sep 2016 deed date

| No. | Date | Payor | ₱ | p. |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0222 | 30 Sep 2016 | Gloria H. Balane | 60,000 | 31 |
| 0223 | 7 Oct 2016 [VERIFY day] | Gloria H. Balane | 10,000 | 31 |
| 0219 | 18 Nov 2016 [VERIFY day] | Gloria H. Balane | 50,000 | 32 |
| 0218 | 27 Nov 2016 | Gloria H. Balane | 20,000 | 32 |
| 0224 | 1 Dec 2016 | Gloria H. Balane | 40,000 | 32 |
| 0225 | Dec 2016, day "1?" [VERIFY] | Gloria Balane | 30,000 | 32 |
| 0227 | 7 Jan 2017 | Gloria H. Balane | 2,000 | 33 |
| 0226 | 30 Jan 2017 | Gloria H. Balane | 3,000 | 33 |
| 0228 | Feb 2017, day "1?" [VERIFY] | Gloria H. Balane | 4,000 [VERIFY] | 33 |
| **Total** | | | **219,000** | |

- No. 0228 is written in the "month" field as "Full payment for lot in San Roque, Mercedes C.N.", with Lot Area "2,587 sq meters" (p.33).
- **The receipts dated before the deed fall short of covering ₱250,000 in Gloria's or Golly's name, unless the monthly ₱2,000 receipts from 2005–2014 are counted as installments** [INFERRED].
- For the months just before the deed:
  - From 17 Nov 2014 to 29 Sep 2016 the receipts total ₱163,850 (pp.28–31).
  - If No. 0222 (30 Sep 2016) is left out, they total ₱103,850.

##### 4.3 Serial order compared with date order (HMWK forms)

| Serial pair | Dates | p. | Observation |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0019 / 0061 | both 28 Mar 2007 | 9 | Two receipts the same day, 42 serials apart |
| 0122 / 0121 | 30 Jun 2014 / 31 Jul 2014 | 26–27 | Higher serial carries the earlier date |
| 0132 / 0124, 0128, 0130 | 9 Oct 2014 / 31 Oct, 17 Nov, 9 Dec 2014 | 27–28 | 0132 dated before three lower serials |
| 0130 / 0129 | 9 Dec 2014 / 31 Mar 2016 | 28–29 | 0129 dated 15 months after 0130 |
| 0133 / 0129 | 2 Mar 2016 / 31 Mar 2016 | 29 | 0129 dated after 0133 |
| 0220 / 0219 / 0218 | 6 Sep / 18 Nov / 27 Nov 2016 | 30, 32 | Serials run backwards against the dates |
| 0221–0223 / 0219, 0218 | 20 Sep–7 Oct 2016 / Nov 2016 | 31–32 | 0218 and 0219 dated after 0221–0223 |
| 0227 / 0226 | 7 Jan 2017 / 30 Jan 2017 | 33 | Higher serial carries the earlier date |

- **Pace of serials:**
  - 0019 to 0083 fall within Mar–Aug 2007 (pp.9–11).
  - 0104 [year VERIFY] and 0106 (Jul 2013) come next (pp.20, 23).
  - 0106 to 0136 run from Jul 2013 to May 2016 (pp.23–30).
  - Then the serials jump from 0136 (21 May 2016) to 0217 (7 Aug 2016 [VERIFY day]) (p.30).
- **Gaps in the serials** (numbers not in the exhibit): 0001–0018, 0020–0060, 0064, 0066–0069, 0071–0080, 0082, 0084–0103, 0105, 0111–0112, 0119–0120, 0125–0127, 0131, 0135, 0137–0216.
- **Caution.** The HMWK forms say "Occupant's Name". They may have been used for other occupants too, so a gap alone does not show a missing Balane receipt [INFERRED]. The stubs or duplicates of the booklets answer this. That is a production item (§8).
- **Same booklet across years [INFERRED].** The imprint reads "25 Bks. (50x2) 0001-1250", which suggests booklets of 50. On that reading:
  - one booklet (0101–0150) would hold No. 0104 (Feb 201? [VERIFY]) through No. 0136 (May 2016);
  - another (0201–0250) would hold Nos. 0217–0228 (Aug 2016–Feb 2017).
  - The printer's authority line ("Auth. No. 451-1998"; "RD64-016-99") is dated about 8 years before the first use (2007) [VERIFY what each number means].

##### 4.4 Wording: rent or purchase

- **Generic forms (2003–2012):** "lot in Mercedes, C.N." or "lot in San Roque, Mercedes" (e.g. pp.2–8, 12–21).
  - "Down payment for lot in San Roque, Mercedes, Cam. Norte": once, 6 Jan 2005 (p.3).
  - "lot occupied in Mercedes, Cam. Norte": 25 Jan 2008 (p.12).
  - "partial payment" box ticked on several form E receipts from 2009 to 2012 (pp.15, 18–22).
- **HMWK forms (2007–2017)** print "Occupant's Name" and "Payment for the month of".
  - The month field is filled with a month on 15 receipts, for example "March 2007" (p.9), "Oct. 2013" (p.24) and "(For May 2014)" (p.26).
  - The month field reads "Full payment …" on No. 0228 (p.33).
- **The word "rent" does not appear in handwriting on any receipt.**
- Salvador's affidavit says he collected both "bayad sa upa at ng mga partial payment para sa bilihan ng lupa" (Def. JA Salvador S18, p.4). It also says the Balanes were renting when the purchase talk began (S14, p.4). The set therefore appears to mix rent and purchase payments [INFERRED]. The receipts do not separate them on their face.

##### 4.5 Other checks requested

- **Receipt dated before 20 Oct 2003:** none. The earliest is 25 Oct 2003 (p.2).
- **Receipt naming Cesar:** none. No receipt carries "Cesar" in print or handwriting.
- **"Authorized Collector":** printed on all 43 HMWK receipts. No name is printed or written beside it (pp.9–11, 20, 23–33). Forms B, E and H print "Authorized Signature". Forms A, C, D, F and G print "Signature".
- **Gaps in the monthly run:** Jan 2006; Jul 2005; Sep–Dec 2007; Mar 2010; Sep–Nov 2010; Aug–Dec 2011; Feb 2012–Jun 2013 (except No. 0104 if it is 2013); all of 2015.

**Section:** 4.6 Serial density (test requested by Jonathan)

---

#### 4.6 Serial density (test requested by Jonathan)

**Method.** The HMWK receipts in the exhibit were put in serial order. For each consecutive pair, the table compares the serial gap with the day gap between the dates written on them. A serial gap of 1 means no number is missing between the two. All payors in the exhibit are Balanes. Full pair list: CSV (serial column) and the working table below.

| Run (serials) | Dates | Serials in run | Present in exhibit | Missing numbers | Days spanned | Pages |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0019 – 0083 | 28 Mar – 29 Aug 2007 | 65 | 8 | 57 | 154 | 9–11 |
| 0083 → 0104 | 29 Aug 2007 → 28 Feb 201? | gap of 21 | — | 20 | 1,279 if 2011 [VERIFY year] | 11, 20 |
| 0104 → 0106 | 28 Feb 201? → 31 Jul 2013 | gap of 2 | — | 1 (0105) | 884 if 2011; about 150 if 2013 [VERIFY] | 20, 23 |
| **0106 – 0136** | **31 Jul 2013 – 21 May 2016** | **31** | **22** | **9** | **1,025 (about 34 months)** | **23–30** |
| **0128 – 0134** | **17 Nov 2014 – 10 Apr 2016** | **7** | **6** | **1 (0131)** | **510** | **28–29** |
| **0136 → 0217** | **21 May 2016 → 7 Aug 2016** [VERIFY days] | **gap of 81** | — | **80** | **78** | **30** |
| **0217 – 0228** | **7 Aug 2016 – Feb 2017** | **12** | **12** | **0** | **about 188** | **30–33** |

**Consecutive pairs spanning long periods (serial gap of 1 or 2):**

| Pair | Serial gap | Day gap | p. |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0128 (17 Nov 2014) → 0129 (31 Mar 2016) | 1 | 500 | 28–29 |
| 0132 (9 Oct 2014) → 0133 (2 Mar 2016) | 1 | 510 | 27, 29 |
| 0104 (28 Feb 201?) → 0106 (31 Jul 2013) | 2 | 884 if the year is 2011 [VERIFY] | 20, 23 |
| 0217 (7 Aug 2016) → 0218 (27 Nov 2016) | 1 | 112 | 30, 32 |
| 0122 (30 Jun 2014) → 0123 (31 Aug 2014) | 1 | 62 | 26–27 |
| 0123 (31 Aug 2014) → 0124 (31 Oct 2014) | 1 | 61 | 27–28 |

- From Jul 2013 to Feb 2017, the serials advance at roughly **one number per Balane payment**, with no or few numbers between them. That holds even across gaps of 500 days and more.
- One reading is that this booklet run was used only for the Balane payments. Another is that the receipts were written up in a batch, not as each payment came in [INFERRED; both readings].
- The out-of-order dates (§4.3) fall inside these same runs.
- By contrast, the 2007 run shows 57 numbers missing in 5 months (pp.9–11). The jump at 0136 → 0217 shows 80 numbers missing in about 11 weeks (p.30).
- A third possibility: other occupants used other booklets, and these runs sat in a booklet kept for this lot [INFERRED]. The stubs or duplicates of every booklet in the 0001–1250 series answer which reading holds (§8, Q6).

**Section:** 4.7 The jump from about 100 to about 200 (noted by Jonathan)

#### 4.7 The jump from about 100 to about 200 (noted by Jonathan)

- **Where:** No. **0136**, dated **21 May 2016** (day "21" [VERIFY]), "Gloria Balane", ₱1,000, is followed by No. **0217**, dated **Aug 2016** (day "3" or "7" [VERIFY]), "Mrs. Gloria Balane", ₱16,000. Both are on Def. Exh. 5 p.30.
- That is **81 serials in about 74–78 days.** Before the jump, the run 0106–0136 used 31 serials in 34 months. After it, the run 0217–0228 used 12 serials in about 6 months.
- **Form check (p.30, 200 dpi):** the two receipts show the **same** heading ("HEIRS OF MARY W. KEESEY ESTATE / Vicente Basit Extension, Daet, Cams. Norte / TIN-[TIN withheld]-Non Vat"), the same layout and field labels, the same "Authorized Collector" label, and the **same printer imprint** ("RD64-016-99 25 Bks. (50x2) 0001-1250 / 4C's Press-Daet, CN Auth. No. 451-1998"). No difference in printer, authority number or layout is visible on the scan [VERIFY on originals: paper stock, typeface, perforation, and the numbering-machine font].
- **Booklet arithmetic [INFERRED].** If the booklets hold 50 numbers each (0001–0050, 0051–0100 …):
  - No. 0136 sits in booklet 3 (0101–0150).
  - No. 0217 sits in booklet 5 (0201–0250).
  - All of booklet 4 (0151–0200), the rest of booklet 3 (0137–0150) and the start of booklet 5 (0201–0216) are missing. That is 80 numbers.
  - "(50x2)" may mean duplicate copies [VERIFY].
- **Other changes at the jump** (from the face of the receipts):
  - No. 0217 is the first receipt to state a lot area, "2,587" (the deed area, DX6 p.1). This matches TRACK_A_KNOWLEDGE l.183.
  - From No. 0217 on, the amounts move from ₱1,000–₱3,500 to ₱10,000–₱60,000 (except 0220, 0226, 0227 and 0228).
- **The open question: where are the about 80 receipts numbered 0137–0216?** Were they issued to other occupants, voided, kept blank, or never used? The record does not say.

#### 4.8 Search for receipts to other payors, and the size of the estate

- **The exhibit:** all 110 collection receipts name a Balane payor (Efren, Golly/Gloria, Princess, Ferdinand). None is to another payor.
- **The case files on the Mac:** text search of CV26360_COMPLETE (CASE_FILE, research) and of the ESTATE_SALES_REVIEW, MWK-001, LandTek-Corp and CLAUDE_GROK_HANDOFF folders was run for "KEESEY ESTATE", "Authorized Collector", "4C's Press" and "[TIN withheld]". It found **no receipt to any non-Balane payor.** Every hit was an analysis note about Def. Exh. 5.
  - **Limit:** image-only PDFs other than Def. Exh. 5 and Salvador's affidavit were not OCR'd [VERIFY].
- **Estate size context** (from the record, not from the receipts):
  - The 1992 SPA covers T-4497, Lot 2 of Psd-12802, Mercedes (Exh. E ¶3; SPA_CLAUSE_DISSECTION l.47).
  - Cesar's other sales of portions appear in the signature exemplars: Ramirez 1996, Pascual 1998, Hansol 1999, Vela 2000, Nacasa 2002, Victa 2004 (CESAR_SIGNATURE_COMPARISON pp.2–3). Era 2005 is on Exh. B p.3.
  - Salvador says Cesar dealt with "mga nagrerenta sa mga bahagi ng lupain", in the plural (Def. JA Salvador S16, p.4).
  - Exh. N (Shirley De Leon) describes other occupants.
  - So the estate had other buyers and occupants [INFERRED from these sources]. On that basis, a printed estate receipt series of 1,250 numbers (25 booklets) used for one family only would call for an explanation [INFERRED].
