#### 5. Jonathan's observations, tested on the scans

##### 5.1 Observation 1 (Jonathan): the "Von" receipts appear in the same black ink across years

**What the scan shows:**

| Receipt | p. | Form / paper | Ink appearance on scan | Stroke appearance |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 25 Oct 2003, Efren, ₱1,000 | 2 | A, beige | Dark, black to blue-black | Medium line; cursive "Von dela Fuente" on the line |
| 27 Dec 2004, Efren, ₱1,000 | 2 | B, beige | Dark, black to blue-black | Same general form |
| 6 Jan 2005, Efren, ₱20,000 | 3 | B, beige | Dark, black to blue-black | Same general form |
| 9 Apr 2005, Efren, ₱2,500 | 3 | B, beige | Dark, black to blue-black | Same general form |

- All four "Von" receipts look like the same dark ink on the scan. They span **18 months** (Oct 2003 – Apr 2005), not many years. The "Von" form does not appear after 9 Apr 2005.
- On each receipt, the body entries and the signature look like the same ink [VERIFY].

**Pen groups across the whole set (visual only):**

| Group | Appearance | Receipts |
|---|---|---|
| G1 | Dark black / blue-black | Oct 2003 – Apr 2005. The 19 Nov 2004 "S" receipt shows a green-black tint (p.2) |
| G2 | Blue / navy | May 2005 – Dec 2006 (pp.3–8), except G3 |
| G3 | **Red** | 3 Feb 2006 and 28 Feb 2006 (p.6) |
| G4 | Violet / purple | Jan 2007 – 2011 (pp.9–21), including the 2007 HMWK receipts. Purple smudges on p.19 |
| G5 | Dark green-black | Jan 2012 (p.22) |
| G6 | Black | HMWK 2013–2017 (pp.23–33). Nos. 0107 and 0123 look violet |

- **Scan limits.** A median colour check of the ink pixels was run on 10 signatures. The result followed the paper tint and the phone processing, so no conclusion is drawn from it. **A scan cannot show that two entries share an ink, or how old an ink is. That is for the examiner, on the originals (§8, Q1–Q3).**
- **As an anomaly item:** for the questions that matter most, the black HMWK run (2013–2017) and the post-deed cluster (Sep 2016 – Feb 2017, pp.31–33) are better targets for an ink-sameness test than the four "Von" receipts.

##### 5.2 Observation 2 (Jonathan): receipts issued as "Heirs of Mary Worrick"

- **Exact printed wording:** "HEIRS OF MARY W. KEESEY ESTATE / Vicente Basit Extension, Daet, Cams. Norte / TIN-[TIN withheld]-Non Vat" (e.g. p.9, p.32). The forms do not say "Heirs of Mary Worrick" in full. The surveyor's receipt (p.1) writes "H[ei]rs of Worrick Keesey" by hand.
- **The 43 HMWK receipts:**
  - 8 in 2007: Nos. 0019–0083, Princess, 28 Mar – 29 Aug 2007 (pp.9–11).
  - 1 dated Feb 201? (No. 0104, Golly; year digit overwritten [VERIFY]) (p.20).
  - 34 from 31 Jul 2013 to Feb 2017: Golly, then Gloria (pp.23–33).
  - Total ₱432,850.
- **The last HMWK receipt** is No. 0228, Feb 2017 (day read as "1?"; [VERIFY]). It is also the last receipt of any kind (p.33).
- **Cesar's date of death in the record:** 21 Jun 2017 (PB §2, cited in PARTY_DOSSIER l.800).
  - That date is still **[VERIFY]**: the PSA death certificate is not in hand (09_REVIEW/T2_FACT_AUDIT.md l.119; 08_GAPS_TO_OBTAIN/T5_GAPS.md G7).
  - Salvador's affidavit says only "Patay na po", with no date (Def. JA Salvador S5, p.2).
- **Gap:** from the last receipt to 21 Jun 2017 is about **130 days**. The range is 127–140 days, depending on whether the day of No. 0228 is read as 1, 11 or 14 Feb [VERIFY].
  - The final cluster (Nos. 0217–0228: Aug 2016 – Feb 2017, ₱287,650) ends about four months before that date.
  - Whether the timing has any cause is **not** shown by the record [INFERRED: timing only].
- **Who the heirs are, per the record.** The 16 Mar 1992 SPA names GERALDINE K. HOPPE, PATRICIA K. ZSCHOCHE and MARCIA ELLEN KEESEY as "the lawful heirs of the late MARY WORRICK KEESEY" (Exh. E, CTC p.2, ¶1, as transcribed in 08_GAPS_TO_OBTAIN/SPA_CLAUSE_DISSECTION.md l.44–45).
- **Is the heading consistent with Cesar acting under the 1992 SPA?** Points from the record:
  - The SPA ¶7 empowers Cesar "to receive the proceeds of the aforementioned sale **in our collective behalf**". The sale it refers to is a sale to the Llamanzares contract-to-sell / partial-payment class, "after prior consultation with us" (Exh. E CTC p.3 ¶7; SPA_CLAUSE_DISSECTION l.52).
  - The receipts are issued in the name of an "ESTATE", with a TIN and a Daet street address. They are not issued in the names of the three principals or "by Cesar M. de la Fuente, Attorney-in-Fact". **The receipts do not identify any agent.**
  - The 2016 deed has Cesar act "as Attorney-In-Fact of the Heirs of Mary W. Keesey" (Exh. H; 08_GAPS_TO_OBTAIN/CONSULTATION_GAP_PLAN.md H1). That is a different style from the receipt heading [INFERRED].
  - The 1992 SPA ¶7 says nothing of collecting monthly occupancy payments. Whether ¶8 (general powers) or the 1991 SPA covers rent collection is a legal question [VERIFY; Art. 1877].
  - The TIN printed on the receipts reads **[TIN withheld]**. The TIN printed under Cesar's name on the 28 Mar 2017 Deed of Confirmation reads **[TIN withheld]** (CESAR_SIGNATURE_COMPARISON p.4). On the scans they differ [VERIFY both on certified copies]. Who registered TIN [TIN withheld], and for what entity, is not in the record [VERIFY: BIR].
  - The HMWK forms run from 2007, which is after the 15 Aug 2005 revocation. Whether the principals knew of or allowed receipts in the estate's name is an open question (CONSULTATION_GAP_PLAN Q15 to Patricia).

#### 6. Collector signatures, compared (visible differences only)

**This is not an expert opinion. It is not a finding on who signed. It describes shapes visible on phone scans.**

![Collector signatures from Def. Exh. 5 (crops at 200 dpi)](RECEIPTS_EXAMINATION_SIGPANEL_2026-10-18.png)

| Item | Source | What is visible |
|---|---|---|
| V form | Def. Exh. 5 pp.2–3 (4 receipts, 2003–2005) | Legible "Von dela Fuente". Open capital "V" and lowercase "on"; "dela" separate; "Fuente" with a looped capital F. About the width of the signature line. |
| S form | Def. Exh. 5 pp.2–33 (106 receipts, Nov 2004 – Feb 2017) | One large angular capital (reads as "G", "S" or "C" [VERIFY]) with a long lead-in stroke from the left, joined to "dela", then "Fuente" with a tall F. Same general shape from 2004 to 2017 on the scans. |
| Cesar, 1991–2007 | CESAR_SIGNATURE_COMPARISON pp.1–3 (1991 SPA specimen; 1996, 1998, 1999 deeds; 2002, 2004 deeds; 2007 affidavit) | Full name "Cesar M. de la Fuente": open-loop capital C, "M." with a period, long looped F rising above the line, 3–4.5 in wide. |
| Cesar, 2016–2017 | CESAR_SIGNATURE_COMPARISON p.4 (27 Sep 2016 affidavit; 29 Sep 2016 deed; 28 Mar 2017 confirmation, two copies) | Short mark, about 0.8–1.2 in, reading "a/c", "af" or "cfle". No legible surname letters. |

**Visible differences:**

- Neither receipt form shows "Cesar" or "M.". The V form starts with "Von". The S form starts with one capital.
- The S form keeps writing "dela Fuente" letters on receipts from Sep 2016 to Feb 2017 (pp.30–33). Over the same months, Cesar's signatures on the 27 and 29 Sep 2016 instruments and the 28 Mar 2017 confirmation are a short mark with no surname letters (comparison p.4).
- The V form and the S form differ from each other in the first element and in letter design. Both appear in Nov–Dec 2004 (p.2), and the V form stops after Apr 2005.
- Whether "Von" is a name used by Salvador, by Cesar or by someone else is not stated in the record [VERIFY on cross: Salvador S19–S22 says only "Nag-iissue po ako ng mga resibo"].
- **No exemplars of Salvador's handwriting are in the file.** A comparison with Salvador cannot be made from the record (§8).

**Section:** 8. Formal examination option for Barandon

---

#### 8. Formal examination option for Barandon

**Status: a Barandon decision. Jonathan has approved pursuing it through Barandon. Held for rebuttal timing per Jonathan.** A draft motion is in DRAFT_MOTION_PRODUCTION_EXAMINATION_DEF_EXH5_2026-10-18.md/.pdf. A draft request for subpoenas duces tecum (Rule 21) for the complete booklets, stubs, duplicates, covers (printer, ATP) and any collection ledger, addressed to the defendants as custodians and to Salvador as collector, is in DRAFT_SUBPOENA_DUCES_TECUM_BOOKLETS_DEF_EXH5_2026-10-18.md/.pdf. Both are held and unsigned.

**Rules (quoted from the local text; [VERIFY 2019 text] on each):**

- **Rule 132 §22** (1989 evidence text, local file ev.txt): "The handwriting of a person may be proved by any witness who believes it to be the handwriting of such person because he has seen the person write, or has seen writing purporting to be his upon which the witness has acted or been charged, and has thus acquired knowledge of the handwriting of such person. Evidence respecting the handwriting may also be given by a comparison, made by the witness or the court, with writings admitted or treated as genuine by the party against whom the evidence is offered, or proved to be genuine to the satisfaction of the judge."
- **Rule 130 §49** (1989 numbering; renumbered in 2019 [VERIFY]): "The opinion of a witness on a matter requiring special knowledge, skill, experience or training which he [is] shown to posses[s], may be received in evidence."
- **Rule 29 §3** (1997 text): on refusal to obey a Rule 27 order, the court may make "such orders in regard to the refusal as are just". These include an order that "the contents of the paper … shall be taken to be established … in accordance with the claim of the party obtaining the order", or "prohibiting him from introducing in evidence designated documents".

**Standards of comparison needed (Rule 132 §22: "admitted or treated as genuine … or proved to be genuine"):**

- **Salvador.** There are no exemplars in the file. Sources:
  - his signature on his Judicial Affidavit (Def. JA Salvador p.7), as an admitted writing;
  - writings he identifies on the stand;
  - request writing (dictated specimens) taken in court if the court allows [VERIFY practice].
  - PNP's charter asks for at least five standards from within five years before and after the questioned writing (fg.pnp.gov.ph Citizen's Charter 2026, QDED item 1). For receipts from 2003 to 2017, that means **dated Salvador writings across 2003–2017.** Possible sources: his tax payments for the estate (S24–S25), barangay, LTO or bank forms [VERIFY availability].
- **Cesar.** The 1991–2007 and 2016–2017 instruments in CESAR_SIGNATURE_COMPARISON. These are certified copies. **Originals or notarial-register entries are needed for a lab** (pp.1–4).
- **"Von".** Ask Salvador whether "Von dela Fuente" is his name or signature. If he says it is, ask for standards of that form.

**Non-destructive first.** Examination under magnification, UV and IR, ESDA (indentations), and comparison of form printing. Ink sampling (plugs or solvent extraction) is destructive and only with leave (draft motion ¶¶ on custody).

**Ink dating, a limit to know.** Labs that do solvent-loss (dynamic) dating say it shows only whether ink went on paper within roughly the last 2 years (Riley Welch LaPorte, rileywelch.com/ink-dating-2) or 3–3.5 years (Speckin, as reported by legalnews.com DataId=1376436). It **cannot** separate a 2005 entry from a 2016 entry tested today. It can test whether entries were written recently (for example, after the 2025–2026 suit [VERIFY filing date]). Static dating (ink formulation and first commercial availability) can test for anachronism. No Philippine lab offering either method was found [unknown].

**Section:** 9. Examiner question list

---

#### 9. Examiner question list

1. **Ink sameness:** Are the entries and signatures on each receipt in one ink? Across receipts, which share an ink formulation? Priority groups:
   - (a) the four "Von" receipts (2003–2005, pp.2–3) [Jonathan obs. 1];
   - (b) the black HMWK run 2013–2017 (pp.23–33);
   - (c) the post-deed cluster Nos. 0222–0228 (pp.31–33);
   - (d) receipts in different years that share a page.
2. **Relative ink age** (solvent loss; only with leave): were any entries written within the 2–3.5 years before testing? Any entry written in an ink first sold after its stated date (static method)?
3. **Sequence of strokes and alterations:** the struck "August" (p.16), "April 31" (p.18), the overwritten year on No. 0104 (p.20), the month-only dates (p.10), "Start 10k-" (p.16). Were these written at the same sitting as the rest of the receipt?
4. **Paper and printing:**
   - Printer name and authority on the HMWK forms ("4C's Press-Daet", "Auth. No. 451-1998", "RD64-016-99", "25 Bks. (50x2) 0001-1250"): when was this stock printed, against entry dates of 2007–2017?
   - Do Nos. 0136 and 0217 come from the same print run and booklet type?
   - The same questions for generic forms A–H: are any form designs or papers later than their entry dates?
5. **Serial order compared with date order:** are the serials machine-numbered in one run? Do the receipts show stub tear lines or indentations consistent with being written in serial order, or in a batch?
6. **Booklet completeness (Jonathan's density test):** do the stubs or duplicates of booklets 0001–0250 exist? Who are the payors on numbers not in Def. Exh. 5, especially **0137–0216**? Do the indentations on the receipts beneath match the next receipt in the exhibit?
7. **Handwriting:**
   - Collector signatures (V form and S form) and the body entries, compared with Salvador's standards and with Cesar's standards.
   - Is one writer responsible for both the V and the S forms?
   - Are the body entries and the signature on each receipt by one writer?
8. **Heading and TIN** (Jonathan obs. 2): is the "HEIRS OF MARY W. KEESEY ESTATE … TIN-[TIN withheld]" heading printed in one impression with the form, or added? Are the TIN digits consistent across all 43 forms? (The BIR registrant of that TIN is a records question, not a lab question [VERIFY].)
9. **Surveyor's receipt (p.1):** form, printer and signature, compared with the Villafuerte office's standards, if put in issue.

**Section:** 12. Timing note (Barandon's decision)

---

#### 12. Timing note (Barandon's decision)

- **Surprise compared with timeliness.**
  - Filing a Rule 27 motion tells the defense, before rebuttal, what will be attacked. That gives them time to prepare Salvador and to find explanations for the serial gaps.
  - Holding it to rebuttal keeps the surprise. But the court may find it late, as discovery after pre-trial. And 00_DEADLINES.md l.22 records "Rule 30 §5(f): no 'rebuttal' backdoor" for case-in-chief matter.
- **Record dates:**
  - Plaintiff closes and makes the formal offer on **9 Dec 2026** (00_DEADLINES.md l.22).
  - Rebuttal dates are to be requested after 10 Mar (l.26).
  - The internal mark for a Rule 27 production motion was **21 Oct 2026** (l.51).
  - The pre-trial order of 13 May 2026 is not in hand (l.42) [VERIFY: whether it reserved discovery or listed Def. Exh. 5 originals].
- **A middle route [CONDITIONAL]:** ask in court, when Def. Exh. 5 is identified or offered, that the originals be produced for comparison (Rule 130 original-document rule [VERIFY 2019 numbering]). Then move under Rule 27 if they are not produced.
- **Lab lead time:** PNP QDED lists about 10–15 working days after receipt. Add court scheduling and custody arrangements.
- **Procedure check [VERIFY]:** if the Rules on Summary Procedure applied, a discovery motion would be barred. The case has run under regular procedure (pre-trial, judicial affidavits, formal offer), so this is likely not an issue, but confirm.
- **No step outside court.** No contact with defendants, counsel or examiners by Jonathan. All requests go through Barandon.

**Section:** 14. Audit protocol (ordered)

---

#### 14. Audit protocol (ordered)

Steps 2–5 are **Barandon decisions**. Nothing in this section has been done outside this file. Nobody has been contacted.

##### Step 1: Internal scan audit (done; this file)

- Done: per-receipt transcription (111 receipts, CSV); aggregates; serial density; signature comparison of visible forms only; anomaly register.
- Limits: phone scan; readings tagged [VERIFY]. No originals were seen.
- **Output:** the questions for steps 2–5.

##### Step 2: External verification of BIR Authority to Print (ATP) and printer records (Barandon decision)

**What the BIR rules require** (quoted from the sources; [VERIFY] against the official BIR text):

- **RR No. 18-2012, Sec. on ATP** (SC E-Library, https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/10/68520):
  - "All persons, whether private or government, who are engaged in business shall secure/apply from the BIR an Authority to Print principal and supplementary receipts/invoices."
  - "The approved ATP shall be valid only upon full usage of the inclusive serial numbers … or five (5) years from issuance of the same, whichever comes first."
  - "The replicate copy of the ATP issued shall be printed at the inside back portion of the cardboard cover of each booklet/pad."
  - "Only BIR Accredited Printers shall have the exclusive authority to print principal and supplementary receipts/invoices."
- **RMO No. 12-2013** (https://www.competitive.org.ph/doingbusiness/reference/downloads/Summit/forupload/RDSAB/SAB_REFERENCE11_BIR_Revenue_Memorandum_Order_12_2013_On_Processing_of_Authority_To_Print_etc.pdf):
  - The bottom of each receipt must show "Name, address and TIN of the accredited printer; Accreditation number and the date of accreditation …; ATP number, OCN, date issued … and valid until …; … Approved inclusive serial numbers".
  - Transitory rule: "All unused/unissued principal and supplementary receipts/invoices printed prior to January 19, 2013 (effectivity of RR No. 18-2012) … shall be valid until June 30, 2013", with surrender to the RDO "on or before July 10, 2013."
- **RR No. 6-2022** (https://mtfcounsel.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/07/RR-No.-6-2022-Removal-of-Five-year-Validity-Period-on-Receipts-or-Invoices.pdf) removed the five-year validity from 16 Jul 2022 (RMC 123-2022, https://gppcpas.com.ph/2022/09/rmc-no-123-2022-clarifications-on-the-provisions-of-revenue-regulations-no-6-2022-relative-to-the-removal-of-the-five-5-year-validity-period-on-receipts-invoices/). It does not reach receipts issued in 2013–2017.
- **Before 2013,** the ATP rules came from older issuances (for example, the NIRC sec. 238 ATP requirement and RR 7-95 [VERIFY]). Not researched further.

**What the receipts show, on their face (no characterization):**

- **HMWK forms** (43 receipts): the imprint shows a printer name ("4C's Press-Daet, CN"), "Auth. No. 451-1998", "RD64-016-99" and a serial range "0001-1250". It shows **no printer TIN, no ATP date, no "valid until" and no accreditation date** in the form required by RMO 12-2013.
  - On the face, the stock predates 19 Jan 2013 [INFERRED from the "1998"/"99" numbers].
  - **34 of these receipts are dated 31 Jul 2013 to Feb 2017,** after the 30 Jun 2013 cut-off for pre-2013 stock (pp.23–33). Whether that cut-off applied to this issuer depends on whether it was a registered taxpayer "engaged in business" [VERIFY].
  - "RD64" matches **RDO No. 64**, which covers Daet and Mercedes. That office was in Daet until RAO 001-16 (6 Jun 2016) moved it to Talisay (https://www.digest.ph/taxation/transfer-and-renaming-of-revenue-district-office-no-64-to-talisay-camarines-norte) [INFERRED].
- **Generic forms** (67 receipts, forms A–H): **no printer, no ATP, no TIN, no serial on the face.** The record shows nothing about who registered them [noted, not characterized].
- **TIN on HMWK forms:** [TIN withheld], "Non Vat". Whether a TIN is registered to an "Heirs of Mary W. Keesey Estate", and since when, is unknown [VERIFY].

**What a BIR ATP record would show:** the taxpayer name, TIN and address; the printer and its accreditation; the ATP number and date; the document type; the number of booklets; and the **approved serial range**. Here that is expected to be "0001-1250", 25 booklets [VERIFY].

**How to obtain it [VERIFY procedure]:**

- (a) Subpoena duces tecum to the **Revenue District Officer, RDO No. 64, Talisay, Camarines Norte** (BIR Bldg., Maharlika Highway; listed email [email withheld] per thepinoyofw.com list). It would cover the ATP application and ATP for TIN [TIN withheld], the registration record (COR / Form 1901 or 1903) for that TIN, and the printer's accreditation and printing report for "4C's Press-Daet".
- (b) Taxpayer records are confidential under the NIRC [VERIFY sec. 270 / sec. 71]. A plain records request from a non-taxpayer will likely be refused, so a court subpoena is the expected route.
- (c) The printer, if it still exists, may hold printing job records: a subpoena to "4C's Press, Daet" [VERIFY existence].

**Signal to the defense: low but not zero.** A subpoena to BIR is issued in the case and appears in the record. It does not require the defendants to produce anything. It tests the booklets without first asking the defense for them.

**Timing:** BIR response time unknown. If the records are to be used in the case-in-chief, they should be in before the **9 Dec 2026** close (00_DEADLINES.md l.22). If they are held for rebuttal, the timing risk in §12 applies.

##### Step 3: Subpoena the whole booklets (Barandon decision)

- Draft: DRAFT_SUBPOENA_DUCES_TECUM_BOOKLETS_DEF_EXH5_2026-10-18.md/.pdf.
- Addressed to the defendants as custodians, and to Salvador as collector.
- Covers: all receipts; duplicate and triplicate stubs; covers. The inside back cover should carry the ATP replicate under RR 18-2012 if the stock was ATP-printed. Also any collection ledger, the collector's authority, and the TIN record.
- **Order:** after step 2 if possible, so that the ATP serial range is known before the booklets come in.
- **Timing:** serving it discloses the line of attack (§12).

##### Step 4: Court-ordered forensic examination with chain of custody (Barandon decision)

- Draft: DRAFT_MOTION_PRODUCTION_EXAMINATION_DEF_EXH5_2026-10-18.md/.pdf. Rule 27; non-destructive first; sampling only with leave; custody terms; costs on the movant.
- **Authentication framework** (1989 numbering [VERIFY 2019 text]):
  - Rule 132 §20: "Before any private document offered as authentic is received in evidence, its due execution and authenticity must be proved either: (a) By anyone who saw the document executed or written; or (b) By evidence of the genuineness of the signature or handwriting of the maker."
  - Rule 132 §22: comparison with writings "admitted or treated as genuine … or proved to be genuine to the satisfaction of the judge" (§8).
  - Rule 130 (original document rule and expert opinion) [VERIFY 2019 sections].
- **Examiner:** PNP QDED, or a court-approved private examiner (§10). Questions: §9. Cost: §11.
- **Timing:** PNP lists about 10–15 working days after receipt, plus court scheduling.

##### Step 5: Request for admission and cross lock-in (Barandon decision)

- **Rule 26 §1** [VERIFY 2019 text]: "At any time after issues have been joined, a party may file and serve upon any other party … a written request for the admission by the latter of the genuineness of any material and relevant document described in and exhibited with the request or of the truth of any material and relevant matter of fact set forth in the request."
- **§2:** matters are "deemed admitted" unless denied under oath within the period, which must be at least 15 days.
- **Proposed RFA items** (to defendants; for Barandon to choose; they tie in with dossier 7.28 / RFA list):
  1. Receipts Nos. 0137–0216 of the "Heirs of Mary W. Keesey Estate" series were not issued to any defendant.
  2. No receipt in Def. Exh. 5 bears the name of Cesar M. de la Fuente.
  3. Nine receipts totalling ₱219,000 in Def. Exh. 5 bear dates after 29 Sep 2016.
  4. No. 0228, marked "Full payment", is dated in February 2017.
  5. The ₱2,000 monthly payments from 2005 to 2014 were for occupancy of the premises.
  6. Defendants have no written appointment of Salvador Osum dela Fuente as collector for the heirs.
- **Cross lock-in:** the §13 questions for Salvador and Gloria. Ask them **before** the examiner's report, so the witnesses fix their account of the booklets, serials and signatures first.
- **Timing:** the RFA tips the defense as much as the subpoena does. Rule 26 §5 bars later evidence on facts that should have been the subject of an RFA, unless the court allows [VERIFY]. The internal mark for an RFA was 21 Oct 2026 (00_DEADLINES.md l.51).

### Auditing Def. Exh. 5 (Receipts): Philippine Law and Practice

**Method.** Each authority is marked **VERIFIED** only if I read its full text today (lawphil.net, the SC E-Library, or a verbatim rendering of the official rule text). Everything else is marked **UNVERIFIED** and is not relied on. Facts about Def. Exh. 5 come from `RECEIPTS_EXAMINATION_2026-10-18.md/.csv`, which is a scan-based record and not an expert opinion. The receipts are called "questioned documents" throughout.

**Document:** [Grok desk] Auditing Def. Exh. 5: Philippine law and practice  
**Source:** `CASE_FILE/PARTIES/RECEIPTS_AUDIT_LAW_2026-10-18.md`  
**Section:** 1. Bottom line

---

#### 1. Bottom line

1. **The burden is on the defense.** Payment is their defense, so they must prove it "with legal certainty" (*Bognot*, *Joseph*). Receipts are the best evidence of payment but not the only evidence, and they are not conclusive (*Multi-International*). Once the defense offers receipts, the burden of going forward shifts to us, so we need some evidence of non-payment or of irregularity. The burden of proof itself does not shift (Rule 131 §1).
2. **Our strongest law point is Art. 1240 plus *Culaba*.** A payor must "ascertain not only the fact of agency but also the nature and extent of authority," and if that authority is disputed, "the burden of proof is upon them to establish it." No receipt names Cesar or any appointed collector.
3. **Our strongest new documentary point is BIR RR 18-2012 §5.** All unused receipts printed before the regulation "shall be valid until June 30, 2013." The estate forms carry a 1998 printer authority. **All 34 estate-form receipts dated 31 Jul 2013 to Feb 2017 are on forms that, by that rule, were no longer valid when they were dated.** This is a regulatory and credibility point, not an automatic bar to proving payment. It is cheap to prove through BIR records, and it does not tip off the defense.
4. **The handwriting law favors a court comparison.** The judge may compare handwriting personally, and an expert is "not mandatory or indispensable" (*Jimenez*, *Pontaoe*, *Gamido*). The comparison has to be against writings "admitted or treated as genuine" by the party against whom the evidence is offered, or "proved to be genuine to the satisfaction of the judge" (Rule 132 §22). **We hold no Salvador exemplars yet.** That is the gap to close.
5. **Rebuttal is a valid route, with limits.** Under Rule 30 §5(f), the parties "may then respectively adduce rebutting evidence only," unless the court allows otherwise. *Abalos* holds that a "new matter" introduced by the defense may be met with "positive evidence" on rebuttal. The receipts attack qualifies because the receipts are the defense's own exhibit. The authority, consultation and revocation case must still stand in chief.
6. **Two presumptions cut against us and have to be answered.** Rule 131 §3(i) presumes "prior rents or installments had been paid when a receipt for the later one is produced," and §3(u) presumes "a writing is truly dated." The serial-order and date anomalies are what rebut §3(u). The authority gap (Art. 1240) defeats §3(i), because the presumption only helps if the later receipt was itself a valid payment to an authorized person.

**Section:** 2. Rules (exact text, VERIFIED)

---

#### 2. Rules (exact text, VERIFIED)

Source for the rules text: the official 2019 text as reproduced verbatim at vivasandnobleslawoffice.com/codes/rules-of-court/… (each page states it was checked against the SC text). **Barandon should confirm against the SC-published A.M. 19-08-15-SC and 19-10-20-SC before quoting in a filing.**

##### 2.1 Authentication of private documents

**Rule 132 §20 (Proof of private documents).** "Before any private document offered as authentic is received in evidence, its due execution and authenticity must be proved by any of the following means: (a) By anyone who saw the document executed or written; (b) By evidence of the genuineness of the signature or handwriting of the maker[;] or (c) By other evidence showing its due execution and authenticity."

- **Application.** The defense must authenticate each receipt. Salvador's judicial affidavit (S18–S22) says only that he "nag-iissue" (issued) receipts. Under §20(a) and (b), the questions are: who wrote each receipt, and who signed the "V" and "S" forms?
- **Disposition.** Object at the formal offer to any receipt not specifically identified by its writer or signer. Preserve the objection even if the exhibit is admitted, because weight remains open.

**Rule 132 §22 (How genuineness of handwriting proved).** "The handwriting of a person may be proved by any witness who believes it to be the handwriting of such person because he or she has seen the person write, or has seen writing purporting to be his or hers upon which the witness has acted or been charged, and has thus acquired knowledge of the handwriting of such person. Evidence respecting the handwriting may also be given by a comparison, made by the witness or the court, with writings admitted or treated as genuine by the party against whom the evidence is offered, or proved to be genuine to the satisfaction of the judge."

- **Application.** Standards for Salvador must come from writings the defense admits or treats as genuine. Examples: his signed judicial affidavit, his ID, notarized instruments, or a signature he admits through a Rule 26 request.
- **Disposition.** Get Salvador's signed affidavit and jurat page into the record as a standard. Serve an RFA asking him to admit specific exemplars.

**Rule 132 §31 (Alteration in document, how to explain).** "The party producing a document as genuine which has been altered and appears to have been altered after its execution, in a part material to the question in dispute, must account for the alteration. … If he or she fails to do that, the document shall not be admissible in evidence."

- **Application.** The record shows "August" struck on 31 Jul 2009, an impossible "April 31, 2010," the overwritten year on No. 0104, and month-only dates on Nos. 0062–0063 (Exam §7 #12). Dates are material because the question is payment before the deed and before the revocation.
- **Disposition.** Object to those receipts specifically under §31, and make the defense explain each one.

##### 2.2 Original document rule

**Rule 130 §3.** "When the subject of inquiry is the contents of a document … no evidence is admissible other than the original document itself, except … (b) When the original is in the custody or under the control of the party against whom the evidence is offered, and the latter fails to produce it after reasonable notice …"

- **Application.** Our copy is a phone scan. The defense must produce the originals at the offer. If they offer photocopies, object.
- **Disposition.** Demand that the originals be produced at the offer for comparison. The scan cannot settle questions of ink, pressure or indentation.

##### 2.3 Opinion evidence

- **Rule 130 §52 (Opinion of expert witness).** "The opinion of a witness on a matter requiring special knowledge, skill, experience, training or education, which he or she is shown to possess, may be received in evidence."
- **Rule 130 §53 (Opinion of ordinary witnesses).** "The opinion of a witness, for which proper basis is given, may be received in evidence regarding - (a) [T]he identity of a person about whom he or she has adequate knowledge; (b) A handwriting with which he or she has sufficient familiarity; …"
- **Note.** The 2019 rules number these §§52–53. Older cases cite them as §§49–50. The task brief's "§§49–52" refers to the 1989 numbering.
- **Application.** Patricia, or any witness who has dealt with Cesar's writing, may give a §53(b) opinion on Cesar's hand: that the receipts are not his. That does not need an expert.

##### 2.4 Burden and presumptions

- **Rule 131 §1.** "Burden of proof is the duty of a party to present evidence on the facts in issue necessary to establish his or her claim or defense … Burden of proof never shifts. Burden of evidence … may shift from one party to the other …"
- **Rule 131 §3.** Disputable presumptions, "satisfactory if uncontradicted, but may be contradicted and overcome by other evidence":
  - "(e) That evidence willfully suppressed would be adverse if produced"
  - "(f) That money paid by one to another was due to the latter"
  - "(i) That prior rents or installments had been paid when a receipt for the later one is produced"
  - "(p) That private transactions have been fair and regular"
  - "(q) That the ordinary course of business has been followed"
  - "(u) That a writing is truly dated"
- **Application.** Use (e) if the defense refuses to produce the full booklets, stubs or duplicates after a Rule 27 order or a subpoena. Rebut (u) with the six serial reversals. Rebut (q) with the no-ATP point and the near-consecutive serials. Rebut (i) by showing the receipts were not issued by an authorized person.

##### 2.5 Civil Code (VERIFIED text)

- **Art. 1233.** "A debt shall not be understood to have been paid unless the thing or service in which the obligation consists has been completely delivered or rendered, as the case may be."
  - The task brief describes Art. 1233 as "burden on debtor." The article does not say that in its words. The burden comes from case law construing it (*Culaba* cites Art. 1233 for it; see also *Bognot* and *Joseph*).
- **Art. 1240.** "Payment shall be made to the person in whose favor the obligation has been constituted, or his successor in interest, or any person authorized to receive it."
- **Art. 1241, ¶2.** "Payment made to a third person shall also be valid insofar as it has redounded to the benefit of the creditor. Such benefit to the creditor need not be proved in the following cases: (1) If after the payment, the third person acquires the creditor's rights; (2) If the creditor ratifies the payment to the third person; (3) If by the creditor's conduct, the debtor has been led to believe that the third person had authority to receive the payment."
  - **Risk.** The defense will argue (2) or (3). Answer: the sisters never ratified; the 2005 revocation; no conduct by the sisters pointing to Salvador; and "redounded to the benefit" requires proof that money reached the sisters. Per the case file, none did [VERIFY with Patricia].
- **Art. 1892.** "The agent may appoint a substitute if the principal has not prohibited him from doing so; but he shall be responsible for the acts of the substitute: (1) When he was not given the power to appoint one; …" A substitute's authority cannot exceed the agent's. After the 15 Aug 2005 revocation, there was nothing left to delegate.
- **Art. 1900.** "So far as third persons are concerned, an act is deemed to have been performed within the scope of the agent's authority, if such act is within the terms of the power of attorney, as written, even if the agent has in fact exceeded the limits of his authority according to an understanding between the principal and the agent."
  - **Risk.** The defense may invoke this. Answer: the SPA "as written" (¶7) limits the buyer class and requires prior consultation. Receipts issued by Salvador are not "within the terms of the power of attorney, as written," which names Cesar.

##### 2.6 Getting the originals (Rules of Civil Procedure 2019, VERIFIED text)

- **Rule 27 §1.** "Upon motion of any party showing good cause therefor, the court … may (a) order any party to produce and permit the inspection and copying or photographing … of any designated documents, papers, books, accounts … which constitute or contain evidence material to any matter involved in the action and which are in his or her possession, custody or control … The order shall specify the time, place and manner of making the inspection and taking copies and photographs, and may prescribe such terms and conditions as are just."
  - Ask that the "terms and conditions" include examination by the PNP Crime Laboratory, under chain of custody.
- **Rule 21 §1.** A subpoena duces tecum may "require him to bring with him or her any books, documents, or other things under his or her control." **§4** allows quashal if it is "unreasonable and oppressive, or the relevancy … does not appear," or if costs are not advanced. Describe the booklets precisely (series 0001–1250, 4C's Press, Auth. No. 451-1998), including stubs, duplicates and covers.
- **Rule 26 §1.** A party may request admission of "the genuineness of any material and relevant document" or "the truth of any material and relevant matter of fact." **§2:** each matter "shall be deemed admitted" unless a sworn specific denial is served within the period stated in the request (at least 15 calendar days).
- **Rule 30 §5(f).** "The parties may then respectively adduce rebutting evidence only, unless the court, for good reasons and in the furtherance of justice, permits them to adduce evidence upon their original case."
- **Procedure caveat [VERIFY].** Confirm whether 26-360 runs under the ordinary rules or the Rules on Summary Procedure. Summary procedure restricts discovery. Also confirm the pre-trial order's limits on new exhibits and witnesses, and the Judicial Affidavit Rule timing for rebuttal witnesses. The pre-trial order is still not in hand.

##### 2.7 BIR (VERIFIED text)

- **NIRC §237(A)** (as amended by RA 10963). "All persons subject to an internal revenue tax shall, at the point of each sale and transfer of merchandise or for services rendered valued at Five hundred pesos (P500.00) or more, issue duly registered … invoices … The original of each receipt or invoice shall be issued to the purchaser … while the duplicate shall be kept and preserved by the issuer, also in his place of business …"
  - Pre-2018 versions spoke of "duly registered receipts or sales or commercial invoices." The text here is the post-TRAIN version, from taxacctgcenter.ph.
  - **[VERIFY]** the version in force for each year 2003–2017.
- **NIRC §238.** "All persons who are engaged in business shall secure from the Bureau of Internal Revenue an authority to print receipts … before a printer can print the same. No authority to print … shall be granted unless the receipts or invoices to be printed are serially numbered and shall show, among other things, the name, business style, Taxpayer Identification Number (TIN) and business address of the person or entity to use the same …"
- **NIRC §113** (invoicing requirements for VAT-registered persons) is **UNVERIFIED** here; I did not read its text today. Low relevance, because the forms print "Non Vat."
- **RR 18-2012** (SC E-Library, read in full):
  - §3(6): "The approved ATP shall be valid only upon full usage of the inclusive serial numbers … or five (5) years from issuance of the same, whichever comes first."
  - §3(8): "The replicate copy of the ATP issued shall be printed at the inside back portion of the cardboard cover of each booklet."
  - **§5:** "All unused/unissued principal and supplementary receipts/invoices printed prior to the effectivity of these Regulations, shall be valid until June 30, 2013. … All unused/unissued … receipts/invoices shall be surrendered to the RDO … for destruction. An Inventory listing of the same shall also be submitted."
- **What a no-ATP or expired-ATP receipt means as evidence.** No case read today holds that such a receipt is inadmissible to prove payment between private parties. Treat it as follows:
  - It does not bar proof of payment by itself.
  - It weakens the presumption of "ordinary course of business" (Rule 131 §3(q)).
  - It is evidence that the forms were used outside the regulated system, which bears on whether they were written when dated.
  - A BIR certification on the ATP and the TIN (Auth. No. 451-1998; RD64; TIN [TIN withheld], registered to whom?) is a public record (Rule 130 §3(d)).
  - **Whether the "estate" was a person "engaged in business" (rental and sales) is a legal question [VERIFY].**

#### 3. Supreme Court holdings (VERIFIED; read in full today)

| # | Case | Holding (exact quote) | Use |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | *Culaba v. CA*, G.R. No. 125862, 15 Apr 2004 | "Persons dealing with an assumed agent are bound at their peril to ascertain not only the fact of agency but also the nature and extent of authority, and in case either is controverted, the burden of proof is upon them to establish it." Payments to a man in an SMC uniform who issued "SMC liquidation receipts which looked genuine" did not discharge the debt. | The core point: payment to an unauthorized collector, with receipts that look genuine, is not payment |
| 2 | *Bognot v. RRI Lending*, G.R. No. 180144, 24 Sep 2014 | "one who pleads payment has the burden of proving it … once the existence of an indebtedness is duly established by evidence, the burden of showing with legal certainty that the obligation has been discharged by payment rests on the debtor." | Burden |
| 3 | *Joseph v. Sps. Joseph*, G.R. No. 234384, 26 Apr 2021 | "Where the debtor introduces some evidence of payment, the burden of going forward with the evidence — as distinct from the general burden of proof — shifts to the creditor, who is then under a duty of producing some evidence to show non-payment." It involved a claimed full payment of a land price, with receipts falling short. | Why rebuttal evidence is needed; full-payment claims on a land sale |
| 4 | *Multi-International Business Data System v. Martinez*, G.R. No. 175378 (SC E-Library) | "Although not exclusive, a receipt of payment is the best evidence of the fact of payment." | Receipts carry weight but are not conclusive. **Status: excerpt read via the E-Library search result, not the full text. Mark PARTIAL; re-read before citing.** |
| 6 | *Pontaoe v. Pontaoe*, G.R. Nos. 159585 & 165318, 22 Apr 2008 | "Section 22 of Rule 132 … explicitly authorizes the court, by itself, to make a comparison of the disputed handwriting 'with writings admitted or treated as genuine by the party against whom the evidence is offered, or proved to be genuine to the satisfaction of the judge.'" | The court may compare; standards requirement |
| 7 | *Gamido v. CA*, G.R. Nos. 111962-72, 8 Dec 1995 | It is "not required that the person identifying the handwriting of another must have seen the latter write." Authenticity "is not a highly technical issue … The signatures on a questioned document can be sighted by a judge who can and should exercise independent judgment." | A familiarity witness on Cesar's hand (Patricia; record custodians) |
| 8 | *Tamani v. Salvador*, G.R. No. 171497, 4 Apr 2011 | "The value of the opinion of a handwriting expert depends not upon his mere statements of whether a writing is genuine or false, but upon the assistance he may afford in pointing out distinguishing marks, characteristics and discrepancies …" Also: "credentials … cannot be the sole factor." It relied on a PNP examiner, and approved disregarding standards too far in time from the questioned writing. | How an examiner's report gains weight; standards should be close in time |
| 9 | *Navarro Marcos v. Heirs of Navarro*, G.R. No. 198240, 3 Jul 2013 | Held it was error to disqualify a PNP document examiner whose examination was done without court authorization or notice: "The value of P02 Alvarez's expert opinion cannot be determined if P02 Alvarez is not even allowed to testify." Expert opinion under the rules "is permissive and not mandatory." | A PNP examiner may testify. Still, a court-ordered examination with notice avoids the fight altogether |
| 10 | *People v. Abalos*, G.R. No. L-29039, 28 Nov 1969 (En Banc) | The defense's claim was "a new matter, not covered directly by the evidence for the prosecution," and the prosecution "was entitled … as a matter of strict legal right, to introduce positive evidence" on rebuttal. Striking the rebuttal witness was reversed. | Rebuttal scope: meeting a new matter. It is a criminal case, so cite it by analogy |
| — | *Monfort v. Aguinaldo* (1950s; via lawyerly.ph excerpt only) | "While receipts are deemed to be the best evidence, they are not exclusive." | **UNVERIFIED** (full SC text not read). Do not cite |

##### UNVERIFIED (not read in full; do not cite until checked)

- *Heirs of Gregorio v. CA* (handwriting; standards).
- *Spouses Cabrera v. Tiu*.
- *Heirs of Bucton v. Sps. Go* (G.R. No. 188395, 2013). Seen quoted: standards should be close in time; a visible difference can make technical rules unnecessary.
- Any SC case on **ink-dating** evidence. None found or read; I found no SC case admitting or rejecting solvent-loss ink dating.
- Any SC case squarely on **receipts post-dating a deed's "paid" recital** (parol evidence or simulation of price).
- SC cases on **rebuttal versus evidence that should have been in chief** in civil cases (beyond *Abalos*).
- NIRC §113 text.
- **PNP Crime Laboratory Questioned Documents SOP** (request form, fees, turnaround). Not read today; get it from the PNP Crime Lab directly.

**Section:** 4. Practice: examination of the originals

---

#### 4. Practice: examination of the originals

1. **Court order first.** Seek it under Rule 27 §1, with "terms and conditions" covering:
   - the examiner (PNP Crime Laboratory, Questioned Documents section; or a court-approved private examiner)
   - the place, a sealed transfer, a custody log, and photographing at every hand-off
   - non-destructive tests first (magnification, UV/IR and video spectral comparison, side-lighting/ESDA for indentations)
   - leave of court before any sampling (TLC ink comparison; solvent-loss dating)
   - notice to both parties

   *Navarro Marcos* lets an examiner testify even without an order, but an order removes the due-process objection.
2. **Standards and exemplars.** These must be "admitted or treated as genuine" (Rule 132 §22), and close in time to the questioned writing (*Tamani*). For each candidate writer (Salvador; Cesar for exclusion), collect:
   - **Collected standards:** signed public filings, IDs, notarized deeds, bank cards, and Salvador's judicial-affidavit signature. Ideally several from 2003–2005, 2007, 2013–2017.
   - **Requested standards:** dictated writing taken in court, if ordered.
   - **RFA** on each standard's genuineness.
3. **Questions for the examiner.**
   - Same ink, or a different ink, across receipts dated years apart (especially Nos. 0106–0136 and 0217–0228)?
   - Is the body and the signature the same ink and the same hand?
   - Indented impressions of other receipts' entries, which would show writing in sequence on a stack?
   - Do the originals and duplicates or stubs match?
   - Is the paper or the numbering-machine font consistent with the 1998 print run?
   - Is relative ink age consistent with the dates? (Only if the court allows sampling. Results are typically "fresh within about 2 years or not." **No SC authority on admissibility was read.**)
4. **Forensic accounting of the books.**
   - Rebuild the full serial ledger for 0001–1250 from the stubs.
   - Mark each number as issued (to whom), voided, blank or missing.
   - Test date order against serial order.
   - Reconcile collections against what was remitted to the principals.
   - Separate rent from price.
   - Compare against the deed price and any installment plan.
   - Output a schedule usable under Rule 130 §3(c) (a summary of voluminous documents) through the witness who prepared it.
5. **Cost.** See RECEIPTS_EXAMINATION §11. No new figures were verified here.

---

#### 5. Full anomaly checklist (questioned-documents and forensic-accounting practice)

Key: **Scan** = testable on the phone scan; **Orig** = needs the originals or stubs. "Def. Exh. 5 result" applies the test to the 110 collection receipts using the CSV and the scan review. FOUND = present on the face. NOT FOUND = tested on the scan and absent. NEEDS ORIGINAL = cannot be tested on the scan.

| # | Anomaly type | What it may indicate | Test (scan / original) | Def. Exh. 5 result |
|---|---|---|---|---|

| Anomaly | Rule | Proof route | Expected weight |

| Collector not Cesar; no appointment before 2012; 2012 paper post-dates revocation | Art. 1240; *Culaba*; Art. 1892 | Face of receipts; Answer ¶44(f) and Gloria's admission of "through Salvador"; Salvador cross; RFA | **High.** Defeats payment as a matter of law unless Art. 1241 benefit or ratification is proved |

| Serial reversals; same-day far-apart serials | Rebuts §3(u) "truly dated" | Face; ledger schedule | **Medium-high** |

| Ink age | Rule 130 §52 | Only with leave for sampling | **Low to uncertain**; no SC authority read |
| Timing before Cesar's death | none on its own | PSA death certificate | **Low.** Timing only |
| Sundays, holidays, round numbers | none | — | **Low.** Keep off the main argument |

**Disposition.** In chief, rely on authority (¶7 class, consultation, revocation). On rebuttal, meet Def. Exh. 5 with:

1. *Culaba* and Art. 1240
2. receipts dated after the deed, and the totals

## [Grok desk] Ink-dating plan for Def. Exh. 5 (timing decision for counsel)

**Document:** [Grok desk] Ink-dating plan for Def. Exh. 5 (timing decision for counsel)  
**Source:** `CASE_FILE/PARTIES/INK_DATING_PLAN_2026-10-18.md`  
**Section:** Preamble and summary

---

### Ink-Dating Plan for Def. Exh. 5 (Receipts)

**Civil Case No. 26-360, MTC Mercedes.** Internal plan for Atty. Barandon. Prepared 18 Oct 2026 (UTC+8). Not for filing. No laboratory or person has been contacted.

**Theory to test (Jonathan's).** The handwritten entries on Def. Exh. 5, dated 2003–2017, were written recently, near the 2026 production, not on their stated dates.

---


**Document:** [Grok desk] Ink-dating plan for Def. Exh. 5 (timing decision for counsel)  
**Source:** `CASE_FILE/PARTIES/INK_DATING_PLAN_2026-10-18.md`  
**Section:** 1. Bottom line

---

#### 1. Bottom line

1. **A real test exists, and it can answer the question only one way.** GC-MS solvent-loss analysis can show that an entry is **young**, meaning written within roughly the last months to two years. It cannot give a calendar date. It cannot prove an entry is old: a "not young" result is equally consistent with 2005 or with 2023.
2. **The window is short and closing.** Published thresholds range from under 3 months (some inks, per a 2023+ ASQDE study) to under 18 months (Gaudreau & Aginsky 2010). Riley Welch LaPorte states "up to 2 years." **If the entries were written in early 2026, a test in late 2027 may show nothing.** Urgency is the main argument for filing early.
3. **Two complementary tests do not depend on the window:**
   - **Ink identification.** This compares the ink against a library for its first date of manufacture. An ink first made after a receipt's stated date is decisive.
   - **Ink comparison.** This tests whether entries dated years apart share one ink formulation and batch.
4. **Destructive sampling needs court leave.** That is now prayer (c-1) and ¶17-A in the draft motion. In the Philippines, admissibility should not be the obstacle: courts weigh expert opinion freely (*Jimenez*; *Tamani*). The fight will be over weight.
5. **"Riley Welch" is real.** The firm is **Riley, Welch, LaPorte & Associates**, Frankenmuth, Michigan, USA (rileywelch.com). It does GC-MS ink dating (2-phenoxyethanol) and lists international work.

---


**Document:** [Grok desk] Ink-dating plan for Def. Exh. 5 (timing decision for counsel)  
**Source:** `CASE_FILE/PARTIES/INK_DATING_PLAN_2026-10-18.md`  
**Section:** 2. What the tests can and cannot show

---

#### 2. What the tests can and cannot show

##### 2.1 Static methods (no time window)

- **Ink comparison** (ASTM E1422; SWGDOC "Test Methods for Forensic Writing Ink Comparison"). Uses optical examination (UV, IR, VSC) and thin-layer chromatography (TLC). It answers "same or different ink formulation."
  - By its own scope, "performing ink dating examinations [is] beyond the scope of this standard" (SWGDOC §1.2, read today).
  - **Use here:** Do the 2013–2017 estate-form entries (Nos. 0106–0228) share one formulation with each other, and with the post-deed cluster? Does the body ink match the signature ink?
- **Ink identification** (ASTM E1789; SWGDOC "Standard for Writing Ink Identification"). Matches the ink against a reference library to identify the manufacturer and the formula's first production date. Some inks carry manufacturer date tags.
  - **Use here:** if a 2005 receipt is written in an ink first sold in 2018, the entry cannot be from 2005.
  - **Limit:** libraries are US-centred. Philippine pen brands may not be covered [VERIFY with the lab].

##### 2.2 Dynamic (ink-age) methods

- **Principle.** Ballpoint inks contain volatile solvents, chiefly **2-phenoxyethanol (2-PE)**. These evaporate quickly at first, then slowly, and then level off as the resin hardens.
  - The 2023 PMC study on black BIC ink, read today, found "a substantial decline between the 1st and the 33rd day … followed by a stabilisation of the signal," with 2-PE still detectable up to 3 years.
  - Source: https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC10302093/
- **Solvent Loss Ratio Method (SLRM)** (Aginsky 1996; Gaudreau & Brazeau 2002; Gaudreau & Aginsky 2010). Paired micro-plugs are taken from the same line. One is heated (about 70 °C, 1–2 h). The lab measures R% = 100 × (P − P_T)/P by GC-MS.
  - Thresholds reported in 2002: "R% larger than 50% … less than 5 months"; ">25% … less than 10 months."
  - The 25% threshold "was abandoned (as it had shown false-positive results)." It was revised in 2010 to "R% value larger than 35% indicating the age of the ink to be less than 18 months."
  - Source: *J. Am. Soc. Questioned Doc. Examiners*, https://journal.asqde.org/articles/10.69525/jasqde.210, read today.
- **Reliability caveats from that same article** (expect the defense to use them):
  - "neither the experimental data … nor the statistical evaluation relating it to any of the claimed SLRM thresholds have been published to date in a peer-reviewed article"
  - SLRM "has yet to be subjected to outside proficiency testing to determine its error rate"
  - in that study, R% "correlates with the age of the inks within a rather short timeframe which proved to be less than 3 months" for 15 common inks
- **Sequential Extraction Technique (SET)** and dye-ratio or percent-extraction methods (Aginsky; Speckin). These are relative-age comparisons against known-dated entries of the **same ink on the same paper** (Speckin: "it is imperative that the same ink formulation from the same type of paper comprise both samples").
- **Practice example (Riley Welch LaPorte report, US federal court, S.D.N.Y., read today).** The firm found "significantly high levels of 2-phenoxyethanol" with an average SLR of 31% on entries dated 2016. It concluded it was "highly probable" they were written "within two (2) years before I performed my testing" and "consistent with … the past six (6) months."
  - That is the form of opinion to expect: probabilistic, about recency, with no calendar date.
  - Source: https://storage.courtlistener.com/recap/gov.uscourts.nysd.500584/gov.uscourts.nysd.500584.128.2.pdf

##### 2.3 What no method can do

- Give an exact date of writing.
- Date gel, felt-tip or many water-based inks by 2-PE. The ink type must be confirmed first.
- Prove an entry is old.
- Survive a poor sample: plugs too close to other ink lines or crossings, uneven pressure, or storage in heat. Speckin's Michael Sinke (2013 talk) said some protocols need about **30 ink plugs**, which can "obliterate much of the signature." Plan sampling from body text, not signatures.

---


**Document:** [Grok desk] Ink-dating plan for Def. Exh. 5 (timing decision for counsel)  
**Source:** `CASE_FILE/PARTIES/INK_DATING_PLAN_2026-10-18.md`  
**Section:** 3. The realistic "fresh ink" window and urgency

---

#### 3. The realistic "fresh ink" window and urgency

| Source | Window in which "recent" can be shown |
|---|---|
| Gaudreau & Aginsky 2010 (as reported in JASQDE) | R% > 35% means under **18 months** |
| Gaudreau & Brazeau 2002 | R% > 50% means under **5 months** |
| JASQDE study (15 inks) | SLRM correlates only within **under 3 months** for those inks |
| Riley Welch LaPorte website | drying "can last up to **2 years**"; GC/MS shows "if a written entry was created in the past two years" |
| Speckin (Sinke, 2013) | ink "has a **three to three and one-half year** drying time" (relative-age method) |
| PMC 2023 (BIC black) | fast change to about **33 days**; detectable to 3 years; time frames 0–33 d / 34–109 d / >109 d |

**Working assumption: about 6–24 months, depending on ink and method.**

- If the entries were written in late 2025 or early 2026, sampling in Q4 2026 to Q1 2027 is likely still inside the window for the more sensitive methods. Every month of delay erodes the result.
- **Argue urgency in the motion** (done, ¶17-A). Evidence that degrades with time is good cause for prompt production and sampling, separate from the rebuttal timing.
- **We do not know when the receipts were "produced."** Confirm the date Def. Exh. 5 was first served or marked [VERIFY], because that date anchors the window.

---


**Document:** [Grok desk] Ink-dating plan for Def. Exh. 5 (timing decision for counsel)  
**Source:** `CASE_FILE/PARTIES/INK_DATING_PLAN_2026-10-18.md`  
**Section:** 4. Sample design

---

#### 4. Sample design

1. **Non-destructive triage first** (PNP or the chosen lab): ink type (ballpoint or gel), VSC/UV/IR grouping, and an ESDA indentation scan. Then choose the plugs.
2. **Questioned entries: 6–10, spread across the years and across visual ink groups.**

   | # | Receipt | Why |
   |---|---|---|
   | Q1 | 25 Oct 2003, "V" form (p.2) | Earliest |
   | Q2 | 6 Jan 2005, "Down payment" (p.3) | Key purpose wording |
   | Q3 | 2006 red-ink receipt (p.6) | A distinct ink group |
   | Q4 | No. 0019 or 0061, 28 Mar 2007 (p.9) | First estate form |
   | Q5 | 2009–2011 violet group (pp.15–21) | Mid-period |
   | Q6 | No. 0106, 31 Jul 2013 (p.23) | First receipt after the 30 Jun 2013 ATP cutoff |
   | Q7 | No. 0129 or 0133, 2016 (p.29) | Near-consecutive pair |
   | Q8 | No. 0217, Aug 2016 (p.30) | After the serial jump |
   | Q9 | No. 0222, 30 Sep 2016 (p.31) | The day after the deed |
   | Q10 | No. 0228, Feb 2017 "Full payment" (p.33) | Last receipt |

3. **Controls:**
   - (a) **Paper blanks** from each receipt sampled, for background solvent.
   - (b) **Known-age references on the same paper stock.** If unused blank estate forms or stubs are produced, write fresh entries with candidate pens, and keep a set aged under a documented protocol.
   - (c) **Known-dated writing by the same hand, if any exists:** Salvador's notarized or filed documents of known date, written in ballpoint.
   - (d) **Duplicate plugs** for each test, and **split samples** offered to the defense.
4. **Sampling protocol** (per the JASQDE description):
   - Plugs come from adjacent, uniform stretches of the same line.
   - No plugs near crossings or other lines.
   - Each plug's location is mapped and photographed.
   - Plugs are sealed in glass vials immediately.
   - Body-text strokes (amounts, dates) are sampled rather than signatures, to preserve the signatures for handwriting comparison.

---


**Document:** [Grok desk] Ink-dating plan for Def. Exh. 5 (timing decision for counsel)  
**Source:** `CASE_FILE/PARTIES/INK_DATING_PLAN_2026-10-18.md`  
**Section:** 5. Chain of custody and court leave

---

#### 5. Chain of custody and court leave

- **Leave is required.** Plug removal is destructive (draft motion ¶17-A; prayer (c-1)). Ask for:
  - named or court-approved labs
  - the list of entries
  - sampling in the presence of both counsel and the Branch Clerk
  - before-and-after photographs
  - split samples
  - sealed international courier
  - a signed transfer form at each hand-off
  - a return inventory
- **Sampling should happen in the Philippines**, before the court or Clerk, with the lab's analyst present or by a trained examiner following the lab's written protocol. Only the vials travel; the originals stay. Speckin's published Hong Kong example used this model: plugs were taken locally and analysed in the US lab.
- **Shipping.** Ink plugs are not hazardous. Use a tracked courier with tamper-evident seals, and record temperature and time in transit (heat ages ink).
- **Report and testimony.** The foreign analyst testifies by videoconference, if the court allows under the Supreme Court's videoconferencing guidelines [VERIFY current A.M.], or by deposition upon written interrogatories (Rule 23) [VERIFY].

---


**Document:** [Grok desk] Ink-dating plan for Def. Exh. 5 (timing decision for counsel)  
**Source:** `CASE_FILE/PARTIES/INK_DATING_PLAN_2026-10-18.md`  
**Section:** 6. Admissibility and weight

---

#### 6. Admissibility and weight

- **United States.** Ink-age testimony has been challenged under *Daubert*, with mixed results.
  - Speckin reports a S.D.N.Y. judge in *Rezende v. Citigroup* found its accelerated-aging and extraction methods "scientifically reliable," with Aginsky as the unsuccessful opposing expert. That is from Speckin's own website, so it is **UNVERIFIED** as to the opinion text.
  - Speckin also reports admission in *Torok v. Summix Mines* (B.C. Supreme Court, 1998), also **UNVERIFIED**.
  - The JASQDE article shows that SLRM thresholds lack published validation data and error rates. Expect a challenge on that.
- **Philippines.** There is no *Daubert* gatekeeping rule. Rule 130 §52 admits opinion on "special knowledge, skill, experience, training or education, which he or she is shown to possess."
  - Courts weigh expert opinion freely and are "not bound" by it (*Jimenez v. Commission on Ecumenical Mission*, G.R. 140472, verified).
  - Value lies in "pointing out distinguishing marks, characteristics and discrepancies" (*Tamani v. Salvador*, G.R. 171497, verified).
  - A PNP examiner may not be excluded merely because the examination lacked prior court authority (*Navarro Marcos*, G.R. 198240, verified). A court order still avoids that fight.
  - **No Philippine Supreme Court decision on ink-age (solvent-loss) evidence was found or read. UNVERIFIED.**
- **How to maximise weight:**
  - use a lab with published methods and peer-reviewed analysts
  - follow the ASTM/SWGDOC methods for comparison and identification
  - test in duplicate
  - use controls on the same paper
  - give a candid statement of limits
  - corroborate with the non-chemical anomalies (serial reversals, ATP validity, receipts dated after the deed)

---

Staff: Todd Welch (D-ABFDE; ex-Michigan State Police FDE); Gerald LaPorte (forensic chemist, ex-US Secret Service chief research forensic chemist) | **None published** | https://rileywelch.com/ink-dating-2/ ; https://rileywelch.com/staff/ |
| **Aginsky Forensic Document Dating Laboratory, Inc.** | USA (see site) | Ink dating; first commercial availability of ink; sequence; "Examining multiple documents … dated over a long period of time, to determine whether they were or were not produced on or around their purported dates" | Cases from many countries, including Malaysia, Hong Kong, China, India and the UAE | **None published** | https://www.documentdating.com/services.html |
| **Speckin Forensic Laboratories** | Michigan, USA | Static (library, date tags) and dynamic (R-ratio, percent extraction, dye ratio, accelerated aging) ink dating. Published example: a receipt book entry dated 1987 shown to be 1989 or later by date tag | Work in Asia, Japan, Hong Kong; testimony in Israel, Mexico, the UK, Hong Kong, Japan and Canada | **2013 report: US$250/hr plus US$1,000 retainer** (handwriting work; Legal News, 2013). No current ink-dating price | https://4n6.com/ink-dating-expert-witness/ ; https://4n6.com/about-us/ |

**Listed only, not checked on its own site:** Global Forensic Services, LLC (Larry F. Stewart, ex-US Secret Service), listed on JurisPro under "ink age." I didn't verify its own website, so it is not recommended until checked.

**Price reality.** No lab publishes an ink-dating fee. JurisPro reports ink-dating experts at about US$200–400/hr. **Budget estimate [INFERRED, not quoted]:** a 10-entry GC-MS plus TLC job with a report would likely run in the low-to-mid five figures USD, plus courier and remote testimony. Get written quotes before the motion is heard.

**Riley Welch (the name given to the user).** **Confirmed real.** The correct name is "Riley, Welch, LaPorte & Associates" (also "Riley Welch LaPorte"). It does ink dating by GC/MS. It is in Frankenmuth, Michigan, not elsewhere.

**Neutrality note.** Valery Aginsky co-authored the SLRM thresholds and has testified against Speckin. Choosing between labs may draw cross-examination on methodology disputes, so let the court approve the lab after hearing both sides.

**Document:** [Grok desk] Ink-dating plan for Def. Exh. 5 (timing decision for counsel)  
**Source:** `CASE_FILE/PARTIES/INK_DATING_PLAN_2026-10-18.md`  
**Section:** 8. Sequence

---

#### 8. Sequence

1. Confirm the date Def. Exh. 5 was produced or marked. This sets the urgency arithmetic.
2. File the production motion with prayer (c-1) as early as the pre-trial order allows (Barandon's call; see the timing note in the draft motion).
3. On production: the PNP or lab does non-destructive triage (ink type, ink groups, indentations).
4. Hold the sampling session before the Clerk; ship the vials; the lab runs comparison, identification and GC-MS age analysis.
5. Report, then offer on rebuttal with the corroborating anomalies (RECEIPTS_AUDIT_LAW §§5–6).

**Document:** [Grok desk] Forensic framework for the payment trail and subpoena map  
**Source:** `CASE_FILE/PARTIES/RECEIPTS_FORENSIC_FRAMEWORK_2026-10-18.md`  
**Section:** 0. Summary

---

#### 0. Summary

1. A genuine installment sale leaves a chain of records. It runs from the terms, through receipts, stubs, a ledger, remittances and tax filings, to the deed recital and registration. Each link has a holder who can be asked for it.
2. Def. Exh. 5 supplies only the receipts. Every other link is missing from the record, or contradicts the receipts.
3. Four hypotheses are tested neutrally:
   - H1: genuine sale installments
   - H2: rent relabeled as price
   - H3: payments to Salvador on his own account, never remitted
   - H4: receipts written after the fact
4. On the present record H1 is the weakest fit. H2, H3 and H4 are not mutually exclusive, and any of them defeats payment to the principals.
5. The discriminating evidence is: stubs and duplicates; remittance to the sisters; the BIR records on the authority to print (ATP) and the TIN; ink age; and the CGT/DST filing date.
6. **New anomaly:** capital gains tax (CGT) and documentary stamp tax (DST) on a deed dated 29 Sep 2016 were paid on **15 Jun 2020**, and the BIR's certificate authorizing registration (eCAR) issued **18 Nov 2021** (DOS l.216, l.250).
   - That is about 3 years 8 months after the deed. The CGT return is normally due within 30 days of the sale [VERIFY].
   - The eCAR declares the ₱250,000 price against a fair market value of ₱2,509,390.
7. The receipts total about ₱602,950, against a ₱250,000 price. The defense's own offer of Exh. 5 is "to prove rents paid and the installment or partial payments" (ANS p.10 ¶61). So by the defense's own account, the set mixes rent and price.
8. The best quiet first moves are certified records from the BIR (RDO 64), the Register of Deeds, the Assessor and the Treasurer, plus the PSA. Court process comes next (stubs, ledger, remittances); bank records come last and only within RA 1405.

| # | Link | What should exist | Usual holder | What the record or Def. Exh. 5 shows | Gap |

| 1 | Authority to sell | An SPA covering this buyer class, and prior consultation | Principals; agent | 1992 SPA ¶7: Llamanzares class, "after prior consultation"; revoked 15 Aug 2005 (DOS 7.6) | No consultation record; sale is post-revocation |
| 2 | Agreement and terms | Contract to sell: price, down payment, installment amount, due dates | Buyer and agent, both copies | **None produced.** Answer pleads an "installment arrangement" (ANS p.4 ¶31). Gloria: a ₱3,000 down payment in the 1980s, unreceipted, "credited" (JA-G S21–S23). Exh. 5 has a separate ₱20,000 "Down payment" receipt dated 6 Jan 2005 | No written terms; two different down payments |
| 3 | Schedule | An amortization or ledger card showing a running balance | Agent or collector | None. No receipt shows a balance carried forward on the estate forms. Form B has a "Balance" box (EXAM §2) [VERIFY whether filled] | No schedule; balance not tracked |
| 4 | Contemporaneous receipts | Serial order matching date order; a registered booklet (ATP); issuer named | Buyer holds originals | 110 collection receipts. 6+ serial reversals; near-consecutive runs over 500+ days; an 81-number jump; 2003–2012 forms are generic and unregistered; the estate forms' 1998 ATP lapsed 30 Jun 2013 under RR 18-2012 §5; no issuer named (EXAM §§4.3–4.7; LAW §5) | Sequence and registration fail on their face |

Yet ₱219,000 is receipted after that date, and "Full payment" in Feb 2017. Gloria: deed made after full payment (JA-G S50) | The recital and the receipts conflict |

**Section:** 2. Competing hypotheses: evidence matrix (ACH)

---

#### 2. Competing hypotheses: evidence matrix (ACH)

- **H1:** genuine, contemporaneous payments of the ₱250,000 price, received for the principals.
- **H2:** genuine payments, but rent or occupancy fees, later treated as price.
- **H3:** genuine payments to Salvador on his own account, not remitted to the principals.
- **H4:** receipts written after the fact, in one or a few sittings.

H2–H4 can co-exist. **Key:** C = consistent; I = inconsistent; N = neutral or not diagnostic. "Now" = the present record; "Test" = what would discriminate.

| Evidence or test | H1 | H2 | H3 | H4 | Now | Diagnostic value |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Receipts total ₱602,950 against a ₱250,000 price | I | C | C | N | Fact | High against H1 |
| Defense offers Exh. 5 for "rents paid and installment" payments (ANS ¶61) | I (for the full sum) | C | N | N | Fact | High |
| "Payment for the month of" form; monthly ₱2,000 for 2005–2014 | I | C | C | N | Fact | Medium |
| ₱219,000 dated after the deed's "in hand paid"; "Full payment" Feb 2017 | I | N | C | C | Fact | High against H1 |
| Serial reversals; same-day receipts 42 numbers apart | I | I (if contemporaneous) | N | C | Fact | High for H4 |
| Near-consecutive serials over 500+ days | I (if a multi-occupant estate booklet) | N | C (a private booklet) | C | Fact | Medium |
| 81-number jump in about 11 weeks | N | N | N | C/N | Fact | Low until stubs |
| Pre-2012 form stock used after 30 Jun 2013 | I (regular estate) | N | C | C | Fact + BIR | Medium |
| Form TIN not Cesar's | I | N | C | N | [VERIFY BIR] | Medium–high |
| **Stubs and duplicates** show other payors between the Balane numbers | C | C | N | I | Test | **Decisive for H4** |
| Stubs missing or withheld | I | N | C | C | Test | High (Rule 131 §3(e)) |
| **Ink age:** entries "young" (written within about 2 years) | I | I | I | **C** | Test (INK) | **Decisive for H4 if positive** |
| Ink comparison: one formulation across 2013–2017 entries | weak I | N | N | C | Test | Medium |
| Ink first manufactured after a stated date | I | I | I | C | Test | Decisive if found |
| **Remittances to the sisters** (bank, letters, accounting) | **C (required)** | N | **I** | N | Test | **Decisive for H3** |
| Salvador or Cesar bank flows matching receipt dates | C | C | C (to Salvador) | I | Test (RA 1405 limits) | Medium; access-limited |
| Gloria / Balane withdrawals matching receipt dates | C | C | C | I | Test (RA 1405 limits) | Medium; access-limited |
| CGT/DST paid 2020 on a 2016 deed | I (diligent buyer) | N | N | C (deed paperwork later) | Fact | Medium |
| eCAR price ₱250,000 against FMV ₱2.5M | N | N | N | N | Fact | Low (context) |
| Two "down payments" (1980s ₱3,000; 2005 ₱20,000) | I | C | N | C | Fact | Medium |
| RPT payor history | C if the buyer paid after 2016 | N | N | N | Test | Low–medium |

**Reading:**
- H1 is inconsistent with every factual row and consistent only with untested rows.
- To survive, H1 needs (a) stubs showing other payors and (b) remittance to the principals. The defense has produced neither.
- H4 turns on the ink and the stubs.
- H3 turns on remittance.
- H2 is supported by the defense's own ¶61 offer.

**Section:** 3. Forensic-accounting procedures

---

#### 3. Forensic-accounting procedures

1. **Rebuild the schedule.** From the CSV, run each payor's receipts as a time series: monthly amounts, gaps, the cumulative total, and the cumulative total at key dates. Key dates: 15 Aug 2005 (revocation); 29 Feb 2012 (the Annex Z delegation, if offered); 29 Sep 2016 (deed); Feb 2017 (last receipt).
2. **Reconciliation table:**

   | Measure | ₱ | Source |
   |---|---|---|
   | All collection receipts, 2003–2017 | 602,950 [VERIFY sum] | EXAM §4.1 |
   | Deed price, "in hand paid" 29 Sep 2016 | 250,000 | DX6 p.1 |
   | eCAR selling price | 250,000 | DX6 p.2 |
   | Receipts dated after 29 Sep 2016 | 219,000 | EXAM §4.2 |
   | Receipts 17 Nov 2014 – 29 Sep 2016 | 163,850 | EXAM §4.2 |
   | Receipts in Gloria's name (all as "Gloria") | 326,000 | EXAM §4.1 |
   | Answer ¶31: "installment arrangement" | not quantified | ANS p.4 |
   | Gloria S50: deed made after full payment | — | JA-G p.10 |
   | Gloria S21–S23: ₱3,000 down payment in the 1980s, credited | 3,000 | JA-G p.5 |

   - **Tensions:**
     - Receipts dated before the deed do not reach ₱250,000 in Gloria's or Golly's name unless the 2005–2014 monthly ₱2,000 entries are counted as price.
     - If they are counted, the total overshoots the price.
     - S50 (paid in full before the deed) conflicts with ₱219,000 receipted after it.
3. **Rent and price allocation.** Classify each receipt by its wording: "Payment for the month of" or a monthly cadence suggests rent; "Down payment", "partial payment" or "Full payment" suggests price. Run three scenarios:
   - (a) all price
   - (b) only receipts worded as price
   - (c) only post-2014 large amounts

   Show that no scenario produces ₱250,000 paid by 29 Sep 2016 on receipts worded as price.
4. **Round-number and digit tests.** These are **low weight**: 106 of 110 are multiples of ₱1,000, which is natural for rent. Benford analysis is not meaningful on about 110 round, fixed-amount entries. Note it and do not argue it.
5. **Source of funds.** The jump to ₱10,000–₱60,000 payments from Aug 2016 (₱287,650 in the final cluster) raises capacity questions. Pursue them only through lawful process:
   - cross-examination and requests for admission on where the money came from
   - documents the defendants voluntarily produce
   - bank records only within RA 1405 (§5)

   No private investigation.
6. **Output.** A summary schedule, offered under Rule 130 §3(c), through the witness who prepared it.

**Section:** 4. RA 1405 (bank deposits): limit on bank records

---

#### 4. RA 1405 (bank deposits): limit on bank records

RA 1405 §2 (lawphil, VERIFIED): "All deposits of whatever nature with banks or banking institutions in the Philippines … are hereby considered as of an absolutely confidential nature and may not be examined, inquired or looked into by any person, government official, bureau or office, except upon written permission of the depositor, or in cases of impeachment, or upon order of a competent court in cases of bribery or dereliction of duty of public officials, or in cases where the money deposited or invested is the subject matter of the litigation."

- **Application.** The subject of 26-360 is land and a deed, not deposited money. The "subject matter of the litigation" exception is unlikely to reach the Balanes' or Salvador's accounts [VERIFY case law on that exception].
- **Remaining routes:**
  - (a) the depositor's written consent, e.g. the sisters' own accounts, to show **non-receipt**
  - (b) the defendants voluntarily producing proof of withdrawals
  - (c) cross-examination and requests for admission
- Other laws (AMLA; tax-code inquiry powers) are not available to a private litigant. Do not pursue.

- **SDT** = Rule 21 subpoena duces tecum
- **R27** = Rule 27 production order
- **RFA** = Rule 26 request for admission

**Tip-off:** L = low, M = medium, H = high.

| Rank | Record | Holder | Route | Relevance | Sensitivity | Value / Cost / Tip-off |
