| 2 | **TIN [TIN withheld] and [TIN withheld]: registered name, type (estate, individual), registration date, business registration** | BIR RDO 64 | RR, or SDT | Who issued the "estate" receipts | Same as above | High / Low / L |
| 3 | **CGT and DST returns, payment receipts (15 Jun 2020), eCAR 201900675224 docket: filer, date filed, declared date of sale, penalties** | BIR RDO 64 | Certified copy (party to the transaction?) or SDT | Late filing; who processed it; consistency of the declared sale date | Taxpayer data [VERIFY] | High / Low / L–M |
| 4 | **PSA death certificate of Cesar M. de la Fuente** | PSA | RR (any person, with a stated purpose) [VERIFY] | Fixes the 21 Jun 2017 date; agency ends (Art. 1919) | Low | High / Very low / L |
| 5 | **Register of Deeds: primary entry book for 23 Nov 2021; documents presented (deed, eCAR, tax clearance, loss affidavit?); name of the presenter; the T-52540 file** | RD, Camarines Norte | Certified copies (RR) | Chain of registration; who handled it | Low | High / Low / L |
| 6 | **Notarial registers:** Asis Doc. 304/Pg. 61/Bk. CXVI (deed); Belen Doc. 560 (27 Sep 2016 loss affidavit); Belen Doc. 55/Pg. 17/Bk. IX/2012 (delegation); Asis Docs. 115 and 402 | OCC, RTC Daet (notarial section); the notaries | Certified copies (RR); SDT for the notaries' own registers | Identity evidence, signatures, dates, Asis PTR "12/7/18" [VERIFY] | Low | High / Low / L |
| 7 | **Stubs and duplicates of all booklets 0001–1250; any collector's ledger; remittance records; any appointment of Salvador** | Defendants / Salvador / Erwin (custody, JA-E S21) | R27 + SDT (drafts exist) | Decisive on H3/H4; Art. 1891 accounting | Medium | Very high / Medium / **H** |
| 8 | **Original receipts, for ink triage, sampling and ink-age testing** | Defendants | R27 with sampling leave (draft ¶17-A) | Decisive on H4 if positive; time-critical | Destructive: leave needed | Very high / High / **H** |
| 9 | **Salvador handwriting exemplars:** his JA signature (Annex Q); the 25 Nov 2025 letter; notarized instruments; court filings; ID | Court record; notaries; agencies | RR for court and notarial copies; RFA on genuineness | Rule 132 §22 standards | Low | High / Low / L (RFA M) |
| 10 | **Municipal Treasurer: RPT receipts on the lot or ARP, 2003–2026, with payor names** | Treasurer, Mercedes | RR (certified tax payment history) | Who acted as owner, and when | Low | Medium / Low / L |
| 11 | Assessor: tax declarations history (TD to ARP GR-9923-I-07-001-00256), transfer request, the requesting party and supporting papers | Assessor, Mercedes | RR | Who requested the transfer; what was filed | Low | Medium / Low / L |
| 12 | Printer 4C's Press, Daet: job order, delivery receipt, customer, print date for the 25 booklets; BIR printer's report | Printer; BIR | SDT (private) / RR to BIR for printer reports | When the booklets were made and for whom | Medium (alerts a third party) | Medium / Low / M |
| 13 | Remittances to the sisters: their own bank records and correspondence | Patricia, Geraldine, Marcia | Their own records (consent) | Proves non-receipt (H3) | Own data | High / Low / L |

| 15 | SP 2156 record (loss of owner's duplicate; filed 20 Oct 2016) | RTC Daet | Certified copies | Timing beside the 27 Sep 2016 loss affidavit and the 29 Sep deed | Low | Medium / Low / L |

**Quiet first (no court process):** ranks 1–6, 9 (court and notarial copies), 10, 11 and 13–17.

**Court process:** ranks 7, 8 and 12 (SDT), plus the RFAs.

**Caveat.** BIR taxpayer data may be released only on a subpoena or with taxpayer consent [VERIFY]. If so, move ranks 1–3 to an SDT addressed to RDO 64. The tip-off is still low, because the subpoena goes to the BIR, although notice of the subpoena goes to the defense [VERIFY Rule 21 practice].

**Section:** 6. New anomalies (not in EXAM's anomaly register)

---

#### 6. New anomalies (not in EXAM's anomaly register)

1. **Late tax on the deed.** CGT/DST receipts are dated 15 Jun 2020 and the eCAR 18 Nov 2021, for a deed dated 29 Sep 2016 (DX6 p.2; DOS l.216, l.250). The 30-day CGT filing rule needs [VERIFY].
   - Questions: who filed, what date of sale was declared, and were surcharges paid?
   - A deed actually executed on its date would ordinarily be taxed in 2016.
2. **eCAR number series.** The number reads "2019…" (201900675224) but the eCAR is dated 2021 [VERIFY whether eCAR numbers encode the year of application]. If they do, it may show an application in 2019. That would be a third date, between the 2016 deed and the 2020 tax payment.
3. **The defense's own purpose statement splits the set.** ANS ¶61 offers Exh. 5 "to prove rents paid and the installment or partial payments." Exh. 5 therefore cannot be summed as price by the defense's own account, yet the receipts do not mark which is which.
4. **Two down payments.** The Answer and Gloria describe a ₱3,000 down payment in the 1980s, unreceipted and later "credited" (ANS ¶40; JA-G S21–S23). Exh. 5 has a ₱20,000 "Down payment" dated 6 Jan 2005. The record does not reconcile them.
5. **The loss affidavit cluster.** The loss affidavit for an owner's duplicate is dated 27 Sep 2016 (Belen Doc. 560), Cesar's affidavit is also dated 27 Sep 2016, and the deed is dated 29 Sep 2016 (DOS l.914). The deed paperwork was assembled within three days, yet the receipts continue for 4½ months afterwards.

# F. The 1992 power: clause, class, consultation (second-reviewer analyses)


## [Other desk] Clause-by-clause dissection of the 1992 SPA

**Document:** [Other desk] Clause-by-clause dissection of the 1992 SPA  
**Source:** `CASE_FILE/08_GAPS_TO_OBTAIN/SPA_CLAUSE_DISSECTION.md`  
**Section:** Preamble and summary

---

---
title: "Clause-by-clause dissection of the 16 March 1992 SPA (Exh. E)"
subtitle: "Civil Case No. 26-360 · Zschoche v. Balane · prepared for counsel · 14 Oct 2026 · DRAFT, not for filing"
---


**Document:** [Other desk] Clause-by-clause dissection of the 1992 SPA  
**Source:** `CASE_FILE/08_GAPS_TO_OBTAIN/SPA_CLAUSE_DISSECTION.md`  
**Section:** Verdict

---

#### Verdict

**Jonathan's claim, that no Filipino lawyer would treat this SPA as a valid instrument to sell, does not survive the document.** Tested against Arts. 1874 and 1878 and the *Cosmic Lumber* test, the SPA is adequate in form:

- It is in writing.
- It identifies the property by title number and technical description.
- It expressly addresses "the sale" and adds "to sign and execute the necessary documents … as well as to receive the proceeds."
- All three registered owners signed it.
- It was acknowledged before a Los Angeles notary and authenticated by the Philippine Consul, which is the route Act No. 2103 §2 provides.

The closest Supreme Court decision, *Tumibay v. Lopez* (2013), examined an SPA built the same way: "offer for sale … subject to our approval … sign all papers." The Court said there "would have been no obstacle to its use … had the ensuing sale been consummated according to its terms." **What defeats the 2016 sale is not the drafting. It is the SPA's own limits and its end:**

- the ¶7 buyer class;
- "after prior consultation with us";
- the ¶6 tax purpose;
- no power to subdivide or partition;
- revocation on 15 Aug 2005;
- Cesar's death on 21 Jun 2017 before registration.

*Tumibay* voided a sale made outside the SPA's stated condition under Art. 1898. That is the decision that actually supports Jonathan's bottom line. **I found no Supreme Court decision holding an SPA drafted like this one facially insufficient to authorize a sale.**

**Sources**

- Exh. E, RD certified copy (Ref. 2025001098), pp. 2–3.
- CTC p. 1 (consular authentication): Annex K p. 8 (dossier p. 126).
- Exh. B p. 3 (1996 annotation).
- Annex K p. 3 (2016 deed).
- Annex AA (1993 annotation on T-4497).

The scan is faint. Words in [brackets] are uncertain readings, to be checked against the original.

Statutes are quoted from the lawphil copies of R.A. 386 and Act No. 2103 (lawphil.net/statutes/acts/act1912/act_2103_1912.html, read in full). Cases were read in full on lawphil.

**Section:** 1. Paragraph by paragraph

---

#### 1. Paragraph by paragraph

| Para. (page) | Text (exact as legible) | Test | Finding |
|---|---|---|---|
| Title (CTC p. 2) | "SPECIAL POWER OF ATTORNEY" | Art. 1878: the label is not controlling (*Veloso*: "what matters is the extent of the power") | Neutral |
| Parties (CTC p. 2) | "We, GERALDINE K. HOPPE, of legal age and a resident of [4814 Adele] Court, Woodland Hills, California, U.S.A.; PATRICIA K. ZSCHOCHE, of legal age, and a resident of [residence withheld], Huntington Beach, California, U.S.A.; and MARCIA ELLEN KEESEY, of legal age and a resident of [6019] [Rosebud(?)] Way, [Roseville(?)], California, U.S.A., after being duly sworn [to] in accordance with law, hereby depose and state that:" | All co-owners joined? | **Adequate.** All three registered owners of T-4497/T-52540 are principals (cf. Exh. B p. 1). |
| ¶1 | "We are the lawful heirs of the late MARY WORRICK KEESEY who died intestate on [March 17], 1988." | Internal consistency | **Oddity [VERIFY]:** the Mac ledger gives the death as 7 Dec 1988, and ¶4 gives the settlement date as "December 7." Check the death certificate. This is not a defect in the power. |
| ¶2 (CTC p. 2) | "The Decedent, at the time of her death, left undivided interests, rights and title over certain real estate properties which include, among others, the following:" | — | "Undivided" refers to the decedent's interests in the land, not to a lot of her own. |
| ¶3 (CTC p. 2) | "(1) A parcel of land situated in the Municipality of Mercedes, Province of Camarines Norte, covered by Transfer Certificate of Title No. T-4497, and more particularly described as follows: 'A parcel of land (Lot 2 of the subdivision plan (LRC) Psd-12802, being a portion of a parcel of land (Parcel 2), LRC (GLRO) Record No. 10784), situated in the [Poblacion] of Mercedes, [Municipality of Daet], Province of Camarines Norte, Island of Luzon. Bounded on the NE., points 5 to 9, by Pacific Ocean; on the SE., points 9 to 10 and 10 to 1, by property of [Rafael …]; on the SW., points 1 to 4, by Provincial Road (Mercedes-Daet Provincial Road); and on the NW., points 4 to 5, by Lot 1 of the Subdivision plan. … containing an area of ONE HUNDRED THIRTY NINE THOUSAND ONE HUNDRED AND THIRTY TWO SQUARE METERS (139,132), more or less.'" | *Woodchild*/*Bautista-Spille*: does the deed "sufficiently include property"? | **Adequate as to identity**: it gives the title number, plan and area. Note "among others": only this parcel is described. It does **not** name any derivative lot or later title. The "Municipality of Daet" reading is an oddity [VERIFY]. |
| ¶4 (CTC p. 2) | "Being the only surviving heirs, on December 7, [1988], we executed a DEED OF EXTRA-JUDICIAL SETTLEMENT of the estate of the decedent where we agreed to jointly own in common and in equal shares, as well as adjudicate among ourselves, the rights, interests and titles of the decedent over the above described real estate property, together with all the improvements existing thereon, such that we shall respectively receive undivided and inchoate one-third (1/3) share each of said decedent's rights, title and interest therein." | Arts. 484, 493 | Confirms co-ownership in equal undivided thirds. That bears on the 2017 partition: a co-ownership partition needs the co-owners (Arts. 494–495), and the SPA grants no power to partition. |
| ¶5 (CTC p. 2) | "The Estate and Transfer taxes due on the Estate of the decedent has been paid in full to the Bureau of Internal Revenue, [Daet], Camarines Norte Office on December 4, [1991]." | — | Background |
| ¶5 cont. / ¶[5-A] (CTC p. 2, foot) | "However, the respective Transfer Certificate of Title over the above-described real estate property evidencing our rights, title and interest thereto have not been issued by the Registry of Deeds pending settlement of the [unpaid real estate] taxes incurred by the deceased." | Purpose (Art. 1881) | Sets up ¶6. |
| ¶6 (CTC p. 3) | "To enable us to settle the aforementioned unpaid real estate taxes, it will be necessary to negotiate for the sale of the above-described real estate property to interested third parties and apply part of the proceeds of said sale in full payment of said unpaid real estate taxes." | Art. 1881: "acts … conducive to the accomplishment of the purpose" | **A purpose limit, and it helps us.** The purpose is to pay pre-1992 real estate taxes. A sale in 2016 with no tax application is hard to fit within it [INFERRED]. Ramos will say the purpose clause is only a recital. |
| **¶7** (CTC p. 3) | "Accordingly, we hereby name, constitute and appoint CESAR M. DE LA FUENTE, of legal age and a resident of Daet, Camarines Norte, to be our true and lawful attorney-in-fact, for us and in our name, place and stead, to negotiate for the sale of the above-described real estate property in favor of third persons in possession of CONTRACTS TO SELL executed by the previous administrator, MR. BEN LLAMANZARES and/or to those who have paid their down-payments or have made partial payments therefor to the latter. Said sale shall be upon such terms and conditions … after prior consultation with us, and to sign and execute the necessary documents, agreements and other legal papers pertaining thereto, as well as to receive the proceeds of the aforementioned sale in our collective behalf." | Art. 1878(5); *Cosmic*: a power "that expressly mentions a sale or that includes a sale as a necessary ingredient" | **Adequate as a power to sell, within its limits.** It mentions "the sale" three times and joins a power to "sign and execute the necessary documents" and "receive the proceeds." The operative verb is "negotiate," and the signing power is attached to it rather than stated separately as "to sell and convey." That is weaker drafting, but under *Tumibay* (offer for sale + sign papers) it suffices for sales that meet the conditions. **The limits are express:** (i) the buyer class; (ii) prior consultation. **No price, minimum consideration or buyer name** is stated; terms are left to be set "after prior consultation." |
| ¶8 (CTC p. 3) | "We hereby grant unto our said Attorney-in-Fact full power and authority to do and perform any and every act and thing whatsoever requisite, necessary or proper to be done in and about the premises as fully to all intents and purposes as we might or could do if personally present and acting in person." | Art. 1877 (general terms cover only administration); *Bautista-Spille* | **Does not enlarge ¶7.** It does not grant a power to partition, subdivide, segregate, mortgage (Art. 1879) or substitute. |
| ¶9 (CTC p. 3) | "We hereby confirm all that our said Attorney-in-Fact shall lawfully do and cause to be done under and by virtue of these presents." | Art. 1900 | Confirms only acts done "lawfully … under and by virtue of these presents," so it adds nothing beyond ¶7. |
| Testimonium (CTC p. 3) | "IN WITNESS WHEREOF, the parties have hereunto set their hands this 16th day of March, 1992 at Los Angeles, California, U.S.A." (day, month and place handwritten) | — | Normal. |
| Signatures (CTC p. 3) | Three signatures over typed names: GERALDINE K. HOPPE, PATRICIA K. ZSCHOCHE, MARCIA ELLEN KEESEY | All principals signed? | **Adequate on the face.** No instrumental witnesses appear on the copy, and marginal signatures on p. 2 are not visible [VERIFY on original]. Neither is required by Act 2103. |
| Acknowledgment (CTC p. 3) | California form: "State of California, County of Los Angeles … On 16th March 1992 before me, the undersigned, a Notary Public in and for said State, personally appeared GERALDINE K. HOPPE, PATRICIA K. ZSCHOCHE AND MARCIA ELLEN KEESEY, [ ] personally known to me [or proved to me on the basis of satisfactory evidence] to be the person(s) whose name(s) are subscribed to the within instrument, and acknowledged to me that they executed it. WITNESS my hand and official seal." Seal: "FLO Y. PASCUAL, Notary Public – California, Los Angeles County, comm. exp. [Dec.] [ ], 1993." | **Act 2103 §2(a)(2):** acknowledgment before "a notary public … duly authorized by law of the country"; **§2(b):** the officer "shall certify that the person acknowledging … is known to him … The certificate shall be under his official seal" | **Substantially compliant.** One point to check: the form prints both "personally known" and "proved … on the basis of satisfactory evidence," and the copy does not show which was used. Act 2103 says "known to him." It is an oddity, and only a weak defect even if "satisfactory evidence" was the box used [VERIFY on original]. Philippine residence-certificate and notarial-register requirements bound Philippine notaries; they did not apply to a California notary. |
| Consular authentication (CTC p. 1; Annex K p. 8) | "Philippine Consulate General, City of Los Angeles, State of California … AUTHENTICATION … I, RAMON C. MUNDA, Consul … do hereby certify that FLO Y. PASCUAL … whose signature appears on the annexed certificate was, at the time he signed [the same], a Notary Public for and in the County of Los Angeles, State of California … [and] that his/her signature affixed thereto is genuine. For the contents of the annexed document, this Consulate General assumes no responsibility. … 16th day of March 1992." Note: "The annexed document is a special power of attorney executed by Geraldine K. Hoppe, et al. in favor of Cesar M. de la Fuente." Margin: "20.00 / 1187 / 3798 / 1165 / 92." | **Act 2103 §2(b):** a notary's certificate "shall be authenticated by an ambassador, minister, … consul, vice-consul" | **Adequate.** This is where "Doc 1165 / Pg 1187 / Bk 3798 / 92" comes from. They are the consulate's register numbers, not a notary's. |

#### 2. Cross-document mismatches

| # | Item | Sources | Classification |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1 | The 2016 deed says the SPA was "executed before Notary Public Ramon C. Munda, as per Doc. No. 1165; Page No. 1187; Book No. 3798; Series of 1992." Munda was the **Consul who authenticated**. The acknowledging notary was **Flo Y. Pascual**. | Annex K p. 3 vs CTC pp. 1, 3 | **A misdescription in the deed, not a defect in the SPA.** Usable on cross: the drafter of the 2016 deed did not read the SPA closely. Ramos: the reference still identifies the instrument. |
| M2 | The 1996 annotation says "acknowledged before Ramon C. Munda, Consul." He authenticated; he did not take the acknowledgment. | Exh. B p. 3 vs CTC p. 1 | Oddity |
| M3 | The 1996 annotation summarizes the power as "to sign, negotiate for the sale of the parcel of land in this title." It omits the ¶7 class, the consultation requirement and the ¶6 purpose. | Exh. B p. 3 vs ¶7 | **Important.** A buyer relying on the annotation alone would miss the limits. *Sarili*: inspect the SPA's face. Exh. B p. 3 also points to the instrument ("File under T-[4501?]"). |
| M4 | Entry numbers: the consular page RD stamp reads "[19621?]-227-21 … 2:30 6-21-96"; the title annotation reads "PE-196220-227-21 … June 21, 1996 at [2:30/3:30] pm." | CTC p. 1 vs Exh. B p. 3 | Probable OCR or handwriting variance [VERIFY] |
| M5 | The 1993 annotation on T-4497 (PE-170453, 20 Aug 1993): "SPECIAL POWER OF ATTORNEY … appointing CESAR M. DE LA FUENTE as Attorney-in-Fact … to negotiate for the sale and to sign necessary documents, agreements and other legal papers … subject to all conditions …" | Annex AA (poor OCR) | **Helps.** The registry's 1993 summary preserves "subject to all conditions" [VERIFY wording]. |
| M6 | The same consular page carries an RD stamp "11/23/2021 … T-52540 … Romel C. Jacinto," matching Entry 2021003235 | CTC p. 1; Exh. B p. 5 | Consistent: the SPA was re-presented in 2021 (after Cesar's death). |
| M7 | ¶1 gives the death date as "[March 17], 1988"; the Mac ledger says 7 Dec 1988 | Exh. E ¶1; draft ledger | [VERIFY]; not a defect |

**1991 Agcaoili SPA.** The only source is Exh. B p. 5, Entry 2016002312 of 10 Oct 2016: "SPECIAL POWER OF ATTORNEY … in favor of CESAR M. DE LA FUENTE, WITH POWER TO SIGN FOR AND IN BEHALF OF GERALDINE K. HOPPE, PATRICIA K. ZSCHOCHE AND MARCIA ELLEN KEESEY, IN ACCORDANCE WITH DOC. NO. 260, PAGE NO. 53, BOOK NO. I, SERIES OF 1991, NOTARY PUBLIC OF MAKATI, METRO MANILA, BY ATTY. AGCAOILI, DATED AUGUST 22, 1991."

- **The text is not on file.** As annotated, "power to sign for and in behalf of" mentions no sale. Under *Bautista-Spille* and *Cosmic*, that could not support a sale unless the text says more.
- **Question for counsel:** how a Makati notary acknowledged an instrument by three U.S. residents [VERIFY appearance]. It was annotated 25 years later, the same day as Cesar's affidavit of loss. The 2016 deed does not invoke it.
- **Obtain** the RD file copy and Atty. Agcaoili's notarial register.


**Document:** [Other desk] Clause-by-clause dissection of the 1992 SPA  
**Source:** `CASE_FILE/08_GAPS_TO_OBTAIN/SPA_CLAUSE_DISSECTION.md`  
**Section:** 3. Defects, ranked (strongest first)

---

#### 3. Defects, ranked (strongest first)

1. **Revocation, 15 Aug 2005 (Arts. 1919(1), 1920).** It ends the power. It is outside the four corners of the SPA, but it is decisive.
2. **The ¶7 buyer class.** The sale is "in favor of" only Llamanzares contract-to-sell holders or payors to Llamanzares (Art. 1881; Art. 1898; *Tumibay*). The deed recites the SPA by number, which charges the buyer with its terms (*San Pedro v. Ong*).
3. **"After prior consultation with us."** It is a condition in the writing, and none was sought (Annex Q p. 4). This is *Tumibay*'s condition of principal approval, in different words.
4. **No power to subdivide, segregate or partition (Art. 1878(15); Art. 1877 for ¶8).** This defeats the 2017 Partition-Subdivision Agreement and the 2021 registration (PD 1529 §58).
5. **¶6 purpose.** The authority is tied to settling pre-1992 real estate taxes (Art. 1881). Medium strength; Ramos calls it a recital.
6. **Property description is the T-4497 whole.** A sale of an unsegregated 2,587 sq m portion of a derivative lot is not described (*Woodchild*: "legally identical"). Medium; the Registry carried the SPA to T-52540 (PD 1529 §59).
7. **No price, minimum or terms.** This cuts both ways. Terms were left to "consultation," which reinforces defect 3. Standing alone it is not a defect: *Veloso* upheld a power "upon such terms and conditions … as my said attorney shall deem fit."

**Oddities that are not defects:**

- the operative verb is "negotiate";
- the deed and annotation misdescribe Munda's role;
- the "known/satisfactory evidence" box on the California form;
- the "Municipality of Daet" reading;
- no instrumental witnesses or marginal signatures on the copy;
- the PE-number variance;
- the ¶1 death date;
- no duration or substitution clause (Art. 1892 permits substitution unless prohibited; *Escueta v. Lim*, T1 audit).

**Where the SPA is adequate (concede):**

- it is in writing (Art. 1874);
- it is special as to the act of sale (Art. 1878(5), *Cosmic*);
- it identifies the property by title and technical description;
- all three co-owners signed;
- it was acknowledged before a Los Angeles notary and authenticated by the Philippine Consul (Act 2103 §2);
- it carries a power to sign and receive proceeds.

**Do not argue the SPA is void on its face.** Our own JA (Q23) concedes Cesar could sign, and the 2005 Era sale under it is unchallenged.

**Section:** 4. Cases on an SPA that mentions sale but still fails

---

#### 4. Cases on an SPA that mentions sale but still fails

All read in full on lawphil.

| Case | SPA wording examined (exact) | Holding (exact) | Bearing |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Tumibay v. Lopez**, G.R. No. 171692, 3 Jun 2013 (lawphil.net/judjuris/juri2013/jun2013/gr_171692_2013.html) | "To administer our real property … To offer for sale said properties, the selling price of which will be subject to our approval. x x x To sign all papers and documents on our behalf in a contract of sale x x x." | "...there would have been no obstacle to its use by Reynalda had the ensuing sale been consummated according to its terms." "...acted beyond the scope of her authority … at a price … not approved by [the principals] … Under Article 1898 of the Civil Code, the sale is void and petitioners are, thus, entitled to the reconveyance of the subject land." | **Best support for Jonathan's bottom line.** A conditioned SPA, a sale outside the condition, and a buyer aware of the limits made the sale void, with the title cancelled and the old title reinstated. **Against his premise:** the SPA form itself was adequate. |
| **Bautista-Spille v. NICORP**, G.R. No. 214057, 19 Oct 2015 | "...to act as my/our general representative(s) and agent(s) with full authority to buy, sell, negotiate and contract for me/us…" | "Nowhere in the General Power of Attorney was Benjamin granted, expressly or impliedly, any power to sell the subject property or a portion thereof. The authority expressed in the General Power of Attorney was couched in very broad terms covering petitioner's businesses and properties." | **Sale was mentioned and still held insufficient**, because the wording was general and named no property. **Distinguishable:** ours names the property. Applies to the 1991 SPA and to ¶8. |
| **Woodchild Holdings v. Roxas Electric**, G.R. No. 140667, 12 Aug 2004 | "to sell Lot No. 491-A-3-B-2 covered by TCT No. 78086, with an area of 7,213 square meters…" | "The act done must be legally identical with that authorized to be done." | Specific property and specific act. Supports defects 2 and 6. |
| **Cosmic Lumber v. CA**, G.R. No. 114311, 29 Nov 1996 | Ejectment and compromise power | "When there is any reasonable doubt that the language so used conveys such power, no such construction shall be given the document." | Supports reading ¶7's limits strictly. Does not void ¶7 itself. |
| **Veloso v. CA**, G.R. No. 102737, 21 Aug 1996 (lawphil …/juri1996/aug1996/gr_102737_1996.html) | "To buy or sell, hire or lease, mortgage … more specifically TCT No. 49138, upon such terms and conditions … as my said attorney shall deem fit and proper." | "...where the power to sell is specific, it not being merely implied, much less couched in general terms, there can not be any doubt that the attorney in fact may execute a valid sale." | **Adverse to Jonathan's premise.** A power naming the title with a sale power was upheld, even without a price. |

**Searched and not found (UNVERIFIED; not cited):** a decision voiding an SPA for:

- using "negotiate" plus "sign";
- naming no price;
- being executed by co-owners over undivided land;
- describing a mother title instead of a derivative lot.

**Plainly: none found.**

## [Other desk] What 'prior consultation' means

**Document:** [Other desk] What 'prior consultation' means  
**Source:** `CASE_FILE/08_GAPS_TO_OBTAIN/CONSULTATION_MEANING.md`  
**Section:** Preamble and summary

---

### What "prior consultation" means in SPA ¶7

*Civil Case 26-360, Zschoche v. Balane, MTC Mercedes · 16 Oct 2026, 10:40 PM PHT · internal research for counsel*


**Document:** [Other desk] What 'prior consultation' means  
**Source:** `CASE_FILE/08_GAPS_TO_OBTAIN/CONSULTATION_MEANING.md`  
**Section:** Verdict

---

#### Verdict

"After prior consultation with us" is a **written limit on Cesar's power to sell**. Under the SPA's own words, the terms of any sale were to be fixed only after the principals had been consulted.

The Supreme Court has treated "prior consultation" in an authority to sell as a **condition** of that authority. In *Esguerra v. CA* (G.R. 119310, 1997), it refused to read the condition in because the instrument did not contain it. Ours does contain it, so the same reasoning cuts the other way. Because the limit is in the power "as written," it binds the buyer (Art. 1900; Art. 1902). Read with "us," and with three principals who are co-owners, it means **each sister, before the terms were set and before the 29 Sep 2016 deed**.

**Where Ramos has a point.** On its plain meaning, consultation is *not* consent. The LGC cases list "prior consultation" and "prior approval" as two separate requirements. Read for itself, ¶7 leaves the final call on terms to the agent ("which said Attorney-in-Fact may deem fit"). So we should not argue that ¶7 required the sisters to approve the sale. Our argument is narrower and stronger: **no consultation of any kind took place**, so the condition never happened and the power to sell on any terms never arose (Arts. 1181, 1881, 1898).

The open risk is proof, not meaning. The burden of that proof is dealt with in the CONSULTATION_GAP_PLAN.

**Source:** `CASE_FILE/08_GAPS_TO_OBTAIN/CONSULTATION_MEANING.md`  
**Section:** 1. The text: ¶7, read on the certified copy

---

#### 1. The text: ¶7, read on the certified copy

Source: Def. Exh. 7 (LRA e-CTC Ref. 2026001843, p. 3 of 4; the instrument's own page "-2-"). Rendered at 400 dpi and read directly. Our Exh. E has 2 pages. The defense copy is the complete one.

> "7. Accordingly, we hereby name, constitute and appoint CESAR M. DE LA FUENTE … to be our true and lawful attorney-in-fact, for us and in our name, place and stead, to negotiate for the sale of the above-described real estate property in favor of third persons in possession of CONTRACTS TO SELL executed by the previous administrator, MR. BEN LLAMANZARES and/or to those who have paid their down-payments or have made partial payments therefor to the latter. **Said sale shall be upon such terms and conditions and under such covenants which said Attorney-in-Fact may deem fit, proper and convenient, after prior consultation with us,** and to sign and execute the necessary documents, agreements and other legal papers pertaining thereto, as well as to receive the proceeds of the aforementioned sale in our collective behalf."

The elided words are "**and under such covenants which said Attorney-in-Fact may deem fit, proper and convenient,**". The left margin of the scan is clipped on two lines. "and" and "deem" are read from the full-page render. [VERIFY on the paper certified copy.] Nothing in ¶7 requires the consultation to be in writing.

**Source:** `CASE_FILE/08_GAPS_TO_OBTAIN/CONSULTATION_MEANING.md`  
**Section:** 2. Rules, quoted from the Civil Code (lawphil.net, RA 386)

---

#### 2. Rules, quoted from the Civil Code (lawphil.net, RA 386)

Source: https://lawphil.net/statutes/repacts/ra1949/ra_386_1949.html. Text read in full.

**Interpretation**
- **Art. 1370.** "If the terms of a contract are clear and leave no doubt upon the intention of the contracting parties, the literal meaning of its stipulations shall control. If the words appear to be contrary to the evident intention of the parties, the latter shall prevail over the former."
- **Art. 1371.** "In order to judge the intention of the contracting parties, their contemporaneous and subsequent acts shall be principally considered."
- **Art. 1372.** "However general the terms of a contract may be, they shall not be understood to comprehend things that are distinct and cases that are different from those upon which the parties intended to agree."
- **Art. 1373.** "If some stipulation of any contract should admit of several meanings, it shall be understood as bearing that import which is most adequate to render it effectual."
- **Art. 1374.** "The various stipulations of a contract shall be interpreted together, attributing to the doubtful ones that sense which may result from all of them taken jointly."
- **Art. 1375.** "Words which may have different significations shall be understood in that which is most in keeping with the nature and object of the contract."
- **Art. 1376.** "The usage or custom of the place shall be borne in mind in the interpretation of the ambiguities of a contract, and shall fill the omission of stipulations which are ordinarily established."
- **Art. 1377.** "The interpretation of obscure words or stipulations in a contract shall not favor the party who caused the obscurity."
- **Art. 1378** (excerpt). "If the contract is onerous, the doubt shall be settled in favor of the greatest reciprocity of interests. If the doubts are cast upon the principal object of the contract in such a way that it cannot be known what may have been the intention or will of the parties, the contract shall be null and void."
- **Art. 1379.** "The principles of interpretation stated in Rule 123 of the Rules of Court shall likewise be observed in the construction of contracts."

**Agency**
- **Art. 1878(5).** Special powers of attorney are necessary "To enter into any contract by which the ownership of an immovable is transmitted or acquired either gratuitously or for a valuable consideration."
- **Art. 1881.** "The agent must act within the scope of his authority. He may do such acts as may be conducive to the accomplishment of the purpose of the agency."
- **Art. 1887.** "In the execution of the agency, the agent shall act in accordance with the instructions of the principal. In default thereof, he shall do all that a good father of a family would do, as required by the nature of the business."
- **Art. 1898.** "If the agent contracts in the name of the principal, exceeding the scope of his authority, and the principal does not ratify the contract, it shall be void if the party with whom the agent contracted is aware of the limits of the powers granted by the principal. …"
- **Art. 1900.** "So far as third persons are concerned, an act is deemed to have been performed within the scope of the agent's authority, if such act is within the terms of the power of attorney, as written, even if the agent has in fact exceeded the limits of his authority according to an understanding between the principal and the agent."
- **Art. 1902.** "A third person with whom the agent wishes to contract on behalf of the principal may require the presentation of the power of attorney, or the instructions as regards the agency. Private or secret orders and instructions of the principal do not prejudice third persons who have relied upon the power of attorney or instructions shown them."

**Conditions**
- **Art. 1181.** "In conditional obligations, the acquisition of rights, as well as the extinguishment or loss of those already acquired, shall depend upon the happening of the event which constitutes the condition."
- **Art. 1182.** "When the fulfillment of the condition depends upon the sole will of the debtor, the conditional obligation shall be void. If it depends upon chance or upon the will of a third person, the obligation shall take effect in conformity with the provisions of this Code."
- **Art. 1184.** "The condition that some event happen at a determinate time shall extinguish the obligation as soon as the time expires or if it has become indubitable that the event will not take place."
- **Art. 1186.** "The condition shall be deemed fulfilled when the obligor voluntarily prevents its fulfillment."
- Arts. 1183 and 1185 were read and are not material here.

**Notice to co-owners**
- **Art. 1623.** "The right of legal pre-emption or redemption shall not be exercised except within thirty days from the notice in writing by the prospective vendor, or by the vendor, as the case may be. The deed of sale shall not be recorded in the Registry of Property, unless accompanied by an affidavit of the vendor that he has given written notice thereof to all possible redemptioners. …"

**Codal point to note.** The Arts. 1181–1186 provisions govern *conditional obligations*. A condition on an agent's authority is not strictly an "obligation." Use them by analogy: until the event happens, the right (here, the power to sell on terms) does not arise. The direct rules are Arts. 1881, 1887 and 1898. [Counsel's call on how to frame it.]


**Document:** [Other desk] What 'prior consultation' means  
**Source:** `CASE_FILE/08_GAPS_TO_OBTAIN/CONSULTATION_MEANING.md`  
**Section:** 3. Supreme Court decisions, read in full on lawphil

---

#### 3. Supreme Court decisions, read in full on lawphil

**A. *Esguerra v. Court of Appeals*, G.R. No. 119310, 3 Feb 1997 (3rd Div., Panganiban, J.).** VERIFIED, read in full. https://www.lawphil.net/judjuris/juri1997/feb1997/gr_119310_1997.html

This is the only SC case found on "prior consultation" as a limit on a power to sell. A co-owner attacked a sale because she "has not been notified nor consulted as to the terms and conditions of the sale." The trial court voided half the sale on that ground. The SC reversed, because the authority "specified no condition that the latter shall first consult with the former prior to selling":

> "the trial court's decision … is tainted with grave abuse of discretion for having injudiciously added 'prior consultation' to VECCI's authority to sell the properties, **a condition not contained** in the judicially-approved compromise agreement."

It also applied Art. 1900: third persons may rely on the power "as written."

- **For us:** the SC calls prior consultation "a condition" of authority to sell, about "the terms and conditions of the sale." Here it *is* written, so Art. 1900 puts it in the buyer's face.
- **For Ramos:** the SC also says one earlier consultation "did not affect nor vary the terms of the authority". Course of dealing does not add or drop terms.

**B. *Lina v. Paño*, G.R. No. 129093, 30 Aug 2001.** VERIFIED, read in full. https://lawphil.net/judjuris/juri2001/aug2001/gr_129093_2001.html

The Court quotes LGC §26, which states what a consultation consists of: the duty "to consult with the local government units, nongovernmental organizations, and other sectors concerned **and explain the goals and objectives of the project or program, its impact upon the people and the community** …". §27 requires "consultations … and prior approval" as separate items. The holding is that §27 is limited to the listed ecological projects; it does not define consultation beyond this.

**C. *Bangus Fry Fisherfolk v. Lanzanas*, G.R. No. 131442, 10 Jul 2003.** VERIFIED, read in full. https://lawphil.net/judjuris/juri2003/jul2003/gr_131442_2003.html

> §§26–27 "require every national government agency … **to hold prior consultations** with the local government unit concerned **and to secure the prior approval** of its sanggunian before implementing …"

Consultation and approval are again two distinct steps. The case also holds that the missing paper proof of consultations in an ECC file does not by itself void the ECC. That is a point about proof in a regulatory setting, not a definition.

**D. *Province of Rizal v. Executive Secretary*, G.R. No. 129546, 13 Dec 2005 (En Banc).** VERIFIED, read in full. https://lawphil.net/judjuris/juri2005/dec2005/gr_129546_2005.html

> "two requisites must be met …: **prior consultation with the affected local communities, and prior approval** of the project by the appropriate sanggunian. **Absent either of these mandatory requirements, the project's implementation is illegal.**"

The government official's claim that "they had conducted the required consultations," qualified by "the officials we may have been talking with … may no longer be incumbent," was called an "ambivalent reply." The Court treated it as not establishing consultation.

- **For us:** where consultation is required beforehand, its absence alone defeats the act. A vague claim of past talks is not proof.

**E. IPRA, RA 8371 §3(g)** (statute, not a decision). https://lawphil.net/statutes/repacts/ra1997/ra_8371_1997.html

> FPIC "shall mean the **consensus of all members** … obtained after **fully disclosing the intent and scope of the activity** …"

This shows the legislature uses "consent" when it means consent. Consultation is the lesser term. Its useful content here is full disclosure *before* the act.

**Not found or not read:**
- No SC decision defining consultation in a labor or CBA setting was read in full for this memo. UNVERIFIED; do not cite.
- No other agency case defining "consultation" was found.
- *Tumibay v. Lopez* (G.R. 171692, 2013; our verified file) concerns an SPA requiring the principals' *approval* of price. It is the consent analogue, not a consultation case.

**Section:** 4. Dictionary meaning

---

#### 4. Dictionary meaning

**Merriam-Webster** (merriam-webster.com, fetched 16 Oct 2026):
- *consult* (v.): "to ask the advice or opinion of"; "to deliberate together : confer"; "to have regard to : consider."
- *consultation* (n.): "the act of consulting or conferring"; "council, conference."

Black's Law Dictionary was not accessed. UNVERIFIED; do not cite.


**Document:** [Other desk] What 'prior consultation' means  
**Source:** `CASE_FILE/08_GAPS_TO_OBTAIN/CONSULTATION_MEANING.md`  
**Section:** 5. Application: rule, application, disposition

---

#### 5. Application: rule, application, disposition

**Rule.** Plain words control (Art. 1370). The clauses are read together and in the sense that makes them work (Arts. 1373, 1374). An agent must keep within the written power and the principal's instructions (Arts. 1881, 1887). A sale beyond those limits, with a buyer aware of them, is void unless ratified (Art. 1898; *Tumibay*). A limit written in the power binds third persons (Arts. 1900, 1902; *Esguerra*). A required prior consultation that did not happen defeats the act (*Province of Rizal*). Consultation means seeking the views of the persons concerned, with disclosure of what is proposed, before acting (M-W; LGC §26 as quoted in *Lina*).

**Application.**

1. **What it requires.** "Said sale shall be upon such terms … which said Attorney-in-Fact may deem fit … **after prior consultation with us**." The agent's discretion over terms is real, but it is timed and conditioned: it begins only *after* consultation. At a minimum, the principals had to be told of the proposed buyer and terms, and asked for their views, before the terms were set.
2. **Who.** "Us" are the three signatories, Geraldine, Patricia and Marcia, who are also co-owners. The same "us" and "our" run through ¶7 ("our true and lawful attorney-in-fact," "in our collective behalf"). Read together (Art. 1374), consulting one sister is not consulting "us." Each co-owner's own share is at stake (Art. 493 context), which supports this under Art. 1375.
3. **When.** "Prior" fixes the time: before the terms and before the deed of 29 Sep 2016. Anything after the deed is ratification, not consultation, and the owners' 2020 publication repudiates ratification (Exh. G).
4. **Form.** Not specified. Oral consultation would satisfy the text. But someone has to prove it happened, with whom and when.
5. **Our facts.**
   - No letter, email or acknowledgment of any consultation exists in either side's exhibits.
   - The deed itself recites only the SPA and that "the provision of Article 1623 … had been complied with." It does **not** recite any consultation with the principals.
   - The Art. 1623 recital is a statement about *written notice to possible redemptioners*. In a sale made by the co-owners themselves through their agent, the "redemptioners" would be the co-owner sisters. If the defense relies on that recital, it amounts to a claim that written notice was sent. They must produce it, or the recital is shown to be unsupported. Use it on cross and at rebuttal (Patricia: no notice received). It is not proof of consultation.
6. **Purpose.** The power was conditioned for an evident reason. The principals lived abroad, so terms were to be shaped with them (Art. 1375). A reading that lets the agent skip the consultation makes the clause meaningless. Art. 1373 favors the reading that gives it effect.

**Ramos's best reading, and our answer**

| Ramos | Answer |
|---|---|
| Consultation is not consent. The agent decides terms "as he may deem fit." | Agreed, and we do not argue consent. The point is that *no* consultation occurred, so the condition on the discretion never happened (Arts. 1181, 1881; *Esguerra* treats it as "a condition"; *Rizal*: absent the prior step, the act is illegal). |
| One sister is enough. | "Us" means all three signatories, in a clause that also says "in our collective behalf" (Arts. 1370, 1374). Each co-owner's share is affected. In any case, the defense has no evidence that even one sister was consulted. |
| Oral is enough. | Accepted as to form. But oral consultation still needs a witness to who, when and what terms. Cesar is dead, and no defense affidavit says he consulted anyone (Gloria's excluded affidavit S43: no direct contact with the sisters). |
| Consultation was a private instruction; the buyer relied on the SPA (Art. 1902). | It is in the SPA itself, "as written" (Art. 1900; *Esguerra*). A full copy was pulled in Aug 2021 (Def. Exh. 7, p. 3: LRA Ref. 2021004160, requested by Karen Gay Elevado). The 1996 annotation pointed to the instrument (PD 1529 §52). |
| Art. 1377: construe against the drafter. The principals drafted it, so read it loosely. | Art. 1377 applies only to "obscure" words. "After prior consultation with us" is clear (Art. 1370). And the agent, not the buyer, is the party the rule would protect. |
| Ratified later by accepting the arrangement or by Patricia's 2023 affidavit. | No proceeds reached any principal. The owners published a repudiation in 2020 (Exh. G). On the same day as the 2023 affidavit, Patricia filed an adverse claim calling the sale void (Exh. I). |

**Disposition.** Present the consultation clause as a **condition on the power to set terms and sell, owed to each of the three principals before 29 Sep 2016**. Its non-occurrence makes the sale exceed the authority, and it is void under Art. 1898 as against a buyer charged with the SPA's terms. Do not argue that consultation meant approval. Pair the clause with the ¶7 buyer class (lead) and the 2005 revocation (backup).

#### 6. Use in the case and gaps

- **Cite:** *Esguerra* (lead on meaning, and Art. 1900 "as written"), *Province of Rizal* (absence of a required prior step invalidates the act), *Tumibay* (Art. 1898 voidness).
- **Cross the defense:** ask for any written notice behind the deed's Art. 1623 recital, and any letter or message to any sister before 29 Sep 2016.
- **Rebuttal:** Patricia (and, if possible, Geraldine and Marcia) states they were never consulted about a sale to Gloria Balane, its terms or its price.
- **VERIFY:** the two clipped words in ¶7 on the paper certified copy; Black's definition; any labor or agency SC case defining consultation; whether counsel frames the clause under Arts. 1181–1186 or only under Arts. 1881, 1887 and 1898.

**Section:** 0 · The breach in one paragraph

---

#### 0 · The breach in one paragraph
- ¶7 of the 1992 SPA (Exh. E p. 2) limits any sale to the Llamanzares class "after prior consultation with us."
- On 14 Oct Ramos showed that Jonathan was not present for anything before 2025. As the record stands, **no witness with personal knowledge has said the owners were not consulted** about a sale to Gloria Balane.
- Ramos will argue this in two steps:
  - non-consultation is a fact the plaintiff must prove (Rule 131 §1);
  - in any case it is a "private" instruction that Art. 1902 disregards.
- The plug is (a) a principal's own testimony, (b) independent records showing no owner act, and (c) the defense's own admissions. The burden argument is only a backstop.

**Section:** 2 · Proof sources for non-consultation

#### 2 · Proof sources for non-consultation

**Deadlines.** Plaintiff's evidence closes **9 Dec 2026**. A new judicial affidavit (JA) must be filed **5 days before** the setting at which the witness testifies (JAR §2). The last such date is **4 Dec** for 9 Dec, or **27 Nov** for 2 Dec.

| # | Source | Holder | How to obtain (rule) | Needed by | Strength |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| P1 | **Patricia's first-hand testimony** that she was never consulted, never approved, and never received anything | Patricia (plaintiff) | Her own JA. Filed after pre-trial, it needs leave (JAR §10(a), one late submission). If abroad, a deposition (Rule 23) or videoconference (see §4) | Decide **21 Oct**; motion with JA **by 27 Nov** (for 2 Dec), at the latest **4 Dec** (for 9 Dec) | **STRONG.** A principal's direct denial is the only first-hand proof. Risk: Ramos's cross on 2005–2016 communications with Cesar or Agcaoili, and on the 2023 affidavit |
| P2 | **The other co-owners**: **Geraldine Keesey Hoppe** and **Marcia Ellen Keesey** (named in T-4497's adjudication entry, Annex AA; Exh. F; Annex J; Exh. B) | Themselves (abroad [VERIFY]); living status and capacity [VERIFY] | Short JAs or depositions abroad (Rule 23 §§4(c)(2), 11); or affidavits used only to support the motion for leave | Same as P1 | **STRONG** if available; each is a principal. Even one corroborating co-owner removes "self-serving" |
| P3 | **The 2005 revocation and its notices**: Exh. F (15 Aug 2005), publication in the Bicol Post 25 Sep, 2 Oct and 9 Oct 2020 (Exh. G, G-1), the CC 8563 pleading served 9 Jul 2021 (Annex N) | Plaintiff; RTC Br. 41 | In evidence (F, G). Annex N by certified copy plus subpoena duces tecum (Rule 21; JAR §5) | Annex N certified copy by **27 Nov** | **MEDIUM.** It proves the owners' will **against** any sale by Cesar after 2005, which makes consultation-and-approval of a 2016 sale implausible. It is not direct proof of no consultation |
| P5 | **Correspondence showing the owners objecting**: e.g., the 21 Jul 2019 Davis/Grippo letter Agcaoili answered; any owner letters to Cesar 2005–2016; the 2025 adverse claim and the 18 Aug 2025 demand (Exh. J) | Patricia, Agcaoili, Jonathan | Production by the witness; subpoena duces tecum to Agcaoili (Rule 21) | **27 Nov** | **MEDIUM.** Shows objection, not the absence of consultation in 2016. Anything before 2016 is the valuable part [PATRICIA TO CONFIRM what exists] |
| P6 | **Notarial registers** (Asis, Daet): Doc. 304/61/CXVI/2016 (deed); Doc. 115/23/CXXII/2017 (confirmation); Doc. 402/81/CXXII/2017 (partition). Makati (Parungo): Doc. 331/28/7/2023 | Notary; Office of the Clerk of Court, RTC Daet (notarial section) | Certified copies of the register entries; subpoena duces tecum (Rule 21; JAR §5). The notarial-rule text is UNVERIFIED | Request **by 21 Oct**; returns by **27 Nov** | **STRONG** (independent). The register shows who appeared and with what ID. If no owner appears and the deed date or number does not match, that is independent proof |
| P7 | **RD files of Entries 2021003235 and 2021003236**: every instrument presented | RD Camarines Norte | Certified true copies (in hand for 3235: Annex K; for 3236 the plan and technical descriptions only: Annex L); subpoena the full 3236 file | **27 Nov** | **STRONG** (independent). No owner signature or consent is in the 3235 file as held [VERIFY on the full certified set]. The 3236 partition instrument must be obtained |
| P8 | **Burden of proof** (backstop, not a plug) | — | Argument | Memorandum | **MEDIUM** (see the text below) |

**P8 text (verified):**
- **Art. 1898:** "If the agent contracts in the name of the principal, exceeding the scope of his authority, and the principal does not ratify the contract, it shall be void if the party with whom the agent contracted is aware of the limits of the powers granted by the principal."
- ***Heirs of Bucton v. Spouses Go*, G.R. No. 188395, 20 Nov 2013:** "The burden of proving the status of a purchaser in good faith and for value lies upon one who asserts that status. This onus probandi cannot be discharged by mere invocation of the ordinary presumption of good faith."
- **Rule 131 §1:** "Burden of proof is the duty of a party to present evidence on the facts in issue necessary to establish his claim or defense by the amount of evidence required by law."

**Reading:** *Bucton* puts the buyer's good faith on the buyer. Rule 131 §1 leaves the elements of our own claim on us. So non-consultation must be **proved** by P1 or P2 with P4, P6 and P7. The burden argument only supports that proof.

#### 3 · Draft judicial affidavit of Patricia Keesey Zschoche (DRAFT, for counsel)

This would be her **first** JA. It is called "supplemental" only because it comes after the Complaint's JAs. Do not file it until every [PATRICIA TO CONFIRM] item is answered by her, and strike any answer she cannot give from her own knowledge.

> **Republic of the Philippines · Municipal Trial Court of Mercedes · Fifth Judicial Region · Mercedes, Camarines Norte**
> PATRICIA KEESEY ZSCHOCHE, herein represented by her attorney-in-fact JONATHAN PAUL ZSCHOCHE, Plaintiff, – versus – SPOUSES EFREN BALANE and GLORIA BALANE, SPOUSES JOMIL TORRALBA and PRINCESS BALANE TORRALBA, and ENGR. ERWIN BALANE, et al., Defendants. Civil Case No. 26-360. For: "Accion Reivindicatoria, Recovery of Possession, Declaration of Nullity of Deed of Absolute Sale, Cancellation of Transfer Certificate of Title No. 079-2021002126 and Declaration of Real Property ARP No. GR-2023-II-07-001-00256, Accounting, and Damages"
>
> **JUDICIAL AFFIDAVIT OF PATRICIA KEESEY ZSCHOCHE**
>

>
> **Purpose.** To prove that neither I nor, to my knowledge, my co-owners were consulted on, approved, or ratified any sale of any portion of the land covered by TCT No. T-52540 to Gloria H. Balane; to identify Exhibit F and the letter of 15 August 2019; to explain the affidavit of loss of 22 February 2023; and to prove that no part of the price was received by me.
>
> **Q1.** Who are the registered owners of the land covered by TCT No. T-52540? **A1.** My sisters Geraldine Keesey Hoppe and Marcia Ellen Keesey, and I, as heirs of our mother, Mary Worrick Keesey. (Exh. B)

**Drafting cautions:**
- No adjectives about anyone. Not one word about Cesar's motives.
- Do not use "terminated for cause".
- If Patricia cannot answer a question first-hand, strike it.
- **Capacity:** TRACK_E flags a "guardianship status" question bearing on authority [VERIFY]. Counsel must confirm Patricia can testify and sign before drafting further.

The court may, however, allow only once the late submission of the same provided, the delay is for a valid reason, would not unduly prejudice the opposing party, and the defaulting party pays a fine of not less than P1,000.00 nor more than P5,000.00 at the discretion of the court." **Section:** 4 · If Patricia is abroad: how she could testify

---

#### 4 · If Patricia is abroad: how she could testify

- **Deposition, Rule 23 (1997 text as on lawphil; the 2019 amendments did not reach Rule 23 [VERIFY]):**
  - **§1:** "By leave of court after jurisdiction has been obtained … or without such leave after an answer has been served, the testimony of any person, whether a party or not, may be taken, at the instance of any party, by deposition upon oral examination or written interrogatories."
  - **§4(c):** the deposition "may be used by any party for any purpose if the court finds: … (2) that the witness resides at a distance more than one hundred (100) kilometers from the place of trial or hearing, or is out of the Philippines, unless it appears that his absence was procured by the party offering the deposition."
  - **§11:** "In a foreign state or country, depositions may be taken (a) on notice before a secretary of embassy or legation, consul general, consul, vice-consul, or consular agent of the Republic of the Philippines, (b) before such person or officer as may be appointed by commission or under letters rogatory; or (c) the person referred to in section 14 hereof."
  - **Practical steps:** notice to the defense; a consular appointment; the transcript must reach the court before 9 Dec. Start by **21 Oct**.
  - **Risk:** a deposition of the plaintiff herself, offered by the plaintiff, invites the argument that "his absence was procured by the party offering the deposition." Counsel to assess.
- **Videoconference testimony:** under the Supreme Court's guidelines on videoconferencing hearings (A.M. No. 20-12-01-SC) **[UNVERIFIED: text not fetched]**. The guidelines reportedly allow a witness abroad to testify from a Philippine embassy or consulate, on motion and with the court's leave. Counsel to fetch the text and file the motion early, with the JA.
- **Jurat abroad:** the JA can be sworn before a Philippine consular officer. The consular-oath rules are [UNVERIFIED].
- **Choice:** testimony by videoconference with a consular-sworn JA keeps cross live and answers Ramos's "personal presence" theme best. Use a deposition as the fallback.

#### Five-line summary
1. **Holes:** the deed's Art. 1623 "complied with" recital; Cesar's Deed of Confirmation ("ratify and confirm"); the unseen 2017 partition; the Answer ¶¶44(d)–(e) and 45 "outward appearance … allowed"; the estate-named receipts; Patricia's 2023 affidavit of loss; and Salvador S31's "Agcaoili law office" channel.
2. **The defense admits more than it claims:** no defense document shows any owner consulted. Salvador S31 says the owners never came home. Gloria's own excluded JA says "Wala po akong naging tuwirang komunikasyon kina Patricia, Geraldine, o Marcia bago ang bentahan" [CONDITIONAL].
3. **Best plug:** Patricia's own JA, plus Geraldine and/or Marcia Ellen if available, decided by 21 Oct and filed with the single §10(a) leave by 27 Nov. Testimony by videoconference or consular deposition (Rule 23 §§4(c)(2), 11).
4. **Independent plugs:** notarial registers for Docs. 304, 115 and 402 (Asis) and 331 (Parungo), plus the full RD 3235/3236 files. These show who actually appeared and signed. Request by 21 Oct.
5. **Corroboration:** the Agcaoili letter of 15 Aug 2019 meets Salvador's own named channel. With Exh. F and G, it shows the owners' contrary will. Burden (*Bucton*, Art. 1898) is a backstop only, because Rule 131 §1 keeps non-consultation on us.

---

---
title: "SPA language in Supreme Court land-sale cases, compared with Exh. E"
subtitle: "Civil Case No. 26-360 · Zschoche v. Balane · prepared for counsel · 14 Oct 2026 · DRAFT, not for filing"
---


**Document:** [Other desk] SPA language jurisprudence  
**Source:** `CASE_FILE/08_GAPS_TO_OBTAIN/SPA_LANGUAGE_JURISPRUDENCE.md`  
**Section:** Verdict

---

#### Verdict

**The Supreme Court reads an SPA's words strictly. Our best uses of that rule are the ¶7 buyer class, the 2005 revocation, Cesar's death, and his own confirmation deed. "Negotiate only" is not one of them.**

**Negotiate vs. sell (test c).** I found **no decision that holds that the words "negotiate for the sale" in an SPA, standing alone, withhold a power to sell.** The closest holding is *Litonjua v. Eternit* (2006): "an authority to find a purchaser of real property does not include an authority to sell." That case involved a broker with no written authority at all. Our SPA ¶7 adds "to sign and execute the necessary documents … as well as to receive the proceeds." Under *Cosmic Lumber*'s test ("expressly mentions a sale or … includes a sale as a necessary ingredient"), a court will likely read ¶7 as a power to complete a sale **for the ¶7 class**. Two other readings are also against us:

- The registry annotation reads "to sign, negotiate for the sale."
- The 1991 Agcaoili SPA (whose text we do not hold) reads "power to sign for and in behalf of."

**Run "negotiate-only" as a minor point at most.** The rows that follow are rated by strength.

**What the cases strongly support:**

1. **Strict construction tied to the property and the act.** *Woodchild* (2004): authority to sell one titled lot did not authorize burdening or selling a portion of a different titled lot. "The act done must be legally identical with that authorized to be done." See also *Bautista-Spille* and *Cosmic Lumber*.
2. **Buyer's duty to read the agent's authority.** See *San Pedro v. Ong*, and *Yabut* ("especially when the property is co-owned").
3. **Acts after the agency ended are void.** *Rallos* (1978): an agent's sale of a co-owner's undivided share after the principal's death is "void ab initio." This applies to anything Cesar is said to have done after 21 Jun 2017, and by parity to acts after revocation (Art. 1919).
4. **A void sale cannot be ratified.** See *AF Realty*, *MCIAA v. Unchuan* and *Aggabao*.

**Main risks:**

- *Sarili*: a notarized SPA's face is "sufficient inquiry." Ramos will say the face says "sign, negotiate for the sale."
- *Pahud*: the Court upheld a co-heir's sale without written authority **by estoppel**, because the other co-heirs later admitted it.

---

**How to read the tables.** Every case below was read in full on lawphil.net, and quotations are verbatim from that text. "SPA clause" means the authority language the Court examined. Under **Comparison**, "ours" refers to:

- Exh. E ¶3: T-4497, Lot 2, Psd-12802, 139,132 sq m, undivided.
- ¶6: tax purpose.
- ¶7: Llamanzares class, plus "prior consultation," plus "sign and execute … receive the proceeds."
- Registry annotation (Exh. B p. 3): "to sign, negotiate for the sale of the parcel of land in this title."
- 2016 deed (Annex K p. 3): cites the 1992 SPA, Doc 1165/Pg 1187/Bk 3798, and names Lot 2-X-6-I-4-C (portion), with no title number.
- 1991 Agcaoili SPA (Exh. B p. 5): "power to sign for and in behalf of." Text not in hand.


**Document:** [Other desk] SPA language jurisprudence  
**Source:** `CASE_FILE/08_GAPS_TO_OBTAIN/SPA_LANGUAGE_JURISPRUDENCE.md`  
**Section:** Tier 1: strongest for us

---

#### Tier 1: strongest for us

| # | Case | SPA / authority clause examined (exact) | Court's holding on the language (exact) | Outcome | Comparison with ours | Rating |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Woodchild Holdings v. Roxas Electric**, G.R. No. 140667, 12 Aug 2004 — lawphil.net/judjuris/juri2004/aug2004/gr_140667_2004.html | Board resolution authorizing the president "to sell Lot No. 491-A-3-B-2 covered by TCT No. 78086, with an area of 7,213 square meters, at a price and under such terms and conditions which he deemed most reasonable and advantageous" | "Powers of attorney are generally construed strictly and courts will not infer or presume broad powers from deeds which do not sufficiently include property or subject under which the agent is to deal. The general rule is that the power of attorney must be pursued within legal strictures, and the agent can neither go beyond it; nor beside it. The act done must be legally identical with that authorized to be done." Also: "Absent estoppel or ratification, apparent authority cannot remedy the lack of the written power required under the statutes." | The deed's provisions on the neighbouring titled lot (TCT 78085) were held **not binding** on the principal. | **Best match on "which property."** There, the authority named one title and the agent dealt with another. Ours named T-4497 Lot 2 as a whole; the sale was of an unsegregated portion of a derivative lot under T-52540. **Distinguishable:** T-52540 lies *inside* the T-4497 parcel described; in Woodchild the other lot was separate from the start. Use it for "legally identical" and "neither go beyond it; nor beside it": a sale outside the ¶7 class is "beside" the power. | **Helps (strong)** |
| 2 | **Cosmic Lumber Corp. v. CA**, G.R. No. 114311, 29 Nov 1996 — lawphil …/juri1996/nov1996/gr_114311_1996.html | "...to initiate, institute and file any court action for the ejectment of third persons and/or squatters of the entire lot 9127 and 443 and covered by TCT Nos. 37648 and 37649 ... and for this purpose, to appear at the pre-trial conference and enter into any stipulation of facts and/or compromise agreement so far as it shall protect the rights and interest of the corporation in the aforementioned lots." | "The express mandate required by law to enable an appointee of an agency (couched) in general terms to sell must be one that expressly mentions a sale or that includes a sale as a necessary ingredient of the act mentioned. For the principal to confer the right upon an agent to sell real estate, a power of attorney must so express the powers of the agent in clear and unmistakable language. When there is any reasonable doubt that the language so used conveys such power, no such construction shall be given the document." "The sale ipso jure is consequently void." | The agent sold a portion through a compromise. The sale was **void**. | **Helps** on strict reading of ¶7's limits (class, consultation). **Cuts against "negotiate-only":** ¶7 does "expressly mention a sale" and "sign and execute." | **Helps / double edge** |
| 3 | **Rallos v. Felix Go Chan & Sons Realty**, G.R. No. L-24332, 31 Jan 1978 — lawphil …/juri1978/jan1978/gr_24332_1978.html | A power of attorney by co-owner sisters to sell their undivided shares in Lot 5983; the agent sold one sister's undivided share after her death. | "...it follows a fortiori that any act of an agent after the death of his principal is void ab initio unless the same falls under the exception provided for in the aforementioned Articles 1930 and 1931. Article 1931, being an exception to the general rule, is to be strictly construed..." | The sale was **void**, even as against a buyer in good faith. | The facts concern an undivided co-owned lot and an act after extinction, which is our setting. Use it for anything executed or presented after Cesar's death on 21 Jun 2017 (e.g., the 2021 registration of the 2016 deed and of the 2017 Confirmation). The analogy to revocation runs through Art. 1919 [INFERRED]. **Distinguishable:** the extinguishing event there was the principal's death; ours is revocation in 2005, and Art. 1921/1922 notice issues apply. | **Helps (strong)** |
| 4 | **San Pedro v. Ong**, G.R. No. 177598, 17 Oct 2008 — lawphil …/juri2008/oct2008/gr_177598_2008.html | An SPA used to mortgage land (the Court examined the dealing party's inquiry). | "...every person dealing with an agent is put upon inquiry, and must discover upon his peril the authority of the agent, and this is especially true where the act of the agent is of unusual nature. If a person makes no inquiry, he is chargeable with knowledge of the agent's authority, and his ignorance of that authority will not be any excuse." | The mortgagee was **not** in good faith. | This supports Art. 1898: the deed cites the SPA by Doc/Pg/Bk, so Gloria is charged with ¶7's class and consultation terms. | **Helps** |
| 5 | **Spouses Yabut v. Nachbaur**, G.R. No. 243470, 12 Jan 2021 — lawphil …/juri2021/jan2021/gr_243470_2021.html | An alleged SPA from co-owner registered owners, used by an attorney-in-fact to mortgage. | "Despite the presence of an alleged SPA from the registered owners, respondent should have inquired with the registered owners as to the extent of the authority of the attorney-in-fact, especially when the property is co-owned by two persons." | The mortgagee was **not** in good faith. | Test (d): the land is co-owned by three sisters abroad. Nobody asked them, which is the consultation gap. | **Helps** |
| 6 | **MCIAA v. Unchuan**, G.R. No. 182537, 1 Jun 2016 — lawphil …/juri2016/jun2016/gr_182537_2016.html | One co-owner acted as agent for co-owned lots without an SPA from the others. | "The rule is that a void contract produces no effect either against or in favor of anyone and cannot be ratified. Similarly, laches will not set in against a void transaction, as in this case, where the agent did not have a special power of attorney to dispose of the lots co-owned by the other registered owners." The Court then applied Art. 493: the sale was "not entirely void because the lack of consent by the other co-owners in the sale was with respect to their shares only." | Void as to the non-consenting co-owners' shares. | Helps on "no ratification, no laches." **Caution:** the Art. 493 split saved the agent-co-owner's *own* share. Cesar owned no share, so no part of our sale is saved that way. | **Helps** |

**Section:** Tier 2: helpful on a single point

---

#### Tier 2: helpful on a single point

| # | Case | Clause examined | Holding (exact) | Outcome | Comparison | Rating |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 7 | **Litonjua v. Eternit Corp.**, G.R. No. 144805, 8 Jun 2006 — lawphil …/juri2006/jun2006/gr_144805_2006.html | A broker "authorized to offer for sale the property for P27,000,000.00 and the other terms of the sale subject to negotiations" (no board resolution). | "A real estate broker is one who negotiates the sale of real properties. His business, generally speaking, is only to find a purchaser who is willing to buy the land upon terms fixed by the owner. He has no authority to bind the principal by signing a contract of sale. Indeed, an authority to find a purchaser of real property does not include an authority to sell." | No perfected sale. | **The only holding on test (c).** It concerns a broker without a written SPA. Our ¶7 adds "sign and execute," so this is weak against ¶7, but usable against the registry annotation's "negotiate" wording. | **Helps (weak)** |
| 8 | **AF Realty & Dev. v. Dieselman Freight**, G.R. No. 111448, 16 Jan 2002 — lawphil …/juri2002/jan2002/gr_111448_2002.html | A letter titled "Authority To Sell Real Estate" authorizing a broker "to look for a buyer/buyers and negotiate the sale" of the lot at P3,000/sq m, issued by a director without board authority. | "Considering that respondent Cruz, Jr., Cristeta Polintan and Felicisima Ranullo were not authorized by respondent Dieselman to sell its lot, the supposed contract is void. Being a void contract, it is not susceptible of ratification by clear mandate of Article 1409." | **Void.** Accepting a P300,000 partial payment was not ratification. | The holding turns on the absence of authority from the owner, not on the word "negotiate." It is useful against arguments that the owners ratified by later conduct. | **Helps** |
| 9 | **Bautista-Spille v. NICORP**, G.R. No. 214057, 19 Oct 2015 — lawphil …/juri2015/oct2015/gr_214057_2015.html | A general power of attorney: "...to act as my/our general representative(s) and agent(s) with full authority to buy, sell, negotiate and contract for me/us..." | "It is a general rule that a power of attorney must be strictly construed, and courts will not infer or presume broad powers from deeds which do not sufficiently include property or subject under which the agent is to deal. Thus, when the authority is couched in general terms, without mentioning any specific power to sell or mortgage or to do other specific acts of strict dominion, then only acts of administration are deemed conferred." | The contract to sell was not enforceable. | Applies against the 1991 Agcaoili SPA if it only says "sign for and in behalf of" [VERIFY its text]. It also applies against SPA ¶8's general clause being read as a power to partition. | **Helps** |
| 10 | **Aggabao v. Parulan**, G.R. No. 165803, 1 Sep 2010 — lawphil …/juri2010/sep2010/gr_165803_2010.html | An SPA "authorizing Ma. Elena to sell the property," later shown not to be the husband's act. | Buyers must show "(b) the diligence in inquiring into the authority of the transacting spouse to sell"; the sale was "not merely voidable but void; hence, it could not be ratified." | **Void.** The buyers were not in good faith. | Use only for the buyer's duty of inquiry beyond the title. The context is conjugal property. | **Helps (analogy)** |

#### Tier 3: adverse or mixed (prepare answers)

| # | Case | Clause examined | Holding (exact) | Outcome | Comparison | Rating |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 11 | **Heirs of Victorino Sarili v. Lagrosa**, G.R. No. 193517, 15 Jan 2014 — lawphil …/juri2014/jan2014/gr_193517_2014.html | An SPA presented by a seller-agent. | "If the proof of capacity consists of a special power of attorney duly notarized, mere inspection of the face of such public document already constitutes sufficient inquiry." | (The buyer there failed because of flaws in the SPA.) | **Ramos's line:** the face of the SPA, and the annotation "to sign, negotiate for the sale of the parcel of land in this title," was enough. **Answer:** the face includes ¶7's class and "after prior consultation with us," so inspecting it reveals the limits (Art. 1900 "as written"). | **Hurts / mixed** |
| 12 | **Pahud v. CA**, G.R. No. 160346, 25 Aug 2009 — lawphil …/juri2009/aug2009/gr_160346_2009.html | A co-heir sold 7/8 of an undivided lot; three co-heirs had no written authority at the time and later executed SPAs. | "While the sale with respect to the 3/8 portion is void by express provision of law and not susceptible to ratification, we nevertheless uphold its validity on the basis of the common law principle of estoppel." | The sale was **upheld by estoppel.** | **Risk:** Ramos will cite the owners' conduct (the 2005 Era sale, silence between 2016 and 2020, the 1996 annotation). **Distinguish:** in *Pahud* the co-heirs admitted the sale in their pleadings; our owners revoked in 2005, published in 2020 and sued. | **Hurts (distinguishable)** |
| 13 | **De Joya v. Madlangbayan**, G.R. No. 228999, 28 Apr 2021 — lawphil …/juri2021/apr2021/gr_228999_2021.html | A GPA and an SPA; the Court described the agent as "vested with the power to negotiate the sale of the subject properties." The powers were revoked 3 May 1996. | No perfected sale: "it was clear that no contract was perfected as the parties failed to agree on the purchase price." The later deed was void as simulated. | Deed **void.** | Revocation followed by a notice and a demand to surrender the owner's duplicate is comparable to our 2005 revocation and the duplicate reissued on Cesar's 2016 affidavit of loss. It does **not** decide "negotiate vs. sell." | **Neutral / analogy** |
| 14 | **Alcantara v. Nido**, G.R. No. 165133, 19 Apr 2010 — lawphil …/juri2010/apr2010/gr_165133_2010.html | Verbal authority to sell 200 sq m (no writing). | The trial court's ruling, discussed by the Court: "since respondent's authority to sell the land was not in writing, the sale was void under Article 1874." | Void. | Art. 1874 only. We have a writing that was exceeded or revoked, which is Art. 1898/1919 territory. | **Distinguishable** |


**Document:** [Other desk] SPA language jurisprudence  
**Source:** `CASE_FILE/08_GAPS_TO_OBTAIN/SPA_LANGUAGE_JURISPRUDENCE.md`  
**Section:** Mapping to Jonathan's six questions

---

#### Mapping to Jonathan's six questions

| Question | Best authority | Bottom line |
|---|---|---|
| (a) Which property / derivative lot / portion | *Woodchild*; *Bautista-Spille*; *Cosmic* | Strict construction is supported. No case found voids a sale because the SPA named the mother title rather than the derivative. **Do not plead that as a stand-alone ground.** |
| (b) Limits on whom, price, consent | *Woodchild* ("legally identical"); *San Pedro*; *Yabut* | Strong for the ¶7 class and the consultation requirement. |
| (c) "Negotiate" vs. "sell/sign" | *Litonjua* (broker); *AF Realty* (facts) | **Not established for an SPA that also says "sign and execute."** Weak. |
| (d) Co-owners' SPA over undivided land | *Yabut*; *MCIAA*; *Rallos* | Inquiry is "especially" required. Cesar had no share of his own, so Art. 493 saves nothing for the buyer. |
| (e) After revocation or death | *Rallos* (death: void ab initio); *De Joya* (revocation context) | Strong as to death on 21 Jun 2017. Revocation is governed by Arts. 1919–1922. |
| (f) Agent's self-confirmation / ratification | *AF Realty*; *MCIAA*; *Woodchild* ("absent estoppel or ratification") | Only the principal ratifies (Arts. 1317, 1910). Cesar's 28 Mar 2017 Deed of Confirmation (Annex K p. 7) is the agent confirming his own act. **No SC case found on an agent's self-confirmation specifically**; that point rests on statute [INFERRED]. |

**UNVERIFIED, not cited:** any case squarely holding that (i) "negotiate for the sale" in an SPA excludes the power to sell, (ii) an SPA over a mother title does not reach a derivative title, or (iii) an agent's self-confirmation is ineffective. None was found and read in full.

**Document:** [Other desk] T-52540 against the SPA's property description  
**Source:** `CASE_FILE/08_GAPS_TO_OBTAIN/T52540_SPA_SCOPE.md`  
**Section:** Verdict

---

#### Verdict

**The argument is real, but as Jonathan framed it, it is mostly a premise problem.** The SPA (Exh. E) describes one property: the undivided 139,132 sq m parcel under T-4497. It names no other title and no lot. On its face it does not name T-52540 or Lot 2-X-6-I-4-C. Under Arts. 1874, 1878(5) and 1881, read strictly, that is a legitimate ground. **Three record facts blunt it as a stand-alone nullity ground:**

1. **T-52540 descends from T-4497.** Its p. 1 cites the same LRC Record No. 10784 the SPA cites (Exh. B p. 1; Exh. E CTC p. 2).
2. **The Registry carried the SPA onto T-52540.**
   - First annotation: 21 Jun 1996, "to sign, negotiate for the sale of the parcel of land in this title" (Exh. B p. 3).
   - Second annotation: Entry 2021003235, "with power to sign and negotiate for the sale of the land in this title" (Exh. B p. 5).
   - PD 1529 §59 *requires* carry-over of subsisting annotations to derivative titles.
3. **The owners' side has never attacked other sales of portions of the same lot made under the same SPA.** The 2005 Era sale of Lot 2-X-6-I-4-C-2 was registered under it (Exh. B p. 3).

**What survives, and is strong:**

- **The 2016 deed names no title at all.** It describes "Lot 2-X-6-I-4-C (portion), Psd-05-026197," 2,587 sq m. It invokes the SPA but does not state the ¶7 class or ¶7 consultation (Annex K p. 3).
- **The link to T-52540 was supplied only by Cesar's own *Deed of Confirmation* of 28 Mar 2017.** It was registered on 23 Nov 2021, after his death on 21 Jun 2017 (Annex K p. 7; Exh. B pp. 5–6).
- **The sale was of an unsegregated portion of co-owned land.** No approved plan existed until 6 Apr 2021. That brings in Art. 493 and PD 1529 §58.

**Recommendation:**

- Run the description point as a **strict-construction amplifier**. Read with the ¶7 class limit, the consultation condition and the 2005 revocation, it supports this line: the SPA authorized sale of portions of the T-4497 estate only to the ¶7 class after consultation.
- Do **not** plead "the SPA never covered T-52540" as a separate nullity ground. Ramos defeats it in one sentence with §59 and the 1996 annotation.
- "A new SPA was needed" is not a rule found in the Code text. It is our inference from Arts. 1878 and 1881 [INFERRED].
