#### 1. Exact text

##### 1.1 The SPA's property description

Source: Exh. E, CTC p. 2, ¶3. The scan is faint. Bracketed readings are [VERIFY against the original].

> "... covered by Transfer Certificate of Title No. T-4497 and more particularly described as follows:
> 'A parcel of land (Lot 2 of the subdivision plan (LRC) Psd-12802, being a portion of a parcel of land (Parcel 2), LRC (GLRO) Record No. 10784), situated in the [Poblacion] of Mercedes, [Municipality of Daet], Province of Camarines Norte, Island of Luzon. Bounded on the NE., points 5 to 9, by Pacific Ocean; on the SE., points 9 to 10 and 10 to 1, by property of [Rafael Lacson(?)]; on the SW., points 1 to 4, by Provincial Road (Mercedes-Daet Provincial Road); and on the NW., points 4 to 5, by Lot 1 of the Subdivision plan. ... containing an area of ONE HUNDRED THIRTY NINE THOUSAND ONE HUNDRED AND THIRTY TWO SQUARE METERS (139,132), more or less.'"

- ¶¶1–3 state that the sisters hold it as "undivided interests" after the 7 Dec 1988 extrajudicial settlement (Exh. E CTC p. 2; dossier F3).
- No other title, lot or plan appears anywhere in the SPA.

##### 1.2 The power-to-sell clause and the class (¶¶6–7)

Source: Exh. E, CTC p. 3. This quote was previously VERIFIED in PHASE1 and the playbook.

> **¶6:** "To enable us to settle the aforementioned unpaid real estate taxes, it will be necessary to negotiate for the sale of the above-described real estate property to interested third parties and apply part of the proceeds of said sale in full payment of said unpaid real estate taxes."
>
> **¶7:** "Accordingly, we hereby name, constitute and appoint CESAR M. DE LA FUENTE ... to be our true and lawful attorney-in-fact, for us and in our name, place and stead, to negotiate for the sale of the above-described real estate property in favor of third persons in possession of CONTRACTS TO SELL executed by the previous administrator, MR. BEN LLAMANZARES and/or to those who have paid their down-payments or have made partial payments therefor to the latter. Said sale shall be upon such terms and conditions ... after prior consultation with us, and to sign and execute the necessary documents ... as well as to receive the proceeds ..."

- ¶6 is a faint scan; the reading is [VERIFY].
- **"Above-described real estate property"** ties the power to the ¶3 description, which is T-4497 as a whole.
- No clause grants a power to partition, segregate or subdivide.

**Section:** 4. Statute table (core)

---

#### 4. Statute table (core)

Statutory text was quoted from the lawphil copies of R.A. 386 and P.D. 1529 held at `grok/law/cc.html` and `pd1529.html`, which T1 verified against lawphil.

| Provision (exact text) | Application to our facts | Disposition / strength | Ramos's counter |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Art. 1874**: "When a sale of a piece of land or any interest therein is through an agent, the authority of the latter shall be in writing; otherwise, the sale shall be void." | There *is* a writing (Exh. E). The question is whether the writing covers *this* sale. | **Weak alone.** It only voids sales with no written authority. | "There's a written SPA, annotated twice." |
| **Art. 1878(5)**: special power needed "To enter into any contract by which the ownership of an immovable is transmitted or acquired ..."; **(12)** "To create or convey real rights over immovable property"; **(15)** "Any other act of strict dominion." | The power must be special as to the act. ¶7 grants it only for the class, after consultation. **Partition and subdivision**, which are acts of strict dominion over co-owned land, are not granted anywhere (15). | **Strong** against the 2017 partition. **Medium** on the description point. | "¶7 is an express special power to sell 'the above-described property', which includes every part of it." |
| **Art. 1879**: "A special power to sell excludes the power to mortgage; and a special power to mortgage does not include the power to sell." | This shows the Code reads special powers narrowly, act by act. By analogy, a power to sell does not include a power to partition. | Supporting (analogy) [INFERRED] | "Off point." |
| **Art. 1881**: "The agent must act within the scope of his authority. He may do such acts as may be conducive to the accomplishment of the purpose of the agency." | The purpose (¶6) is to pay taxes by sale to ¶7 buyers. A 2016 sale outside the class, of an unsegregated portion, eleven years after revocation, is outside scope. | **Strong** | Second sentence: carving a portion was "conducive" to selling. |
| **Art. 1898**: "...exceeding the scope of his authority, and the principal does not ratify the contract, it shall be void if the party with whom the agent contracted is aware of the limits of the powers granted..." | The deed recites the SPA by Doc/Page/Book (Annex K p. 3), so Gloria was charged with its terms. | **Strong (void)** if knowledge is proved; otherwise Art. 1403(1). | "She didn't know the limits." |
| **Art. 1900**: "So far as third persons are concerned, an act is deemed to have been performed within the scope ... if such act is within the terms of the power of attorney, as written..." | "As written" includes ¶7's class and consultation, and the ¶3 description (T-4497 whole). | **Double-edged.** Helps on class; hurts on description, because the T-52540 land *is* part of the "above-described" parcel as written. | "As written, Lot 2-X-6-I-4-C is part of T-4497 Lot 2." |
| **Art. 1317 ¶2**: "...acted beyond his powers, shall be unenforceable, unless it is ratified, expressly or impliedly, by the person on whose behalf it has been executed..." | Only the principals (the sisters) ratify. Cesar's 2017 Deed of Confirmation is the agent confirming himself. | **Strong** against the Confirmation | "Owners' later conduct ratified" (Era sale, silence). |
| **Art. 1403(1)**: unenforceable: "Those entered into in the name of another person by one who has been given no authority or legal representation, or who has acted beyond his powers" | This is the fallback if Art. 1898 knowledge fails. | Fallback | Ratification: receipts and Patricia's 2023 affidavit of loss (Doc 331). |
| **Art. 1409(1),(7)**: void: "cause, object or purpose is contrary to law..."; "expressly prohibited or declared void by law" | Ties to Art. 1874/1898 "void." | Supporting | — |
| **Art. 1910**: "As for any obligation wherein the agent has exceeded his power, the principal is not bound except when he ratifies it expressly or tacitly." | The sisters never ratified the 2016 sale. | **Strong** | Tacit ratification (silence 2016–2020). |
| **Art. 1318**: requisites: consent, "Object certain," cause | The deed's object is "Lot 2-X-6-I-4-C (portion)," described by adjoining lots, with no title. Certainty of object is arguable but probably satisfied by the metes. | **Weak** | "Metes and bounds make it certain." |
| **Art. 1458 / 1475**: sale = obligation to transfer "a determinate thing"; perfected on meeting of minds "upon the thing ... and upon the price" | The sellers (sisters) never consented. The agent's consent binds only within authority. | Supporting | — |
| **Art. 484**: "There is co-ownership whenever the ownership of an undivided thing or right belongs to different persons." | The sisters co-own T-52540 undivided (Exh. B p. 1). | Foundation | — |
| **Art. 493**: "...the effect of the alienation ... with respect to the co-owners, shall be limited to the portion which may be allotted to him in the division upon the termination of the co-ownership." | Here no co-owner sold her own share. An agent sold a specific, metes-and-bounds portion for all three before partition. Art. 493 does not authorize that. At most a co-owner's ideal share passes. | **Medium–strong** | "The agent acted for *all* co-owners, so Art. 493 doesn't apply; all shares were sold." |
| **Arts. 494/495**: partition on demand; no physical division where unserviceable | Partition is the co-owners' act. The 2017 "Partition-Subdivision Agreement" was not signed by any co-owner [per playbook; instrument not in hand]. | Strong against Entry 2021003236 | "Cesar signed for them." |
| **Art. 1620**: co-owner's right of redemption | The deed recites Art. **1623** compliance (notice to co-owners). That presupposes a sale of a *co-owner's share*, not a sale by all. The recital is inconsistent with the deed's own structure [INFERRED]. | Cross-exam point | — |
| **Arts. 1919(1), 1920**: agency extinguished "By its revocation"; principal "may revoke the agency at will" | Revoked 15 Aug 2005, eleven years before the deed. | **Strongest** | Arts. 1921/1922: no notice to Gloria. |
| **Art. 1921**: specified persons not prejudiced "if they were not given notice" / **Art. 1922**: general powers, good-faith third persons; newspaper notice "sufficient warning" | ¶7 names a class (specified persons). Gloria is not in it, so Art. 1921 does not protect her. Art. 1922 is for general powers; this power is special. | Medium | Publication was only in 2020, after the deed. |
| **PD 1529 §51**: deed "shall operate only as a contract between the parties ... registration shall be the operative act" | The 2016 deed sat unregistered until 23 Nov 2021, after Cesar's death and after the 2020 publication. | Supporting | — |
| **§52**: registration is "constructive notice to all persons" | The 1996 SPA annotation charged Gloria with ¶7's terms. *Cuts both ways*: no revocation annotation. | Double-edged | "No revocation was annotated." |
| **§53**: owner's duplicate must be presented; registration "procured by fraud" proviso | The duplicate was reissued on Cesar's Affidavit of Loss (27 Sep 2016) by RTC order (Entry 2017000693). Strait Times line. | Medium [verify the whereabouts of the original duplicate] | Court order is regular. |
| **§57**: conveyance of registered land; "a reference by number to the last preceding certificate" | The 2016 deed contains no title reference. | Supporting | Formal defect cured by the Confirmation. |
| **§58**: for "a part only of the land," no TCT until "a plan ... shall have been verified and approved"; meanwhile, "may only be annotated by way of memorandum" | No approved plan existed in 2016. PSD-E2020005406 was surveyed 8 Sep 2020 and approved 6 Apr 2021 (Def. Exh. 1 p. 2). The deed was not annotated in 2016; it was registered only in 2021. | **Strong** on the 2021 registration chain | "§58 was complied with in 2021." |
| **§59**: subsisting annotations "shall be carried over and stated in the new certificate" | **Adverse.** This is why the 1992 SPA appears on T-52540. The Registry treated it as reaching the derivative. | **Ramos's best** | — |
| **§47**: "No title to registered land in derogation of the title of the registered owner shall be acquired by prescription or adverse possession." | Defeats any possession-based claim by the Balanes. | Supporting | — |


**Document:** [Other desk] T-52540 against the SPA's property description  
**Source:** `CASE_FILE/08_GAPS_TO_OBTAIN/T52540_SPA_SCOPE.md`  
**Section:** 5. Jurisprudence (secondary)

---

#### 5. Jurisprudence (secondary)

All cases below are reused from T1_CITATION_AUDIT, which read them in full on lawphil.

| Case | Exact passage / holding | Helps or hurts | Distinguishing facts |
|---|---|---|---|
| *Bautista-Spille v. NICORP*, G.R. No. 214057, 19 Oct 2015 (lawphil juri2015/oct2015/gr_214057_2015) | "must be strictly construed, and courts will not infer or presume broad powers from deeds which do not sufficiently include property or subject under which the agent is to deal" | **Helps**, and is the best fit for the description point | There, the property was not in the SPA at all. Ours is part of the described parcel. |
| *Cosmic Lumber v. CA*, G.R. No. 114311, 29 Nov 1996 (juri1996/nov1996/gr_114311_1996) | "the sale ipso jure is consequently void"; "...so express the powers of the agent in clear and unmistakable language" | Helps | There, the SPA gave a power to sue or settle, not to sell. Ours gives a limited power to sell. |
| *MCIAA v. Unchuan*, G.R. No. 182537, 1 Jun 2016 | void sale "cannot be ratified"; context: "where the agent did not have a special power of attorney to dispose of the lots co-owned by the other registered owners" | Helps (co-owned lots) | — |
| *Spouses Yabut v. Nachbaur*, G.R. No. 243470, 12 Jan 2021 | "Despite the presence of an alleged SPA ... especially when the property is co-owned" | Helps (buyer's diligence) | — |
| *San Pedro v. Ong*, G.R. No. 177598, 17 Oct 2008 | "Every person dealing with an agent is put upon inquiry, and must discover upon his peril the authority of the agent" | Helps (Art. 1898 knowledge) | — |
| *Heirs of Sarili v. Lagrosa*, G.R. No. 193517, 15 Jan 2014 | "mere inspection of the face of such public document already constitutes sufficient inquiry" | **Hurts / mixed**: the face shows ¶7 *and* "T-4497", and T-52540 is within it | — |
| *Republic v. Ciruelas*, G.R. No. 239505, 17 Feb 2021 | §64 non-registration does not make authority ineffective | Double edge: an unannotated revocation is still valid, and the SPA's force never depended on the annotation | — |
| *Central Surety v. Hodges*, G.R. No. L-28633, 30 Mar 1971 | Art. 1922 governs agents dealing "with the public in general" | Use only for special vs. general | Not for 2020 notice. |
| *Alcantara v. Nido*, G.R. No. 165133, 19 Apr 2010 | Unauthorized agent's conveyance void | Art. 1874 only | No writing there; we have one. |
| (a) A sale "of a specific portion" of undivided land by a co-owner before partition; (b) a "new SPA" requirement for a derivative title | — | **UNVERIFIED. Not cited.** No decision was read in full this session. | Research item. |


**Document:** [Other desk] T-52540 against the SPA's property description  
**Source:** `CASE_FILE/08_GAPS_TO_OBTAIN/T52540_SPA_SCOPE.md`  
**Section:** 6. Bottom line for counsel

---

#### 6. Bottom line for counsel

1. Lead with **revocation (Art. 1919–1920)** and the **¶7 class (Arts. 1881, 1898, 1900 "as written")**.
2. Use the **T-4497 description** under *Bautista-Spille* to show the SPA is construed strictly. Do not use it to argue that T-52540 is outside it.
3. Add the new, record-based points:
   - The deed names no title (Annex K p. 3).
   - The T-52540 link was supplied by the agent alone in 2017 and registered after his death (Annex K p. 7; Exh. B pp. 5–6). Arts. 1317 and 1910 let only the principal ratify.
   - §58: no approved plan existed until 2021.
   - Art. 493: an agent's specific-portion sale for all co-owners before partition.
4. Obtain the **1991 Agcaoili SPA** (Entry 2016002312) before trial. Ramos may use it as an alternate source of authority.

#### 3. Spouses Efren and Gloria Balane

##### 3.1 Identity and role

- **Gloria H. Balane.**
  - Registered owner of TCT 079-2021002126 (2,587 sqm), shown as "GLORIA H. BALANE … MARRIED TO EFREN M. BALANE". The title was entered 23 Nov 2021 and transferred from T-52540 (PEC p.1; DX1).
  - Vendee in the 29 Sep 2016 deed, where she is described as "Filipino and a resident of Barangay San Roque" (DX6 p.1).
  - Age 68; residing at 49 Pidgeon St., Toronto, Ontario (JA-G p.2 S1).
  - Barangay Kagawad 1997–2004 and Konsehal 2004–2017 (JA-G p.11 S59).
- **Efren M. Balane.** Gloria's husband (JA-G p.3 S3), sued "as spouse" (ANS p.5 ¶37; CMP p.4 ¶11).
- **Residence of both.** Both are "presently in Canada" (ANS p.1 ¶4).
- **Family.** Princess and Erwin are their children, and Jomil is Princess's husband (ANS p.1 ¶4; JA-G p.3 S3).
- **Gloria's citizenship documents in her own JA annexes:**
  - a Canadian passport page, No. P584814NW (JA-G p.15);
  - a Philippine Consulate Toronto Identification Certificate No. TO-533-2025, dated 12 Mar 2025. It recognizes "GLORIA MORALES HANSOL aka GLORIA HANSOL BALANE" as a Philippine citizen under RA 9225 (Order TO-RA-9225-533-2025) (JA-G p.16).

##### 3.2 Filings and positions (through counsel)

- **¶7:** the property is the 2,587 sqm under TCT 079-2021002126 in Gloria's name (ANS p.1).
- **¶8:** they admit Cesar executed the deed to Gloria in Sept 2016.
- **¶9 (SPA):** the SPA "read as a whole" authorized sales to "qualified buyers". Gloria qualified through possession and prior "down or partial payments through the previous administrator and his representative" (ANS p.2).
- **¶31 (payment):** completed payment to Cesar under "the parties' installment arrangement" (ANS p.4).
- **¶32 (diligence):** they examined the SPA and the mother title and "found no annotation of revocation, lis pendens, or adverse claim" (ANS p.4).
- **¶40 (class membership):** ANS p.5.
  - They have occupied the land "since 1975" and "paid monthly rent to the previous administrator Ben Llamanzares through Manuel Violeta".
  - "In the 1980s they were told they could buy".
  - They made a "downpayment of Php3,000.00 in installments", which was "recognized and later credited in the transaction with Cesar".
- **¶44(a)–(j), good-faith circumstances** (ANS pp.6–7):
  - (a) possession since 1975 "after acquiring the rights and possession from Kenneth Asistin Jr.";
  - (f) ₱250,000 paid in installments through Salvador as collector;
  - (g) the Rosalina M. Hansol sale, TCT T-50192.
- **¶27 (rentals):** "certain rooms on the property were occasionally rented out after 2016 … minimal, intermittent" (ANS p.4).
- **¶24 and ¶33:** they deny holding the owner's duplicate of TCT 079-2021002127 (ANS pp.3–4).
- **¶49 (builders in good faith):** reimbursement and retention for "Gloria and Efren, together with the family members occupying under them" (ANS p.7).
- **Counterclaim (¶¶51–57; prayer p.11):** ₱200k moral, ₱100k exemplary, ₱100k attorney's fees and ₱50k litigation expenses. The prayer also asks to cancel the adverse claim on 079-2021002126.

##### 3.3 Documents signed or held

- **29 Sep 2016 deed** (DX6 pp.1–2):
  - Signed by Cesar M. dela Fuente as "Attorney-In-Fact of the Heirs of Mary W. Keesey".
  - ₱250,000 "to me in hand paid by and receipt whereof is hereby acknowledged from GLORIA H. BALANE".
  - The vendee does not sign (PB §2).
  - Recites compliance with "Article 1623".

  - Attached eCAR 201900675224, dated 11/18/2021, for "T52540", location "Brgy. 1, Mercedes". It shows a selling price of ₱250,000.00 and a fair market value of ₱2,509,390.00. Tax receipts are dated 6/15/2020 (DX6 p.2).

##### 3.5 Admissions and contradictions usable on cross

- **C1. Lessee status admitted.**
  - "Nagbibigay po kami ng renta… para sa lupa" (JA-G p.5 S18).
  - The Answer says they "paid monthly rent" from 1975 (ANS p.5 ¶40) and were "renting the property" (ANS p.6 ¶43).
  - [INFERRED] This bears on possession in the concept of owner and on Art. 1678 rather than Art. 448 for improvements.
- **C2. Two origins for the 1975 possession.**
  - "Rights and possession" bought from Kenneth Asistin Jr. for ₱16,500 (JA-G p.4 S12; ANS p.6 ¶44(a)).
  - Versus rent to the owners' administrator over the same period (JA-G p.5 S18).
- **C3. "In hand paid" versus installments.**
  - The deed recites ₱250,000 "in hand paid" on the date of execution (DX6 p.1).
  - The Answer pleads an "installment arrangement" (ANS p.4 ¶31).
  - Gloria says the deed was made only after full payment ("Matapos po naming mabayaran ang presyo, ginawa ang Deed", JA-G p.10 S50).
  - Yet the receipts run to Feb 2017, with No. 0228 "Full payment" (PB §2) [VERIFY DX5].
- **C4. The amounts do not reconcile.**
  - ₱3,000 in the 1980s (JA-G p.5 S21; ANS p.5 ¶40).
  - ₱20,000 "Down payment" in Efren's name in 2005 (PB §2).
  - ₱250,000 total price (JA-G p.7 S33).
  - TRJ flags the amounts conflict [VERIFY DX5].
- **C5. No direct authority from the owners.**
  - "Wala po akong naging tuwirang komunikasyon kina Patricia, Geraldine, o Marcia… hindi ko po sila nakita nang personal" (JA-G p.8 S43).
  - Her belief in Cesar's authority rested on what Cesar said and showed (JA-G p.6 S27–S31).
- **C6. Transfer handler.**
  - "Si Atty. Elaine Belen po ang tumulong at nag-follow up sa mga dokumento para sa transfer" (JA-G p.10 S53).
  - [VERIFY] whether Atty. Belen appears in the 2019 Agcaoili correspondence or the 27 Sep 2016 loss affidavit (PB §2 lists "Belen Doc. 560"). If so, notice imputation is an open legal point (T3).
- **C7. Survey under Cesar.**
  - "Sa pamamahala po ni Cesar M. de la Fuente ay nagkaroon ng survey"; Princess paid (JA-G p.7 S36–S37).
  - The 29 Dec 2006 survey receipt reads "owned by the H[ei]rs of Worrick Keesey" (PB §2) [VERIFY DX5].
- **C8. Columnar book.** Gloria says Violeta's book went to Cesar and then to the Agcaoili Law Office (JA-G p.5 S24). There is no writing from Llamanzares or Violeta in the defense exhibits (ANS p.10 ¶61).
- **C9. Exhibit numbering in JA-G does not match the Answer.**

  | Document | JA-G | Answer ¶61 |
  |---|---|---|
  | SPA | "6" | 7 |
  | T-50192 | "2" | 3 |
  | Hansol deed | "3" | 4 |
  | Receipts | "4" | 5 |
  | 2016 deed | "5" | 6 |
  | ARP | "1-A" | 2 |

  (JA-G pp.4–10; ANS p.10 ¶61.)
- **C10. Improvements.** Gloria says Efren "personally" did or managed carpentry and repairs, costing at least ₱100,000 (JA-G p.12 S69–S70). Both are in Canada (ANS p.1 ¶4); the dates of the works are not stated [VERIFY].
- **C11. Rentals.** "Kung mayroon mang paminsan-minsang renta, maliit po iyon" (JA-G p.13 S73). The Answer admits room rentals after 2016 (ANS p.4 ¶27). No figures are given [gap].
- **C12. Notice.**
  - Gloria says she first learned of the revocation in Aug 2025 (JA-G p.12 S63).
  - Versus her own 28 May 2020 SPA to litigate or manage (PB §2).
  - Versus Jomil's 2021 civil case (see 4.5) [VERIFY].
- **C14. Who received the money [VERIFY: Jonathan statement 18 Oct].** Gloria says the installments went to Salvador as "collector at assistant ni Cesar" (JA-G p.9 S45; ANS p.6 ¶44(f)). The SPA names Cesar only (DX6 p.1). Ask: who appointed the "Authorized Collector", when, by what document, and did the money reach the sisters (see 7.28). Do not argue "no power to delegate".
- **C13. Citizenship.** Rated LOW, credibility only, per Jonathan (see 7.27). The record points follow.

#### 6. The defendants as a whole: leverage

##### 6.1 How the roles interlock

| Role | Who | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Registered owner | Gloria alone ("married to Efren M. Balane") | PEC p.1; DX1 |
| Vendee in the 2016 deed | Gloria | DX6 p.1 |
| Spouse; payor on the 2003/2005 receipts; improvements | Efren | ANS p.5 ¶37; PB §2; JA-G p.12 S69 |
| Occupants on the lot | Princess and Jomil | ANS p.3 ¶25; JA-P p.2 S5 |
| Survey payor; Occupant receipts | Princess | JA-P p.3 S8–S10; PB §2 |
| Custodian of documents; CTC requester; Municipal Engineer | Erwin | JA-E p.2 S3, p.4 S16; DX1; DX6 |
| Attorney-in-fact in PH (2020) | Nelly Juntilla (Gloria's sister, not a defendant) | PB §2; Answer Annex "1" |
| Residence | Gloria and Efren: Canada. Princess and Jomil: Purok 4 (the lot). Erwin: Purok 1A | ANS p.1 ¶4; JA-P p.2; JA-E p.2 |

**Chain timeline (defendants' acts only):**

- 1975: entry; rights bought from Asistin and rent paid to Llamanzares (JA-G p.4 S12, p.5 S18).
- 1980s: ₱3,000 (JA-G p.5 S21).
- 2003–2005: receipts in Efren's name (PB §2).
- 2006: survey paid by Princess (PB §2).
- 2007–2014: Occupant receipts to Princess and "Golly" (PB §2).
- 29 Sep 2016: deed to Gloria (DX6).
- To Feb 2017: post-deed receipts (PB §2).
- 28 May 2020: Gloria's SPA to Juntilla (PB §2).
- 15 Jun 2020: taxes (DX6 p.2).
- 8 Sep 2020: survey (DX1 p.2).
- 2021: CC 8563 (Juntilla and Torralba) (PB §2).
- 23 Nov 2021: TCT (PEC).
- Mar 2026: Erwin's CTCs (DX1; DX6).
- Apr–May 2026: JAs (JA-P; JA-E; JA-G).

##### 6.2 Contradictions and divergent interests between defendants

- **D1. Who paid.** Payments are attributed to:
  - Efren (2003/2005);
  - Princess (survey, and receipts from 2007);
  - "Golly" (2013–14);
  - Gloria (2016) (PB §2).

  Gloria says "Kami po ng aking asawang si Efren ang gumastos" for the improvements (JA-G p.12 S69). [INFERRED] If the deed is void, any claim for return of the price runs against Cesar's estate, as the Answer itself pleads (ANS p.7 ¶45). Princess's contributions are then a claim separate from Gloria's.
- **D2. Who holds title.** Gloria alone (PEC p.1). Princess paid for the survey that segregated the lot titled to her mother (JA-P p.3 S8–S9).
- **D3. Who occupies.** Princess and Jomil occupy "by authority of Gloria and Efren" (ANS p.2 ¶10). Yet ¶49 claims builder-in-good-faith rights for Gloria and Efren "together with the family members occupying under them" (ANS p.7). The occupants' own claim to improvements is pleaded only through the owners.
- **D4. Who collects rents.** The Answer admits room rentals (ANS p.4 ¶27), and Gloria calls them occasional and small (JA-G p.13 S73). No defendant is named as the receiver. The Complaint asks for an accounting (CMP p.18 prayer). [INFERRED] The occupants, Princess and Jomil, are the likely witnesses on this, and their account may differ from Gloria's.
- **D5. Who holds the documents.** Princess (ANS p.9 ¶59(c)) or Erwin (JA-E p.4 S16, p.5 S20–S21).
- **D6. Who knew what, and when.**
  - Gloria: Aug 2025 (JA-G p.12 S63).
  - Princess: "no objection" (JA-P p.3 S11).
  - Jomil: CC 8563 in 2021 (PB §2) [VERIFY].
  - Gloria's own 2020 SPA (PB §2).
- **D7. Exposure if the deed is void.**
  - The Complaint prays, solidarily against all defendants: ₱200,000 moral, ₱100,000 exemplary, ₱50,000 litigation expenses, and ₱100,000 plus ₱6,000 per hearing in attorney's fees. It also asks that defendants vacate, "losing everything they may have built", and render an accounting (CMP p.18 prayer; pp.13 ¶¶61–64).
  - The First Affirmative Defense asks that four of the five defendants (all except Gloria) be dropped for lack of a cause of action (ANS p.5 ¶37).
  - [INFERRED] Erwin's and Efren's exposure rests on different allegations than Gloria's. Erwin's rests on the conspiracy-in-office allegation (CMP p.4 ¶11, p.8 ¶35). Efren's rests on spouse status and the conjugal character of the property [VERIFY the property regime].
  - [VERIFY] PB's draft judgment figures against the Complaint prayer (exemplary ₱100,000 in CMP).
- **D8. Exhibit sets diverge** between Gloria's JA and the Answer (C9). Erwin's JA follows his own numbering (JA-E pp.4–5) [VERIFY].

##### 6.3 Lawful leverage, ranked by impact versus risk

**Ground rules (apply to every item):**

- All steps are taken by Atty. Barandon, in court or through formal service on defense counsel.
- Jonathan has no contact with any defendant, any occupant, any defense witness, or Atty. Ramos.
- No criminal or administrative complaint, no ARTA or CSC referral, no publicity.
- No statement that could be read as a threat.

RPC Art. 286, as amended by RA 10951 §72: "The penalty of prisión correccional and a fine not exceeding One hundred thousand pesos (₱100,000) shall be imposed upon any person who, without any authority of law, shall, by means of violence, threats, or intimidation, prevent another from doing something not prohibited by law, or compel him to do something against his will, whether it be right or wrong." [VERIFY the current text.] Grave threats (RPC Art. 282) is a related risk [VERIFY the text; not quoted from LAW].

Settlement talk must never link any concession to testimony content. An exchange may cover only truthful stipulations of fact made through counsel.

| Rank | Item | Impact | Risk | Barandon decision |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Notice of lis pendens on TCT 079-2021002126 | High (protective) | Low | ☐ File ☐ Hold |
| 2 | Rule 26 request for admission (targeted, per defendant) | High | Low–Med (timing) | ☐ Serve ☐ Hold |
| 3 | Rule 21 subpoenas (records custodians; rebuttal) | High | Low | ☐ Issue ☐ Hold |
| 4 | Rule 27 production (receipts, columnar book, rentals, 002127 duplicate) | Med–High | Med (good cause; timing) | ☐ File ☐ Hold |
| 5 | Rule 25 written interrogatories to individual defendants | Medium | Med (timing) | ☐ Serve ☐ Hold |
| 6 | Art. 1410 imprescriptibility (answer to laches) | Medium (defensive) | Low | ☐ Brief |
| 7 | Damages and attorney's-fees exposure (as pleaded) | Medium | Low if kept to pleadings | ☐ Note at CAM/JDR |
| 8 | CAM/JDR (if available at this stage) | Medium | Low | ☐ Ask court ☐ Not now |
| 9 | Separating interests: compromise under Art. 2028 with a stipulation of facts | Med–High | Med | ☐ Explore via counsel ☐ No |
| 10 | Rule 23 depositions (incl. Gloria/Efren abroad) | Low–Med | Med–High (cost; timing) | ☐ Hold |

**Item 1. Notice of lis pendens.**

- *Record.* DX1 p.3 (the CTC requested by Erwin on 23 Mar 2026) shows only one entry: Entry 2023001324, 26 May 2023, Patricia's Notice of Adverse Claim. No lis pendens appears, two months after the 23 Jan 2026 filing. PEC (26 Feb 2025) shows the same single entry.
- *Rule.* PD 1529 §76: "No action to recover possession of real estate, or to quiet title thereto, or to remove clouds upon the title thereof, or for partition, or other proceedings of any kind in court directly affecting the title to land or the use or occupation thereof or the buildings thereon, and no judgment, and no proceeding to vacate or reverse any judgment, shall have any effect upon registered land as against persons other than the parties thereto, unless a memorandum or notice stating the institution of such action or proceeding and the court wherein the same is pending, as well as the date of the institution thereof, together with a reference to the number of the certificate of title, and an adequate description of the land affected and the registered owner thereof, shall have been filed and registered." (LAW pd1529.txt.)
- *Related.* PD 1529 §70 (adverse claim): "The adverse claim shall be effective for a period of thirty days from the date of registration. After the lapse of said period, the annotation of adverse claim may be canceled upon filing of a verified petition therefor by the party in interest…" The defense prays that the adverse claim be cancelled (ANS p.11 prayer).
- *Action.* Order a fresh CTC first [VERIFY no annotation since 23 Mar 2026].

**Item 2. Rule 26 request for admission.**

- *Rule.*
  - §1: "At any time after issues have been joined, a party may file and serve upon any other party a written request for the admission by the latter of the genuineness of any material and relevant document described in and exhibited with the request or of the truth of any material and relevant matter of fact set forth in the request."
  - §2: "Each of the matters of which an admission is requested shall be deemed admitted unless, within a period designated in the request, which shall not be less than fifteen (15) days after service thereof… the party to whom the request is directed files and serves upon the party requesting the admission a sworn statement either denying specifically the matters of which an admission is requested or setting forth in detail the reasons why he cannot truthfully either admit or deny those matters."
  - §3: an admission is "for the purpose of the pending action only".
  - §5: a party who fails to serve a request on facts within the other party's personal knowledge may not later present evidence on those facts, unless the court allows it for good cause. (LAW rc_1-71.) [VERIFY 2019 text.]
- *Use.* Address separate requests to individual defendants on facts each knows:
  - Erwin: custody of the papers and the 002127 duplicate.
  - Princess and Jomil: rents, the receipts in Princess's name, and CC 8563.
  - Gloria and Efren: lessee status, no Llamanzares writing, the price not paid on 29 Sep 2016.
  - Draft items are in MEMO 02_RFA (A1–A5; B-items).
- *Risk and conflict.*
  - MEMO 00_COVER_MEMO (15 Oct) says "No discovery motions" and holds the RFA in reserve.
  - DL (15–16 Oct updates) lists serving it by 21 Oct.
  - OVR notes that discovery "may be refused as late".
  - **Flag to Barandon: the two Claude documents conflict.**

**Item 3. Subpoenas (Rule 21).**

- *Rule.* Rule 21 §1: a subpoena "is a process directed to a person requiring him to attend and to testify at the hearing or the trial of an action, or at any investigation conducted by competent authority, or for the taking of his deposition. It may also require him to bring with him any books, documents, or other things under his control, in which case it is called a subpoena duces tecum." (LAW rc_1-71.) [VERIFY 2019 text.]
- *Targets* (PB §6.2):
  - the Registry of Deeds (the 2021 registration file);
  - the OCC notarial section (Asis Doc. 304; Docs. 115 and 402);
  - DENR (PSD-E2020005406);
  - the Building Official (permits in the defendants' names);
  - the Assessor (ARP 00256);
  - the BIR (eCAR 201900675224);
  - the Treasurer;
  - CC 8563 records.
- *Note.* Record custodians, not defendants, are the lowest-risk route.

**Item 4. Rule 27 production.**

- *Good cause from the defendants' own words:*
  - Erwin holds the papers (JA-E p.4 S16).
  - Princess holds "certain receipts" (ANS p.9 ¶59(c)).
  - Rentals are admitted (ANS p.4 ¶27).
  - The columnar book is referenced (JA-G p.5 S23–S24).
  - The 002127 duplicate is denied (ANS ¶¶24, 33).
- *Draft.* MEMO 03_PRODUCTION.

**Item 5. Written interrogatories to individual defendants (Rule 25).**

- *Rule.*
  - §1: "Under the same conditions specified in section 1 of Rule 23, any party desiring to elicit material and relevant facts from any adverse parties shall file and serve upon the latter written interrogatories to be answered by the party served…"
  - §2: answers within "fifteen (15) days after service".
  - §6: "Unless thereafter allowed by the court for good cause shown and to prevent a failure of justice, a party not served with written interrogatories may not be compelled by the adverse party to give testimony in open court, or to give a deposition pending appeal." (LAW rc_1-71.) [VERIFY 2019 text.]
- *Use.* §6 matters if Barandon may want to call any defendant (for example, Jomil on rents) as an adverse witness.

**Item 6. Art. 1410.**

- *Rule.* Civil Code Art. 1410: "The action or defense for the declaration of the inexistence of a contract does not prescribe." (LAW cc.txt.)
- *Use.* This answers the estoppel and laches defense (ANS p.6 ¶43). The plaintiff has no time pressure to settle on the nullity count. [VERIFY that the void, not merely unenforceable, theory is pleaded; ANS p.6 ¶42 pleads Art. 1403(1).]

**Item 7. Damages and attorney's-fees exposure.**

- *Record.* CMP pp.13 ¶¶61–64 and p.18 prayer, solidary (see D7). The plaintiff also invokes Art. 449: "He who builds, plants or sows in bad faith on the land of another, loses what is built, planted or sown without right to indemnity." (LAW cc.txt.)
- *Attorney's fees.* Art. 2208 lists the exceptions under which fees may be awarded (LAW cc.txt) [VERIFY which paragraph is pleaded].
- *Use.* This is stated only in pleadings and at court-annexed settlement. It is never communicated to defendants outside court.

**Item 8. CAM and JDR.**

- *Record.* NPT: "1. Court Annexed Mediation: (to be scheduled at the pre-trial) 2. Judicial Dispute Resolution: (to be scheduled at the pre-trial if deemed necessary by the court.)" Party representatives must be authorized to settle.
- *Unknown.* Whether CAM was held, and its outcome, is not known; the pre-trial order is not in hand [VERIFY the current stage].
- *Rule.* Civil Code Art. 2029: "The court shall endeavor to persuade the litigants in a civil case to agree upon some fair compromise." Art. 2030 provides for suspension where willingness to compromise is shown. Under the 2019 amendments and the 2020 CAM/JDR Guidelines, JDR after a failed CAM depends on the judge's view that settlement is still possible [VERIFY the text].

**Item 9. Separating interests through compromise.**

- *Rule.*
  - Civil Code Art. 2028: "A compromise is a contract whereby the parties, by making reciprocal concessions, avoid a litigation or put an end to one already commenced."
  - Art. 2037: a compromise has the effect of res judicata (LAW cc.txt).
  - Rule 130 §27 (1989 text; renumbered in 2019 [VERIFY]): "In civil cases, an offer of compromise is not an admission of any liability, and is not admissible in evidence against the offeror." (LAW rc_128-134.)
- *Use.*
  - Statements made in negotiation cannot be used as admissions. Any factual concession must be put in a signed stipulation or compromise filed in court.
  - [INFERRED] The defendants whose exposure differs from Gloria's (Erwin; possibly Efren; the occupants on the rents) are where interests diverge (D1–D7).
  - Approach only through Ramos, by Barandon, preferably within CAM/JDR.
- *Risk.*
  - Ramos represents all five defendants (ORD p.3).
  - Any proposal reaches all of them through him.
  - No concession may be tied to what a person will say on the stand.

**Item 10. Depositions (Rule 23).**

- *Rule.* Rule 23 §1: "By leave of court after jurisdiction has been obtained over any defendant or over property which is the subject of the action, or without such leave after an answer has been served, the testimony of any person, whether a party or not, may be taken, at the instance of any party, by deposition upon oral examination or written interrogatories." (LAW rc_1-71.) [VERIFY 2019 text.]
- *Use.* A deposition of Gloria or Efren in Canada, if the court admits Gloria's evidence by videoconference [CONDITIONAL on the ruling on MAN].
- *Risk.* Cost and timing.

**Excluded (not leverage, do not use):** criminal complaints; administrative complaints; ARTA/CSC; publicity; any direct contact by Jonathan with defendants, occupants, witnesses or Atty. Ramos; any statement that could read as a threat (RPC Arts. 282, 286).

#### 7. All legal issues in 26-360 (ours and the defense's)

**How to read the ratings.**

- **HIGH**: can decide the case or a whole claim by itself.
- **MED**: shifts a claim or the remedy, or decides a procedural gate.
- **LOW**: marginal, or credibility only.

The rating measures weight on the outcome, not which side it favours. "Favours" gives the current reading [INFERRED from the cited record].

**Law sources.** Statute text is as read in LAW and in Claude's WAR_OF_THE_STATUTES_2026-10.md ("WAR"). Case names are limited to those on CW/CASE_FILE/06_LAW/VERIFIED_AUTHORITIES.md ("VA"). Any other case is marked [UNVERIFIED].

##### 7.1 SPA ¶7 buyer class: HIGH

- **Text.** ¶7 authorizes sale "in favor of third persons in possession of CONTRACTS TO SELL executed by the previous administrator, MR. BEN LLAMANZARES and/or to those who have paid their downpayments or have made partial payments therefor to the latter … after prior consultation with us." (Exh. E ¶7, as quoted in WAR §2.)
- **Defense position.** The SPA "read as a whole" covers "qualified buyers" (ANS p.2 ¶9; p.5 ¶39). Class membership rests on the 1980s ₱3,000 (ANS p.5 ¶40).
- **Record against the defense:**
  - No Llamanzares contract or receipt is in the defense exhibits (ANS p.10 ¶61).
  - The ₱3,000 had "Wala pong ibinigay na resibo" (JA-G p.5 S22).
  - The earliest receipt is from 2003 (PB §2).
- **Parties.** Gloria and Efren (payors and buyer); Erwin (JA-E S12–S13); Princess (JA-P S7).
- **Strict construction.** *Bautista-Spille* (VA): "a power of attorney must be strictly construed."

##### 7.2 "Prior consultation with us": HIGH (proof risk)

- **Text.** Exh. E ¶7, last clause (WAR §2).
- **Record.** Gloria had no direct communication with Patricia, Geraldine or Marcia (JA-G p.8 S43).
- **Risk.** Proof of non-consultation through Jonathan alone may be attacked as hearsay (TRJ, demurrer row; OVR). Patricia's rebuttal is the planned answer (OVR) [CONDITIONAL].
- **Parties.** Gloria; Cesar's estate (not a party).

##### 7.3 Arts. 1898 / 1900 / 1902: HIGH

- **Text.**
  - Art. 1898: void "if the party with whom the agent contracted is aware of the limits of the powers granted by the principal" (VA).
  - Art. 1900 protects acts "within the terms of the power of attorney as written" (WAR §3).
  - Art. 1902: "Private or secret orders … do not prejudice third persons" (VA).
- **Record.**
  - The SPA was annotated on T-52540 on 21 Jun 1996 (Exh. B p.3; WAR §3), and PD 1529 §52 makes a registered instrument "constructive notice to all persons" (VA).
  - The Answer says "annotation of the SPA on TCT No. T-52540 was made only later" (ANS p.4 ¶32) [VERIFY against Exh. B].
  - Gloria says Cesar showed the "original" SPA (JA-G p.6 S30); Erwin says he saw it (JA-E S14).
- **Parties.** Gloria, Efren, Erwin.

##### 7.4 Void vs unenforceable: HIGH

- **Text.**
  - Art. 1874: "otherwise, the sale shall be void."
  - Art. 1409(7): "These contracts cannot be ratified."
  - Defense: Art. 1403(1) and Art. 1317 ¶2 (VA; ANS p.6 ¶42).
- **Case.** *Cosmic Lumber* (VA): "the sale ipso jure is consequently void"; a power of attorney "must so express the powers of the agent in clear and unmistakable language."
- **Parties.** All defendants, through the deed (DX6).

##### 7.5 Ratification by acceptance of proceeds: MED

- **Text.** Art. 1910 ¶2: "the principal is not bound except when he ratifies it expressly or tacitly." Art. 1911 (solidary liability where the principal "allowed" the agent) (VA).
- **Record.**
  - Payments went to Cesar and Salvador as collector (ANS p.6 ¶44(f); JA-G p.9 S45).
  - There is no record that any owner received or accepted proceeds (WAR §4) [VERIFY with Patricia's rebuttal].
  - The confirmation (Doc. 115, 28 Mar 2017) is the agent confirming himself (WAR §4; PB §2) [VERIFY].
- **Case.** *Unchuan* (VA): a void contract "cannot be ratified".
- **Parties.** Gloria, Efren; Salvador (witness).

##### 7.6 Revocation (15 Aug 2005), notice and publication: MED

- **Record.**
  - Revocation of 15 Aug 2005 (Exh. F).
  - Bicol Post publication, Sep–Oct 2020 (Exh. G), admitted, with retroactivity denied (ANS p.3 ¶18).
  - No notice before Sept 2016 (ANS p.3 ¶¶15, 19; JA-G pp.11–12 S60–S64).
- **Text.**
  - Arts. 1921, 1922; PD 1529 §64 ("Any instrument revoking such power of attorney shall be registered in like manner") (VA).
  - *Republic v. Ciruelas* and *Central Surety* are double-edged (VA).
- **Plan.** Argue that the buyer was outside the class first, so revocation is irrelevant. Use the 2020 publication only as a pre-registration notice fact, not as an Art. 1922 effect (WAR §5).
- **Parties.** Gloria, Efren; Princess (JA-P S11 "no objection").

##### 7.7 Partition power and subdivision: HIGH

- **Record.**
  - Partition dated 15 Mar 2017 (Doc. 402; PB §2), signed by no co-owner (MEMO 02_RFA) [VERIFY].
  - The plan it relies on, PSD-E2020005406, was surveyed 8 Sep 2020 and approved 6 Apr 2021 (DX1 p.2), after Cesar died on 21 Jun 2017 (PB §2).
  - Entry 2021003236 (Exh. B; WAR §7).
- **Defense.** "Ordinary process" (ANS p.3 ¶22); PD 1529 §48.
- **Parties.** Gloria (title); Princess (paid the survey, JA-P S8–S9); Erwin (alleged in CMP p.8 ¶35; denied, ANS ¶26).

##### 7.8 Buyer in good faith / innocent purchaser for value: HIGH

- **Text.** Arts. 526–528; PD 1529 §§51, 53 (VA).
- **Cases** (VA):
  - *Bucton*: "The burden of proving the status of a purchaser in good faith and for value lies upon one who asserts that status."
  - *Duenas* (En Banc): good faith runs "until registration of the sale on the title".
  - *San Pedro v. Ong*: duty to inquire.
  - *Yabut*: co-owned land.
  - *Sarili* and *Tolentino* are adverse; distinguish them (VA).
- **Record.**
  - Diligence claims: ANS p.4 ¶32; JA-G p.10 S56; p.11 S57.
  - Pre-registration facts:
    - the 28 May 2020 SPA (Answer Annex "1");
    - King's 9 Jul 2021 answer received by the Aquino Law Office (Annex N);
    - Elevado's Aug 2021 SPA pull (DX7 p.1 footer);
    - the 15 Sep 2021 "my property" SPA (Annex K p.9) (WAR §6; PB §2).
- **Parties.** Gloria (the buyer); Jomil (CC 8563) [VERIFY]; Juntilla (non-party).

- **Cases** (VA):

- **Effect.** Bears on SPA class (7.1), on good faith (7.8) and on improvements (7.17).

  - CMP ¶28 ("February 22, 2022") vs DX1 p.3 (22 Feb 2023).

##### 7.27 Gloria's citizenship: LOW (credibility only)

##### 7.28 Payee authority: receipts signed by a non-agent. MED (proof-dependent) [VERIFY: Jonathan statement 18 Oct]

**Statement (Jonathan, 18 Oct 2026; unverified; he holds no documents).** Salvador de la Fuente, Cesar's son, is the only person who signs the Balane receipts (Def. Exh. 5, signed "Authorized Collector" and "…fuente").

**Do not argue "no power to delegate".** Art. 1892 lets an agent appoint a substitute unless the principal prohibits it. The SPA is read as not prohibiting it [VERIFY the Exh. E text for any prohibition clause]. The point is framed in three parts below.

**Text** (Civil Code, LAW cc.txt, exact):

- **Art. 1892:** "The agent may appoint a substitute if the principal has not prohibited him from doing so; but he shall be responsible for the acts of the substitute: (1) When he was not given the power to appoint one; (2) When he was given such power, but without designating the person, and the person appointed was notoriously incompetent or insolvent. All acts of the substitute appointed against the prohibition of the principal shall be void."
- **Art. 1240:** "Payment shall be made to the person in whose favor the obligation has been constituted, or his successor in interest, or any person authorized to receive it."
- **Art. 1241 ¶2:** "Payment made to a third person shall also be valid insofar as it has redounded to the benefit of the creditor. Such benefit to the creditor need not be proved in the following cases: (1) If after the payment, the third person acquires the creditor's rights; (2) If the creditor ratifies the payment to the third person; (3) If by the creditor's conduct, the debtor has been led to believe that the third person had authority to receive the payment."

**What the record already shows:**

- The defendants paid "through Salvador as collector" (ANS p.6 ¶44(f)).
- Gloria: "Karaniwang ibinibigay po namin ang mga hulog kay Salvador Osum dela Fuente, na anak at nagsilbing collector at assistant ni Cesar" (JA-G p.9 S45). She also refers to receipts issued "ni Cesar M. de la Fuente o ni Salvador Osum dela Fuente" (S47).
- The first receipt (25 Oct 2003) is signed "Von dela Fuente" (PB §2; Annex D p.2). The 6 Jan 2005 "Down payment" was made to Salvador (PB §2; Annex D p.3).
- Only Cesar is named as attorney-in-fact (DX6 p.1: "CESAR M. DELA FUENTE … as Attorney-In-Fact"; Exh. E; DX7).

**The three-part framing:**

1. **No proof of appointment in the defense's evidence.**
   - No written appointment of Salvador by Cesar is among the defense exhibits (ANS p.10 ¶61).
   - Per the parent's instruction, the receipts bear no printed name or statement of authority beyond the signature block [VERIFY against the DX5 originals; Jonathan's statement mentions an "Authorized Collector" label].
   - **Caution:** Claude's playbook lists a 29 Feb 2012 paper by Cesar delegating to Salvador the 1991 SPA's powers, including collections with power to sign receipts (Annex Z; notary Elaine Gay R. Belen, Doc. 55/Pg. 17/Bk. IX/2012; PB §2). It is in "OFFER" status and not in evidence. If either side puts it in, point 1 narrows to:
     - it post-dates the 2005 revocation;
     - it refers to the 1991 SPA, not the 1992 SPA;
     - no owner signed it (PB §2).

     [VERIFY; Barandon to decide whether to use it.]
   - Receipts dated before 2012 (2003, 2005) have no appointment document at all on any reading [INFERRED from PB §2].
2. **A substitute holds no more than the agent.**
   - Under Art. 1892, Cesar is "responsible for the acts of the substitute".
   - [INFERRED] A substitute cannot receive more authority than the agent had. Any substitution is therefore bounded by:
     - the ¶7 class (7.1);
     - prior consultation (7.2);
     - the 15 Aug 2005 revocation (Exh. F; 7.6).
   - Payments to Salvador after 15 Aug 2005 were made to a substitute of a revoked agent [VERIFY dates against DX5].
3. **Did the proceeds reach the principals?**
   - Under Arts. 1240 and 1241 ¶2, payment to a third person counts only insofar as it "redounded to the benefit of the creditor", or on ratification, or on owner conduct that created the belief in authority.
   - No record fact shows any amount reaching Patricia, Geraldine or Marcia (WAR §4) [VERIFY with Patricia's rebuttal].
   - This also bears on ratification (7.5), price and receipts (7.10), and the "credited" down payment (7.1).

**Rating.** MED, proof-dependent. It rises if the originals and exemplars show Salvador signed every receipt, with no appointment document predating the payments and no remittance to the owners.

**Parties.** Gloria and Efren (payors); Princess (receipts in her name); Salvador (defense witness, ANS p.9 ¶59(f)); Cesar's estate (not a party).

**Cross questions** (Gloria if admitted, Princess, Salvador, Erwin as custodian):

- Who appointed the "Authorized Collector"?
- When?
- By what document? Is it produced?
- Did any of the money reach Patricia, Geraldine or Marcia? When, how much, and is there a receipt or remittance record?
- Who signed receipt No. 0228 "Full payment"?

**Proof plan (all through Barandon, in court):**

1. **Signature exemplars for Salvador:**
   - an ID;
   - notarized documents (Annex Z if used; his 25 Nov 2025 signed letter, PB §2);
   - court filings, including his JA in 26-360 (Annex Q) and any SP 2156 filings [VERIFY whether he signed any].
2. **Rule 26 request for admission, new item:** "Admit that Salvador de la Fuente signed Def. Exh. 5 as collector." Companion items:
   - "Admit that no receipt in Def. Exh. 5 bears the signature of any of the registered owners."
   - "Admit that no written appointment of Salvador de la Fuente by Cesar M. de la Fuente is among Defendants' exhibits."

   These fit MEMO 02_RFA. The hold-versus-serve conflict in 6.3 applies.
3. **Subpoena duces tecum (Rule 21) or production (Rule 27)** for the receipt originals, any collector's ledger or remittance record, and any appointment document (MEMO 03_PRODUCTION; JA-E p.5 S21, where Erwin says the receipts are in his custody).

#### 8. Top gaps (all subjects)

1. **Pre-trial order of 13 May 2026.** Needed for stipulations, marked exhibits, the CAM/JDR referral, and whether the Efren and Jomil JAs were filed (IDX).

#### Request for Issuance of Subpoenas Duces Tecum and Ad Testificandum 
(Rule 21, Sections 1 to 4, Rules of Court; Section 5, Judicial Affidavit Rule)

Plaintiff, through counsel, respectfully states:

1. 2. The plaintiff's documentary case rests on registered instruments, notarial records, survey records, permit records and tax records in the custody of public offices. Plaintiff's representative has requested certified copies from several of these offices; some records have been furnished in part and others not at all. The custodians are government officers who are neither witnesses of the adverse party nor hostile witnesses, and plaintiff therefore avails of the subpoena under Section 5 of the Judicial Affidavit Rule and Rule 21 of the Rules of Court.

3. Plaintiff respectfully requests that subpoenas duces tecum and ad testificandum issue to the following custodians, each to appear on **December 2, 2026 at 1:30 in the afternoon**, or on such date as the Court may fix, and to bring the records described, which are described with the particularity the plaintiff can give from the public entries on Transfer Certificate of Title No. T-52540 (Exhibit "B"):

No. 402, Page No. 81, Book No. CXXII, Series of 2017, of Notary Public Donald E. Asis**, with every page, signature, acknowledgment and attachment; (iii) the complete registration file of Entry No. 2021003235, including the Special Power of Attorney of Gloria H. Balane in favor of Karen Gay A. Elevado dated September 15, 2021; (iv) the record of the surrender, upon the registration of November 23, 2021, of the owner's duplicate of TCT No. T-52540 issued under Entry No. 2017000693; and (v) if any of the foregoing is not on file, a certification to that effect. _Relevance:_ these records establish who presented the Deed of Absolute Sale of September 29, 2016 and the partition for registration more than four years after the death of the agent who executed them, and whether any of the registered owners executed or consented to the partition on which TCT No. 079-2021002126 was issued.

Elaine Gay R. Belen**: Doc. No. 560, Page No. 112, Book No. XI, Series of 2016, and Doc. No. 55, Page No. 17, Book No. IX, Series of 2012; (iii) the register entry for Doc. No. 019, Page No. 04, Book No. XIV, Series of 2021 of whichever notary public of Daet holds it; (iv) the complete record of **Special Proceeding No. 2156** of Branch 41, including the order of November 2016 in full and the certificate of finality of March 13, 2017; and (v) the complete record of **Civil Case No. 8563** of Branch 41, including the complaint and the Verified Answer received July 9, 2021 with its annexes. _Relevance:_ the register entries show who appeared before the notary and when, for the deed, the confirmation and the partition on which the defendants' title rests; the Special Proceeding record shows what the agent alleged and who was notified; the Civil Case 8563 record shows service, on counsel for the defendants' attorney-in-fact, of the published revocation of the agent's authority before registration.

Balane or Ferdinand Balane, from 1975 to date; or a certification that no such permit or application is on file. _Relevance:_ the defendants claim to be builders in good faith entitled to indemnity and retention; a permit application shows what right over the land the applicant claimed and when; the absence of any permit bears on the same claim. 8. **The Provincial Prosecutor, Camarines Norte, Daet**: the records of **NPS-V-09-INV-2020F-00383 and NPS-V-09-INV-2020G-00417**, including the counter-affidavit of Nelly H. Juntilla and the letter of counsel dated February 11, 2020 annexed to it. _Relevance:_ the identity of the counsel acting for defendant Gloria H. 10. **The Election Officer, COMELEC, Mercedes, and the Secretary to the Sangguniang Bayan, Mercedes**: a certification of the elective positions and terms held by Gloria H. Balane (Barangay Kagawad, 1997 to 2004; Sangguniang Bayan Member, 2004 to 2017, as she has alleged), and a certified copy of Sangguniang Bayan Resolution No. 103-86 dated July 16, 1986 with its attendance. _Relevance:_ the defendants' opportunity to know who owned the land and who administered it.

11. The records requested are relevant to the issues of the authority of the agent who executed the Deed of Absolute Sale of September 29, 2016, the good faith of the defendants up to the registration of November 23, 2021, the validity of the partition on which TCT No. 079-2021002126 was issued, and the defendants' claim to indemnity for improvements, all of which are in issue under the pleadings. They are in the exclusive custody of the offices named. The requests are made now so that the returns are in before the plaintiff's last two settings of December 2 and 9, 2026.

**REQUEST FOR ADMISSION**

TO: Defendants, through their counsel, ATTY. RONALD A. RAMOS.

Plaintiff, through counsel, under Rule 26 of the Rules of Court, requests each of the defendants to admit, within fifteen (15) days from service hereof, the genuineness of the documents described and exhibited below and the truth of the facts set forth below. Each matter of which an admission is requested shall be deemed admitted unless, within that period, the defendant to whom it is directed files and serves a sworn statement either denying it specifically or setting forth in detail the reasons why he or she cannot truthfully either admit or deny it (Rule 26, Section 2).

**A. GENUINENESS OF DOCUMENTS**

1. The Special Power of Attorney dated May 28, 2020, executed by defendant Gloria H. Balane before the Philippine Consulate General in Toronto, attached to the defendants' Answer as Annex "1".

2. The Special Power of Attorney executed by defendant Gloria H. Balane in favor of Karen Gay A. Elevado, on file with the Registry of Deeds for the Province of Camarines Norte under Entry No. 2021003235, a copy of which is attached as Annex "A".

3. The receipt dated December 29, 2006 for "Partial payment for subdivision survey," attached as Annex "B".

4. The receipt dated January 6, 2005, marked "Down payment," in the amount of Twenty Thousand Pesos (₱20,000.00), attached as Annex "C".

5. Receipt No. 0228, marked "Full payment," issued in February 2017, attached as Annex "D".

**B. TRUTH OF FACTS**

1. Neither defendant Gloria H. Balane nor defendant Efren Balane holds any contract to sell over the subject lot executed by Benjamin Llamanzares.

2. Neither defendant Gloria H. Balane nor defendant Efren Balane holds any receipt issued by Benjamin Llamanzares for any payment toward the purchase of the subject lot.

3. Before September 29, 2016, the defendants occupied the subject lot as lessees and paid monthly rent for it.

4. Before September 29, 2016, neither defendant Gloria H. Balane nor anyone acting for her communicated with Geraldine K. Hoppe, Patricia K. Zschoche or Marcia Ellen Keesey about the purchase of the subject lot.

5. The price of Two Hundred Fifty Thousand Pesos (₱250,000.00) recited in the Deed of Absolute Sale dated September 29, 2016 as "in hand paid" had not been fully paid on that date.

6. Cesar M. de la Fuente died on June 21, 2017.

7. The subdivision survey under plan (LRA) PSD-E2020005406 was conducted on September 8, 2020, and the plan was approved on April 6, 2021.

8. On August 16, 2021, Karen Gay A. Elevado, acting for defendant Gloria H. Balane, obtained from the Registry of Deeds a certified copy of the Special Power of Attorney dated March 16, 1992.

9. No building permit was issued by the Office of the Building Official of Mercedes for any structure built by any of the defendants on the subject lot.

10. The owner's duplicate certificate of Transfer Certificate of Title No. 079-2021002127 is in the possession of one or more of the defendants.

11. Rooms on the subject lot are rented out by one or more of the defendants, and rentals have been received since August 22, 2025.

12. The Deed of Absolute Sale dated September 29, 2016 does not state the number of any certificate of title.

13. None of Geraldine K. Hoppe, Patricia K. Zschoche or Marcia Ellen Keesey signed the Deed of Confirmation dated March 28, 2017 or the Partition-Subdivision Agreement dated March 15, 2017.

Mercedes, Camarines Norte, ____ October 2026.

BARANDON LAW OFFICES 
Counsel for Plaintiff 
Traders Road corner Shoppers' Street, Central Plaza Complex, 
Barangay Lag-On, Daet, Camarines Norte 4600 
 
By: 
 
______________________________ 
[Name, Roll No., PTR, IBP, MCLE]

Copy furnished: 
ATTY. RONALD A. RAMOS, Counsel for Defendants, [address]

Item 8 repeats Answer ¶¶40 and 43 so the sworn reply cannot narrow it. Item 11 rests on our records; the PSA certificate is being obtained. Item 13 rests on the footer of Defendants' Exhibit "7", page 1. Items 17 and 18 rest on pages 11 and 16 of the Registry file. Rule 26 §5 bars evidence on facts "within the personal knowledge" of the adverse party if no request was served, unless the court allows it for good cause, so serving now also protects our own proof.

Plaintiff, through counsel, respectfully states:

**Section:** I. The documents

#### I. The documents

1. Defendants have marked as Exhibit "5" a set of receipts: 33 pages of photocopies or scans showing 111 receipts dated 25 October 2003 to February 2017 (Def. Exh. 5 pp.1–33).
2. Of these, 43 are printed forms headed "HEIRS OF MARY W. KEESEY ESTATE … TIN-[TIN withheld]-Non Vat". They are serially numbered, from No. 0019 to No. 0228, and printed "Authorized Collector" (Def. Exh. 5 pp.9–11, 20, 23–33). The rest are on generic receipt forms (pp.2–22). One is a surveyor's receipt (p.1).
3. Defendants' witness Salvador Osum dela Fuente states that he issued the receipts in Exhibit "5" and collected the payments (Judicial Affidavit of Salvador Osum dela Fuente, Q&A 18–22).
4. The receipts are offered "[t]o prove the identification of the receipts for partial payments and subdivision survey" (Judicial Affidavit of Salvador Osum dela Fuente, statement of purposes, item 6 [VERIFY numbering]). The deed in issue recites a price of ₱250,000.00 "in hand paid" on its date, 29 September 2016 (Def. Exh. 6 p.1).


**Document:** [Grok desk, HELD draft] Motion for production and examination of Def. Exh. 5 (parallel to Claude's held Rule 27 draft, 17_)  
**Source:** `CASE_FILE/PARTIES/DRAFT_MOTION_PRODUCTION_EXAMINATION_DEF_EXH5_2026-10-18.md`  
**Section:** II. The rules

---

#### II. The rules

5. **Rule 27, Section 1** provides [VERIFY 2019 text]:


6. **Rule 132, Section 22** provides [VERIFY 2019 text]:

> "The handwriting of a person may be proved by any witness who believes it to be the handwriting of such person because he has seen the person write, or has seen writing purporting to be his upon which the witness has acted or been charged, and has thus acquired knowledge of the handwriting of such person. Evidence respecting the handwriting may also be given by a comparison, made by the witness or the court, with writings admitted or treated as genuine by the party against whom the evidence is offered, or proved to be genuine to the satisfaction of the judge."

7. **Rule 130 on expert opinion** provides that "[t]he opinion of a witness on a matter requiring special knowledge, skill, experience or training which he is shown to possess, may be received in evidence" (Rule 130, Sec. 49 of the 1989 Rules [VERIFY 2019 section number and text]).

8. **Rule 29, Section 3** provides that if a party refuses to obey an order under Rule 27, "the court may make such orders in regard to the refusal as are just" [VERIFY 2019 text].

**Source:** `CASE_FILE/PARTIES/DRAFT_MOTION_PRODUCTION_EXAMINATION_DEF_EXH5_2026-10-18.md`  
**Section:** III. Application: good cause

---

#### III. Application: good cause

9. **Materiality.** The receipts are defendants' proof of payment, so their authenticity, dates and authorship are matters "involved in the action" (Rule 27, Sec. 1).

10. **The originals are needed.** The copies on record cannot show ink, pressure, stroke order, alterations, paper or printing. They cannot show whether the serially numbered forms were filled in on the dates they bear. Specifically, on the face of the copies:
    - (a) the serial numbers do not follow the dates in several places. Examples: No. 0122 is dated 30 June 2014 and No. 0121 is dated 31 July 2014. No. 0130 is dated 9 December 2014 and No. 0129 is dated 31 March 2016. No. 0220 is dated September 2016, and Nos. 0219 and 0218 are dated November 2016. No. 0227 is dated 7 January 2017 and No. 0226 is dated 30 January 2017 (Def. Exh. 5 pp.26–33);
    - (b) No. 0136 is dated May 2016 and the next receipt, No. 0217, is dated August 2016 (p.30);
    - (c) nine receipts totalling ₱219,000.00 bear dates after 29 September 2016. One of them, No. 0228, is marked "Full payment" (pp.31–33);
    - (d) several dates show strike-throughs or overwriting (pp.16, 18, 20).
11. **The documents are in defendants' possession, custody or control.** Defendants attached and marked them, and their witness says he issued them (Q&A 20–22).
12. **The examination requires a qualified examiner.** The questions are technical: ink, writing sequence, paper and printing, and comparison of handwriting under Rule 132, Sec. 22. Rule 130 allows the opinion of a witness with the needed special knowledge.
13. **No prejudice.** The examination proposed is non-destructive first. Any sampling would occur only with separate leave. The documents stay under court-ordered custody throughout. Plaintiff bears the costs.

**Source:** `CASE_FILE/PARTIES/DRAFT_MOTION_PRODUCTION_EXAMINATION_DEF_EXH5_2026-10-18.md`  
**Section:** IV. Proposed terms

#### IV. Proposed terms

14. **Production.** Defendants produce the originals of all receipts in Exhibit "5" before the Branch Clerk of Court on a date the Court sets. They also produce any receipt booklets, stubs or duplicates from which those receipts came, if in their possession, custody or control. The Branch Clerk inventories and photographs them in the presence of both counsel.
15. **Examiner.** The originals are examined by the PNP Forensic Group, Questioned Document Examination Division, Camp BGen Rafael T. Crame, Quezon City, on a request or order of this Court. In the alternative, they are examined by a private questioned-documents examiner whom the Court approves after both parties are heard on qualifications.
16. **Scope** (Annex "A"). Non-destructive examination: magnification, ultraviolet and infrared examination, indentation analysis, comparison of paper and printing, and comparison of handwriting and signatures with standards admitted or proved genuine under Rule 132, Sec. 22.
17. **Sampling only with leave.** Removal of ink or paper samples, including solvent-extraction ink-age testing, will not take place without a separate motion, notice and hearing, and a specific order identifying the entries and the method.
17-A. **Separate request: leave to take micro-samples for ink-age analysis.** Plaintiff separately asks leave to have micro-samples (hollow-needle plugs about 0.5 mm in diameter, taken from ink lines away from signatures where possible) removed from up to ten (10) designated receipts, and from control entries on the same paper stock, for chemical ink analysis:
    - (a) ink comparison (thin-layer chromatography, ASTM E1422 / SWGDOC);
    - (b) ink identification against a reference library for first date of manufacture (ASTM E1789 / SWGDOC); and
    - (c) relative ink-age analysis by gas chromatography–mass spectrometry of volatile solvent components (2-phenoxyethanol), to determine whether the entries are consistent with their stated dates or were written within the period such methods can detect.

    The analysis would be done by a laboratory named by the Court after both parties are heard. Plaintiff proposes Riley, Welch, LaPorte & Associates (Frankenmuth, Michigan, USA) or Aginsky Forensic Document Dating Laboratory, Inc. (USA), or another qualified laboratory the Court approves [VERIFY availability and engagement terms before filing].

    Sampling is done in the presence of both counsel and the Branch Clerk, photographed before and after, with each plug's location mapped. Samples travel sealed by international courier with a chain-of-custody form signed at each hand-off. Defendants may have an expert observe, and may request split samples.

    **Urgency.** Published solvent-loss methods detect recent writing only within a limited window after the ink is placed on paper, reported in the range of months to about two years depending on the ink and method. If the entries were written recently, any delay in sampling permanently reduces or destroys the evidential value of this test. Plaintiff therefore asks that this request be resolved promptly and, if granted, that sampling be scheduled immediately after production.

18. **Custody and chain of custody.**
    - (a) The originals pass from the Branch Clerk to the examiner and back only under a written receipt signed at each transfer, stating date, time, items and persons.
    - (b) They are transported by a court-designated officer, sealed in an envelope signed across the seal by both counsel.
    - (c) The examiner keeps them in secure storage and returns them with an inventory within the period the Court sets.
    - (d) Counsel for both parties, or their representatives, may observe the opening and resealing.
    - (e) Each item is photographed before and after examination.
19. **Standards.** Plaintiff will submit standards for comparison: certified copies, and originals where available, of instruments signed by Cesar M. de la Fuente. Plaintiff asks that Salvador Osum dela Fuente be directed to give writing and signature specimens before the Branch Clerk, or to identify admitted writings, as the Court finds proper [VERIFY practice].
20. **Costs.** All examination and transport costs are borne by plaintiff, without prejudice to later taxation.
21. **Report.** The examiner's report is furnished to the Court and both parties. The examiner may be called to testify.

#### ANNEX "A" — Proposed scope of examination (questions for the examiner)

1. **Ink sameness:** Are the entries and the signature on each receipt in one ink? Which receipts share an ink formulation? Priority:
   - (a) the receipts signed "Von dela Fuente" (Def. Exh. 5 pp.2–3);
   - (b) the "Heirs of Mary W. Keesey Estate" receipts dated 2013–2017 (pp.23–33);
   - (c) Nos. 0222–0228 (pp.31–33).
2. **Relative ink age:** only upon further leave (¶17). Whether any entry was written within the period the chosen method can detect. Whether any ink formulation post-dates the date written.
3. **Alterations and sequence:** strike-throughs and overwriting on the dates (pp.16, 18, 20). Whether these entries were written at the same sitting as the rest of each receipt.
4. **Paper and printing:**
   - Printer imprint and authority numbers on the printed forms ("4C's Press-Daet", "Auth. No. 451-1998", "RD64-016-99", "25 Bks. (50x2) 0001-1250"), compared with the dates written on them.
   - Whether Nos. 0136 and 0217 come from the same print run.
   - Whether any generic form or paper is of later manufacture than its written date.
5. **Serial and booklet sequence:** whether the receipts were written in serial order. Whether indentations on any receipt correspond to the next receipt in the series. If the stubs or duplicates are produced, their payors and dates, especially Nos. 0137–0216.
6. **Handwriting and signatures:** comparison of the collector signatures (both forms) and the body entries with standards of Salvador Osum dela Fuente and with standards of Cesar M. de la Fuente. Whether one writer is responsible for the two signature forms.
7. **Heading and TIN:** whether the heading and TIN on the printed forms are part of the original printing, and are consistent across all forms.

- **The ink-age prayer loses value with every month of delay.** Jonathan's theory is that the entries were written near the 2026 production. If the receipts were written in, say, early 2026, the window may close during 2027.

Recommendation for Barandon's decision: file the production motion with (c-1) as soon as the pre-trial order allows. The surprise value is in the results, not the request.
- **First confirm the inks are ballpoint.** GC-MS 2-PE methods suit ballpoint inks containing 2-phenoxyethanol, and are of limited use for many gel or felt inks. The non-destructive examination (¶16) should establish ink type before samples are taken.
- **Engagement.** No laboratory has been contacted. Engagement terms, cost and availability need confirming through Chief of Staff, with Jonathan's go-ahead.

#### I. Rule

1. **Rule 21, Section 1** [VERIFY 2019 text]: "Subpoena is a process directed to a person requiring him to attend and to testify at the hearing or the trial of an action … It may also require him to bring with him any books, documents, or other things under his control, in which case it is called a subpoena duces tecum."
2. **Rule 21, Section 3** [VERIFY 2019 text]: "A subpoena shall state the name of the court and the title of the action or investigation, shall be directed to the person whose attendance is required, and in the case of a subpoena duces tecum, it shall also contain a reasonable description of the books, documents or things demanded which must appear to the court prima facie relevant."
3. **Rule 21, Section 4** [VERIFY 2019 text]: the court may quash a subpoena duces tecum "if it is unreasonable and oppressive, or the relevancy of the books, documents or things does not appear, or if the person in whose behalf the subpoena is issued fails to advance the reasonable cost of the production thereof."
4. **Rule 21, Section 6** [VERIFY 2019 text]: service is to be made by tendering "the fees for one day's attendance and the kilometrage allowed by these Rules", and "[i]f the subpoena is duces tecum, the reasonable cost of producing the books, documents or things demanded shall also be tendered."
